| Farm-side marketplace listings you post (AgriFood Market, AgriSupply, AgriServices) | This browser only | Back up |
| Bookings and saved items on the Agro Dealers, Equipment Hire and Vet Services directories | This browser only (the directory itself may load from the platform) | Back up |
| Backup history ("Last backup") | This browser only | |
| Saved items on the individual customer page | Not saved at all | Lost on reload |

> **Tip:** If a colleague says "I can't see what you entered", ask which page it was. If it is in the browser-only list, the data really is on your device only. Until those pages are moved to the company account, enter such data on one agreed computer and make regular backups.

> **Note:** Automatic entries created by production (for example a purchase that posts a cost to Finance, or a harvest that adds stock) wait in a small queue in the browser for a moment before they are delivered to the company account. If you were offline, they are delivered when you are next connected.

# Part 2 — Templates, Record Fields and Care Guides

## About this part

Templates are the heart of AGRINUXES for farmers and cooperatives. A template is a ready-made description of one kind of farming: how a broiler batch, a tilapia pond, a maize field or a dairy herd normally runs, what you should write down every day, and what you should feed, vaccinate, spray or check on which day.

This part explains, step by step:

- what a template is and what is inside it;
- how to choose templates when you set up and later when you add a new enterprise;
- how to start a cycle from a template;
- how the Daily Log gets its boxes from the template, and what every box means;
- how the Care Plan tab reminds you what to do each day;
- how to change the boxes and the care guide to match **your own way of farming**, and save the result as **your own template**;
- how to share and reuse your saved templates;
- how to load many days of records at once from a spreadsheet.

This part applies to **Farmer / Farm** and **Cooperative** accounts, which get the farm workspace. Other business types (agro dealers, vets, equipment hire, transport and so on) use vendor workspaces and are covered in Parts 6 and 7.

> **Note:** For how cycles, stages, events and outputs work once a cycle is running, see Part 3 — Running Production: Cycles, Stages, Events and Outputs. This part concentrates on the template side: choosing, understanding and customising.

---

## Understanding templates

### What a template is

**What it is for.** A template saves you from starting with a blank page. When you pick "Broiler Chicken" you do not have to invent the stages of a broiler batch, the measurements to take, or a vaccination calendar. The template already contains a sensible starting point, and you can then adjust it.

There are two kinds of template:

| Kind | What it is | Marked as |
|---|---|---|
| **Built-in** | One of the 94 templates that come with AGRINUXES. Everyone has the same ones. You cannot change a built-in template itself. | **Built-in** |
| **My template** | A copy of a built-in template that your company has customised and saved. It carries your own daily boxes and your own care guide. | **My template** (with a version number, for example **My template · v2**) |

Built-in templates are never altered. When you customise one and save it, AGRINUXES makes a copy and stores it with your company. Your copy remembers which built-in template it came from.

### What a template contains

Every template holds the following pieces.

| Piece | What it means for you | Where you see it |
|---|---|---|
| **Name, icon and colour** | How the template is shown, for example "Tomato" with a tomato icon. | Add Enterprise, New Cycle, cycle header |
| **Stages** | The steps a cycle goes through, each with a typical length in days. Example: Brooding (14 days), Grower (14 days), Finishing (14 days), Harvest / Sale (1 day) for broilers. | New Cycle (step 1 preview), **Stages** tab |
| **Measurements** | What should be measured at each stage, with units, how often (daily, weekly, per event) and a target or normal range where one exists. | **Stages** tab, under "Measurements to track" |
| **Key activities** | A checklist-style list of the jobs in each stage, such as "Place chicks" or "Manage brooder temperature". | **Stages** tab, under "Key activities" |
| **Alerts** | Short warning messages for the stage, marked as information, warning or critical. Example: "High mortality — check temperature, ventilation and disease." | **Stages** tab |
| **Expected outputs** | What the stage produces (for example live broilers) and where it can go: sale, inventory, processing, incubation, hatchery, transfer, waste or own use. | **Stages** tab, "Expected outputs" |
| **Production KPIs** | The key figures for this kind of farming with a benchmark, such as Feed Conversion Ratio, Mortality Rate or Average Body Weight. | **Overview** tab, "Production KPIs" |
| **Daily record fields** | The boxes on the Daily Log. | **Daily Log** tab |
| **Care guide** | The feeding, water, vaccination, medicine, spray, fertiliser, weighing, check and task reminders, each on a day counted from the start of the cycle. | **Care Plan** tab |

The last two pieces, **daily record fields** and **care guide**, are the ones you can change and save as your own. Stages, measurements, activities, alerts, outputs and KPIs are fixed in the template (see "Limits" in the chapter "My templates").

> **Note:** The built-in daily fields are shared by all templates of the same category. For example, every aquaculture template (tilapia, catfish, shrimp and so on) starts with the same aquaculture daily boxes. The care guide, however, is different for each template.

### Enterprise, template and cycle

People often mix these three words up. They are three different layers:

- A **template** is the recipe. It describes a kind of farming in general. Example: "Broiler Chicken".
- An **enterprise** is one operation on your farm that follows a template. You name it and give it a location. Example: "Sunrise Broilers, House A".
- A **cycle** (also called a batch, season or production run) is one run of that enterprise from start to finish, with a start date. Example: "Broiler Oct 2026".

In words, the picture looks like this:

1. Your **company** (the farm business account) owns many **enterprises**.
2. Each **enterprise** points to exactly one **template** (built-in, or one of your saved versions of it).
3. Each **enterprise** can have many **cycles**, one after another or several at the same time.
4. Each **cycle** has its own **stages**, **daily records**, **events**, **care ticks** and **outputs**.

For example:

> **Example:** Mwansa farms near Chongwe. The company has two enterprises. "Chongwe Broilers" follows the Broiler Chicken template. "Maize Block A" follows the Maize (Field Crop) template. Chongwe Broilers has three cycles so far: "Broiler Jun 2026" (completed), "Broiler Aug 2026" (completed) and "Broiler Oct 2026" (active). The Daily Log of "Broiler Oct 2026" uses the broiler boxes; the Care Plan counts days from the start date of that cycle only.

Two consequences are worth remembering:

- Changing the template that an enterprise uses changes the Daily Log boxes and the Care Plan for **all** cycles of that enterprise, including running ones. Records you already saved stay saved.
- The stages of a cycle are copied from the template **when the cycle is created**. They do not change afterwards.

### The 14 template categories

The 94 built-in templates are grouped into 14 categories. Each category has its own set of daily boxes.

| Category | Templates | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Poultry & Birds** | 12 | Broiler Chicken, Layer Chicken, Indigenous / Village Chicken, Turkey, Quail, Ostrich |
| **Livestock** | 9 | Dairy Cattle, Beef Cattle, Pig Production, Meat Goat, Rabbit |
| **Aquaculture** | 6 | Tilapia (Pond), Catfish (Tank / Pond), Aquaponics System |
| **Crops & Grains** | 15 | Maize (Field Crop), Soybean, Groundnut, Cassava, Sugarcane |
| **Horticulture** | 12 | Tomato, Onion, Cabbage, Kale & Spinach, Carrot, Garlic |
| **Greenhouse** | 5 | Cut Flower Rose, Hydroponic Lettuce & Microgreens, Strawberry (Greenhouse) |
| **Orchards & Tree Fruit** | 9 | Mango Orchard, Avocado Orchard, Banana, Macadamia |
| **Apiary & Beekeeping** | 2 | Honey Bee (Langstroth Hive), Honey Bee (Top-Bar / Kenya Hive) |
| **Mushroom Cultivation** | 2 | Oyster Mushroom, Button Mushroom |
| **Insects & Worms** | 7 | Black Soldier Fly (BSF), Cricket Farming, Vermiculture (Earthworms), Stingless Bee |
| **Aquatic Plants & Algae** | 2 | Seaweed Farming, Spirulina / Microalgae |
| **Forestry & Agroforestry** | 2 | Timber Forestry, Agroforestry / Silvopastoral |
| **Agro-Processing** | 6 | Maize Milling, Dairy Processing, On-Farm Feed Mill |
| **Agricultural Services** | 5 | Veterinary Clinic / Mobile Vet Services, Agricultural Equipment Hire, Farmer Training & Extension Services |

The full list of all 94 templates is in the template catalogue at the end of this manual.

Notes on the table:

- **Every one of the 94 templates comes with a starter care guide.** The number of items ranges from about 5 to 23 per template.
- The Stingless Bee template sits under **Insects & Worms**, not under Apiary.
- Irish Potato, Sweet Potato and Cassava are under **Crops & Grains**; Tomato, Onion and Carrot are under **Horticulture**; Banana, Pawpaw and Moringa are under **Orchards & Tree Fruit**.

---

## Choosing templates

### At set-up (onboarding)

**What it is for.** When you first create a Farmer / Farm or Cooperative account, the set-up wizard asks what your farm operates and creates one enterprise for each operation you choose. Other business types skip this step and choose the products or services they offer instead (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account).

**Before you start.** You have chosen **Farmer / Farm** or **Cooperative** as your business type and entered your business name, country and currency.

**Step by step**

1. In the set-up wizard, reach step 4 of 5, called **Setup**. The heading reads **What does your farm operate?**
2. Click one or more category cards. Each card shows an icon, the category name and a short description. A tick appears on the cards you select. You can select as many as you like.
3. Under every selected category a box appears listing its templates. Each template row shows its icon, name and a one-line description.
4. Click a template row to add an enterprise from it. The enterprise appears in the green **Added enterprises** box, named after the template with " 1" at the end, for example "Broiler Chicken 1".
5. Click the same template again to add a second enterprise of the same kind (it will also be named with " 1" at the end, so rename it).
6. In the **Added enterprises** box, click the name of an enterprise and type a better name, such as "Chongwe Broilers". Click the small **✕** to remove an enterprise you added by mistake.
7. When you have at least one enterprise, click **Finish setup**.
8. On the final screen, **You're all set!**, check the list of your enterprises and click **Open my workspace**.

**What you will see**

| Message or control | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Select at least one category to continue.** | You have not ticked any category card yet. |
| **Add at least one enterprise above to continue.** | You ticked categories but have not clicked any template row. **Finish setup** stays greyed out. |
| **Added enterprises (n)** | The list of enterprises that will be created when you finish. |

**Good to know**

- The set-up wizard shows all 14 categories.
- There is no search box at set-up. Use the category cards.
- The enterprise gets one of eight colours automatically.
- You cannot enter a location at set-up. You can add enterprises later with a location (next section).
- At set-up you only see built-in templates, because your company has not saved any of its own yet.
- Nothing is lost if you pick the wrong template: add the right one later and stop using the other one.

**If something goes wrong.** If **Finish setup** is greyed out, you have not added any enterprise. Click a template row under a selected category.

### Later: Add Enterprise (Settings)

**What it is for.** You can add a new enterprise at any time, for example when you start fish farming next to your maize. This is also the place where your own saved templates appear.

**Before you start.** You need access to **Settings** in the left menu (owners and directors have it; other team members depend on the permissions given in Team & Permissions).

**Step by step**

1. In the left menu, click **Settings**. The page is titled **Platform Settings**.
2. Find the **Enterprises (n)** box and click **Add Enterprise** (top right of the box).
3. In the **Add Enterprise** window, type an **Enterprise Name** (required), for example "Mpongwe Fish Ponds".
4. Under **Production Template**, narrow the list. You can click a category tab, type in the search box, or both. The category tabs are **All**, **Poultry & Birds**, **Livestock**, **Aquaculture**, **Crops**, **Horticulture**, **Greenhouse**, **Orchard**, **Apiary**, **Mushroom**, **Processing** and **Services**.
5. Click a template tile to select it. A green tick appears on it. The bottom of the window shows its name and category.
6. Optionally type a **Location (optional)** such as "Plot 5, Kabwe Road", and **Notes (optional)**.
7. Click **Add Enterprise** at the bottom right. The button changes to **Saved!** and the window closes by itself.

**What you will see**

- Your company's own saved templates are listed **first**, each tile marked with a small green **My template** label. The built-in templates follow.
- The search box matches the template name or the species (for example typing "tilapia" or "chicken").
- When nothing matches, the grid says **No templates match your search**.
- The bottom bar says **No template selected** until you click a tile.
- **Add Enterprise** stays greyed out until both a name and a template are chosen.

**What is created.** Only the enterprise: its name, the template it follows, a colour taken from the template, an "active" marker, and the location and notes if you typed them. **No cycle is created.** The new enterprise appears in the **Enterprises** list in Settings and can now be chosen when you start a new cycle in **Production**.

**Good to know**

- The category tabs in this window do not include Insects & Worms, Aquatic Plants & Algae or Forestry & Agroforestry. Those templates still appear under **All** and when you search, for example "cricket", "seaweed" or "timber".
- An enterprise follows one template. To grow a different crop or animal, add a different enterprise.
- You can add as many enterprises from the same template as you like, for example three broiler houses.

> **Tip:** Name enterprises after the place or purpose ("Chongwe Broilers", "Block A Maize"), not after the template. The template name is always shown underneath anyway.

---

## Cycle types

### What a cycle type is

**What it is for.** Within one category there are different ways of running the same kind of enterprise. A poultry enterprise might be a 42-day broiler batch, a flock of layers you buy as day-old chicks, or an egg-hatching run in an incubator. Each of these needs different information. When you start a cycle you choose the **cycle type** (the wizard asks **What type of cycle?**). The choice does three things:

1. It suggests how long the cycle will last, which fills in **Expected End** for you.
2. It decides which **setup questions** appear in step 2 of the wizard.
3. It decides which **daily boxes** and which **care guide items** are shown, where the template defines variants (this matters most for poultry; see below).

### Cycle types for every category

The wizard lists the types below. The number is the usual length in days that the wizard suggests.

| Category | Cycle types (suggested days) |
|---|---|
| **Poultry & Birds** | **Broiler Grow-Out** (42), **Layer — from Day-Old** (500), **Layer — from Point-of-Lay** (380), **Layer — Continuing Flock** (90), **Egg Incubation & Hatch** (22), **Breeder Flock** (365), **Turkey Grow-Out** (120), **Duck Cycle** (60), **Quail Cycle** (90) |
| **Livestock** | **Dairy Production Season** (305), **Beef Grow-Out** (180), **Pig Grow-Out** (120), **Sow Farrowing Cycle** (90), **Goat Production Season** (180), **Sheep Season** (120), **Rabbit Batch** (80) |
| **Aquaculture** | **Pond Grow-Out** (180), **Cage Culture** (150), **Tank / RAS System** (120), **Shrimp Pond Cycle** (100), **Aquaponics Cycle** (120), **Hatchery / Nursery Cycle** (45) |
| **Crops & Grains** | **Rain-Fed Season** (120), **Irrigated Season** (90), **Nursery / Seedbed** (30), **Ratoon Crop** (180) |
| **Horticulture** | **Transplant Cycle** (90), **Direct-Sown Crop** (75), **Perennial Season** (120) |
| **Greenhouse** | **Hydroponic Cycle** (35), **Greenhouse Tomato** (180), **Cut Flower Production** (90), **Strawberry Cycle** (75) |
| **Orchards & Tree Fruit** | **Annual Production Season** (365), **New Orchard Planting** (180), **Harvest Window** (45) |
| **Apiary & Beekeeping** | **Honey Production Season** (120), **Colony Build-Up** (60), **Queen Rearing Cycle** (30) |
| **Mushroom Cultivation** | **Spawn Block Cycle** (60), **Bag Spawn Cycle** (45) |
| **Agro-Processing** | **Processing Batch** (7), **Monthly Operation** (30) |
| **Agricultural Services** | **Service Contract** (30), **Equipment Hire Period** (14) |
| **Insects & Worms**, **Aquatic Plants & Algae**, **Forestry & Agroforestry** | These three categories have no cycle types of their own. The wizard shows the four Crops types (**Rain-Fed Season**, **Irrigated Season**, **Nursery / Seedbed**, **Ratoon Crop**) and asks no setup questions. |

Each type in the wizard also shows a one-line description. A few examples:

- **Broiler Grow-Out** — "Day-old chicks → live weight / slaughter. Typically 35–45 days."
- **Egg Incubation & Hatch** — "Set fertile eggs → candle → lock-down → hatch → chick grading."
- **Dairy Production Season** — "Track lactation, milk yield, breeding and dry-off for a dairy herd."
- **Transplant Cycle** — "Seedling transplant → harvest. For tomato, cabbage, kale, pepper."
- **Harvest Window** — "Focus on the active harvest period only."

> **Tip:** If the category has no exact match for your animal or crop, pick the nearest type. For example, for guinea fowl or geese choose **Duck Cycle** or **Broiler Grow-Out**; for a cattle-fattening group choose **Beef Grow-Out**. The type only affects the suggested length, the setup questions and (for poultry and orchards) which items show.

### How the cycle type changes the daily boxes and care items

| Category | Effect of the cycle type |
|---|---|
| **Poultry & Birds** | Strong effect. Egg boxes show only for laying types; incubator boxes show only for **Egg Incubation & Hatch**; litter condition shows only for **Broiler Grow-Out** and **Turkey Grow-Out**. Full table in the Daily Log chapter. |
| **Orchards & Tree Fruit** | The built-in orchard care guides are written separately for **Annual Production Season**, **New Orchard Planting** and **Harvest Window**. Only the items for the type you chose appear in the Care Plan. |
| **Broiler Chicken and Layer Chicken templates** | Their care guides are tied to the matching types: broiler items show for **Broiler Grow-Out**; layer items show for **Layer — from Day-Old** (rearing items) or **Layer — from Point-of-Lay** and **Layer — Continuing Flock** (laying items). |
| **All other categories** | The daily boxes are the same for every cycle type. The cycle type changes only the suggested length and the setup questions. |

> **Watch out:** If you create a cycle of the wrong type for the template (for example a **Layer — from Day-Old** cycle under the Broiler Chicken template), the Care Plan will find no items that apply and will show an empty whole guide. Start a new cycle with the right type, or choose the right template.

If you have customised a template, you can also tie a box or care item to certain cycle types; the editor keeps any such restrictions that were in the built-in version and shows "Shown only for some cycle types of this enterprise" or "Applies only to some cycle types of this enterprise" under them. Items you add yourself apply to every cycle type.

### The setup questions in step 2, per category

Step 2 of the New Cycle wizard asks questions that depend on the category (and, for poultry and livestock, on the cycle type). None of them is compulsory: the red star on some of them is only a reminder and the wizard lets you continue without answering. Answers are stored with the cycle as a note, and the main quantity creates the cycle's first **production unit** (for example the flock size).

**Poultry & Birds**

| Question | Type | Shown for |
|---|---|---|
| **Number of Birds** (*) | number, birds | All types except Egg Incubation & Hatch |
| **Eggs Set** (*) | number, eggs | Egg Incubation & Hatch only |
| **Breed / Strain** | text, e.g. Ross 308, Lohmann Brown | All |
| **House / Pen ID** | text, e.g. House A, Pen 3 | All |
| **Chick / Egg Source** | text, hatchery or farm name | All |
| **Target Slaughter Age** | number, days | Broiler Grow-Out, Turkey Grow-Out |
| **Starting Age** | number, weeks | Layer — from Point-of-Lay, Layer — Continuing Flock |
| **Egg Routing Intent** | choice: Table eggs → market; Fertile eggs → incubation (in-house); Fertile eggs → sale; Mixed (split between uses) | Layer types and Breeder Flock |
| **Setter / Incubator ID** | text, e.g. Cabinet 1 | Egg Incubation & Hatch |
| **Expected Hatch Date** | text, YYYY-MM-DD | Egg Incubation & Hatch |
| **Housing System** | choice: Deep litter, Cages (battery), Free range, Semi-intensive, Aviary | All |

**Livestock**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Number of Animals** (*) | number, head |
| **Breed** | text, e.g. Friesian, Boer |
| **Average Age** | number, months |
| **Sex Breakdown** | text, e.g. 10 cows, 1 bull (shown for Dairy Production Season and Goat Production Season only) |
| **Primary Purpose** | choice: Dairy, Beef / Meat, Breeding, Mixed |
| **Pen / Paddock** | text |
| **Average Starting Weight** | number, kg |
| **Feeding System** | choice: Zero-grazing (cut & carry), Grazing (pasture), Mixed grazing + supplement, Feedlot (total confinement) |

**Aquaculture**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Number of Ponds / Tanks** (*) | number |
| **Pond Area per Unit** | number, m² |
| **Fingerlings Stocked** (*) | number, fish |
| **Stocking Density** | number, fish/m² |
| **Species / Strain** | text, e.g. Nile Tilapia, African Catfish |
| **Avg Fingerling Weight** | number, g |
| **Water Source** | choice: Borehole, River / stream, Municipal, Rainwater harvested, Recirculating (RAS) |
| **Primary Feed Type** | choice: Commercial pellets, Farm-made feed, Natural (algae/plankton), Mixed |
| **Target Harvest Weight** | number, g |

**Crops & Grains**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Field / Plot Name** (*) | text, e.g. Plot A1, North Field |
| **Area** (*) | number, ha |
| **Variety / Cultivar** | text, e.g. SC403, DK8031 |
| **Irrigation System** | choice: Rain-fed (no irrigation), Drip irrigation, Flood / furrow, Centre pivot, Sprinkler, Manual watering |
| **Soil Type** | choice: Sandy loam, Clay loam, Sandy, Clay, Silt loam, Not assessed |
| **Expected Yield** | number, bags/ha |
| **Seed Rate** | number, kg/ha |

**Horticulture**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Field / Bed Name** (*) | text |
| **Area** (*) | number, ha |
| **Variety / Cultivar** | text, e.g. Roma |
| **Transplant / Sowing Date** | text, YYYY-MM-DD |
| **Plant Count** | number |
| **Irrigation** | choice: Drip, Furrow, Sprinkler, Manual, Rain-fed |
| **Expected Yield** | number, kg/ha |

**Greenhouse**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Greenhouse / Tunnel Name** (*) | text |
| **Growing Area** (*) | number, m² |
| **Growing System** | choice: Soil (raised beds), Drip to soil, NFT Hydroponics, DWC Hydroponics, Coco coir / substrate, Aeroponics |
| **Plant Count** | number |
| **Variety / Cultivar** | text |
| **Lighting** | choice: Natural light only, Supplemental LED, Full artificial lighting |
| **Climate Control** | choice: None (passive), Shade nets, Evaporative cooling, Full HVAC |

**Orchards & Tree Fruit**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Plot / Block Name** (*) | text |
| **Number of Trees** (*) | number |
| **Plot Area** | number, ha |
| **Tree Age** | number, years |
| **Variety / Cultivar** | text, e.g. Tommy Atkins, Hass |
| **Tree Spacing** | text, e.g. 5m × 5m |
| **Irrigation** | choice: Rain-fed, Drip, Micro-jets, Flood / furrow |
| **Expected Yield** | number, tonnes |

**Apiary & Beekeeping**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Number of Hives** (*) | number |
| **Hive Type** | choice: Langstroth, Kenya Top Bar (KTBH), Top Bar (other), Warré, Log hive (traditional) |
| **Apiary Location / Name** | text |
| **Average Colony Strength** | choice: Weak (< 3 frames brood), Medium (3–5 frames), Strong (5+ frames) |
| **Expected Honey Yield** | number, kg/hive |

**Mushroom Cultivation**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|
| **Number of Growing Blocks / Bags** (*) | number |
| **Substrate Type** | choice: Sawdust (hardwood), Rice straw, Wheat straw, Coffee grounds, Cotton waste, Compost (button mushroom) |
| **Growing Room / Shed** | text |

| **Inoculation Date** | text, YYYY-MM-DD |

**Agro-Processing**

| Question | Type |

**Agricultural Services**

| Question | Type |
|---|---|

**Insects & Worms, Aquatic Plants & Algae, Forestry & Agroforestry:** no setup questions. Step 2 says "No additional setup required for this cycle type. Continue to step 3."

---

## Starting a cycle: the New Cycle wizard

**What it is for.** A cycle is one run of an enterprise. The wizard creates the cycle, copies the template's stages into it, makes the first stage active and starts the day counting for the Care Plan.

**Before you start.** You need at least one active enterprise (see the previous chapter). You need access to **Production** in the left menu.

**Step by step**

1. In the left menu click **Production**. The left panel is titled **Production Engine** and shows how many cycles are open and closed.
2. Click **New Cycle** (green button, top right of the left panel). The window **New Production Cycle** opens. A progress bar and the words **Step 1 of 3** show where you are.

**Step 1 of 3 — Enterprise and cycle type**

3. Under **Which enterprise?** click the enterprise. Each row shows its icon, your name for it and the template name underneath. If you opened Production from an enterprise's own page, that enterprise is already chosen.
4. Under **What type of cycle?** click the type that fits (see the tables above). Each card shows its description and "~n days".
5. Look at **Stages from template**. It lists the stages the cycle will go through, joined by arrows. This is only a preview.
6. Click **Next →**. (It stays greyed out until an enterprise and a type are chosen. **Cancel** closes the window.)

**Step 2 of 3 — Category-specific setup**

7. The top of the step repeats the cycle type and enterprise name. Fill in the questions for your category (see the tables in the previous chapter). All of them are optional, even those marked with a red star.
8. Click **Next →**. Use **← Back** to go back.

**Step 3 of 3 — Name, dates, notes**

9. The green summary repeats the cycle type, enterprise and template, and shows up to six of your answers.
10. **Cycle Name \*** is filled in for you, for example "Broiler Oct 2026" (template short name plus month and year). Change it to something you will recognise, such as "Broiler Batch 3 — House A".
11. **Start Date \*** is today's date. Change it if the cycle really started on another day. **This date is day 0 of the Care Plan** (see the Care Plan chapter).
12. **Expected End (auto)** is the start date plus the suggested days for the cycle type. You can change it.
13. Optionally type **Notes**, for example the supplier and the delivery condition.
14. Click **Start Cycle**.

**What is created**

- A cycle with status **active**.
- One stage for every stage in the template. The first stage is **active** and the rest are waiting.
- The cycle's **production unit** (for example "Flock — Broiler Grow-Out, 500 birds"), if you entered the main quantity in step 2. For services, one unit for the client is always created.
- Notes that combine what you typed with a line "Config: …" listing your step 2 answers and a line "Cycle type: …".

**Good to know**

- The cycle takes its stages from the template at the moment you click **Start Cycle**. Later changes to the template do not change the stages of cycles that already exist.
- The **Expected End** is recalculated whenever you change the cycle type or the start date, so set those first and edit **Expected End** last.
- If you pick another enterprise in step 1, the cycle type, name and answers are reset to match that enterprise.
- A start date in the past makes the Care Plan treat items from the first days as overdue (it looks back up to 30 days). A start date in the future shows "Cycle starts in n days" on the Care Plan.

**If something goes wrong**

| What you see | Meaning and fix |
|---|---|
| **Next →** is greyed out in step 1 | No enterprise is selected, or you have no enterprises. Add one under Settings → Enterprises → **Add Enterprise**. |
| **Start Cycle** is greyed out | The **Cycle Name** or **Start Date** is empty. |
| The enterprise list is empty | Only active enterprises are shown. Add an enterprise first. |

> **Tip:** Start the cycle on the day the first stage really begins (the day chicks arrive, the day you stock fish, the day you start land preparation). The built-in care guides count their days from the first stage. For the tomato guide, for example, day 0 is sowing in the nursery and day 28 is transplanting.


---

## The Daily Log

### How the Daily Log gets its boxes

**What it is for.** The **Daily Log** tab is where you write down what happened today: feed given, eggs collected, water readings, harvest weight, medicine used. The boxes on this form come from the template of the enterprise, narrowed down by the cycle type. If you have saved your own template, your boxes are used instead.

**Before you start.** You have a cycle that is **active** (see "Starting a cycle"). Finished cycles show their records but accept no new ones.

**Step by step: record one day**

1. Click **Production** in the left menu, then click the cycle in the list on the left.
2. Click the **Daily Log** tab.
3. Under **Stage**, check the stage. It starts on the current stage. The list shows the stages that have started or finished. Choose a different one if you are catching up on an earlier stage.
4. Under **Date**, check the date. It starts on today. Change it to enter a record for another day.
5. Fill in the **Key Measurements** first (the boxes marked with a red star).
6. Fill in the other boxes you have information for: **Status & Condition** (lists), **Additional Measurements** (numbers) and **Activity Log** (text). You do not have to fill every box.
7. Type any extra remarks in **Notes / Observations**.
8. Click **Save Daily Record**. The button turns green and reads **Record saved!** for a moment, and the form empties, ready for the next entry.
9. Your new record appears at the top of **Recent Records** at the bottom of the tab.

**What you will see on the tab**

| Part of the form | What it contains |
|---|---|
| A line under the tab name | **Using the built-in record fields**, or **Using your template** followed by your template's name. A **Customise fields** link sits at the right (see "Customising"). |
| **Stage** and **Date** | Which stage and which day this record belongs to. |
| **Key Measurements \*** | The boxes marked **Required** in the template. They are shown first. |
| **Auto-Calculated** | Appears only after you have typed the numbers it depends on. Shows values worked out for you, such as total milk. |
| **Status & Condition** | Boxes where you pick one answer from a list. |
| **Additional Measurements** | The other number boxes. |
| **Activity Log** | Free-text boxes (what was given, what was sprayed). The hint, if any, is shown beside the label and as grey text inside the box. |
| **Notes / Observations** | One free-text box for the whole day. |
| **Save Daily Record**, **Import CSV**, **Template** | Save one day, or load many days from a spreadsheet (see the CSV chapter). |
| **Recent Records** | The seven most recent records of the chosen stage. |

> **Note:** If the enterprise of a cycle has been removed from your workspace, the Daily Log shows **Enterprise no longer found** and cannot show the boxes. Old records are kept and the five most recent are listed.

### The four kinds of box

| Kind | What you do | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Number** | Type a figure. Decimals are allowed. A unit is shown next to the box (kg, L, %, °C and so on). The figure is saved as a number and can be added up and compared. | Feed Consumed: 18 kg |
| **Choice from a list** | Pick one answer from the list (or leave it on **— select —**). | Type of Feed: Starter |
| **Free text** | Type words. | Vaccine / Medicine Given: Newcastle La Sota, eye drop |
| **Calculated automatically** | You cannot type here. The box fills itself from other boxes. | Total Milk Yield |

**Required boxes.** A red star after a label means the template marks the box as required. In the Daily Log the star is a reminder only: the form still saves if the box is empty. (The CSV import is stricter. See the CSV chapter.)

> **Watch out:** AGRINUXES saves whatever you have typed, even an empty form. Check the day's numbers before you click **Save Daily Record**.

> **Watch out:** Required boxes are always shown in the **Key Measurements** panel as number boxes. Mark only number fields as required when you customise a template.

### Normal ranges and the colour cues

Many number boxes have a **normal minimum**, a **normal maximum** and sometimes a **target**. Examples: water temperature for fish 25 to 32 °C with a target of 28 °C; house temperature for poultry 18 to 32 °C.

| What you see | What it means |
|---|---|
| A small grey "· target 28" after the label | The template's target value. It is for guidance only and never turns the box red. |
| A **green border** on the box | You typed a value inside the normal range. |
| A **red border**, a pink background and a message "**Below min (25)**" or "**Above max (32)**" | The value is outside the normal range. The record **still saves**. Check your reading, and act if it is real. |
| In **Recent Records**, a number tile with a pink background and red figure | A saved value that is outside the normal range. |
| No colour at all | The box has no normal range (for example Feed Consumed) or is empty. |

> **Tip:** A red box is a prompt to check, not an error. A fish pond at 4 mg/L dissolved oxygen is a real warning. A 33 °C reading in a hot brooder house may be perfectly normal for your farm. Use **Customise fields** to set ranges that suit your site.

### Auto-calculated boxes

Two built-in boxes are worked out for you:

| Box | Where | How it is worked out | What it needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Total Milk Yield** (L) | Livestock | Morning Milk + Afternoon Milk + Evening Milk, to one decimal place | All three milk boxes must contain a figure. Type 0 for a milking you did not do. |
| **Processing Yield** (%) | Processing | Finished Output ÷ Raw Input Received × 100, to one decimal place | Both **Raw Input Received** and **Finished Output** must be filled. |

The result is shown in a green **Auto-Calculated** panel above the lists, and is saved with the record. If you delete one of the source boxes while customising, the calculated box can no longer work.

### Saving, history, editing and deleting

**Recent Records.** Each saved record is shown as a card. The card shows:

- the date (for example "Mon 6 Oct");
- the count of fields filled in;
- a **pencil** button (edit) and a **bin** button (delete);
- one small tile for each number, with its unit (red if out of range);
- grey chips for each choice or text answer;
- your note, if you typed one.

Only the seven newest records of the chosen stage are shown. Choose another stage in the **Stage** list to see that stage's records.

**Edit a record**

1. On the record card click the **pencil** button. The window **Edit Record — (date)** opens.
2. Change the numbers or the **Notes**. The numbers are listed with their short names (for example "feed kg").
3. If your company requires a reason, type it in **Reason for edit**.
4. Click **Save Changes**.

> **Note:** The edit window can change only the numbers and the notes. It cannot change the date, the stage, or the choice and text answers. To change those, delete the record and enter it again.

**Delete a record**

1. Click the **bin** button on the record.
2. In **Delete Record?** read "Remove record for (date)?" and click **Delete**.

**The approval workflow (if your company turned it on)**

An owner can switch on **Require approval for edits** in **Settings → Edit & Approval Control**. When it is on:

- Editing a record changes the button to **Submit for Approval**, and a note says "This edit will be sent for approval before applying."
- Deleting a record changes the button to **Submit Deletion**.
- If **Require a reason for every edit or deletion** is ticked, you must type a reason before the edit button works.
- The request then waits in **Settings → Edit & Approval Control → Pending Edits**, where the approver sees the before and after values and the reason, and clicks **Approve** or **Reject**. Rejecting lets the approver type a reason. Decisions are listed under **Recent Decisions**.
- The record changes only after approval.

Approval applies to **editing and deleting** records. Adding a new daily record, importing a CSV file, ticking Care Plan items and saving templates do not need approval.

### Daily boxes for every category

Below, each category's built-in boxes are explained in plain words. "Required" means the red-star boxes in **Key Measurements**. A range such as "25 to 32" is the normal range that turns the box red when you go outside it.

#### Poultry & Birds

Poultry has the most boxes (23), because layers, broilers, hatcheries and breeders need very different records. The cycle type decides which boxes show.

| Cycle type | Boxes shown |
|---|---|
| **Broiler Grow-Out** | Feed, Type of Feed, Water Additive, Vaccine / Medicine Given, Water Consumed, Mortality Count, Mortality Reason, Avg Body Weight Sample, Litter Condition, House Temperature, Live Bird Count |
| **Layer — from Day-Old** | Eggs Collected, Cracked / Broken, Trays Collected, then the general set (feed, water, medicine, mortality, house temperature, live bird count) plus Avg Body Weight Sample |
| **Layer — from Point-of-Lay**, **Layer — Continuing Flock** | The three egg boxes plus the general set (no body weight, no litter) |
| **Breeder Flock** | The three egg boxes, **Eggs Set for Incubation**, plus the general set |
| **Egg Incubation & Hatch** | Only incubator boxes: Setter Temperature, Setter Humidity, Hatcher Temperature, Hatcher Humidity, Turner / Auto-Turn, Candle Rejects Removed, Chicks Hatched, Chicks Culled / Weak |
| **Turkey Grow-Out** | The general set plus Avg Body Weight Sample and Litter Condition |
| **Duck Cycle**, **Quail Cycle** | Eggs Collected, Cracked / Broken, plus the general set and Avg Body Weight Sample |

> **Note:** For the layer-from-day-old type, the egg boxes are present but chicks do not lay for the first months. Leave them empty until laying begins. The star on **Eggs Collected** is only a reminder.

What each poultry box means:

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Eggs Collected** (required) | number, eggs | Total eggs picked up today. |
| **Cracked / Broken** | number, eggs | Eggs that cannot be sold as good eggs. |
| **Trays Collected** | number, trays | Trays of eggs (hint: 30 eggs per tray). |
| **Eggs Set for Incubation** | number, eggs | Fertile eggs put in the setter today (breeder flocks). |
| **Setter Temperature** (required) | number, °C | The setter (the incubator for the first 18 days). Normal 37.2 to 37.8, target 37.5. |
| **Setter Humidity** (required) | number, %RH | Humidity inside the setter. Normal 55 to 65, target 60. |
| **Hatcher Temperature** | number, °C | The hatcher (where eggs go for the last days). Normal 36.9 to 37.2, target 37.0. |
| **Hatcher Humidity** | number, %RH | Normal 70 to 80, target 75. |
| **Turner / Auto-Turn** | list | Running OK, Manual turn done, or Off — check immediately. |
| **Candle Rejects Removed** | number, eggs | Candling means shining a light through the egg. Remove infertile or dead eggs and record how many. |
| **Chicks Hatched** | number, chicks | Chicks that hatched. |
| **Chicks Culled / Weak** | number, chicks | Chicks removed as weak or deformed. |
| **Feed Consumed** (required) | number, kg | Total feed given to the flock today. Over a cycle this is used with the weight gained to judge **FCR** (feed conversion ratio: kilograms of feed needed to make one kilogram of bird; lower is better; the broiler template benchmark is 1.7). |
| **Type of Feed** | list | Starter, Grower, Finisher, Layer mash, Breeder feed, Own mix, Other. |
| **Water Additive** | text | Vitamins, electrolytes (salts that help birds in heat), acidifier or medicine put in the drinking water. |
| **Vaccine / Medicine Given** | text | Name, route and dose, for example "Newcastle Lasota, eye drop". |
| **Water Consumed** | number, L | Total water drunk. Birds drink roughly twice the weight of feed they eat. |
| **Mortality Count** | number, birds | Number of birds that died today. **Mortality rate** is deaths as a percentage of birds started; the broiler benchmark is 3%. |
| **Mortality Reason** | list | Unknown, Disease, Injury, Predator, Heat stress, Cold stress, Culled, Other. |
| **Avg Body Weight Sample** | number, g | Weigh 10 to 20 birds and enter the average (hint on screen). |
| **Litter Condition** | list | Dry & crumbly (excellent), Slightly damp (acceptable), Wet / caked (action needed), Very wet (critical). |
| **House Temperature** | number, °C | Normal 18 to 32. |
| **Live Bird Count** | number, birds | Current total after subtracting deaths. |

#### Livestock

The same boxes show for every livestock cycle type.

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Morning Milk** (required) | number, L | Milk from the morning milking. |
| **Afternoon Milk** | number, L | Milk from the afternoon milking. |
| **Evening Milk** | number, L | Milk from the evening milking. |
| **Total Milk Yield** | calculated, L | Sum of the three milkings (all three must be filled). |
| **Animals Milked** | number, head | How many animals were milked. Divide total milk by this to see litres per cow. |
| **Concentrate Feed** | number, kg | Dairy meal or other bought feed. |
| **Fodder / Roughage** | number, kg | Hay, silage, cut grass. |
| **Water Consumed** | number, L | Total water. |
| **Type of Feed** | list | Pasture / grazing, Hay, Silage, Crop residue, Dairy meal, Grower ration, Finisher ration, Mineral lick, Own mix, Other. |
| **Vaccine / Medicine / Dip** | text | Product, dose and animals treated, for example "FMD vaccine, 12 head". |
| **Deaths / Culls** | number, head | Animals that died or were culled. |
| **Births / Calvings** | number, head | New births. |
| **Health Status** | list | All healthy, Monitor 1–2 animals, Sick animal(s) — see notes, Emergency — vet called. |
| **Avg Body Weight Sample** | number, kg | Weigh 3 to 5 animals and average. |

> **Note:** The milk boxes make most sense for dairy cycles. For a beef, pig or goat meat enterprise leave them empty, or remove them when you customise the template.

#### Aquaculture (fish, prawns, aquaponics)

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Feed Given** (required) | number, kg | Total feed thrown in today. |
| **Water Temperature** (required) | number, °C | Normal 25 to 32, target 28. |
| **Dissolved Oxygen** (required) | number, mg/L | The oxygen fish breathe. Normal 5 to 10, target 7. Low readings, especially before sunrise, are dangerous. |
| **pH Level** | number, pH | How acid or alkaline the water is. Normal 6.5 to 8.5, target 7.5. |
| **Ammonia (NH₃)** | number, mg/L | A poisonous waste from fish. Maximum 0.02. |
| **Turbidity / Secchi** | number, cm | How murky the water is. The Secchi reading is the depth at which a white disc disappears; a green pond is typically 25 to 40 cm according to the starter tilapia guide. |
| **Type of Feed** | list | Fingerling starter, Grower pellet, Finisher pellet, Own mix, Natural (pond fertiliser), Other. |
| **Treatment / Pond Input** | text | Lime, salt, fertiliser or medicine, with product and amount. |
| **Mortality Count** | number, fish | Dead fish removed. |
| **Water Change** | number, % | Share of the water replaced. |
| **Avg Weight Sample** | number, g | Weigh 10 to 20 fish from one pond or tank. |
| **Feeding Response** | list | Vigorous — all food consumed; Good — consumed within 30min; Poor — leftover feed; Very poor — not feeding. |

#### Crops & Grains

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Irrigation Applied** | number, mm | Water applied. 1 mm over a hectare is 10,000 litres. |
| **Fertilizer Applied** | number, kg/ha | Fertiliser rate applied. |
| **Pest Pressure** | list | None observed, Low — monitor, Moderate — spray threshold, High — immediate action. |
| **Disease Pressure** | list | None observed, Low — monitor, Moderate — treat, High — severe. |
| **Growth Stage** | list | Germination, Seedling, Vegetative, Flowering, Grain fill, Maturity, Harvest ready. |
| **Harvest Collected** | number, kg | Weight harvested today. |
| **Spray Applied** | text | Product name and rate. |
| **Fertiliser Type** | text | For example Compound D, urea, CAN, manure. |
| **Weeding / Cultivation** | list | No, Hand weeding, Herbicide, Cultivator. |
| **Rainfall** | number, mm | Rain gauge reading. |

#### Horticulture (vegetables)

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Harvest Weight** (required) | number, kg | Total weight picked today. |
| **Units Harvested** | number, units | Heads, bunches or pieces. |
| **Grade A** | number, kg | Top-quality produce, fit for the best market. |
| **Grade B / Seconds** | number, kg | Lower-quality produce. |
| **Rejects / Waste** | number, kg | Produce thrown away. |
| **Irrigation Applied** | number, mm | Water applied. |
| **Spray Applied** | text | Product, rate and target (fungicide / insecticide). |
| **Fertiliser Applied** | text | Type and amount, for example "CAN 50 kg". |
| **Pest / Disease Flag** | list | None, Aphids, Spider mite, Whitefly, Botrytis, Downy mildew, Other — see notes. |
| **Plant Health** | list | Excellent, Good, Fair — monitor, Concern — action needed. |

#### Greenhouse

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Nutrient Solution EC** (required) | number, mS/cm | **EC** means electrical conductivity. It tells you how strong the plant-food solution is: too low and plants starve, too high and roots burn. Normal 1.2 to 2.5, target 1.8. |
| **Nutrient Solution pH** (required) | number, pH | Acidity of the solution. Plants take up food best in a narrow band. Normal 5.5 to 6.5, target 6.0. |
| **Air Temperature** | number, °C | Normal 18 to 28. |
| **Humidity** | number, % | Normal 60 to 80. |
| **Nutrient Top-Up** | number, L | Litres of solution or water added to the tank. |
| **Harvest Weight** | number, kg | Weight harvested. |
| **Plants Harvested** | number, plants | Count harvested. |
| **Plant Health** | list | Excellent, Good, Yellowing — check nutrients, Wilting — check roots, Disease spotted. |
| **Pest Observation** | list | None, Aphids, Spider mite, Fungus gnats, Thrips, Other. |

#### Orchards & Tree Fruit

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Irrigation Duration** | number, hours | How long the system ran. |
| **Irrigation Amount** | number, mm | Water applied. |
| **Spray Applied** | text | Product, concentration and target. |
| **Pest / Disease Scouting** | list | None detected, Low — continue monitoring, Moderate — schedule spray, High — immediate action. |
| **Fruit Thinned** | number, fruits | Hand-thinning count (removing small fruit so the rest grow bigger). |
| **Harvest Collected** | number, kg | Weight harvested. |
| **Grade A Fruit** | number, kg | Top-quality fruit. |
| **Rejects / Drops** | number, kg | Fallen or damaged fruit. |
| **Tree Health Status** | list | All healthy, Minor yellowing — monitor, Suspected disease — isolate, Confirmed disease — action. |
| **Rainfall** | number, mm | Rain gauge reading. |

#### Apiary & Beekeeping

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Hives Inspected** | number, hives | Hives opened today. |
| **General Hive Status** | list | All healthy & queenright; 1–2 hives weak — monitor; Queenless hive detected; Swarming activity; Disease signs (varroa/EFB/AFB). "Queenright" means the hive has a working queen. |
| **Honey Super Weight** | number, kg | Total weight of honey supers (the boxes where bees store honey). |
| **Honey Harvested** | number, kg | Honey taken today. |
| **Varroa Mite Count** | number, mites/100 bees | Varroa is a parasite of bees. Maximum 3 per 100 bees. |
| **Treatment Applied** | text | Product name and dose. |
| **Syrup Fed** | number, L | Sugar syrup fed to the bees. |

#### Mushroom Cultivation

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Air Temperature** | number, °C | Normal 16 to 24, target 20. |
| **Humidity** | number, % | Normal 80 to 95, target 90. |
| **CO₂ Level** | number, ppm | Carbon dioxide in the room. Maximum 1000. High CO₂ gives long stalks and small caps; open the vents. |
| **Pinning Count** | number, fruiting bodies | "Pins" are the tiny mushrooms just starting to form. |
| **Harvest Weight** | number, kg | Weight picked. |
| **Substrate Moisture** | list | Optimal (60–70%), Too dry — mist, Too wet — air out, Contamination visible. |
| **Contamination** | list | None, Slight — isolated, Moderate — quarantine block, Severe — dispose. |
| **Misting Done** | list | Yes, No, Partial. |

#### Insects & Worms

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Feed / Substrate Added** (required) | number, kg | Feed or waste added to the bins or beds. |
| **Type of Feed** | list | Kitchen waste, Crop residue, Manure, Commercial feed, Bran / grain, Other. |
| **Temperature** | number, °C | Normal 24 to 32. |
| **Humidity** | number, % | Normal 60 to 80. |
| **Substrate Moisture** | list | Optimal, Too dry — mist, Too wet — add dry matter. |
| **Harvest Weight** | number, kg | Larvae, crickets, worms or compost harvested. |
| **Mortality / Pests** | list | None, Some die-off, Mites / flies present, Heavy loss — see notes. |

| Box | Kind and unit | What to enter |
|---|---|---|

| **pH Level** | number, pH | Acidity of the culture water. |
