---

## The Care Plan tab

### What the Care Plan is for

**What it is for.** The Care Plan is a day-by-day reminder list for the cycle. It tells you what to feed, which vaccine is due, when to spray, when to weigh or check, and what the whole programme looks like. The list comes from the template's **care guide**. You tick items off as you do them, so the farm owner and the team can see what has been done and what has been missed.

**Before you start.** Open a cycle (**Production**, then click the cycle). Click the **Care Plan** tab. You can read the Care Plan on any cycle, but you can tick items off only while the cycle is **active**.

### Every template has a starter guide

All 94 built-in templates come with a **starter guide**. The Care Plan marks it with an amber box:

> This is a **starter guide** — typical practice, not a prescription. Review the products and doses with your vet or extension officer, then use **Set my standards** to adjust it to how you farm and save it as your template.

> **Watch out:** The starter guides are typical smallholder and commercial programmes offered as a guide only. Doses, products, vaccination ages, spray intervals and withdrawal periods vary by breed, region, disease pressure and product. **Always confirm every product and dose with your vet, agronomist, extension officer or the product label before you use it.** AGRINUXES does not replace professional advice.

The amber box disappears once you save your own version, because then the guide is yours and you have reviewed it.

### Counting the days

Every item in the guide is placed on a **day number counted from the start of the cycle**. The start date you entered in the New Cycle wizard is **day 0**. The day after is day 1, and so on.

- The Care Plan always tells you where you are: "Today is **day 12** of this cycle."
- If the start date is still in the future, it says "Cycle starts in 3 days."
- The cycle header at the top of the page shows "Day n" as well.

> **Example:** You start a broiler cycle on Monday 6 October. Monday is day 0. The Newcastle + Infectious Bronchitis vaccination is on day 7, which is Monday 13 October. The Gumboro vaccination is on day 14, Monday 20 October.

### The three kinds of care item

Each item in the guide takes one of three shapes. You choose the shape in the editor by filling in the "Starts on day", "Repeat every" and "Until day" boxes (see "Customising").

| Kind | How it is set | What it does | Example from the starter guides |
|---|---|---|---|
| **One-off** | Only **Starts on day** | Appears once, on that day. | Broiler: "Gumboro (IBD)" on day 14. Maize: "First weeding" on day 35. |
| **Repeating** | **Starts on day**, **Repeat every** (days) and **Until day** | Appears on the start day, then again every so many days up to the end day. Each occurrence is ticked off separately. | Broiler: "Weigh a sample of 20 birds" starting day 7, every 7 days, until day 42. Tomato: "Preventive fungicide against early and late blight" from day 40, every 10 days, until day 125. |
| **Phase** | **Starts on day** and **Until day**, with no repeat | A programme that applies for a whole stretch of days. It cannot be ticked. It shows under "The programme right now" while today falls inside it. | Broiler: "Starter feed" days 0 to 13, "Grower feed" days 14 to 27, "Finisher feed" days 28 to 42. |

> **Watch out:** A repeating item needs an **Until day**. If you set "Repeat every" but leave "Until day" empty, the item appears only once, on its start day.

### The type of each item (colour tag)

Every item has a type, shown as a coloured tag. The type does not change how the item behaves. It helps you scan the list.

| Type | Use it for |
|---|---|
| **Feed** | Feeding programmes and feed changes (starter, grower, finisher, dry-cow ration, minerals). |
| **Water** | Water quality and drinking-water programmes (vitamins and electrolytes, acidifier, irrigation). |
| **Vaccination** | Vaccines. |
| **Medicine** | Dewormers, coccidiosis prevention, dry-cow treatment, other treatments. |
| **Spray** | Fungicides, insecticides, herbicides, tick dips and sprays. |
| **Fertiliser** | Basal fertiliser, top-dressing, lime, manure, pond fertilising. |
| **Weigh / sample** | Weighing birds, animals or fish, body condition scoring. |
| **Check** | Inspections and scouting (brooder temperature, mastitis test, pest scouting, oxygen check). |
| **Task** | Any other job (land preparation, transplanting, moving to the laying house, harvest). |

### What an item shows

Each item shows:

- its **type tag** and its **title** (the "What");
- a grey line joining the **product**, the **dose** and the **how** (route or method) when they are filled in, for example "Broiler starter (crumbs) · Free access — about 15–60 g/bird/day";
- if a **withdrawal period** is set, an amber line: "Stop 7 days before harvest / slaughter / milk use — check the label."
- any **notes**;
- its day, for example "Day 14".

### The sections of the tab

| Section | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Template in use** | The template name with a tag: **Built-in**, or **My template · v** and a number. If you have saved versions of this template, a drop-down lets you switch between **Built-in: …** and **Mine: …** versions. The button **Set my standards** (or **Edit my standards** if the template is already yours) opens the editor. |
| **The programme right now** | The phases that cover today (for example the current feed). Shown only when there is at least one. |
| **Overdue (n)** | Items from the last 30 days that were not ticked. Shown in red, newest first, with "n days late". At most 20 are listed, with "+ n older" under them when there are more. |
| **Due today (n)** | Items that fall on today's day number. If there are none, "Nothing scheduled for today." |
| **Coming in the next 7 days (n)** | Items due in the following 7 days, with "in n days". |
| **Whole guide (n items)** | Click to open a table of the complete guide for this cycle type: **When**, **Type**, **What**, **Product / dose**. Phases show as "Day 0–13", repeating items as "Every 7 days, day 7–42". |

If the template has no care guide at all (for example after you removed every item), the tab says "No care guide for (name) yet" with a button **Build my care guide**.

### Marking items done

**Step by step**

1. On the **Care Plan** tab find the item under **Overdue** or **Due today**, or under **Coming in the next 7 days** if you did it early.
2. Click **Done**. The title is crossed out, the row fades, and it says "done 6 Oct by (your name)".
3. If you ticked the wrong item, click **Undo** on that row.

**Good to know**

- **Done** and **Undo** appear only while the cycle is **active**. On a paused, planning or completed cycle you can read the plan but not change ticks.
- The tick is recorded with the date and the name on your profile, so the team can see who did what.
- Each occurrence of a repeating item is ticked on its own day. Ticking today's weighing does not tick next week's.
- Phase rows ("The programme right now") have no button, because they are programmes, not jobs.
- Ticks are saved with the cycle, which lives in the company account, so everyone in the company sees the same ticks.
- Ticking an item does **not** create a daily record, an event, a cost or a stock movement. If you vaccinate or treat and want the cost in Finance, also log it as an event in the **Events** tab (see Part 3).

### The reminders at the top of the cycle

You do not have to open the Care Plan to notice that something is due:

- **Header banner.** While the cycle is active, if anything is due today or overdue, a banner appears under the progress bar: **Care guide:** "2 due today · 1 overdue" with **Open Care Plan** at the right. It turns red when something is overdue and light blue when things are only due today. Click it to jump to the Care Plan tab.
- **Tab badge.** The tab itself reads **Care Plan (3)**, where the number is today's unticked items plus all overdue items.

### Which items apply to your cycle

Some starter guides contain items for particular cycle types (see "Cycle types"). A broiler guide shows only for **Broiler Grow-Out** cycles; the layer guide has one set of items for the rearing type and another for the two laying types; orchard guides have separate items for **Annual Production Season**, **New Orchard Planting** and **Harvest Window**. The tab counts and lists only the items that apply to the cycle type you chose. Items you add yourself apply to every cycle type.

### Example: reading a broiler guide

The built-in Broiler Chicken starter guide (17 items) looks like this for a **Broiler Grow-Out** cycle:

| When | Type | What | Product / dose |
|---|---|---|---|
| Day 0 | Task | Brooder pre-heated and chicks placed | — |
| Day 0–6 | Check | Brooder temperature 32–35 °C | — |
| Day 7–13 | Check | Brooder temperature 29–32 °C | — |
| Day 14–20 | Check | Brooder temperature 26–29 °C | — |
| Day 21–42 | Check | Brooder temperature 23–26 °C, then ambient | — |
| Day 0–13 | Feed | Starter feed | Broiler starter (crumbs) · Free access — about 15–60 g/bird/day |
| Day 14–27 | Feed | Grower feed | Broiler grower (pellets) · Free access — about 65–145 g/bird/day |
| Day 28–42 | Feed | Finisher feed | Broiler finisher (pellets) · Free access — about 150–220 g/bird/day |
| Day 0–4 | Water | Vitamins and electrolytes against stress | Vitamin/electrolyte powder · As per label · Drinking water |
| Day 0–42 | Water | Clean, cool drinking water at all times | About twice the weight of feed eaten; wash drinkers daily |
| Day 0 | Vaccination | Confirm hatchery vaccinations (Marek's, Newcastle/IB) | — |
| Day 7 | Vaccination | Newcastle + Infectious Bronchitis | e.g. Hitchner B1 + H120 · Eye drop or drinking water |
| Day 14 | Vaccination | Gumboro (IBD) | IBD vaccine (intermediate) · Drinking water |
| Day 21 | Vaccination | Newcastle booster | e.g. La Sota · Drinking water |
| Day 14–21 | Medicine | Coccidiosis prevention | Anticoccidial (in feed or water) · As per label / vet |
| Every 7 days, day 7–42 | Weigh / sample | Weigh a sample of 20 birds | — |
| Day 41 | Task | Remove feed 6–8 hours before catching | — |

This table is the starting point for the worked examples in the next chapter. The examples show how the numbers and products can be changed to fit your own programme.


---

## Customising a template: set your own standards

This chapter is the heart of the part. It shows how to turn a built-in template into **your** template: your boxes on the Daily Log, and your feeding, vaccination, spray and check calendar on the Care Plan.

### Why customise?

The built-in templates are a good start, but every farm is different. You may use a certain feed brand, follow a vet's own vaccination calendar, spray on a rotation your agronomist gave you, feed your cows in different phases, or want to record something the template does not ask for. By customising you can:

- add, rename, reorder or remove daily boxes;
- set the normal minimum, maximum and target that turn a box red;
- change the days, products and doses in the care guide, and add or remove items;
- save the result under your own name so that you can use it again for the next cycle and for other enterprises.

### Opening the editor

There are two doors to the same editor. The window is called **Set your standards — (enterprise name)**.

**Door 1: from the Care Plan**

1. Click **Production** and open the cycle.
2. Click the **Care Plan** tab.
3. In the **Template in use** box click **Set my standards** (it reads **Edit my standards** if the template is already yours). The editor opens on the **Care guide** tab.
4. If the template has no care guide, click **Build my care guide** instead.

**Door 2: from the Daily Log**

1. Click **Production** and open the cycle.
2. Click the **Daily Log** tab.
3. Click **Customise fields** at the top right, above the form. The editor opens on the **Daily record fields** tab.

Both doors show both tabs. You can switch between them with the two buttons at the top of the window.

> **Note:** The editor edits the template that this enterprise is using. Whatever you change is saved as a template; nothing changes in the built-in template or in other enterprises until you choose to use your saved template there.

### The layout of the editor

| Part | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Header** | "Set your standards — (enterprise name)" and the line "Based on (built-in template name). Change what you record each day and your feeding, vaccination and spray guide, then save it as your own template." |
| **Two tabs** | **Daily record fields (n)** and **Care guide (n)**. The number in brackets is how many boxes or items are in the list right now. |
| **Back to built-in defaults** | A button at the right of the tabs (see below). |
| **Body** | The list of boxes or care items, one card each. |
| **Footer** | Red error line (if any), **Template name**, and the buttons. |

Click the **X** at the top right, or **Cancel**, or click outside the window to leave without saving. Nothing is changed until you click a save button.

### The Daily record fields tab

The tab starts with the explanation "These are the boxes on the Daily Log. Add anything you want to record — type of feed, water treatment, medicine given — and mark what is required."

Each box on the Daily Log is one card. The cards list all of the template's boxes, including boxes that are shown only for some cycle types (those cards carry the line "Shown only for some cycle types of this enterprise.").

**Controls on every field card**

| Control | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Name** | The label shown on the Daily Log and in history. Required. Example: "Feed Brand". |
| **Kind** | **Number**, **Choice from a list** or **Free text**. For a box that is calculated automatically (such as Total Milk Yield) this shows "Calculated automatically" and cannot be changed. |
| **Unit** | For numbers only. Text such as kg, L, g, ml, °C. Shown beside the box. |
| **Required** | Tick to show the box in **Key Measurements** with a red star. It does not block saving on the Daily Log, but it does stop a CSV row that lacks it. Not offered for calculated boxes. |
| **Options — one per line** | For choice boxes only. Type one answer per line. |
| **Normal minimum**, **Normal maximum**, **Target** | For number boxes only. All optional. Below the minimum or above the maximum turns the Daily Log box red. The target is shown next to the label for guidance. |
| **Hint shown under the box (optional)** | A short help line. Not offered for calculated boxes. |
| **Move up**, **Move down**, **Remove** | The arrow buttons change the order of the cards; the bin removes the card. |

**Add a record field.** The link **Add a record field** at the bottom adds a new empty card at the end (a number box with no name). Fill in at least the name.

**What happens when you change a box**

- **Changing the kind to "Choice from a list"** fills the options with Yes and No if the box had none. Change them to suit.
- **Changing the kind away from Number** removes the unit and the normal range. If you switch back, you must type them again.
- **Changing the kind away from Choice** removes the options.

**Where each kind of box appears on the Daily Log**

| Kind | Panel on the Daily Log | Hint appears |
|---|---|---|
| Required numbers | **Key Measurements \*** | Under the box |
| Choice | **Status & Condition** | Not shown |
| Other numbers | **Additional Measurements** | Under the box |
| Free text | **Activity Log** | Beside the label and as grey text inside the box |
| Calculated | **Auto-Calculated** (only when it has a value) | Not shown |

The order of the cards decides the order inside each panel.

**Calculated boxes.** You cannot add a calculated box in the editor. The built-in ones (**Total Milk Yield** for livestock and **Processing Yield** for processing) can be moved, renamed or removed. They only work while the boxes they are calculated from are still on the list.

**Boxes tied to cycle types.** A box that the built-in template shows only for some cycle types keeps that restriction when you save. For example, the incubator boxes keep showing only for the **Egg Incubation & Hatch** type. A box you add yourself shows for every cycle type.

> **Watch out:** Mark a box as **Required** only if it is a **Number** box. Required boxes are always placed in **Key Measurements** and drawn as number boxes. A required choice or text box would not work properly there.

> **Watch out:** Behind the scenes, every box you add gets a hidden code that is made from a common name and a number, and the app reuses the first unused code. If you delete a box and add a new one afterwards, the new box can get the same hidden code as the deleted one, and old saved values of the deleted box would then show up under the new box's name. If you want to stop recording something, it is safer to keep the box and leave it empty, or rename it. Remove a box only when it has never been used.

### The Care guide tab

The tab starts with the explanation: "Days count from the start of the cycle (day 0). A single day is a one-off task; add a repeat to make it recur; give an end day without a repeat to cover a whole phase (such as 'Starter feed, day 0–13'). Check every dose and product with your vet or extension officer."

Each care item is one card. The tab lists all items in the guide, including items that apply only to some cycle types (these carry the line "Applies only to some cycle types of this enterprise.").

**Controls on every care card**

| Control | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Type** | Feed, Water, Vaccination, Medicine, Spray, Fertiliser, Weigh / sample, Check or Task. Sets the coloured tag. |
| **What** | The title of the item, for example "Gumboro vaccine". Required. |
| **Product / feed type** | The feed, vaccine, chemical or fertiliser, for example "Broiler starter (crumbs)". |
| **Dose / amount** | Free text, for example "25 g/bird/day" or "200 kg/ha". |
| **How (route / method)** | For example "Drinking water", "Eye drop", "Foliar spray". |
| **Withdrawal (days)** | The number of days before harvest, slaughter or milk use that this must stop. The Care Plan then shows "Stop (n) days before harvest / slaughter / milk use — check the label." |
| **Starts on day** | The cycle day the item starts. 0 is the start date. If left empty it counts as 0. |
| **Repeat every (days)** | Leave empty (it shows "no repeat") for a one-off or a phase. Type a number of days for a repeating item. |
| **Until day** | The last day. With a repeat, the item stops after this day. Without a repeat, it turns the item into a phase covering the whole stretch. If you leave it empty the item is one day only. |
| **Notes** | Any extra information that shows under the item. |
| **Remove** | The bin button deletes the item. |

**Adding an item.** Under the list, next to the word **Add:**, click one of the coloured buttons: **+ Feed**, **+ Water**, **+ Vaccination**, **+ Medicine**, **+ Spray**, **+ Fertiliser**, **+ Weigh / sample**, **+ Check** or **+ Task**. A new empty card of that type is added at the end, starting on day 0.

**Sort by day.** The link **Sort by day** (right side) puts the cards in order of their start day. This only tidies the editor; the Care Plan always orders items by day by itself.

The line below the buttons shows the item count and lists the colour-coded types.

**How the three shapes are made**

| You want | Fill in | Leave empty |
|---|---|---|
| A one-off (a vaccine on day 14) | **Starts on day** = 14 | **Repeat every**, **Until day** |
| A repeating job (weigh every 7 days from day 7 to day 42) | **Starts on day** = 7, **Repeat every** = 7, **Until day** = 42 | — |
| A phase (starter feed, days 0 to 13) | **Starts on day** = 0, **Until day** = 13 | **Repeat every** |

> **Tip:** Think of "Until day" as the last day the item still applies. For phases that follow each other, set the next phase to start the day after the previous one ends (starter ends on day 13, grower starts on day 14), so that there is no gap in "The programme right now".

### Back to built-in defaults

The button **Back to built-in defaults** (at the right of the two tabs) asks "Replace everything here with the built-in defaults?" If you confirm, **both tabs** are refilled with the built-in daily boxes for the category and the built-in starter care guide of the template. Your edits in the open editor are discarded. Nothing is saved until you click a save button.

Use it when you made a mess and want to start again. It restores the built-in category boxes (all of them, including those for other cycle types) and the starter guide.

### Naming your template

At the bottom left of the window, **Template name** is filled in for you:

- For a built-in template the suggested name is "My" followed by the template name, for example "My Tomato".
- For a template that is already yours, it shows its current name.

Change it to something meaningful. The name is also used as the short name, so it appears in cycle names, in the **Add Enterprise** window and in the Care Plan drop-down. See "Good practice" for naming ideas.

### The save buttons

| Button | Shown when | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **Save as my template** | The template in use is **Built-in** | Creates a new template of yours from the editor's contents. Version 1. The enterprise you opened the editor from switches to it at once. |
| **Save changes** | The template in use is **already yours** | Overwrites your template with the new contents and raises the version by one (v1 becomes v2). Every enterprise that uses this template switches to the new contents at once. |
| **Save as new copy** | The template is already yours | Leaves your existing template as it is and saves the editor's contents as a **new** template (version 1), under the name in **Template name**. This enterprise switches to the copy. Other enterprises keep the old one. |
| **Delete template** | The template is already yours | Starts a delete. The button changes to **Confirm delete (back to built-in)**. Click it to remove the template. Enterprises that used it return to the built-in template it was copied from. |
| **Cancel** | Always | Closes the editor without saving. |

A line under the buttons reminds you: "Saved templates are shared with everyone in your company and can be chosen for your next cycle."

> **Watch out:** **Save as my template** always makes a **new** template, even if you have already saved one from the same built-in template. If you open the editor on the built-in version a second time, you will end up with two saved templates. To improve an existing one, switch to it first (Care Plan drop-down), then open the editor and use **Save changes**.

### Messages you may see when saving

When something is wrong a red message appears above the footer and nothing is saved. Fix the problem and click save again. The checks cover both tabs at once, so the message may refer to a card on the other tab.

| Message | What to do |
|---|---|
| **Every record field needs a name.** | A box on the Daily record fields tab has an empty **Name**. Fill it in or remove the card. |
| **"(name)" is a choice field — give it at least two options.** | A **Choice from a list** box has fewer than two non-empty lines in **Options — one per line**. Add options or change the kind. |
| **Every care-guide item needs a title.** | A card on the Care guide tab has an empty **What**. |
| **"(title)": the end day is before the start day.** | The **Until day** is smaller than **Starts on day**. |
| **"(title)": repeat every must be 1 day or more.** | **Repeat every** is 0 or negative. Leave it empty if you do not want a repeat. |
| **Give your template a name.** | **Template name** is empty. |

### What saving does, in plain terms

When you save:

1. The editor's boxes and care items are stored in the template. Blank lines in choice options are dropped, and names are trimmed.
2. The template is stored with your company, next to your other saved templates.
3. The enterprise you opened the editor from is switched to it right away.
4. The Daily Log and Care Plan of that enterprise show your version straight away, for every cycle of the enterprise.

The starter-guide warning disappears, because the guide now belongs to you.

> **Note:** Your saved template is a copy. If AGRINUXES later improves its built-in starter guides or boxes, your copy is not changed. You can compare by switching the enterprise back to the built-in version on the Care Plan tab.

---

### Worked example A: a broiler farmer adds a feed brand and a water additive and changes the vaccination days

**The situation.** Chanda runs "Chongwe Broilers" on the built-in Broiler Chicken template. He buys feed from two local mills and wants to record the brand. His vet gives him a Gumboro programme with two doses and wants an acidifier in the drinking water from day 10 to day 20. Chanda wants all this in his Daily Log and Care Plan.

> **Note:** The numbers in this example are made up to show how the editor works. They are not veterinary advice. Chanda would use the days and products his own vet gave him.

**Before.** The built-in broiler boxes already include **Type of Feed** (Starter, Grower, Finisher and so on), **Water Additive** (text), **Vaccine / Medicine Given** (text) and **Water Consumed**. The starter vaccination calendar has Newcastle + IB on day 7, Gumboro on day 14 and a Newcastle booster on day 21. There is no acidifier item.

**Step by step: the Daily Log**

1. Click **Production**, then open Chanda's broiler cycle.
2. Click **Daily Log**, then click **Customise fields**. The editor opens on **Daily record fields**.
3. Scroll to the bottom and click **Add a record field**.
4. In the new card, type the **Name**: Feed Brand.
5. Change **Kind** to **Choice from a list**. In **Options — one per line** replace "Yes" and "No" with three lines: Local mill A, Local mill B, Own mix.
6. Use **Move up** until the card sits just below **Type of Feed**.
7. Click **Add a record field** again. **Name**: Water Additive Dose. **Kind**: Number. **Unit**: ml per 20 L. In **Hint shown under the box (optional)** type "Measure with a syringe".
8. Leave **Required** unticked on both. Keep the existing **Water Additive** box to record the product name.

**Step by step: the Care guide**

9. Click the **Care guide (17)** tab.
10. Find the card **Gumboro (IBD)**. Change **Starts on day** from 14 to 12.
11. Find **Newcastle booster**. Change **Starts on day** from 21 to 28.
12. Click **+ Vaccination**. In the new card: **What** = Gumboro second dose; **Product / feed type** = IBD vaccine as advised by my vet; **How (route / method)** = Drinking water; **Starts on day** = 21.
13. Click **+ Water**. **What** = Acidifier in the drinking water; **Product / feed type** = Water acidifier; **Dose / amount** = As per label; **How** = Drinking water; **Starts on day** = 10; **Until day** = 20 (leave **Repeat every** empty, so this is a phase).
14. Click **Sort by day** to tidy the list.
15. In **Template name** type "Chongwe broilers — vet programme v1".
16. Click **Save as my template**.

**After**

| | Before (built-in) | After (Chanda's template) |
|---|---|---|
| Daily Log boxes | 11 for this cycle type | 13 for this cycle type: **Feed Brand** (list) under Type of Feed, and **Water Additive Dose** (number, ml per 20 L) |
| Daily record fields tab count | (23) | (25) |
| Gumboro (IBD) | Day 14 | Day 12 |
| Gumboro second dose | not in the guide | Day 21 |
| Newcastle booster | Day 21 | Day 28 |
| Acidifier in the drinking water | not in the guide | Phase, days 10 to 20 |
| Whole guide | 17 items | 19 items |
| Template tag on Care Plan | Built-in | My template · v1 |
| Starter guide warning | Shown | Gone |

The Daily Log now says "Using your template **Chongwe broilers — vet programme v1**". On days 10 to 20 the Care Plan shows the acidifier under **The programme right now**.

> **Tip:** If the cycle is already on day 13 when you move Gumboro to day 12, the item is now behind: it appears under **Overdue** as "1 day late". Tick **Done** if you already gave it, or give it and then tick.

---

### Worked example B: a tomato grower adds a spray programme with withdrawal days

**The situation.** Mutale grows tomatoes in Block B. Her agronomist gave her a spray calendar with two fungicides in rotation and a rule: the last spray of each product must be early enough to respect its waiting period before the first pick. She wants the calendar on her Care Plan with the waiting periods visible.

**What the waiting period means.** The **withdrawal period** (also called pre-harvest interval) is the minimum number of days between the last spray and harvest. It is printed on the product label. If the label says 14 days, you must not harvest for 14 days after that spray.

> **Note:** The product names, days and withdrawal figures below are placeholders to show how the screen works. Use the exact products, rates and waiting periods from your agronomist and the product label.

**Before.** The built-in tomato guide (11 items) has two spray lines: "Preventive fungicide against early and late blight" (day 40 to 125, every 10 days, 7 days withdrawal) and "Insecticide — only if scouting finds pests" (day 42 to 125, every 10 days, 7 days withdrawal). Both run until day 125, longer than the time the crop is picked.

The tomato template's stages add up as follows: Nursery 28 days, Land Prep & Transplanting 7, Vegetative Growth 30, Flowering & Fruit Set 21, so picking starts at about **day 86**. Harvesting runs from there for about 45 days.

**Step by step**

1. Click **Production** and open Mutale's tomato cycle.
2. Click **Care Plan**, then **Set my standards**. The editor opens on the **Care guide** tab.
3. Find the card **Preventive fungicide against early and late blight**. Change it as follows:
   - **What**: Fungicide A (protectant) — spray every 14 days
   - **Product / feed type**: Fungicide A (name from my agronomist)
   - **Dose / amount**: As per label
   - **How (route / method)**: Foliar spray, cover both sides of the leaves
   - **Withdrawal (days)**: 7
   - **Starts on day**: 35, **Repeat every (days)**: 14, **Until day**: 77
4. Click **+ Spray** and fill in a second card:
   - **What**: Fungicide B (systemic) — alternate weeks
   - **Product / feed type**: Fungicide B (name from my agronomist)
   - **Dose / amount**: As per label
   - **How**: Foliar spray
   - **Withdrawal (days)**: 14
   - **Starts on day**: 42, **Repeat every (days)**: 14, **Until day**: 70
   - **Notes**: Do not spray after day 70. The 14-day wait must finish before the first pick (about day 86).
5. Leave the insecticide item as it is, or change its **Until day** to 77 so that it also stops before picking.
6. Click **+ Task**. **What**: Last chance to spray before picking — check every product's waiting period. **Starts on day**: 77.
7. Click **Sort by day**. In **Template name** type "Tomato — Block B spray calendar v1". Click **Save as my template**.

**Before and after**

| Item | Before (built-in) | After (Mutale's template) |
|---|---|---|
| Fungicide | One line, every 10 days, day 40 to 125, 7 days withdrawal | **Fungicide A** every 14 days, days 35, 49, 63, 77, 7 days withdrawal |
| Second fungicide | Not in the guide | **Fungicide B** every 14 days, days 42, 56, 70, 14 days withdrawal |
| Insecticide | Day 42 to 125, every 10 days | Unchanged, or stopped at day 77 |
| Reminder to stop | Not in the guide | Task on day 77 |
| Whole guide | 11 items | 13 items |

**What Mutale sees on the Care Plan.** On day 42 the section **Due today** shows a green **Spray** tag with "Fungicide B (systemic) — alternate weeks", then "Fungicide B (name from my agronomist) · As per label · Foliar spray" and an amber line "Stop 14 days before harvest / slaughter / milk use — check the label." After she sprays she clicks **Done**, and the team sees "done 12 Nov by Mutale".

> **Watch out:** The app only displays the waiting period. It does not calculate the last safe spray date, and it will not stop you from harvesting or selling. You must check the dates yourself. That is why Mutale ended Fungicide A at day 77 (7 + 77 = 84) and Fungicide B at day 70 (14 + 70 = 84), both before the first pick at about day 86.

---

### Worked example C: a dairy farmer changes the feeding phases

**The situation.** Banda keeps dairy cows near Kafue. The built-in Dairy Cattle guide splits the year into a dry-cow ration, an early-lactation ration and one long mid / late-lactation ration. Her nutrition adviser wants a short close-up ration before calving, a shorter early-lactation phase and a separate late-lactation phase with less dairy meal.

**How the dairy guide counts the days.** Day 0 is the start of the dry period, which is where the built-in Dairy Cattle stages begin (Dry Period 60 days, Calving 3 days, Early Lactation, Mid & Late Lactation). Calving is on day 60. Banda therefore starts her cycle on the first day of the dry period.

**Before (built-in feed phases).**

| Phase | Days | Product | Dose |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dry-cow ration | 0 to 59 | Good hay / pasture + minerals | Limit concentrates to about 1–2 kg/day |
| Early-lactation ration | 63 to 163 | Dairy meal + hay / silage | About 1 kg dairy meal per 2–3 L of milk above maintenance; roughage free access |
| Mid / late-lactation ration | 164 to 368 | Dairy meal + hay / silage | Adjust dairy meal to the milk yield |
| Mineral lick | 0 to 368 | Dairy mineral lick | Free access |

**Step by step**

1. Click **Production**, open the dairy cycle, click **Care Plan** and then **Set my standards**.
2. On the **Care guide** tab, find **Dry-cow ration**. Change **Until day** from 59 to 44.
3. Click **+ Feed**. **What**: Close-up (transition) ration. **Product / feed type**: Dry-cow hay with a little lactation meal, as advised. **Dose / amount**: Build up slowly to about 2 kg a day. **Starts on day**: 45. **Until day**: 62. Leave **Repeat every** empty.
4. Find **Early-lactation ration**. Change **Until day** from 163 to 120.
5. Find **Mid / late-lactation ration**. Change **What** to Mid-lactation ration, **Starts on day** from 164 to 121 and **Until day** from 368 to 250.
6. Click **+ Feed**. **What**: Late-lactation ration. **Product / feed type**: Dairy meal + hay / silage. **Dose / amount**: Reduce dairy meal as the milk yield falls. **Starts on day**: 251. **Until day**: 368.
7. Click **Sort by day**. In **Template name** type "Dairy — Banda feeding phases v1" and click **Save as my template**.

**After (Banda's feed phases).**

| Phase | Days | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Dry-cow ration | 0 to 44 | Shortened |
| Close-up (transition) ration | 45 to 62 | New |
| Early-lactation ration | 63 to 120 | Shortened |
| Mid-lactation ration | 121 to 250 | Renamed, new days |
| Late-lactation ration | 251 to 368 | New |
| Mineral lick | 0 to 368 | Unchanged |

On the Care Plan, only the phase that covers today is shown under **The programme right now**. On day 50, for example, Banda sees **Close-up (transition) ration** and **Mineral lick**. The built-in calving task on day 60 and the other reminders are untouched, and the whole guide now has 17 items (the 15 built-in items, one of them renamed, plus 2 new ones).

> **Tip:** After you change phases, open **Whole guide** and look down the **When** column. Every day from the start to the end should be covered by one feed phase. A gap means that on those days the Care Plan shows no feed programme.

---


## My templates: saving, sharing and reusing

### Where your saved templates live

**What it is for.** Every template you save is stored in your **company account**, not on one phone or one computer. Everyone in the company, on every device, sees the same saved templates. If a manager saves "Chongwe broilers — vet programme v1" on the office computer, the supervisor sees it on the phone as soon as the page has loaded.

There is no separate "My templates" page. You meet your templates in these places:

| Where | What you see |
|---|---|
| **Settings → Enterprises → Add Enterprise** | Your templates are listed first, each tile marked **My template**. |
| **Production → New Cycle** (step 1) | Under each enterprise the name of the template it follows, which is your template's name if it follows one of yours. Step 3 shows it again in the green summary. |
| **Production → cycle → header** | Next to the enterprise name, the template name. |
| **Production → cycle → Daily Log** | The line **Using your template (name)**. |
| **Production → cycle → Care Plan → Template in use** | The name with the tag **My template · v** and a number, and a drop-down to switch. |

### Switching an enterprise between the built-in and your versions

**Before you start.** You have saved at least one version of the template (or someone in your company has).

**Step by step**

1. Open **Production**, then the cycle, then the **Care Plan** tab.
2. In **Template in use**, find the drop-down at the right. It appears only when there is more than one choice. The choices read **Built-in: (name)** and **Mine: (name)**.
3. Pick the version you want. The switch happens at once. There is no confirmation window.

**What changes when you switch**

- The Daily Log boxes and the Care Plan of **every cycle of that enterprise** now come from the version you picked.
- Records already saved stay as they are.
- Care ticks stay as they are, as long as the same item still exists on the same day.
- The stages of existing cycles do not change.

> **Tip:** Switching back to **Built-in** is a quick way to compare your version with the starter guide, or to go back if you are unhappy with your changes.

### Reusing a template for the next cycle

The enterprise keeps pointing to your template. When you start the next cycle with **New Cycle**, that cycle uses your boxes and your care guide from day 0. Nothing more to do.

### Using one template for a second enterprise of the same kind

There are two ways.

**Way 1: choose it when you add the enterprise**

1. Click **Settings**, then **Add Enterprise**.
2. Type the **Enterprise Name**.
3. Find your template in the grid (it is marked **My template** and is listed first) and click it.
4. Click **Add Enterprise**.

**Way 2: switch an enterprise you already have**

1. Make sure the second enterprise exists (it may follow the built-in version).
2. Open a cycle of that enterprise, click **Care Plan**.
3. Use the drop-down in **Template in use** and pick **Mine: (your template)**.

If you want the second enterprise to have a slightly different version, choose your template for it, open **Set my standards**, change what you need and click **Save as new copy** with a new name.

### What happens to cycles when you change, switch or delete

| What you do | Effect on existing cycles of the enterprise |
|---|---|
| **Save changes** to your template | All cycles that use it show the new boxes and care guide immediately. The version number goes up by one. |
| **Save as my template** or **Save as new copy** | The enterprise you used it from switches to the new template. Its cycles show the new boxes and care guide. Other enterprises are not affected. |
| Switch the enterprise with the **Template in use** drop-down | Its cycles show the chosen version. |
| **Delete template** | Every enterprise that used it goes back to the built-in template it was copied from. Its cycles show the built-in boxes and starter guide. |

Things that stay the same in every case:

- **Daily records you saved are never deleted by a template change.** If a box no longer exists in the new version, the old value is still shown in **Recent Records**, but under its internal code instead of a friendly name, and without its unit or red-range check.
- **Stages.** A cycle keeps the stages it was created with. Editing a template does not add or remove stages in running cycles.
- **Care ticks.** Each tick belongs to one care item on one day. If you delete an item, its ticks no longer appear. If you move an item to another day, the new day starts with no tick.

> **Watch out:** Because a change reaches running cycles at once, do not rewrite the care guide for a batch that is half-way through unless you mean to. Moving Gumboro from day 14 to day 12 on a flock that is on day 13 will show it as one day late. Use **Save as new copy** with a clear name and switch only the enterprise (or start a new cycle) that should use the change.

### Limits

What you can and cannot change:

| You can change | You cannot change in the editor |
|---|---|
| The boxes on the Daily Log (names, kinds, units, required, normal ranges, targets, options, hints, order) | The stages of the template and their lengths |
| The care guide (items, types, products, doses, methods, withdrawal days, start day, repeat, end day, notes) | The measurements, key activities, alerts and expected outputs shown on the **Stages** tab |
| The name of your template | The Production KPIs and their benchmarks |
| | The list of cycle types and the setup questions in the New Cycle wizard |
| | The event types in the **Events** tab |
| | The built-in templates themselves |

Also remember:

- A saved template always descends from one built-in template. You cannot turn a tomato template into a cattle template. Add a new enterprise from the right built-in template instead.
- There is no history of older versions. **Save changes** overwrites. If you want to keep an older version, use **Save as new copy** and put the version in the name.
- Saved templates belong to your company. They cannot be sent to another company.
- The editor does not ask for any extra permission. Anyone in your company who can open a cycle in **Production** can open the editor, and can save or delete your company's templates. Tell your team who is allowed to make changes.
- The approval workflow ("Require approval for edits") does not cover template changes or Care Plan ticks.

---

## Importing daily records from a spreadsheet (CSV)

### What it is for

If you keep your records on paper or in Excel, you can load many days at once instead of typing them one by one. You prepare a file with one row per day, import it, check the preview, and AGRINUXES creates one daily record for each good row. This is also the quickest way to load past records when you start using the app mid-way through a cycle.

**Before you start**

- The cycle must be **active**, and you must be on its **Daily Log** tab.
- Choose the right **Stage** in the stage list first. Rows with no stage in the file are put into this stage.
- You need a spreadsheet program that can save as CSV (Excel, Google Sheets, LibreOffice). CSV means "comma-separated values", a plain text file that every spreadsheet program can write.

Two buttons sit under **Save Daily Record**: **Import CSV** and **Template**.

### Step 1: download the template

1. On the **Daily Log** tab click **Template**. A file downloads with a name made from the cycle name, for example `Broiler_Oct_2026_daily_records_template.csv`.
2. Open it in your spreadsheet program.

The template file has three rows:

| Row | Contents |
|---|---|
| **Row 1: column headings** | `date`, `stage`, then one column for each box on your Daily Log that you can type into, then `notes`. The headings are the boxes' internal codes. |
| **Row 2: guidance line** | Every cell starts with `#`. It tells you what each column wants: the date format, the stage names, each box's label with its unit, the list of allowed answers for choice boxes, and a star for required boxes. The import ignores this row. |
| **Row 3: sample row** | Today's date, the first started stage, a `0` in every number box, the first answer of every choice box, and an empty text box. |

> **Watch out:** The sample row is real data. If you leave it as it is, it will import with zeros and the first choice answers. Replace it with your first day's figures or delete it.

Calculated boxes (such as Total Milk Yield) are not in the template. They are worked out for you during the import.

Only the boxes that apply to your cycle type are included. For a broiler cycle you will not get the egg or incubator columns.

### Step 2: fill in the rows

Type one day per row. The rules:

| Column | What to type |
|---|---|
| **date** | Required. (The headings `day` and `record date` also work.) Use `2026-10-06` (year-month-day). Day-first dates such as `06/10/2026`, `6-10-2026` or `6.10.26` are also read, and are always read **day first**, so `10/6/2026` means 10 June. Two-digit years count as 20xx. |
| **stage** | Optional. (The heading `phase` also works.) The stage name exactly as shown in the Daily Log stage list, for example `Brooding`. Capitals and spaces do not matter. If you leave it empty, or have no stage column, the row goes into the stage you had selected. |
| Any box | The column heading may be the box's internal code (as in the template) or its label on the screen, for example `Feed Consumed` or `feed_kg`. Capitals, spaces and punctuation do not matter. |
| Number boxes | Plain numbers. Use a full stop for decimals (`2.5`). Do not type the unit. |
| Choice boxes | One of the allowed answers, spelled as on screen. |
| Text boxes | Any words. |
| **notes** | Optional. Headings `notes`, `note`, `observations`, `comments` and `remarks` are all accepted. |

You may leave any box empty. Keep the heading row at the top. You can delete columns you do not need.

> **Watch out:** Do not use a comma as the decimal sign. A comma inside a number is removed by the import, so `12,5` becomes `125`. If your computer's regional settings use decimal commas, change the numbers to use a full stop before you save the CSV.

> **Tip:** In Excel, save with **Save As → CSV UTF-8 (Comma delimited)**. In Google Sheets use **File → Download → Comma-separated values (.csv)**. Files with semicolons or tabs between the columns are accepted too.

### Step 3: import and read the preview

1. Click **Import CSV**. The window **Import daily records** opens with the cycle name and "one row per day".
2. Click the dashed area (**Drop a CSV here or click to choose**) and pick your file, or drag the file onto it. The grey box under it lists the **Expected columns**: `date`, `stage`, `notes` and the box codes (a red star marks required boxes).
3. The preview appears. At the top you see the file name, a green **n ready** badge, and a red **n with errors (skipped)** badge if some rows are bad. Click **Choose another file** to start over.
4. If some of your column headings were not recognised, an amber box says **Ignored columns not recognised for this enterprise** and lists them. Their data is not imported. Check the spelling, or check that the box exists for your cycle type.
5. The table shows each row: its line number in the file, the date, the stage, the first four boxes, and a **Status** column.
6. Click **Import n records**. The window shows **Imported n records.**

**How to read the Status column**

| Status | Colour | Meaning | Imported? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OK** | Green | Nothing wrong. | Yes |
| A warning text | Amber | Something looks odd but the row is usable. | Yes |
| An error text | Red row | The row cannot be imported. | No, skipped |

**Warnings (the row is still imported)**

| Warning | Meaning |
|---|---|
| (Box) (value) outside expected (min)–(max) | The number is outside the normal range of that box. |
| (Box): "(value)" is not one of (list) | A choice answer does not match any allowed option. It is imported exactly as typed. |
| Stage not started yet | The stage named in the row has not begun in this cycle. |
| A record for this day already exists | There is already a record for that date and stage. The new row is imported anyway, so you will have two. |

**Errors (the row is skipped)**

| Error | Meaning |
|---|---|
| No "date" column | The file has no date column at all. Every row fails. |
| Unreadable date "(text)" | The date is empty or cannot be understood. |
| Unknown stage "(text)" | The stage name does not match any stage of this cycle. |
| No stage | No stage was given and none is selected. |
| (Box): "(text)" is not a number | A number box has text in it. |
| (Box) is required | A required box is empty. |
| No measurements on this row | The row has a date but no data. |

A row can have several messages, joined with semicolons.

**Good to know**

- Only the **ready** rows are imported. Bad rows are skipped, and the rest still go in. To fix the skipped rows, put only those rows in a second file and import that. Do not import the whole file again, or the good rows will be doubled.
- The same day appearing twice **inside the file** is not flagged. Only clashes with records that are already saved are flagged.
- Imported records are added straight away. They do not go through the approval workflow.
- The preview shows the first 200 rows; all good rows are imported even beyond the 200.
- Imported records look like ordinary records. You can edit or delete them in **Recent Records**.
- Import and Template are not shown on a completed cycle.

### Worked example: a broiler batch

Mrs Phiri kept her first five days of records on paper. She downloaded the template for her broiler cycle, kept only the columns she uses, and typed her figures. Her sheet:

| date | stage | feed_kg | feed_type | mortality_count | avg_body_weight | house_temp | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-01 | Brooding | 18 | Starter | 2 | 45 | 33 | Chicks placed |
| 02/10/2026 | Brooding | 20 | Starter | 1 | 52 | 31 | |
| 2026-10-03 | | 22 | Starter feed | 0 | | 30 | |
| 2026-10-04 | Brooding | abc | Starter | | | | |
| 2026-10-05 | Growing | 25 | Starter | 1 | | 30 | |

What the preview says:

| Row | Status | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 1 (1 Oct) | Amber warning, imported | House Temperature 33 is outside the expected 18–32. |
| 2 (2 Oct) | **OK**, imported | The date `02/10/2026` was read day first as 2 October. |
| 3 (3 Oct) | Amber warning, imported | "Starter feed" is not one of the allowed answers (Starter, Grower, Finisher, Layer mash, Breeder feed, Own mix, Other). It is imported as typed, and goes into the stage she had selected because the stage cell is empty. |
| 4 (4 Oct) | **Error, skipped** | Feed Consumed: "abc" is not a number; Feed Consumed is required. |
| 5 (5 Oct) | **Error, skipped** | Unknown stage "Growing". The broiler stage is called **Grower**. |

The summary reads **3 ready** and **2 with errors (skipped)**. She fixes rows 4 and 5, saves a new file with just those two rows, and imports it.

### If something goes wrong

| What you see | What to do |
|---|---|
| **No data rows found in this file.** | The file has only a heading row, or is empty. |
| Everything has the error No "date" column | Rename the first column to `date`. |
| Your whole column is under **Ignored columns** | The heading does not match a box. Use the label or the code from the template. |
| Numbers look wrong (125 instead of 12.5) | A decimal comma was used. Use a full stop. |
| Dates are swapped (June instead of October) | A month-first date was written. Use `2026-10-06`. |
| **Import n records** is greyed out | There are no ready rows. Fix the errors first. |
| The **Import CSV** button is missing | The cycle is completed, or the template has no boxes. |

---

## Good practice

### Setting your own standards

Your standards should describe what you really do, written down so that every worker follows the same plan.

1. **Start with the starter guide, not a blank page.** Open the Care Plan and read the whole guide once. Mark what you already do, what you do differently, and what you never do.
2. **Bring your vet, agronomist or extension officer.** Sit down with the printed whole guide and let them correct the products, doses, ages and withdrawal periods. Get their advice for your region and your disease history.
3. **Change only what you are sure of.** Leave other items as they are.
4. **Write products and doses the way they are on the pack.** For example "Newcastle La Sota, 1 dose per bird, drinking water", so that a new worker can follow it.
5. **Always fill in withdrawal days for sprays and medicines** that need them, so that the amber warning shows on the day.
6. **Use phases for programmes and repeats for routines.** Feed rations, brooder temperature and drinking-water programmes are phases. Weighing, scouting and sprays are repeating items. Vaccines and transplanting are one-offs.
7. **Keep the cycle start date honest.** Day 0 of the guide must match day 0 of the real cycle. If your crop starts with land preparation in the guide, start the cycle on the day you begin land preparation.
8. **Review after each cycle.** After a batch or season, compare your Daily Log with the plan. Adjust the days, then use **Save changes** or **Save as new copy**.

### Examples of standards worth writing down

| Area | What to put in the care guide |
|---|---|
| **Feed programme** | One phase per ration with the feed name and the amount per animal or per day, the days it applies, and a note on when to change over. |
| **Vaccination calendar** | One one-off item per vaccine with the day, product, route (drinking water, eye drop, injection) and a note if the vet administers it. |
| **Spray calendar** | One repeating item per product with the interval and the last day, the withdrawal days, and the label rate in the dose. Add a "last spray before harvest" task. |
| **Fertiliser calendar** | A one-off for basal fertiliser and one item per top-dress, with product and rate per hectare. |
| **Checks** | Repeating items for scouting, weighing and water-quality checks, so that they appear as reminders. |

### Choosing record boxes

- Record what you will actually use to make decisions: feed, deaths, weight, yield, treatments.
- Add a box for anything your buyer, bank or programme asks you to prove (for example the feed brand, or the batch number of the vaccine).
- Set the **Normal minimum** and **Normal maximum** to what is right for your site. A box that is always red teaches people to ignore red.
- Prefer a **Choice** box over free text when the answers are always the same, so that reports can compare like with like.
- Do not add boxes you will not fill in. A short form is more likely to be completed every day.

### Naming your templates

Names are shown everywhere, so make them clear and unique.

- Say what and for whom: "Broilers — Chongwe house programme".
- Add a **version** at the end: "v1", "v2", "2026 season". The app's own version number goes up when you click **Save changes**, but older versions are overwritten, so a version in the name is your only way to keep a history. Use **Save as new copy** to keep the old one.
- Keep names short enough to read in a small tile.
- Do not use the same name for two templates.

### Before you rely on a starter guide

- Read the amber warning on the Care Plan. A starter guide is "typical practice, not a prescription".
- Check each product against your country's registered products and the label. Vaccines, dewormers, dips and pesticides differ by country, age of animal and season.
- Confirm withdrawal periods with the label. Milk, meat, eggs and produce must not be sold before the waiting period is over.
- Ask the Department of Veterinary Services or your extension officer for the official vaccination and tick-control schedule for your district.
- If the guide has an item that does not apply to your farm, remove it so that it does not create false overdue reminders.

---

## Troubleshooting and questions

**I picked the wrong template when I set up. What now?**
Add an enterprise with the right template (**Settings → Add Enterprise**). An enterprise cannot be moved to a different kind of farming. Nothing is lost by having an extra enterprise you never use.

**The template I want is not on the category tabs in Add Enterprise.**
The category tabs do not include Insects & Worms, Aquatic Plants & Algae or Forestry & Agroforestry. Click **All**, or type part of the name in the search box.

**My search finds nothing.**
The search matches the template name and the species only. Try a shorter word, for example "tilapia" or "maize".

**The wizard offers cycle types that do not look like mine (I keep crickets or trees).**
Insects & Worms, Aquatic Plants & Algae and Forestry & Agroforestry have no cycle types of their own, so the wizard shows the four Crops types. Pick any; the Daily Log boxes are the same either way.

**A box I expect is missing from the Daily Log.**
Poultry boxes depend on the cycle type. Eggs show only for laying types, incubator readings only for **Egg Incubation & Hatch**, litter only for broilers and turkeys. Check the type of the cycle, or customise the template.

**The Care Plan says "Whole guide (0 items)", or shows nothing, although the template has a guide.**
The items you see depend on the cycle type. A broiler guide is attached to **Broiler Grow-Out**; if the cycle was started with another type, none of those items apply. Start a new cycle with the right type.

**Everything is overdue.**
The Care Plan counts days from the cycle's start date. If you back-dated the start, every earlier item that was not ticked is overdue (it looks back up to 30 days). Tick the items you already did, or correct the start date if it was entered wrongly.

**I cannot see Done or Undo.**
They appear only on an **active** cycle. On a paused, planning or completed cycle the Care Plan is read-only.

**I cannot add a record on the Daily Log.**
A completed cycle shows "Cycle complete — viewing historical records only." If the template has no boxes left (after removing them all) **Save Daily Record** stays greyed out and the Import buttons disappear. Add a box, or go back to the built-in defaults.

**It let me save an empty record.**
Yes. The Daily Log does not check that boxes are filled in. Required stars are reminders only. (The CSV import does check required boxes.)

**A box turned red but the record saved.**
Red means outside the normal range you or the template set. It is a prompt to check, not a block.

**Total Milk Yield does not appear.**
It needs a figure in all three milking boxes. Type 0 for any milking you did not do.

**Why did my edit not change a record straight away?**
Your company may have **Require approval for edits** switched on in **Settings**. The edit waits as **Pending Edits** until the approver clicks **Approve**.

**I saved a template and the amber starter-guide warning disappeared.**
That is expected. The guide is now yours.

**Can two people edit the same template at once?**
Saved templates are shared by the whole company, so agree who is responsible for maintaining them and tell your team before changing one that is in use.

**I deleted a template and my enterprise changed.**
Enterprises that used the deleted template return to the built-in template it was copied from. Your records are untouched.

**Will my saved template get the improvements if AGRINUXES updates the built-in guides?**
No. Your saved template is a copy and stays as you saved it. To see the latest built-in guide, switch the enterprise to the **Built-in** version on the Care Plan tab and compare.

**Can I change the stages of a cycle?**
No. Stages come from the template when the cycle is created and cannot be edited in the template editor.

---

## Quick reference: what to click

| I want to… | Click path |
|---|---|
| Choose my farm's templates when I first sign up | Set-up wizard → step 4 **Setup** → select category cards → click template rows → **Finish setup** |
| Add a new enterprise from a template | **Settings** → **Enterprises** → **Add Enterprise** → type the name → pick a template tile → **Add Enterprise** |
| Use one of my saved templates for a new enterprise | **Settings** → **Add Enterprise** → click the tile marked **My template** |
| Start a new cycle | **Production** → **New Cycle** → choose enterprise and cycle type → **Next →** → answer setup questions → **Next →** → name and dates → **Start Cycle** |
| Record today's figures | **Production** → open the cycle → **Daily Log** → fill in the boxes → **Save Daily Record** |
| Change a saved record | **Production** → cycle → **Daily Log** → **Recent Records** → pencil button → **Save Changes** |
| Delete a saved record | **Production** → cycle → **Daily Log** → **Recent Records** → bin button → **Delete** |
| Add or change daily boxes | **Production** → cycle → **Daily Log** → **Customise fields** |
| See what is due today and what is overdue | **Production** → cycle → **Care Plan** |
| Mark a care item as done | **Production** → cycle → **Care Plan** → **Done** (use **Undo** if wrong) |
| See the whole care guide | **Production** → cycle → **Care Plan** → **Whole guide** |
| Change feeding, vaccination or spray days | **Production** → cycle → **Care Plan** → **Set my standards** (or **Edit my standards**) → **Care guide** tab |
| Save my changes as my template | In the editor: type a **Template name** → **Save as my template** |
| Update my template later | Open **Edit my standards** → change → **Save changes** |
| Keep the old version and make a new one | **Edit my standards** → new **Template name** → **Save as new copy** |
| Go back to the built-in version | **Care Plan** → **Template in use** drop-down → **Built-in: …** |
| Switch to another version of mine | **Care Plan** → **Template in use** drop-down → **Mine: …** |
| Throw away my version | **Edit my standards** → **Delete template** → **Confirm delete (back to built-in)** |
| Start again with the built-in boxes and guide | In the editor: **Back to built-in defaults** (then save) |
| Download a spreadsheet template for daily records | **Production** → cycle → **Daily Log** → **Template** |
| Load many days of records at once | **Production** → cycle → **Daily Log** → **Import CSV** → choose file → check the preview → **Import n records** |
| Turn on approval for edits and deletions | **Settings** → **Edit & Approval Control** → **Require approval for edits** |
| Approve or reject an edit | **Settings** → **Edit & Approval Control** → **Pending Edits** → **Approve** or **Reject** |
