# Part 3 — Running Production: Cycles, Stages, Events and Outputs

This part explains how to run a crop season, a batch of broilers, a fish pond or any other farm activity from start to finish on the **Production** page. It covers the list of cycles, the cycle header, the **Overview**, **Stages**, **Events**, **Outputs** and **Close Cycle** tabs, how events post money to **Finance** and stock to **Inventory**, and the optional approval workflow for edits and deletions.

Two tabs of the cycle screen are explained in Part 2 — Templates, Record Fields and Care Guides, and are not repeated here: the **Daily Log** tab (the numbers you record every day, the CSV import and customising the record fields) and the **Care Plan** tab (the care-guide checklist). Templates themselves, and how to change them, are also in Part 2. How the Finance and Inventory pages work once the entries arrive is in Part 4 — Running the Farm Business: Inventory, Sales, Finance, Credit and Reports.

## Words used in this part

| Word | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Enterprise** | One line of farming in your workspace, for example "Broilers", "Maize" or "Tilapia Ponds". Each enterprise uses one template. |
| **Template** | The ready-made plan behind an enterprise: its stages, what to measure, expected outputs and target figures (KPIs). |
| **Cycle** | One run of an enterprise from start to finish: one batch of chicks, one maize season, one pond. A cycle has a start date, stages, daily records and events. |
| **Stage** | One part of the cycle, for example Brooding, Grower, Finishing. A cycle moves through its stages one at a time, in order. |
| **Event** | A one-off thing that happens: a purchase, a vaccination, a death, a harvest, a sale. Events are what feed Finance and Inventory. |
| **Output** | What the cycle produces (eggs, grain, fish, milk) and where it goes (sale, inventory, processing, own use...). |
| **KPI** | Key performance indicator: a target figure to judge how well the cycle is doing, such as feed conversion ratio. |

## Where your production data is kept

Cycles, stages, daily records, events, transfers and the care-guide ticks are saved to your **company account**. Everyone in your company sees the same cycles on every device, and nothing is lost if you change phone or computer. A copy is also kept in your browser so the page opens quickly and keeps working for a moment if the signal drops; the next change you make saves it again.

The money and stock that events create are saved to the company account too, in **Finance** and **Inventory**.

---

## The Production page

### What it is for

The **Production** page (in the left menu) is where you manage every cycle on the farm. The left side lists your cycles; the right side shows the cycle you pick, with its tabs.

### Before you start

- You need access to the **Production** module. If your role does not include it, the **Production** item does not appear in your menu. Ask the account owner to change your role in **Team & Permissions** (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account).
- You need at least one enterprise. If you have none, add one first (see Part 1).
- If you open Production from a single enterprise (for example by clicking that enterprise on the Dashboard), the page shows only that enterprise's cycles, and the **New Cycle** form offers only that enterprise. Open **Production** from the menu to see everything.

### What you will see

The page has two panels.

**Left panel: the cycle list.** The heading is **Production Engine**, with a count underneath such as "3 open · 5 closed". The green **New Cycle** button is at the top right of this panel. Below it the cycles are grouped:

| Section | What it holds |
|---|---|
| **Open — n** | Every cycle that is *active*, *planning* or *paused*, plus programs that still have an open phase. A small pulsing green dot shows these are live. |
| **Individual Cycles** | A small label that appears only when you have programs and ordinary cycles together. |
| **Planning** and **Paused** | Small labels inside the open section for cycles in those states. Active cycles come first with no label. |
| **Closed / Completed — n** | Finished cycles. This section is folded away. Click it to open or close it. |

**Each cycle row shows:**

- A coloured dot for the status: green for active, blue for planning, amber for paused, grey for completed, red for aborted.
- The cycle name in bold.
- The enterprise name with its template icon.
- For an *active* cycle: **Day n** in green (the number of whole days since the start date; the start day itself is Day 0), the name of the current stage, and a thin green progress bar. The bar shows the share of stages already completed.
- For a *completed* cycle: the word **Closed** and a full grey bar.
- A small dustbin icon at the right of the row, which deletes the cycle (see "Deleting a cycle" below).

If a cycle belongs to an enterprise that has since been removed, the row has an amber tint and shows **Enterprise removed** in place of the enterprise name. See "If something goes wrong" at the end of this part.

If you have no cycles at all, the list says **No cycles yet.** and **Start a new production cycle above.**

**Right panel: the cycle detail.** Until you click a cycle it says **Select a cycle to view details**. Once you pick one, the header, the tabs and the tab contents appear.

### Step by step: open a cycle

1. Click **Production** in the left menu.
2. In the left panel, click the cycle you want. The right panel fills with its header and tabs.
3. Click a tab name (**Overview**, **Stages**, **Daily Log**, **Care Plan**, **Events**, **Outputs**, **Close Cycle**) to move between them.

### Good to know

- **On a phone or a narrow window** the two panels do not sit side by side. You see the list first. When you tap a cycle, the list disappears and the cycle takes the whole screen. To go back to the list, tap the small **X** at the top right of the cycle header. On a wide screen (a computer or a large tablet) the list stays on the left and the X is not shown.
- The **Close Cycle** tab appears only while a cycle is *active*. Once a cycle is closed the tab is gone.
- A cycle that you just created is *active* straight away; it does not start in *planning*. A *planning* cycle only appears when it was created by a transfer (see "Transfer Output").
- The list does not have a search box or filters. Cycles are shown in the order they were created.

---

## Starting a new cycle

### What it is for

A cycle is how you tell the system "I am starting a new batch or season". It builds the stages from your enterprise's template and begins counting days.

### Before you start

Have your enterprise set up. Decide the start date and, for animals, how many you are starting with.

### Step by step

1. On the **Production** page, click **New Cycle**.
2. **Step 1 of 3.** Under **Which enterprise?** tap the enterprise. Under **What type of cycle?** tap the kind of cycle that fits (for example **Broiler Grow-Out** for poultry or **Rain-Fed Season** for crops). Each choice shows a short description and its usual length ("~42 days"). A grey box named **Stages from template** previews the stages the cycle will have. Click **Next**.
3. **Step 2 of 3.** Fill in the setup questions for your kind of farming (number of birds, area in hectares, number of ponds, and so on). A red star marks the questions that matter most, but the form does not stop you if you skip them. Click **Next**.
4. **Step 3 of 3.** Check the summary, then fill in **Cycle Name** (required), **Start Date** (required), **Expected End** (filled in for you from the cycle type; you can change it) and **Notes** (optional). Click **Start Cycle**.

### What you will see

- The cycle appears in the **Open** list with a green dot and **Day 0**.
- One stage per template stage is created. The **first stage is active**, the rest are *pending*.
- The numbers from Step 2 become the cycle's **production units** (for example "Flock — Broiler Grow-Out, 500 birds"), which show on the **Overview** tab.
- The setup answers are copied into the cycle **Notes** as a line beginning "Config:", together with a line "Cycle type: ...".

### Good to know

- The cycle type you choose (Step 1) sets the suggested end date and which setup questions you see. It does **not** change the stages. The stages always come from the enterprise's template. To change the stages, change or copy the template (see Part 2).
- The default cycle name is the template's short name and the current month, for example "Broiler Jul 2026". Rename it to something you will recognise, such as "Broiler Batch 3 — House A".
- Eggs set in an incubation cycle are counted as eggs; flocks as birds; herds as head; fish as fish; fields as hectares; greenhouses as square metres; orchards as trees; apiaries as hives; mushroom units as blocks; processing as kilograms of raw material.
- Some enterprise types (insects and worms, aquatic plants such as seaweed and spirulina, and forestry) do not have their own list of cycle types or setup questions. They are given the general crop list. Pick the closest option; the stages still come from your template.

### If something goes wrong

- **The Next button is greyed out in Step 1.** An enterprise and a cycle type must both be chosen.
- **The Start Cycle button is greyed out.** The cycle name or the start date is empty.
- **Your enterprise is not in the list.** Only active enterprises are offered, and when you came from one enterprise's own page only that enterprise is offered.

---

## Production programs and phases

### What it is for

A **production program** links several cycles into a chain, called **phases**. The typical example is a breeding programme: Phase 1 raises keets (baby guinea fowl) to growers, Phase 2 keeps the best birds as breeders, Phase 3 hatches their eggs. The program groups these cycles in the list, shows how far along the chain you are, and lets you move part of the output of one phase into the next phase, selling the rest.

### Before you start — read this first

> **Note:** In the current version there is **no button that creates a program from scratch**. The **New Cycle** form always creates an ordinary stand-alone cycle. The program grouping, the purple program banner and the **Transfer Output** button appear only on cycles that already belong to a program. If your cycles do not show a purple program banner, your farm does not have a program yet and this section does not apply to you. Programs on your account can be set up for you by the AGRINUXES team; ask support if you need one. Once a program exists, every step below works for you.

### What you will see in the list

A program is shown as a single card in the **Open** section (or in **Closed / Completed** when every phase is finished):

- A purple stacked-layers icon and the program name.
- A small line such as "3 phases · 1 active" (or "3 phases · 3 completed" when none is active).
- Click the card to unfold the phases. Each phase shows a dot, the phase label (for example "Phase 1: Keet Grow-Out"), the cycle name and a status tag (*active*, *completed*, *planning* or *paused*). Dots are joined by a thin line so it reads as a chain. The dustbin icon appears when you hover over a phase.
- Phases are listed in phase-number order.

Opening a cycle that belongs to a program also shows a **program banner** in the header, in purple. It contains:

- The program name in capital letters.
- A row of chips, one per phase, joined by arrows. The phase you are looking at is solid purple; finished phases are grey with a line through the text; the rest are outlined.
- **Next: Phase n** (with "— planning" if that cycle has not been started), or **Final phase of the program** if you are on the last phase.
- The **Transfer Output** button (see below).
- The **Open Phase →** button (only when there is a next phase). It jumps straight to the next phase's cycle and unfolds the program in the list.

The banner appears only when the program has at least two phases.

### How phases link: the source reference

When a transfer creates a new phase (see below), the new cycle remembers which cycle it came from. This record of "where did the starting stock come from" is called the **source reference**. When you move stock into an existing planning cycle instead, that cycle's source reference is set to the cycle you moved from as well. This is how you can trace a flock or a batch back to the cycle that produced it.

### Step by step: use the Open Phase button

1. Open a cycle that belongs to a program.
2. In the purple banner, click **Open Phase →**.
3. The next phase opens in the right panel, and its program unfolds in the left list.

### Good to know

- Deleting one phase deletes only that cycle, not the whole program. The remaining phases stay grouped.
- A program is "open" while at least one of its phases is active, planning or paused.
- The program name comes from its phases. If a phase has no name stored, the card is titled **Production Program**.

---

## The cycle header

### What it is for

The header at the top of the right panel is your at-a-glance status: which cycle, what state it is in, how many days it has run, which stage you are in, and the buttons to move on.

### What you will see

| Item | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Template icon, cycle name, enterprise and template name | Which cycle this is. |
| Status tag | **active**, **completed**, **paused**, **planning** (or aborted, shown in the same way). Green for active, grey for completed, amber for paused, blue for planning. |
| Dustbin icon | Deletes the whole cycle (see below). |
| **X** icon | Closes the cycle view and returns to the list. Shown on small screens only. |
| **Started** date | The start date in "day month year" form. |
| **Day n** | Whole days since the start date. |
| **n/m stages** | Stages completed out of the total number of stages. |
| Green line with a triangle | The current stage name (active cycles only). |
| Progress bar | Completed stages divided by total stages. |

### Care guide banner

If the cycle is active and the care guide has tasks due today or overdue, a coloured bar appears reading **Care guide:** followed by "n due today" and/or "n overdue", and **Open Care Plan** at the right. Blue means something is due today; red means something is overdue. Clicking it opens the **Care Plan** tab. The same number appears in brackets on the tab name. The care guide itself is explained in Part 2.

### Advance bar: moving to the next stage

For an active cycle that is not on its last stage, a green bar reads "Currently in **[stage]**. Ready to move to **[next stage]**?" with an **Advance** button.

#### Step by step

1. Check you have logged what belongs to the current stage (daily records, events).
2. Click **Advance**.
3. The current stage is marked **completed** and gets today's date as its end date. The next stage becomes **active** and gets today's date as its start date. The header, the list row and the progress bar update at once.

#### Good to know

- There is no confirmation question and no undo. If you click **Advance** by mistake, there is no button to go back a stage. Think before you click.
- You can only move forward one stage at a time and only in the template's order. You cannot skip a stage or jump ahead.
- Stage dates are set only by this button: the end date of the stage you leave and the start date of the stage you enter. The very first stage of a new cycle has no start date of its own, so its length is not shown on the **Stages** tab.
- You can still log events against any stage, including earlier ones: both the **Events** form and the **Daily Log** let you choose the stage.

### Last stage bar

On the last stage the green bar is replaced by an amber bar: "You are on the final stage. When done, close the cycle." with a **Close Cycle** button. It takes you to the **Close Cycle** tab.

### Deleting a cycle

Click the dustbin in the header (or in the list row). A box asks **Delete this cycle?** (from the header) or **Delete cycle?** (from the list). It names the cycle and says that all its daily records, events and logs will be permanently removed and that this cannot be undone. Click **Delete** to confirm or **Cancel** to keep it.

> **Watch out:** Deleting a cycle is permanent and immediate. It is **not** sent for approval, even when the approval workflow is switched on (see "The approval workflow" below). Money and stock that the cycle's events already posted to **Finance** and **Inventory** are **not** taken back; they stay in those pages and you must correct them there. If you only want to finish a cycle, use **Close Cycle** instead, which keeps all the history.

---

## The Overview tab

### What it is for

The **Overview** tab is the summary of the cycle: what you started with, simple running totals, and the targets (KPIs) the template says you should aim for.

### What you will see

The tab has up to four blocks, from top to bottom.

**1. Production Units.** One card for every unit created when you started the cycle: the big number (for example 500), the unit (birds, ha, fish, trees...), the name of the unit and, when you entered one, the breed or variety. If none were recorded it says **No production units recorded.** The quantities are the starting figures from the setup form; they are not reduced when you log deaths or sales.

**2. Running Statistics.** Four small tiles:

| Tile | What it counts |
|---|---|
| **Total Records** | The number of daily records saved across all stages. |
| **Total Events** | The number of events logged across all stages. |
| **Mortality** | Total animals or plants lost, added up from the **Count** on every **Mortality / Loss** event; shown in red when above zero, a dash when none. |
| **Harvested** | The quantities on your **Harvest** and **Partial Harvest** events that you routed to **Inventory** or **Direct Sale**, added together and shown in green; a dash when none. |

**3. Production KPIs.** One row for each target figure in the template, for example (broiler template) **Feed Conversion Ratio** with a note "Feed consumed ÷ live weight gained", **Mortality Rate** and **Avg Body Weight**. Each row shows the **Benchmark**: the target the template suggests (for example 1.7 for broiler feed conversion, 3 per cent for mortality, 2.2 kg for body weight). Different templates have different KPIs (a layer template has **Hen Day Production**, **Eggs per Hen Housed**, **Feed per Egg**; a maize template has **Yield per Hectare**; a tilapia template has **Feed Conversion Ratio**, **Survival Rate**, **Average Body Weight** and **Yield per m²**). If the template has no KPIs, this block is not shown.

**4. Feed Consumed.** Shown only once your daily records contain a feed figure. It adds up the daily feed intake or feed (kg) numbers recorded in the **Daily Log** and shows the total in kg. If the cycle has starting units it also shows **FCR estimate**: the total feed divided by the quantity harvested (with a minimum of 1).

### Good to know

- The KPI rows show only the **benchmark**, not your own result. Work out your own result yourself and compare it with the benchmark. Examples: feed conversion = total feed given ÷ total live weight gained; mortality rate = birds lost ÷ birds started × 100.
- The **Harvested** tile adds the quantities as plain numbers and labels the total "kg". If you log some harvests in kg and others in head or crates, the total mixes units, so log each product in a consistent unit if you want this tile to mean something.
- **Mortality** counts only events of the type **Mortality / Loss**. Deaths you type into the mortality box of a **Daily Log** record are not added in here, so use one place consistently. Events are better for a cause (disease, predator, heat) and a date; the Daily Log is better for a quick daily count.
- The **Total Records** and **Total Events** tiles are reliable.
- Stage alerts (warnings such as "Temperature too low for chicks") are not on this tab; they show on the **Stages** tab.

### If something goes wrong

- A tile shows a dash: nothing has been logged yet for it. Open the **Events** tab and log a **Mortality / Loss** or **Harvest** event.
- **Feed Consumed** is missing: it appears only once your **Daily Log** records contain a feed figure.

---

## The Stages tab

### What it is for

The **Stages** tab shows every stage of the cycle in order, what is expected in each one, and what has happened so far.

### What you will see

One card per stage. The card header shows:

- A status icon: a green tick for **completed**; a green ring with a pulsing dot for the current (active) stage; an empty grey circle for stages not reached yet. Stages not yet reached are shown faded.
- The stage name. A green **Current** tag marks the active stage. A small "~14d" shows the template's usual length of the stage.
- A line such as "12 records · 5 events · 14d": how many daily records and events belong to this stage, and how many days the stage has taken. For a finished stage this is its end date minus its start date. For the active stage it is the days since it started. The first stage of a cycle has no start date, so it shows no day count.
- An arrow to unfold or fold the card. The active stage is unfolded when you open the tab.

#### Stage status

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **pending** | Not reached yet. |
| **active** | The stage the cycle is in now. Only one stage is active at a time. |
| **completed** | You moved past it with **Advance**, or the cycle was closed while it was active. |
| **skipped** | A status the system knows about, but nothing in the current version sets it. You will not normally see it. |

#### Unfolded stage card

When a card is unfolded it shows, taken from the template:

- A short description of the stage.
- **Measurements to track.** A grid of boxes, each with the measurement name, its unit and how often to take it (daily, weekly, per event or as needed), and a green **Target** where the template has one. These are the numbers that go into the **Daily Log**.
- **Key activities.** A bullet list of the jobs to do in this stage (for example "Maintain brooder temperature").
- **Alerts.** Coloured notes: red for critical, amber for warning, blue for information, each with an exclamation icon and a message. These are advice from the template, not live alarms that react to your numbers.
- **Expected outputs.** What the stage should produce, with its unit and where it can be sent, for example "Live Broilers (head) → sale, own_use".

> **Note:** If the enterprise behind a cycle has been removed, the template is gone and the stage cards cannot unfold to show details; you only see names, counts and days.

### Good to know

- The stages tab is for reading. You cannot edit the stages, dates or measurements here. To change a template, see Part 2.
- Use this tab at the start of every stage to remind yourself what to measure and what to look out for.

---

## The Events tab

### What it is for

An **event** is a one-off happening: you bought feed, vaccinated, lost birds, harvested, sold. The **Events** tab is where you log them. Many events also create the matching entry in **Finance** or **Inventory** for you, so you do not enter the same thing twice.

### Before you start

- The cycle must not be closed. For a closed cycle the **Log Event** button is hidden (you can still read, edit and delete old events).
- Know the date of the event and which stage it belongs to.
- If you want the money to appear in **Finance**, fill the money fields of the event (see "What each event does automatically").

### Step by step: log an event

1. Open the cycle and click the **Events** tab.
2. Click the green **Log Event** button. The form opens (the button then reads **Hide Form**).
3. Under **Event Type** tap the type you need. The label shows how many types your kind of farming has, for example "(12 for poultry)". A grey line under the chips explains the chosen type.
4. Choose the **Stage**. It starts on the current stage. Change it if the event belongs to another stage.
5. Set the **Date**. It starts as today.
6. Fill in the fields for this event type. Fields marked with a red star are the important ones. Money fields show **(cost)** next to the name. Yes/no questions appear as tick boxes and long notes appear at the bottom.
7. Click **Save [event name]**, for example **Save Direct Sale**. The button turns to **Saved!** for a moment and the form closes.
8. Your event appears at the top of **Event History**.

### What you will see

#### The event history list

Below the form, **Event History (n)** lists every event of the cycle, from all stages, newest date first. If there are none it says **No events recorded yet.** Each card shows:

- The event icon and name, the date (day and month) and the stage name.
- On the right, one or more quick figures when the event has them: the count, total collected, honey in kg, total litres, quantity or total weight.
- A pencil icon (edit) and a dustbin icon (delete).
- The description, if it differs from the event name. The description is taken for you from the first of Subject, Product or Item that you filled in; otherwise it is the event name.
- Up to six small chips with the other values you entered (field name, the value and the unit). Empty values and the two internal fields for inventory and routing are not shown.

There is no search or filter on the history. The list is one long list, newest first; use the event icon and the stage name to find what you want.

#### Computed fields

Some fields fill themselves and are greyed out so you cannot type in them:

- **Total Cost** = Quantity × Unit Cost (Input Purchase).
- **Total Value** = Quantity × Unit Price (Direct Sale).
- **Total Yield** of Milk Collection adds up the morning, afternoon and evening yields as you type them; you can still overwrite it.

The other "computed" fields are filled in for you as you type: **Total Cost** in a Stocking event is **Number Stocked** × **Cost per Fingerling**, and **Total Applied** in a Fertilisation event is **Application Rate** × **Area Applied**.

### Good to know

- **Required fields are only marked, not enforced.** The red star tells you what is important, but the form does not stop you saving without it. Check the form before you click save. An event without its money field posts nothing to Finance.
- **Numbers and dates**: type numbers only (no commas, no "ZMW"). Dates are chosen with the date picker.
- **Editing is limited.** The pencil lets you change only the **Date** and the **Description** (see below). To correct a quantity, price or any other figure, delete the event and log it again.
- **Deleting an event does not reverse its side effects.** If the event added money to Finance or stock to Inventory, that entry stays where it is. Remove or correct it in Finance or Inventory too (see Part 4).
- **Editing an event does not change its Finance or Inventory entry** either.
- Logging the same event twice creates two events and two entries. Check the history before saving again.
- The list of event types depends on the kind of farming. If your enterprise is insects and worms, aquatic plants (seaweed, spirulina) or forestry, the current version offers no event types at all: the type list is empty and **Save** does nothing. For these enterprises use the **Daily Log** and the **Care Plan**, and note events in the Daily Log notes.

### Editing an event

1. In **Event History**, click the pencil icon on the event.
2. The **Edit Event** box shows **Date** and **Description**. Change them.
3. If approval is on or a reason is required, a **Reason** line appears (a red star shows when it is compulsory).
4. Click **Save Changes** (or **Submit for Approval** when the approval workflow is on, see below). Click **Cancel** to leave without changing.

### Deleting an event

1. Click the dustbin icon on the event.
2. A box asks **Delete Event?** and says "Remove this *[type]* event?".
3. Click **Delete** (or **Submit Deletion** when approval is on). **Cancel** keeps it.
4. When approval or a required reason is on, a **Reason for deletion** line is shown; type why.

### If something goes wrong

- **Nothing happens when I click Save.** The event type list is empty for your enterprise type (see above), or no event type is selected. Pick a chip first.
- **No entry arrived in Finance.** Check you filled the money fields named in the next sections (for example Unit Cost and Quantity so Total Cost fills in).
- **The Log Event button is missing.** The cycle is closed.

---

## Event types by kind of farming

The chips you see depend on the kind of farming (the category of your template). The table shows which categories get which events. "All" means every kind of farming except insects and worms, aquatic plants and forestry.

| Event type | Offered for |
|---|---|
| **Observation**, **Treatment**, **Input Purchase**, **Transfer / Move**, **Direct Sale**, **Harvest** | All: poultry, livestock, aquaculture, crops, horticulture, greenhouse, orchard, apiary, mushroom, processing, services |
| **Mortality / Loss**, **Vaccination**, **Weighing / Sampling** | Poultry, livestock, aquaculture |
| **Egg Collection** | Poultry |
| **Milk Collection**, **Pregnancy Check**, **Weaning**, **Drying Off** | Livestock |
| **Birth / Calving / Farrowing**, **Breeding / Mating** | Livestock, poultry |
| **Water Quality**, **Stocking / Seeding**, **Feeding Record**, **Partial Harvest** | Aquaculture |
| **Planting / Sowing**, **Fertilisation**, **Irrigation**, **Spraying**, **Field Scouting**, **Weeding** | Crops, horticulture, greenhouse, orchard |
| **Pruning / Trimming**, **Pollination** | Orchard, horticulture, greenhouse |
| **Fruit Thinning** | Orchard, horticulture |
| **Hive Inspection**, **Honey Harvest**, **Swarm Control**, **Queen Management** | Apiary |
| **Substrate Preparation**, **Inoculation** | Mushroom |

So a broiler enterprise sees 12 types (the 6 for all, the 3 animal types, **Egg Collection**, **Birth / Calving / Farrowing** and **Breeding / Mating**); a maize enterprise sees 12; a tilapia enterprise sees 13; a bee enterprise sees 10; a mushroom enterprise sees 8; processing and services see 6.

In the tables below, **Req.** shows a star (*) for fields marked with a red star on screen. "Choice" means you pick from a drop-down list; "Yes/No" means a tick box.

### Events for every kind of farming

#### Observation

For any note about what you saw. No money, no stock.

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Subject | Text | * | "What did you observe?" Becomes the event description. |
| Severity | Choice | | Info, Watch, Warning, Critical |
| Action Taken | Long text | | What was done, if anything |
| Additional Notes | Long text | | |

#### Treatment

Disease treatment, medicine or pest control. **Posts a cost to Finance** when Cost is filled.

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product / Chemical | Text | * | Becomes the description |
| Target (disease/pest) | Text | | e.g. Coccidiosis, Aphids |
| Dose / Rate | Text | | e.g. 2ml/L, 500g/ha |
| Route / Method | Choice | | Oral (water), Injectable, Topical, Spray, Pour-on, Drench, Dip, In-feed, Fumigation, Other |
| Animals Treated (head) | Number | | |
| Area Treated (ha) | Number | | |
| Withdrawal Period (days) | Number | | Days before the animal's meat, milk or eggs, or the crop, may be used |
| Cost | Number (cost) | | Triggers the Finance entry |
| Batch / Lot No. | Text | | |
| Prescribed By | Text | | |

#### Input Purchase

For feed, seed, chemicals, animals and any other input you buy. **Posts a cost to Finance** and can **add the item to Inventory**.

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Item Name | Text | * | e.g. Layer Mash, Maize Seed |
| Category | Choice | * | Feed; Seed / Planting material; Fertiliser; Chemical / Medicine; Animals / Birds / Fingerlings; Packaging; Equipment; Fuel; Labour; Other |
| Quantity | Number | * | |
| Unit | Text | | kg, bags, head, litres... |
| Unit Cost | Number (cost) | * | |
| Total Cost | Number (cost) | | Filled for you: Quantity × Unit Cost |
| Supplier | Text | | |
| Receipt / Invoice No. | Text | | Carried to the Finance entry as its reference |
| Add to Inventory | Yes/No | | Tick to put the purchased quantity into stock |

#### Transfer / Move

Moving animals, produce or materials between places on the farm. Records only; no money, no stock.

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Item / Animal | Text | * | |
| Count / Quantity | Number | * | |
| Unit | Text | | head, kg, crates... |
| From | Text | | e.g. House 1, Pond A |
| To | Text | * | e.g. House 2, Quarantine |
| Reason | Text | | |
| Notes | Long text | | |

> **Note:** The **Transfer / Move** event is only a record of a movement inside the farm. It is not the same as **Transfer Output** (moving produce into the next cycle of a program), which is described under the **Outputs** tab.

#### Direct Sale

A farm-gate or direct sale. **Posts income to Finance.**

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product | Text | * | e.g. Live broilers, Maize bags |
| Quantity | Number | * | |
| Unit | Text | * | kg, head, bags, crates... |
| Unit Price | Number (cost) | * | |
| Total Value | Number (cost) | | Filled for you: Quantity × Unit Price |
| Buyer | Text | | |
| Payment Method | Choice | | Cash, Mobile Money, Bank Transfer, Cheque, Credit, Other |
| Notes | Long text | | |

#### Harvest

A harvest or slaughter output. **Adds stock to Inventory** when you route it there.

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product / Output | Text | * | e.g. Maize grain, Live birds, Tomatoes |
| Quantity | Number | * | |
| Unit | Text | * | kg, tonnes, head, crates... |
| Area Harvested (ha) | Number | | |
| Quality Grade | Choice | | Premium / Grade A, Grade B, Grade C, Below Grade, Mixed |
| Route To | Choice | * | Inventory, Direct Sale, Processing, Own Use, Waste |
| Est. Price / Unit | Number (cost) | | Estimate only; posts nothing |
| Method | Choice | | Manual, Mechanical, Combined, Other |
| Notes | Long text | | |

#### Mortality / Loss

| Field | Kind | Req. | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|

## What each event does automatically

Most events only write a line in the cycle's history. Some also create entries in **Finance** and **Inventory** the moment you save them. This table is the complete list. Entries are tagged as coming from Production, linked to the cycle, and use the event's date (today if none).

| Event | Finance | Inventory | Only happens when |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Input Purchase** | Expense (category chosen from your Category answer: Feed, Seed, Fertiliser, Medicine, Labour, Equipment, Transport/Fuel and so on; otherwise "other cost"). Description is the item name. The receipt number is kept as the reference. | If **Add to Inventory** is ticked: stock in, named after the item, with the quantity, unit and cost per unit. | Total Cost has a value (so Quantity and Unit Cost are filled). |
| **Treatment** | Expense, category **medicine**. | none | Cost is filled. |
| **Vaccination** | Expense, category **medicine**. | none | Cost is filled. |
| **Direct Sale** | Income, category **sale income**, described as "Farm sale: product (quantity unit)". | none | Total Value has a value (so Quantity and Unit Price are filled). |
| **Harvest** | none | Stock in, named after the Product, if **Route To** is **Inventory**. | Route To is Inventory. |
| **Partial Harvest** | none | Stock in (the total weight) if **Route To** is **Inventory**. | Route To is Inventory. |
| **Egg Collection** | none | Stock in of "Table Eggs" for the Table Eggs figure. | Table Eggs is more than zero. |
| **Milk Collection** | none | Stock in of "Fresh Milk" for the Total Yield, in litres. | Total Yield is more than zero. |
| **Honey Harvest** | none | Stock in of "Honey" in kg. | Honey Yield is filled. |
| **Stocking / Seeding** | An expense of the fingerling cost (Other Cost). | none | Total Cost has a value (it is filled in from Number Stocked × Cost per Fingerling). |

Everything not in this table (for example **Planting / Sowing**, **Fertilisation**, **Spraying**, **Irrigation**, **Weeding**, **Feeding Record**) records what happened but **does not** post money or stock, even though some of them have a cost field. For money to reach Finance, use **Input Purchase** (costs) and **Direct Sale** (income).

### Good to know

- **Each entry is posted once.** The system gives every event a unique reference, so re-saving the page does not double-post it. Saving a second event does.
- **Direct Sale versus Harvest routing.** Choosing **Direct Sale** in a **Harvest** event's **Route To** list does not create income. Log the money with a separate **Direct Sale** event. For example: log a **Harvest** of 40 bags routed to **Inventory**, then later, when you sell 30 bags, log a **Direct Sale** of 30 bags. Selling from stock through the Sales page is explained in Part 4.
- **Egg Collection and hatching eggs.** When you fill **Hatching Eggs**, the system announces "eggs set" for the cycle so that an incubation function can pick it up, and adds them to Inventory as a separate **Hatching Eggs** item so they stay on record. Nothing starts an incubation cycle for you. To hatch those eggs, start an **Egg Incubation and Hatch** cycle yourself with **New Cycle** and enter the number of eggs set.
- **Milk and honey always go to stock.** Milk is added to Inventory whatever you choose in **Route To** (even Waste). Honey is added whatever the route. If part of it is not usable, adjust the stock in Inventory.
- **Deleting or editing an event does not undo these entries.** Fix them in Finance and Inventory (Part 4).
- To see the result, open **Finance** (expenses and income, filtered to this cycle's reference) and **Inventory** (stock movements). Stock entries show the cycle in their reference.

---

## The approval workflow

### What it is for

On many farms a worker records data and the owner or manager wants to check changes to old records. When **Require approval for edits** is switched on, changes and deletions of **events** and **daily records** do not apply at once. They wait in a queue until an approver accepts them.

### What is covered, and what is not

| Covered by approval | Not covered |
|---|---|
| Editing an event (date and description) | Logging a **new** event or daily record |
| Deleting an event | Deleting a whole cycle |
| Editing a daily record (the numbers and notes) | Advancing a stage |
| Deleting a daily record | Closing a cycle |
| | Ticking care-guide items |

### Before you start

- Approvals are set up and decided on the **Settings** page (left menu, bottom). You need access to the **Settings** module to open it.
- Approval is a company-wide switch, not a per-person one.

### Step by step: switch the workflow on

1. Open **Settings** from the left menu.
2. Find the box **Edit & Approval Control**.
3. Switch on **Require approval for edits** ("When enabled, changes go to an approver before applying").
4. Type **Your name / Staff member** (the name that will be written on every request, for example "John Banda").
5. Type **Approver name** (for example "Farm Manager"). This name is written on every decision.
6. Tick **Require a reason for every edit or deletion** if you want each request to carry a reason.

The changes save as you type.

> **Note:** These two names are stored once for the whole company. They are not tied to who is logged in. Everyone's requests carry the name typed in **Your name / Staff member**, and every decision carries the **Approver name**. If you want requests to show different people, change the name before you send them. The system does not check who clicks **Approve**: anybody who can open Settings can approve.

### Step by step: ask for a change (staff)

1. Open the cycle and the **Events** tab (or the **Daily Log** tab for records).
2. Click the pencil (edit) or dustbin (delete) icon on the item.
3. Make your change. Fill the **Reason** line. It shows when approval is on or a reason is required, and a red star means it is compulsory.
4. A note reads **This edit will be sent for approval before applying.** (in an edit window) or "This will be submitted for approval." (in a delete window).
5. Click **Submit for Approval** (edit) or **Submit Deletion** (delete).

The window closes. Nothing changes in the cycle yet.

### Step by step: review requests (approver)

1. Open **Settings**. In the left menu the **Settings** item shows a number badge when requests are waiting. At the top of the **Edit & Approval Control** box an amber tag reads "n pending".
2. Under **Pending Edits (n)** each request is an amber card with: what is being changed (for example "Event — vaccination" or "Daily Record 2026-07-04"), what is changed ("date & description", "measurements & notes" or "DELETE"), who asked, a tag for the type (event, daily record), and a **Before** / **After** comparison (each cut off at 80 characters). If a reason was given it shows as **Reason**.
3. To accept, click **Approve**. The change is applied to the cycle straight away and the request moves to **Recent Decisions** as approved (green).
4. To refuse, click **Reject**. A box titled **Reject Edit** asks for a **Reason for rejection (optional)**. Type why and click **Reject** (or **Cancel**). The change is **not** applied. The request moves to **Recent Decisions** as rejected (red) with your note in quotation marks.
5. Under **Recent Decisions** (the last ten) each card shows the item, the field, "requested by → decided by" and the rejection note if any. Click the small **X** at the right of a card to dismiss it and remove it from the list.

### What you will see

There are no banners on the Production page that announce a pending or rejected request. The person who asked can only see the result by checking the item (if it was approved, it has changed) or by asking the approver. A rejected request simply leaves the item as it was. The rejection note is visible to people who open Settings.

### Good to know

- When approval is **off**, edit and delete buttons apply immediately and the reason line shows only if **Require a reason** is on. With the reason compulsory, the edit window's save button stays greyed out until you type a reason. The delete windows ask for a reason but do not block you if you leave it empty.
- If a requested item was deleted by someone else before approval, the approval does nothing harmful; the request is still marked approved.
- Approving a deletion of an event or record does **not** remove the Finance or Inventory entries that the event created. See "What each event does automatically".
- Requests are saved to the company account, so an approver on another phone sees them.

---

## The Outputs tab

### What it is for

The **Outputs** tab shows what the cycle has produced and where it went: the produce counted per destination, and the log of transfers made to other phases of a program.

### What you will see

- **If nothing has been recorded**, the tab says **No outputs recorded yet.** and "Log a Harvest or Sale event, or use Transfer Output to record decisions."
- **Transfer Decisions** (purple cards, with a circular-arrows icon). One card per transfer made from this cycle: the output type, the date, the total quantity and unit, an amber chip with how many were **sold** (and the money earned), a purple chip with how many were **transferred** and to which cycle ("→ cycle name", with "(new cycle)" if a new one was created), and any notes.
- **Totals by destination** (small coloured tiles). One tile for each destination used, with the total quantity.
- **All Logged Outputs.** One line per output: the material name, date and stage, the quantity with its unit, and a coloured destination tag.

The destinations the system knows are:

| Destination | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Sales** (sale) | Sold. |
| **Inventory** (inventory) | Put into stock. |
| **Processing** (processing) | Sent to be processed (for example maize to meal). |
| **Incubation** (incubation) | Hatching eggs set. |
| **Hatchery** (hatchery) | Sent to a hatchery. |
| **Transfer** (transfer) | Moved to another cycle. |
| **Waste** (waste) | Lost or thrown away. |
| **Own Use** (own_use) | Used by the household or the farm itself. |

These are the same eight routes that the template's "Expected outputs" lists on the **Stages** tab.

> **Note:** **Harvest** and **Partial Harvest** events appear here automatically, with the route you chose, in "Totals by destination" and "All Logged Outputs". **Direct Sale** events do not appear on this tab (their money is in **Finance**). The **Transfer Decisions** list is filled by **Transfer Output**, so a cycle that has had a transfer shows it here.

### Transfer Output

#### What it is for

**Transfer Output** is for programs (see "Production programs and phases"). You have, for example, 300 grown keets at the end of Phase 1. You want to sell 100 and move 200 to Phase 2 to become breeders. The Transfer Output window records that decision and creates or fills the next phase for you.

#### Before you start

The button is in the purple program banner of a cycle that belongs to a program with at least two phases. Cycles that are not in a program do not show it.

#### Step by step

1. Open a cycle that belongs to a program and click **Transfer Output** in the purple banner. The window title is **Transfer Output**, with the line "Decide what to sell and what moves to the next cycle". The three steps are shown as **What**, **How to split** and **Where to transfer**.
2. **Step 1 — What.** Under **Output type** pick one of the boxes. The choices depend on the kind of farming:

| Kind of farming | Output types offered (unit) |
|---|---|
| Poultry | Live Birds (birds), Hatched Keets / Chicks (chicks), Hatching Eggs (eggs), Table Eggs (eggs) |
| Livestock | Live Animals (head), Weaners (head), Milk (litres) |
| Aquaculture | Fingerlings (count), Harvest Fish (kg) |
| Crops | Harvested Crop (kg), Seed Stock (kg) |
| Other kinds | The poultry list is shown |

   Each box also shows a suggestion such as "→ Grow-Out Cycle". Then type **Total available** (a number of at least 1): how many you have ready to decide on. Click **Next →**.
3. **Step 2 — How to split.** The grey bar shows what is **Available**. Use the amber **Sell** box and the purple **Transfer to next cycle** box:
   - Under **Sell**: **Qty to sell** and **Price per [unit] (ZMW)**. The line "= ZMW … revenue" shows the money. A link **Sell all [n]** sets all of it to sell.
   - Under **Transfer to next cycle**: **Qty to transfer**. A link **Transfer all [n]** sets all of it to transfer.
   - A balance strip shows what you allocated, for example "100 sold + 200 transferred" with "✓ All accounted for", or "n unallocated", or in red "Exceeds [total]!".
   - You may sell some, transfer some, sell all, or transfer all. At least one of the two must be above zero, and together they must not exceed the total. Otherwise **Next →** stays greyed out. Click **Next →**.
4. **Step 3 — Where to transfer.**
   - If you transfer **nothing** and only sell, you see a **Sale summary** (output, quantity, price per unit, **Total revenue**) and a **Notes (optional)** box ("Buyer name, delivery notes…").
   - If you transfer some, a purple strip repeats the numbers. If the program has other phases that are planning or active, you can choose **Add to existing cycle** or **Create new cycle**. If there are none, you go straight to creating a new one.
   - **Add to existing cycle:** choose the destination under **Select destination cycle**. Each choice shows the phase label, the cycle name and its status.
   - **Create new cycle:** fill **New cycle name \*** (required), **Phase label (optional)** (if empty, the suggested label such as "Phase 3: Grow-Out Cycle" is used) and **Enterprise** (starts with the current one; you can pick another active enterprise). A grey box **New cycle will start with:** repeats: the quantity and output type, "Linked to [program] as Phase n" and "Status: Planning — activate when ready".
   - Add **Notes (optional)** if you wish.
5. Click **✓ Confirm Transfer**. (The button is greyed out until a destination or a new cycle name is given.) **← Back** returns to the earlier step; **Cancel** on step 1 closes the window.

#### What happens

- The decision is written into the source cycle's transfer list (date, output type, total, sold quantity and price and revenue, transferred quantity, destination and notes). It then shows in **Transfer Decisions** on this tab.
- **Create new cycle:** a new cycle is created with status **planning**, starting today, in the same program, with the next phase number and your phase label. It has a production unit named "[Output type] from [source cycle]" with the transferred quantity. Its stages come from the template of the enterprise you chose, but they are not started yet. After you confirm, the screen jumps to the new cycle.
- **Add to existing cycle:** the chosen cycle gets a new production unit for the quantity, and its source reference is set to the cycle you moved from.
- Nothing is taken off the source cycle's own numbers.

> **Watch out:** The sale part of a transfer is only a **record**. It does **not** post income to **Finance** and does not take anything out of **Inventory**. To bring the money into your books, also log a **Direct Sale** event (or record the sale in **Sales**), as described above and in Part 4.

> **Note:** A new cycle made by a transfer starts in **planning**. It does not appear as active and its day count does not run. To start working in it, open it from the list. Note that a planning cycle shows in the **Planning** section of the open list.

### If something goes wrong

- **Next is greyed out in step 1.** The total available must be a number above zero.
- **Next is greyed out in step 2.** You must sell or transfer something, and not more than the total.
- **Confirm Transfer is greyed out.** Choose a destination cycle or give the new cycle a name.

---

## The Close Cycle tab

### What it is for

When the batch or season is finished, you close the cycle. This ends it, records the end date and your closing notes, and moves it to **Closed / Completed**.

### Before you start

- Log all outstanding daily records, events and sales first. You cannot add a new event to a closed cycle.
- The tab appears only while the cycle is **active**. On the last stage the header also has a **Close Cycle** button.

### Step by step

1. Open the cycle and click **Close Cycle** (the tab, or the amber button in the header on the last stage).
2. Read the amber warning: **Closing a cycle is permanent.** Make sure all records and outputs are logged before closing.
3. Check the **Cycle Summary**: **Duration** (days from start to the end date you give), **Daily Records** (how many), **Mortality Events** and **Total Output**.
4. Set the **End Date** (starts as today).
5. Type **Closing Notes** (optional): performance summary, lessons learned, final observations.
6. Tick **I confirm all records and outputs have been entered for this cycle.** The button stays grey until you tick it.
7. Click **Close & Complete Cycle**.

### What changes

- The cycle status becomes **completed** and the end date is saved.
- The stage that was active is marked **completed** with your end date. Stages that were never reached stay *pending*; they are not marked skipped.
- The cycle moves to **Closed / Completed**. The row shows **Closed** and a full grey bar. The header shows "completed".
- The **Close Cycle** tab disappears and the **Log Event** button is hidden.

### Good to know

- **There is no way to reopen a closed cycle.** If you closed too early, start a new cycle, or ask the AGRINUXES team.
- The **Closing Notes** you type **replace** the cycle's existing notes, including the setup summary that started with "Config:". If you leave the box empty the old notes are kept. Copy anything you want to keep into the closing notes first.
- You can still read a closed cycle, open its tabs, and edit or delete old events and daily records (with approval if the workflow is on).
- **Mortality Events** is the total count on your **Mortality / Loss** events, and **Total Output** adds the quantities of your harvest outputs (shown in kg, so log harvests in one unit). **Duration** and **Daily Records** come straight from the cycle.
- Closing does not post anything to Finance or Inventory. It does not change the shares of costs or income already posted.

---

## Worked examples

These examples use only features that exist. Amounts are illustrations in ZMW, not real prices.

### Example 1: A 42-day broiler cycle in Chongwe

**Setup (Day 0, 1 July).** You have an enterprise "Broilers" using the Broiler Chicken template. Click **New Cycle**, choose the enterprise, choose **Broiler Grow-Out**, enter **Number of Birds** 500, **Breed / Strain** "Ross 308", **House / Pen ID** "House A", **Chick / Egg Source** "your hatchery's name", **Target Slaughter Age** 42. Name the cycle "Broiler Batch 3 — House A". The template gives four stages: **Brooding** (about 14 days), **Grower** (14), **Finishing** (14), **Harvest / Sale** (1). Brooding is active. **Day 0** shows.

**On Day 0 (Events).** Log **Input Purchase** for the chicks: Item "Day-old Ross 308 chicks", Category **Animals / Birds / Fingerlings**, Quantity 500, Unit "head", Unit Cost 14, Total Cost fills in as 7000. Do not tick Add to Inventory (you will not sell them from stock). Log another **Input Purchase** for feed: "Starter mash", Category **Feed**, 10 bags at 380, tick **Add to Inventory** if you track feed stock.
  *Result:* Finance shows two expenses (other cost and feed) tagged to this cycle; Inventory shows starter mash if you ticked it.

**Every day (Daily Log).** Record the morning figures: feed given, water, temperature, deaths and so on (see Part 2). Tick the care-guide items on the **Care Plan** tab as you do them.

**Day 7.** **Vaccination**: Vaccine "Newcastle (La Sota)", Route **Oral (water)**, Animals Vaccinated 500, Cost 150, Next Due Date Day 21. A medicine expense of ZMW 150 is posted.

**Day 7 and Day 14.** **Weighing / Sampling**: Sample Count 20, Average Weight 0.19 kg, then 0.45 kg. Use this to compare with your targets.

**Day 14.** Check the header: it says "Currently in **Brooding**. Ready to move to **Grower**?" Click **Advance**. Brooding is completed; Grower starts today. The progress bar shows 1/4 stages.

**When birds die.** **Mortality / Loss**: Count 3, Cause **Disease**. Add a **Treatment** if you medicate: Product "Amprolium", Target "Coccidiosis", Cost 90.

**Day 28.** **Advance** to **Finishing**.

**Day 42.** **Advance** to **Harvest / Sale**. Log a **Direct Sale** for the buyer: Product "Live broilers", Quantity 470, Unit "head", Unit Price 85, Buyer "Lusaka market trader", Payment Method **Mobile Money**. Total Value fills in as 39,950. Income of ZMW 39,950 is posted to Finance. If part is for your own table, log a **Harvest** with Route To **Own Use**.

**Closing.** Open **Close Cycle**, check the summary, set the End Date, type your closing notes (for example "Feed conversion 1.8, mortality 4 per cent, next batch order starter earlier"), tick the box and click **Close & Complete Cycle**.

**What you see afterwards.** The cycle sits in **Closed / Completed**. The Dashboard's **Active Cycles** figure goes down by one. **Total Revenue** and **Total Expenses** on the Dashboard include the entries the events posted. In Finance you can review the batch's costs and income. In the **Overview** tab you see **Total Records**, **Total Events** and the KPI benchmarks to compare with your own results.

### Example 2: A maize season (rain-fed)

**Setup (November).** Enterprise "Maize" (Maize Field Crop template). **New Cycle**, type **Rain-Fed Season**, Field / Plot Name "North Field", Area 5 ha, Variety "SC403", Irrigation **Rain-fed (no irrigation)**, Seed Rate 25 kg/ha. The stages are **Land Preparation**, **Planting**, **Emergence & Establishment**, **Vegetative Growth**, **Tasselling & Silking**, **Grain Fill & Maturity**, **Harvest**, **Drying & Storage**: eight in all.

**Land Preparation.** Look at the stage card on the **Stages** tab for measurements (for example soil pH target 5.5 to 7). Log an **Input Purchase** for lime or manure if you buy any. Log **Weeding** and **Irrigation** as they happen.

**Before planting.** **Input Purchase**: "Maize seed SC403", Category **Seed / Planting material**, Quantity 125, Unit "kg", Unit Cost 45. **Input Purchase**: "D-Compound", Category **Fertiliser**, 10 bags. **Input Purchase**: "Urea", Category **Fertiliser**, 10 bags. Finance receives seed and fertiliser expenses.

**Planting day.** Click **Advance** to move to **Planting**. Log **Planting / Sowing**: Crop "Maize SC403", Area Planted 5, Seed Rate 25, Row Spacing 75, Plant Spacing 25, Method **Hand planting**. This is a record; the money was already posted by the purchases. Then **Fertilisation**: Product "D-Compound", Type **Basal dressing**, Area Applied 5.

**Through the season.** **Advance** at each growth change. Log **Fertilisation** (Urea, Type **Top dressing**), **Weeding** (twice), **Field Scouting** (Pest "Fall armyworm", Severity **Moderate (10–30%)**), **Spraying** (Product "your insecticide", Target "Fall armyworm", Area Sprayed 5, Pre-Harvest Interval). Use the **Care Plan** reminders and the stage **Alerts**.

**Harvest.** **Advance** to **Harvest**. Log **Harvest**: Product "Maize grain", Quantity 3,000, Unit "kg", Route To **Inventory**. Stock of 3,000 kg of "Maize grain" is added to Inventory. Later, for each sale, log **Direct Sale**: Product "Maize grain", Quantity 1,000, Unit "kg", Unit Price 6 per kg. Income is posted to Finance.

**Drying and storage, then close.** **Advance** to **Drying & Storage**, record moisture in the Daily Log, then close the cycle with your yield and lessons in the closing notes.

**What you see afterwards.** The costs in Finance by category (seed, fertiliser, and so on); maize grain in Inventory; income from the sales; the cycle in **Closed / Completed**.

### Example 3: A tilapia pond

**Setup.** Enterprise "Tilapia Ponds" (Tilapia Pond template). **New Cycle**, type **Pond Grow-Out**, Number of Ponds 2, Fingerlings Stocked 5,000, Species "Nile Tilapia", Water Source **Borehole**, Primary Feed Type **Commercial pellets**, Target Harvest Weight 400. Stages: **Pond Preparation** (about 14 days), **Stocking** (1), **Grow-out** (about 150) and **Harvest** (2).

**Pond Preparation.** Log **Water Quality** (Pond / Tank "Pond A", pH 7.2, Temperature 26, Dissolved Oxygen 6) and an **Observation** if something is wrong. The stage card shows targets: pH 6.5 to 8.5, dissolved oxygen above 5, temperature 22 to 30.

**Stocking day.** **Advance** to **Stocking**. Log an **Input Purchase**: "Tilapia fingerlings", Category **Animals / Birds / Fingerlings**, Quantity 5,000, Unit "fish", Unit Cost 1.50. (This is how the cost reaches Finance; see the note on the Stocking event.) Also log **Stocking / Seeding**: Species "Nile Tilapia", Pond "Pond A", Number Stocked 5,000, Avg Fingerling Weight 5. Then **Advance** to **Grow-out**.

**Grow-out (months).** Daily: record feed and water readings in the Daily Log. Weekly: **Water Quality**. Every two weeks: **Weighing / Sampling** (Sample Count 30, Average Weight in kilograms: 0.05, 0.12, 0.25 ...). Whenever you buy feed: **Input Purchase** with Category **Feed**, tick **Add to Inventory**. As fish die: **Mortality / Loss**. If a disease appears: **Treatment**.

**Partial harvest.** **Partial Harvest**: Pond "Pond A", Total Weight 600, Route To **Inventory** (stock of 600 kg added). When you sell: **Direct Sale**: Product "Fresh tilapia", Quantity 150, Unit "kg", Unit Price 45.

**Final harvest and close.** **Advance** to **Harvest**; log the final **Partial Harvest** and **Direct Sale** events; then **Close Cycle** with your survival rate and average weight in the closing notes.

---

## Good practice

### Every day

1. Open **Production** and the active cycle.
2. Fill in the **Daily Log** for today (Part 2).
3. Look at the header: does the **Care guide** bar show anything due or overdue? Open **Care Plan** and tick off what you did.
4. Log any events that happened today: purchases, vaccinations, deaths, treatments, collections (eggs, milk).
5. Check the stage card (on **Stages**) if you are unsure what to measure today.

### Every week

1. Log a **Weighing / Sampling** (animals) or **Field Scouting** (crops) event.
2. Read through **Event History**: is anything missing or entered twice?
3. Open **Finance** and **Inventory** and check that the entries from your events look right.
4. Compare your own figures with the **Production KPIs** benchmarks on the **Overview** tab.
5. If approval is on, an approver should clear the **Pending Edits** under **Settings**.
6. Use **Backup & Restore** (left menu) to keep a copy (see Part 1).

### At the end of a stage

1. Check you have logged everything for the stage.
2. Click **Advance**. Remember there is no way back.

### At the end of a cycle

1. Log the last harvest, the last sales and any leftover stock.
2. Log any transfer to the next phase if you work with a program.
3. Check Finance for this cycle's costs and income, and fix anything missing.
4. Open **Close Cycle**, check the summary, set the end date, write useful closing notes.
5. Tick the confirmation and click **Close & Complete Cycle**.
6. Start the next cycle with **New Cycle** and use your closing notes to improve it.

---

## Troubleshooting

| What you see | What it means and what to do |
|---|---|
| **Enterprise removed** in amber on a cycle row | The enterprise this cycle belonged to was deleted. The cycle and its data are still there, but its template is gone. Details and stage cards cannot show the template information, and event types fall back to the crop list. Do not log new data in it. Add the enterprise again (Part 1) or close/delete the cycle. |
| **Select a cycle to view details** | Nothing is chosen yet. Click a cycle in the list. |
| **No cycles yet.** | You have no cycles, or you came from an enterprise page with no cycles. Click **New Cycle**. |
| A cycle I made is not visible | You may be viewing one enterprise's page. Open **Production** from the menu. Closed cycles are hidden under **Closed / Completed**. |
| There is no **Close Cycle** tab | The cycle is already closed (or not active). |
| **Log Event** missing | The cycle is closed. |
| No event type chips (insects, seaweed, forestry) | These enterprise types have no event types in the current version. Use the **Daily Log** and **Care Plan**. |
| Save does nothing in Events | No event type selected, or no types for your enterprise. |
| Income or cost missing in Finance | The event did not have its money field filled. Purchases need Quantity and Unit Cost; sales need Quantity and Unit Price; treatments and vaccinations need Cost. See the table "What each event does automatically". |
| Entry appears in Finance but I deleted the event | Deleting an event does not delete its entry. Delete or correct it in Finance. |
| Overview shows a dash for **Mortality** or **Harvested** | These tiles do not read the Events tab. Count from **Event History**. |
| My edit did not change anything | Approval is on. Look under **Settings** for the request; it changes only after **Approve**. |
| I clicked **Advance** by mistake | There is no undo. Carry on in the next stage, or ask support if records must be moved. |
| The Transfer Output button is not there | Only cycles that belong to a program with two or more phases show it. |
| New cycle from a transfer is not active | It starts in **planning** on purpose. Find it under **Planning** in the list. |
| I deleted a cycle by mistake | It cannot be recovered. The team may be able to help only if you have a **Backup & Restore** file from before. |

# Part 4 — Running the Farm Business: Inventory, Sales, Finance, Credit and Reports

This part is about the money and the goods on your farm. It covers the pages in the left-hand menu that sit between production and the bank: **Dashboard**, **Processing**, **Inventory**, **Sales**, **Trade & Export**, **Finance**, **Credit Profile** and **Reports**.

These pages are connected. When you record a purchase in production, the cost can appear in Finance. When you fulfil a sales order, the income appears in Finance and the goods leave Inventory. The chapter "How the modules talk to each other" explains every link, and ends with a checklist so that you never enter the same thing twice. Read it early: it will save you a lot of work.

> **Note:** Who sees what depends on your role. The owner (the Director) sees everything. Other team members only see the pages their role allows, and each page can allow four things: **view**, **create**, **edit** and **delete**. If a page is missing from your menu, or a button is missing or refuses to work, ask your Director to check **Team & Permissions** (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account).

Default role permissions for the pages in this part:

| Role | Inventory | Sales | Trade & Export | Finance | Credit Profile |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Director / Owner | All four | All four | All four | All four | All four |
| Production Manager | View, create, edit | None | None | None | None |
| Finance Manager | View, create, edit | View, create, edit | View only | View, create, edit | View, create, edit |
| Sales Manager | View only | View, create, edit | View, create, edit | None | None |
| Supervisor | View, create, edit | None | None | None | None |
| Farmhand, Vet Officer, Driver | None | None | None | None | None |

Only the Director can **delete** by default. The Director can give a person different permissions in **Team & Permissions**, so your own team may differ from this table.

---

## Dashboard

### The Dashboard

**What it is for.** The Dashboard is the first page you see. It is a one-screen picture of the whole business: money in and out, active production cycles, stock, staff, purchases, and live readings from farm devices. It does not let you change records. It only shows them and gives you shortcuts to the pages where you work.

**Before you start.**
- Everyone who can sign in to the farm workspace can open the Dashboard.
- The money figures come from Finance and the stock figures come from Inventory. If your role has no access to Finance or Inventory, those figures simply show zero or "—" on your Dashboard. That does not mean the farm has no money or stock. It means your login cannot see it.
- Some blocks (staff, procurement, device readings, activity feed) are read from the data saved on the device you are using. See "Where each number comes from" below.

**Step by step: using the Dashboard.**
1. Click **Dashboard** in the left-hand menu.
2. Read the heading: your business name, today's date, and a line such as "2 enterprises · 3 active cycles · 5 staff".
3. Click **New Cycle** (top right) to go to Production and start a new production cycle. Click **View Reports** to go to Reports.
4. Read the eight cards in the top strip (described below).
5. Look at the **Revenue vs Expenses** chart, the **Live Readings** block and the **Activity Feed**.
6. Scroll down to **Your Enterprises** to see one card per active enterprise. Click the arrow on a card to open the enterprise, or **View cycle** to open its running cycle.
7. At the bottom, use **View Procurement**, **View Staff** and **View Inventory** to jump to those pages.

**What you will see.**

#### The eight cards at the top

| Card | What it shows | Where the number comes from |
|---|---|---|
| **Total Revenue** | All income ever recorded, in your currency (ZMW by default). Subtitle: "Recorded income". | The Finance ledger, all income entries, all time. Fulfilled sales orders are already in the ledger, so they are not added twice. |
| **Total Expenses** | All costs ever recorded. Subtitle: "All recorded costs". | The Finance ledger, all expense entries, all time. |
| **Net Profit** | Income minus expenses. The number is always shown without a minus sign; the subtitle says **Surplus** (you are ahead) or **Deficit** (you are behind). | Calculated from the two cards above. |
| **Profit Margin** | Net profit as a percentage of income, for example "18.5%". Shows "—" when there is no income yet. | Calculated. The left edge of the card is green at 20% or more, amber from 0% to just under 20%, and red when negative. |
| **Active Cycles** | How many production cycles are running now. Subtitle: how many enterprises are active. | Your farm workspace (production cycles with the status Active). |
| **Inventory Items** | The number of stock items. Subtitle: "All stocked", or "N low stock" with an orange badge. | Inventory. An item counts as low when its quantity is at or below its minimum level. |
| **Unresolved Alerts** | The number of open alerts from farm devices (water, fish ponds, poultry houses, energy, soil and the device gateway). Subtitle: "Across all modules". Turns red when above zero. | The device pages, saved on this device only. |
| **Active Staff** | People marked active. Subtitle: "N total registered". | Staff & Labour, saved on this device only. |

The small arrows on some cards are only decoration: an up arrow when the value is positive, a down arrow when it is negative.

#### Revenue vs Expenses chart

A pair of bars (green for income, red for expense) for each of the last six months, ending with the current month. Above each pair is the net result in thousands, for example "+12k" or "-3k". Hover over a bar to see its exact amount. Below the chart three small figures give **This Month Income**, **This Month Expense** and **This Month Profit** (without a minus sign, green when positive and red when negative).

The chart reads the Finance ledger by the date of each entry.

#### Live Readings

A small block with a pulsing green dot. It shows four quick readings taken from your device pages:

| Reading | What it shows | Colours |
|---|---|---|
| **Battery** | The charge percentage of the first battery listed in Energy. | Green at 60% or more, amber from 30% to 59%, red below 30%. |
| **Water Tanks** | How many tanks are more than 80% full, for example "2/4 full". | Blue label. |
| **Aqua DO** | The average dissolved oxygen of your fish ponds in mg/L. | Green at 5 or more, amber from 4 to just under 5, red below 4. |
| **Poultry Temp** | The average temperature across your poultry houses. | Green between 24 and 32 degrees C, amber outside that range. |

If you have no device data for a reading, it shows "—". These readings are only as fresh as the last time the device pages were updated on this device. They are not a live feed from the sensor itself; see Part 5 — Farm Management, Intelligence and Devices for how readings arrive.

#### Activity Feed

The eight most recent lines from the Activity Log, newest first. Each line shows a coloured module tag (finance, inventory, production, sales, procurement, hr and so on), a subject, some detail and how long ago it happened ("5m ago", "3h ago", "2d ago"). Click the small refresh button to reload the list. When nothing has been logged it says "No activity recorded yet". The Activity Log is kept on this device only.

#### Your Enterprises

One card for every active enterprise (for example "Broilers" or "Maize"). Each card shows:
- The enterprise icon, its name, its template name and a small category tag.
- If a cycle is running: four **category figures** that depend on the kind of enterprise (see the table below), the cycle name with a pulsing dot, "Day N" since it started, the current stage, "x/y complete", a progress bar with a percentage, a small **Revenue** and **Expenses** strip, and a **View cycle** button.
- If no cycle is running: a message such as "2 completed cycles. Start a new one." with a **Start cycle** button.
- A footer with how many cycles in total and how many are completed.

If you have no enterprise at all, the page shows "No enterprises yet" and an **Add enterprise** button.

| Category | The four figures on the card |
|---|---|
| Poultry | **Flock Count** (birds), **Days in Stage**, **Current Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Livestock | **Herd Count** (animals), **Days in Stage**, **Current Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Aquaculture | **Fish Stocked**, **Ponds / Tanks**, **Current Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Crops | **Area Planted** (ha), **Current Stage**, **Days in Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Horticulture | **Area Planted** (ha), **Plant Count**, **Current Stage**, **Days to Harvest** |
| Greenhouse | **Growing Area** (square metres), **Plants**, **Current Stage**, **Days to Harvest** |
| Orchard | **Trees**, **Plot Area** (ha), **Current Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Apiary (bees) | **Active Hives**, **Days in Cycle**, **Current Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Mushroom | **Growing Blocks**, **Days in Stage**, **Current Stage**, **Days to End** |
| Processing | **Current Stage**, **Days in Stage**, **Stages Done**, **Days to End** |
| Services | **Current Stage**, **Days in Cycle**, **Stages Done**, **Days to End** |

These figures come from the cycle's own settings (its production units, stages and target dates). "Days in Stage" counts from the day the current stage started. "Days to End" counts down to the target end date you set for the current stage; it shows "—" if you did not set one. If a figure cannot be worked out it shows "—".

#### The three panels at the bottom

| Panel | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Procurement Pipeline** | **Pending POs** (purchase orders waiting), **Delivered**, **Paid**, the **Next delivery** date, and **Pending spend** (the value of orders that are pending or ordered). Button: **View Procurement**. |
| **Staff & Labour** | **Active staff**, **Hours this week** (last 7 days), **Approved hours**, **Pending approval** (hours not yet approved). Button: **View Staff**. |
| **Inventory Snapshot** | **Total value** of stock (quantity times cost per unit), **Low stock items** (shown only when there are some), and the three most valuable items. Button: **View Inventory**. |

**Good to know.**
- **Where each number comes from.** Money, stock and cycles are saved to your company account and are the same for everyone in the business on every device. Staff hours, procurement orders, device readings, device alerts and the Activity Feed are saved on the device you are using. So a colleague on another phone may see different staff or procurement figures. Use **Backup & Restore** (see Part 5) to protect that device-only data.
- The Dashboard has no period selector. The top cards are all-time totals. Use the Finance page for a period such as "Last 3 months".
- The Dashboard looks the same for every role. What differs is the data: a login without Finance access sees zero income and expenses.
- The small **Revenue** and **Expenses** strip on an enterprise card only adds up ledger entries that are linked to one of that enterprise's cycles. If your entries were made without choosing a cycle, or if the figure shows ZMW 0, open **Finance** for the true totals.
- The enterprise card strip always shows ZMW, even if the business currency is different.

**If something goes wrong.**
- *Everything shows zero.* Your role probably has no access to Finance or Inventory, or the page is still loading. Reload the page, then ask your Director to check your permissions.
- *"Low stock" count looks too high.* An item with a minimum level of 0 and a quantity of 0 counts as low. Set a sensible minimum, or add stock. See the Inventory chapter.

---

## Processing

### Processing Engine

**What it is for.** Processing turns raw produce into finished goods: maize grain into maize meal, raw milk into pasteurised milk, live broilers into dressed birds. You choose a **recipe**, start a **batch**, record what went in and what came out, record quality and costs, and complete it. Finished goods are added to Inventory and the batch cost is added to Finance as an expense.

**Before you start.**
- Open **Processing** in the left-hand menu.
- To take your own raw material out of stock, and to put finished goods into stock, you need permission to create or edit in Inventory or in Production. If you do not have it, the stock entries are quietly dropped (see "Watch out" below).
- To have the batch cost appear in Finance, the same applies for Finance, Inventory or Production.

> **Watch out:** The recipes and batches themselves are **saved on this device only**. They are not shared with your team and are not backed up automatically. Only the results (stock movements and the cost entry) go to your company account. Use **Backup & Restore** often (see Part 5) and do not clear your browser data.

**What you will see on the page.**
- A left panel headed **Processing Engine** with the number of batches, a purple button (**New Batch** or **New Recipe**, depending on the tab) and two tabs: **batches** and **recipes**.
- A right panel that shows the details of the batch you click. Before you click one it says "Select a batch to view details".

### Recipes

**What it is for.** A recipe says what goes in (inputs), what comes out (outputs, with the expected percentage of each), and the steps to follow.

**Built-in recipes.** Five recipes are ready for you:

| Recipe | Inputs | Outputs (expected share) |
|---|---|---|
| **Maize Milling** | 100 kg Maize Grain | Maize Meal 68% (main), Maize Bran 22% (by-product), Maize Germ 6% (by-product), Waste/Dust 4% (waste) |
| **Milk Pasteurisation** | 100 litre Raw Milk | Pasteurised Milk 97% (main), Cream 2% (by-product), Waste 1% (waste) |
| **Yoghurt Production** | 100 litre Pasteurised Milk, 2 g Starter Culture, 5 kg Sugar (optional) | Yoghurt 95% (main), Whey 5% (by-product) |
| **Broiler Processing** | 100 kg Live Broilers | Whole Dressed Bird 70% (main), Offal 8% (by-product), Feathers 8% (by-product), Blood & Bone 14% (waste) |
| **Tomato Sauce / Passata** | 100 kg Fresh Tomatoes, 1.5 kg Salt | Tomato Sauce 55% (main), Skin & Seeds 12% (by-product), Water Loss 33% (waste) |

Each built-in recipe also lists its steps (for example, for maize: "Clean & de-stone grain", "Mill to required fineness", "Sieve and grade", "Bag finished product"), an estimated labour time per batch and the equipment needed. The inputs are written per 100 units; they are a guide, because you enter the real amounts on the batch.

**Step by step: look at a recipe.**
1. Click the **recipes** tab.
2. Click a recipe name to open it. You see **Inputs**, **Outputs** (green for main product, amber for by-product, grey for waste) and numbered **Steps**.
3. Click **Start batch with this recipe** to begin a batch with that recipe already chosen.

**Step by step: create your own recipe.**
1. On the **recipes** tab click **New Recipe**.
2. Type the **Recipe Name** (required), for example "Sausage Casing".
3. Optionally type a **Description**.
4. Optionally type the **Processing Steps**, one per line.
5. Click **Create Recipe**.

> **Watch out:** In this version a recipe you create yourself has a name, description and steps only. The form says inputs and outputs can be added later in a recipe editor, but no such editor exists yet. A batch made from your own recipe therefore has no input or output rows, and no stock or cost entries are produced. Until that is added, use one of the built-in recipes if you want Processing to talk to Inventory and Finance, or record the stock yourself in Inventory.

### Batches

**What it is for.** A batch is one run of a recipe, for example "the 2 tonnes of maize milled on Tuesday".

**Step by step: start a batch.**
