1. On the **batches** tab click **New Batch** (or **Start batch with this recipe** from a recipe).
2. Choose the **Recipe**. A grey box shows its description and coloured tags with the expected output percentages.
3. Check the **Batch Number**. The app suggests one such as "BATCH-202610-482". You can change it.
4. Check the **Start Date** (today by default).
5. Click **Start Batch**. The button is disabled if the recipe or batch number is empty.

The batch opens straight away with the status **In Progress**.

**Batch statuses.**

| Status | Meaning | Colour dot |
|---|---|---|
| **Planned** | Waiting to start. Shows a **Start Processing** button. | Blue |
| **In Progress** | Being processed now. | Purple |
| **Completed** | Finished. Outputs and cost have been sent on. | Green |
| **Failed** | The run was lost or abandoned. Nothing is sent to Inventory or Finance. | Red |

The list is grouped under the headings **In Progress**, **Planned**, **Completed** and **Failed**, each with a count. Each row shows the batch number, the recipe name, the start date and, once known, the yield percentage.

> **Note:** A batch you start with **New Batch** begins as In Progress, not Planned. The **Start Processing** button only appears for a batch whose status is Planned, so you will normally not see it. This matters for the next point.

**Batch tabs.** Open a batch to see its header (number, recipe, status, start date, end date, yield and QC result) and five tabs: **summary**, **inputs**, **outputs**, **quality**, **costs**.

**Summary tab.**
- Four cards: **Total Input**, **Total Output**, **Overall Yield** and **Main Product Yield**. Overall yield is all outputs (including by-products and waste) divided by all inputs. Main product yield counts only the output marked main. Colours: overall yield is green at 90% or more, amber from 75%, red below; main yield is green at 65% or more, otherwise amber.
- The recipe name, description and numbered steps, and the estimated labour hours.
- For a batch that is In Progress, a **Complete Batch** box with two buttons: **Mark Completed** and **Mark Failed**.

**Inputs tab.**
1. Type the **actual quantity** used next to each ingredient. The unit is shown beside it.
2. Click **Save Inputs**.

**Outputs tab.**
1. If you have not yet saved inputs, a warning says "Enter actual inputs first to calculate yield."
2. Next to each output you see its type (main, byproduct or waste), the target percentage and the target amount worked out from your inputs.
3. Type the **actual quantity** you got. Beside it the app shows the difference from target, for example "+2.5%" in green or "-4.0%" in red.
4. Click **Save Outputs & Calculate Yield**.

**Quality tab.**
1. Choose **pass**, **pending** or **fail**.
2. Type **QC Notes** (colour, texture, taste, lab results, certificates).
3. Click **Save QC Result**.

**Costs tab.**
1. Type the **Raw Material Cost**, **Labour Cost** and **Overhead / Utilities** for the batch.
2. The app shows the **Total Cost** and the **Cost per** unit of the main product (total cost divided by the main output quantity).
3. Click **Save Costs**.

**Completing the batch.**
1. Make sure you have saved the **inputs**, **outputs** and **costs** first.
2. On the **summary** tab click **Mark Completed**. The batch gets today's date as its end date, the yield is recorded, and its quality status is set to **pending**.
3. Then open **quality** and save the real QC result.

> **Watch out:** Click **Mark Completed** only after saving inputs, outputs and costs. The stock and cost entries are created at the moment you complete, using what is saved at that moment. Costs saved later are never sent to Finance. Also, completing sets quality back to "pending", so record quality after you complete.

**What Processing sends to other pages.**

| When | Inventory | Finance |
|---|---|---|
| Batch moves to **In Progress** from **Planned** | Each input with a quantity above zero is taken out (Stock Out) to "processing". | Nothing. |
| Batch **Completed** | Each output with a quantity above zero is added (Stock In), tagged with the quality status. | One **expense** for the batch cost, dated the end date, category **Other Cost**, description "Processing batch: ..." and the note "Auto-recorded from processing". Only if the cost is above zero. |
| Batch **Failed** | Nothing. | Nothing. |

Items are matched by name. A finished product that does not exist yet in Inventory (for example "Maize Meal") is created automatically. An input that does not exist in Inventory is skipped without an error.

> **Note:** A batch created with **New Batch** starts already In Progress, and its input quantities are still zero at that moment. The inputs you entered are therefore taken out of Inventory (Stock Out) when you complete the batch and the real quantities are known. The cost entry sent to Finance on completion is the batch total once: raw material, labour and overhead together.

**Good to know.**
- The cost entry is added once per batch. Nothing repeats if you open the batch again.
- Quality is recorded on the batch but does not stop stock from being added.
- The Reports page has a "Processing Efficiency" block and a "Batch Profitability" table that read batch weights in kilograms; the Processing page does not record those exact fields, so these report blocks often show 0 (see the Reports chapter).

**If something goes wrong.**
- *The Start Batch button is grey.* The recipe or the batch number is empty.
- *Finished goods do not appear in Inventory.* The output quantities were still 0 when you completed, or you do not have permission to add stock, or you did not click **Save Outputs & Calculate Yield** first.
- *My batches disappeared.* Processing is saved on this device only. Restore a backup (see Part 5).

---

## Inventory

### Inventory (stock items and movements)

**What it is for.** Inventory is your store book. It lists every item you keep (feed, medicine, seed, fertiliser, packaging, eggs, milk, finished goods), how much of each you have, and every time that amount changed, who did it and why. The quantity of an item can only change through a **movement**, so you always have a trail.

**Before you start.**
- Everything here is saved to your company account. Your team sees the same stock on every phone and computer.
- Permissions: you need **view** to see stock, **create** to add an item or record **Stock In**, **edit** to change an item or record **Stock Out**, **Adjust** or **Transfer**, and **delete** to delete an item.
- If you have no access you see a padlock and "You do not have access to Inventory". Ask your Director to give you access under **Team & Permissions**.

**Step by step: add an item.**
1. Click **Inventory** in the left-hand menu.
2. Click **Add Item** (top of the left panel). The button stays grey until the stock has loaded.
3. Fill in the form (the fields are listed in the table below). Only **Item Name** is required.
4. Click **Add Item**. The item appears in the list and the left panel counts it.

**The Add Inventory Item form.**

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| **Item Name** * | A clear name, for example "Broiler Starter Feed". The name is how production, sales and processing find this item, so choose it with care (see "How automatic entries find your item"). |
| **SKU / Code** | Your own code. The **Auto** button builds one from the first letters of the name plus four random digits, for example "BSF-4821". Optional. |
| **Category** | One of ten (list below). Default is **Other**. |
| **Unit** | How you count it: kg, litre, bag, count. Default is kg. |
| **Opening Stock** | What you hold today. It is saved as a first Stock In movement with the note "Opening stock", dated today, so it shows in the history. |
| **Min Stock Level** | The level at or below which the item counts as low. |
| **Cost per Unit** | What one unit costs you. Used to value your stock. |
| **Location** | Where it is kept, for example "Feed Store A". |
| **Supplier** | Who you buy it from. |
| **Notes** | Anything else. |

The expiry date is not on the Add form. Add it afterwards on the **edit** tab.

**The ten categories.**

| Category | Typical items |
|---|---|
| **Feed & Fodder** | Starter, grower and finisher feed, hay, silage, bran |
| **Medicines** | Vaccines, antibiotics, dewormers |
| **Seeds** | Seed and planting grain |
| **Fertiliser** | Compound D, urea, NPK, DAP |
| **Chemicals** | Pesticides, herbicides, fungicides |
| **Equipment** | Pumps, pipes, nets, tools |
| **Packaging** | Bags, boxes, crates, trays |
| **Produce** | Eggs, milk, honey, fish, meat, harvested crops |
| **Processed Goods** | Flour, oil, maize meal and other finished goods |
| **Other** | Anything else |

**What you will see.**

*The left panel.* A heading with the number of items and, in red, how many are low (for example "12 items · 3 low"). Below it:
- A **search box** ("Search items…") that finds a match in the item name or the SKU.
- A **category filter** (**All Categories** or one category).
- A stock filter with three buttons: **All**, **OK** and **Low**. **Low** shows items at or below their minimum. **OK** shows items above it.
- The list. Each row shows an icon, the name, the SKU and category, the current quantity (red when low) and the unit, and a small red dot for low stock.
- At the bottom, the item count and the **Total value** (quantity times cost per unit, added up for all items that have a cost).

*The right panel.* Click an item to open it. You see its name and quantity, a red warning "Low stock (min: N unit)" when low, the cost and value if a cost is set, three quick buttons (**Stock In**, **Stock Out**, **Adjust**) and three tabs:
- **overview**: Name, SKU, Category, Unit, Min Stock, Cost/Unit, Location, Supplier, Expiry Date and Notes.
- **movements**: the history, newest first (up to the latest 100 entries).
- **edit**: change the details, or delete the item.

**Low stock rule.** An item is low when its quantity is **at or below** its minimum level (equal counts as low). Beware of items with a minimum of 0: when their quantity reaches 0 they are low, and a brand-new item with minimum 0 and quantity 0 is low too.

### Recording a movement (Stock In, Stock Out, Adjust, Transfer)

**Step by step.**
1. Open the item.
2. Click **Stock In**, **Stock Out** or **Adjust**. (You can switch type inside the window.)
3. Choose the **Movement Type** if you need to change it. The four types are **Stock In (Receive)**, **Stock Out (Dispatch)**, **Stock Adjustment** and **Transfer Location**.
4. Type the **Quantity**.
5. For a transfer, fill in **Moved to**.
6. Check the **Date** (today by default). Optionally add a **Reference** (for example a PO number, delivery note or batch number) and **Notes**.
7. For Stock In you may also type a **Unit Cost (optional)**.
8. Click **Record Movement**.

**The four movement types and their rules.**

| Type | What it does | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| **Stock In** | Adds to the quantity. Use it for deliveries, purchases and goods you produced. | Quantity must be above zero. Needs "create" permission. |
| **Stock Out** | Takes away from the quantity. Use it for feed used, goods sold, wastage you want to log as usage. | Quantity must be above zero and cannot be more than what is in stock. Needs "edit" permission. |
| **Adjust** | Corrects the quantity after a count, or for spillage, loss or theft. | A positive number adds, a **negative number removes**. It cannot be zero. A removal cannot be more than what is in stock. |
| **Transfer** | Moves stock out of this item to another place. | Needs **Moved to** (for example "Store B" or "another farm"). It takes the quantity away from this item. It does not add it to another item, so if the other place is also an item in your list, record a **Stock In** there. |

The **Record Movement** button stays grey until the quantity is valid (above zero, or non-zero for an adjustment) and, for a transfer, until you have typed where it is going.

> **Tip:** When you correct a stock count, use **Adjust** with the difference. If the book says 50 bags and you counted 46, adjust by -4 and write "Stock count" in Notes.

> **Watch out:** The unit cost you type on a Stock In is kept on that movement only. It does not change the item's **Cost per Unit**. To change the value of the item, use the **edit** tab.

**What the movement history shows.** For each movement: its type, the reference (as "ref: ..."), the date, "to ..." for a destination, the notes, and the name of the person who recorded it. The quantity has a plus sign for Stock In, a minus sign for Stock Out and Transfer, and its own sign for an adjustment. Entries that arrived automatically (from production, sales or processing) show the name of the person whose action triggered them.

**If something goes wrong.**

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| **Only N unit in stock** | You asked to take out, transfer or adjust away more than you have. Lower the quantity. |
| **The quantity must be greater than zero** | Stock In, Stock Out and Transfer need a positive number. |
| **An adjustment cannot be zero** | Type a positive or negative number. |
| **Say where the stock is going** | A transfer needs the **Moved to** field. |
| **Item not found** | The item was deleted or belongs to another business. Reload the page. |
| **Permission denied: you need edit access to inventory** (or create/delete) | Your role does not allow this action. Ask your Director. |
| **Inventory could not be loaded** | A connection problem. Click **Try again**. |

### Editing an item

**Step by step.**
1. Open the item and click the **edit** tab.
2. Change any of: **Name**, **SKU**, **Category**, **Unit**, **Min Stock Level**, **Cost per Unit**, **Location**, **Supplier**, **Expiry Date**, **Notes**.
3. Click **Save Changes**.

The page reminds you: "The quantity in stock changes only through Stock In, Stock Out and Adjust, so every change is on record." There is no field to type the quantity directly.

**Good to know.**
- Setting **Cost per Unit** to blank removes the cost. A cost of exactly 0 is treated as no cost.
- Minimum stock and cost cannot be negative. The message is "Minimum stock and cost cannot be negative".
- Quantities are kept to three decimal places.

### Deleting an item

1. Open the item, go to the **edit** tab and scroll to the red **Danger Zone**.
2. Click **Delete this item**.
3. Read the warning "This will permanently delete this item and all its movement history." and click **Yes, Delete**, or **Cancel**.

> **Watch out:** Deleting an item also deletes its whole movement history, and it cannot be undone. If you only want to stop using an item, set its minimum to 0 and leave it. Only roles with "delete" permission can do this.

### How automatic entries find your item

Several pages add to or take from Inventory for you, so you do not enter the same movement twice.

| Where it starts | What happens in Inventory |
|---|---|
| **Production**: a *purchase* event with "add to inventory" ticked | **Stock In** of the item name, quantity, unit and unit cost you typed in the event. |
| **Production**: a *harvest* or *partial harvest* event sent to Inventory | **Stock In** of the product name and quantity. |
| **Production**: an *egg collection* event | **Stock In** of "Table Eggs", unit "count", and **Stock In** of "Hatching Eggs" for any hatching eggs you enter. |
| **Production**: a *milk collection* event | **Stock In** of "Fresh Milk", unit "litre". |
| **Production**: a *honey harvest* event | **Stock In** of "Honey", unit "kg". |
| **Sales**: an order is marked **Fulfilled** | **Stock Out** for each order line. |
| **Processing**: a batch is completed | **Stock In** of each finished product (and **Stock Out** of inputs when the batch moves from Planned to In Progress). |

**Matching by name.** The app compares the names after ignoring capital letters, spaces and punctuation. "Broiler Starter Feed", "broiler-starter feed" and "BROILER STARTER FEED" are the same item. The code (SKU) can match too. If two items match, the oldest one is used.

**Incoming goods that do not match** create a new item automatically. Its name is the name sent; its unit is the unit sent (kg if none); its minimum is 0; its category is guessed from the name (words such as feed, bran, hay give **Feed & Fodder**; vaccine, drug give **Medicines**; seed, grain, maize give **Seeds**; fertiliser, urea, NPK give **Fertiliser**; bag, box, crate give **Packaging**; milk, egg, honey, fish, meat give **Produce**; flour, oil give **Processed Goods**; pump, pipe, net, tool give **Equipment**; anything else gives **Produce**). The notes say "Created automatically from production" (or processing). You can then correct the category and cost on the **edit** tab.

**Outgoing goods that do not match** are ignored without an error. A sale of "Live broilers" does nothing to stock if you have no item with that name.

**Not enough stock.** An automatic Stock Out takes what is available and notes the shortfall in the movement ("short by N unit"). If the item has nothing at all, nothing is recorded. The sale or batch itself is never blocked.

**No double entries.** Every automatic movement carries a unique key, so if your connection drops and the app retries, the stock is not added twice.

> **Tip:** Give each item the same name your team uses in production events and on sales order lines. "Maize" in one place and "Maize grain" in another are two different items and will not match.

> **Watch out:** An automatic entry made while you are offline waits on your device and is sent again every 30 seconds and whenever you are back online, up to 20 tries. If your login has no permission to add stock (no Inventory create right and no create or edit right on the production pages), the entry is discarded after the first refusal and does not come back. Ask your Director to correct your permissions, then enter that movement by hand.

**Good to know.**
- The Inventory page does not show a "syncing" counter. If an automatic movement is slow to arrive, wait a minute and reload.
- The Dashboard and the Reports pages read the same stock list.
- The **Total value** is quantity times **Cost per Unit** for items that have a cost. Items without a cost add nothing.

---

## Sales

### Sales (customers and orders)

**What it is for.** Sales keeps your customers, your sales orders (quotations and invoices in one), and the payments you receive. The server calculates the totals and the order numbers, so they are always consistent.

**Before you start.**
- Everything is saved to your company account and is shared by your team.
- You need **view** to see Sales, **create** to add customers and orders, **edit** to change an order's status, add payments, notes and edit a customer, and **delete** to delete a customer.
- With no access you see "You do not have access to Sales".

**What you will see.** A left panel with three small figures at the top, two tabs (**orders** and **customers**), a button (**New Order** or **Add Customer**), a search box, and the list. A right panel shows the order or customer you click.

| Figure | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Revenue** | The total of all orders that are not cancelled, including drafts and confirmed orders not yet fulfilled. |
| **Outstanding** | What customers still owe: for orders that are not fully paid and not cancelled, total minus paid. |
| **Active Orders** | Orders with the status Draft or Confirmed. |

> **Note:** The **Revenue** figure on the Sales page counts orders from the moment they exist. The **Finance** page only counts income when an order is **fulfilled**. The two will differ until your orders are fulfilled.

The search box on the **orders** tab finds an order number or a customer name. On the **customers** tab it finds a customer name. Orders are listed newest first by their order date.

### Customers

**Step by step: add a customer.**
1. Click the **customers** tab, then **Add Customer**.
2. Fill in the form and click **Add**.

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| **Name** * | The customer's name. |
| **Type** | **individual**, **business**, **wholesale** or **export**. |
| **Phone** | For example +260 ... |
| **Email** | Optional. |
| **Address** | Optional, up to a few lines. |

**Step by step: see, edit or delete a customer.**
1. Click a customer in the list. The panel shows the name, type, phone, email and address, then **Total Spend** (all orders except cancelled), **Outstanding** (unpaid balance) and the **Order History** with each order number, date and total.
2. Click **Edit** to change the details. When you change the name, the name on all their existing orders is updated too.
3. Click **Delete** to remove them. The app asks "Delete [name]? Their orders will remain." Click OK to confirm.

**Good to know.** Deleting a customer does not delete their orders. The orders keep the customer's name. Customer names are not required to be unique.

### Creating an order

**Step by step.**
1. On the **orders** tab click **New Order**.
2. Under **Customer**, choose a customer from the list, or click **New** and type a new customer's name. If you have no customers yet, the name box is shown straight away. A customer created this way has the type individual and only a name; you can add details later.
3. Set the **Date** (today by default). Optionally set a **Due Date (opt.)**, the day payment is expected.
4. Under **Line Items** fill in, for each line: **Description**, **Qty**, **Unit** (default kg) and **Price** (per unit). Click **+ Add line** for more lines. Click the small cross to remove a line (you cannot remove the last one).
5. Optionally fill in **Tax %** and **Discount Amount**.
6. Check the grey box: it shows the **Subtotal** and **Total** as you type.
7. Click **Create Order**.

The new order opens with the status **Draft**.

**How the total is worked out.** Subtotal is the sum of quantity times price for every line. Total is subtotal plus tax (subtotal times tax % divided by 100) minus the discount amount. It never goes below zero. The server repeats this calculation and its answer is the one saved.

> **Tip:** Type the **Description** of each line exactly like the stock item it comes from (for example "Broiler Starter Feed" or "Live broilers"). When you fulfil the order, the lines are matched to your Inventory items by name and taken out of stock.

**Order numbers.** Every order gets a number in the form **ORD-YYYYMM-NNN**, for example ORD-202610-007. YYYYMM is the year and month of the **order date** you chose, and NNN counts up from 001 within that month.

**Limits and messages.**

| Message | When you see it |
|---|---|
| **Choose a customer or add a new one** | No customer selected and no new name typed. |
| **An order needs between 1 and 100 lines** | No lines, or more than 100. |
| **Every line needs a description** | A line has a quantity or price but no description. (The form drops lines with no description before sending.) |
| **"[line]": quantity must be greater than zero** | A line quantity is 0 or negative. |
| **"[line]": price cannot be negative** | A line price is below 0. |
| **Tax must be between 0 and 100 %** | Tax % outside that range. |
| **Discount cannot be negative** | A negative discount. |
| **The due date is before the order date** | Due date earlier than the date. |
| **Could not allocate an order number — please try again** | Two orders were created at the same moment. Click **Create Order** again. |

### The order status flow

An order moves in one direction:

**Draft** to **Confirmed** to **Fulfilled**. A Draft or a Confirmed order can instead be **Cancelled**.

| Status | Meaning | Buttons you see |
|---|---|---|
| **Draft** | Created, not yet agreed. | **Confirm**, **Record Payment**, **Cancel** |
| **Confirmed** | The customer has agreed. | **Mark Fulfilled**, **Record Payment**, **Cancel** |
| **Fulfilled** | Goods delivered or service done. Final. | **Record Payment** (if a balance remains) |
| **Cancelled** | Dropped. Final. It cannot receive payments. | None |

Any other jump is refused with a message such as "A draft order cannot become fulfilled".

> **Watch out:** A fulfilled order cannot be cancelled or edited (other than notes). Fulfil only when the goods have really left. If you make a mistake, fix it with a Finance edit and an Inventory adjustment.

**What Mark Fulfilled does.** Two things happen on the server, once only (never again if you later change a note):
1. **Finance:** one income entry is added with the category **Sales Income**, the full order total as the amount, today's date as the date, the order number as the reference and the description "Sales order [number] — [customer]". The note is "Auto-recorded from sales". It is not added when the order total is zero. The entry is not linked to a production cycle.
2. **Inventory:** every order line is taken out of stock (Stock Out), matched by the line description to an item name. A line with no matching item is skipped. If stock is lower than the line quantity, only what is there is taken and the shortfall is noted. The reference is the order number and the destination is the customer.

> **Watch out:** The income is posted when you **fulfil**, not when the customer pays. Recording a payment does not create a second income entry. So you never need to type a sale in Finance when it is already a sales order that you fulfil.

### Recording payments

**Step by step.**
1. Open the order and click **Record Payment** (green). It shows while the order is not fully paid and not cancelled.
2. The window shows the **Outstanding** amount. The **Amount Received** is filled with the full balance. Change it for a part payment.
3. Optionally type a **Note** (for example "Cash", "Mobile money", "Bank transfer").
4. Click **Record**. The button is grey while the amount is zero.

Each payment is saved with its date (today), amount, note and the name of the person who recorded it.

**Rules.**
- A part payment is allowed. You can record as many as you need until the balance is zero.
- A payment cannot be more than the balance: the message is "That is more than the balance due (N)".
- A payment must be above zero: "The payment must be greater than zero".
- A cancelled order cannot receive payments: "A cancelled order cannot receive payments".
- You cannot delete or edit a payment once saved.

**Payment statuses.**

| Status | When |
|---|---|
| **Unpaid** | Nothing received and the order is not late. |
| **Partial** | Something received, balance remains, and the order is not late. |
| **Paid** | The amount received equals or exceeds the total (and the total is above zero). |
| **Overdue** | There is a **due date** that is earlier than today, the order is not cancelled, and the order is not fully paid (including part payments). |

An order without a due date can never become Overdue. A cancelled order never shows Overdue.

### The order panel: invoice, payments and notes

Click an order to open it. The top shows the order number, customer and date, the total, the order status, the payment status and the **Outstanding** balance in red. There are three tabs.

- **invoice**: a printable-style statement with the order number, **Date**, **Due** date, the customer, a table of **Description**, **Qty**, **Unit**, **Price** and **Amount**, then **Subtotal**, **Tax (n%)**, **Discount**, **Total**, **Paid** and **Balance Due**.
- **payments**: a **Payment Summary** (**Order Total**, **Amount Paid**, **Balance Due**) and a list "Payments received (n)" with date, note, who recorded it and the amount. When nothing has been paid it says to use the **Record Payment** button.
- **notes**: a free text box. Whatever you type is saved when you click outside the box.

The invoice shown here is a view on screen. The Sales page has no separate PDF button; to print it, use your browser's print function.

**Good to know.**
- Search, summary figures and the customer's own panel all update as soon as you save.
- The page loads up to 5,000 of your newest orders.
- After you fulfil an order, Finance and Inventory are refreshed so you can see the income and the stock change at once.

**If something goes wrong.**
- *"Sales could not be loaded"* with **Try again**: a connection problem. Click it.
- *An error banner appears at the top of the list* (for example "Permission denied: you need edit access to sales"): your role does not allow that action. Click the small cross to hide it.
- *The goods did not leave stock after Fulfil.* The line description does not match any Inventory item name, or the item was empty.

---

## Trade & Export

### Trade & Export Documents

**What it is for.** When you sell a larger consignment to a buyer, especially across the border, you need paperwork: a contract, a commercial invoice, a packing list and often certificates. This page keeps your **buyers**, your **trade contracts**, and the **documents** for each contract, and makes a PDF of each one for you.

**Before you start.**
- The buyers, contracts and documents are saved to your company account, so your team shares them.
- Buyers and contracts follow the **Sales** permission: you need "create" to add a buyer or contract and "edit" to move a contract along.
- Documents follow the **Trade & Export** permission: "create" to make documents, "edit" to change them and "delete" to remove them. By default the Sales Manager and the Director have these; the Finance Manager can only view.
- Add a buyer first, so the contract can name them.

> **Watch out:** The PDFs are prepared by AGRINUXES from your data. They are working documents, not official certificates. A phytosanitary certificate, a veterinary health certificate or a certificate of origin only has legal force when the issuing authority (for example the Plant Quarantine and Phytosanitary Service, the Department of Veterinary Services, the Chamber of Commerce or ZRA) issues it. Draft PDFs print a notice that they have not been submitted to or issued by the authority.

**What you will see at the top.** A heading, a refresh button, and five cards:

| Card | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Open contracts** | Contracts that are not Fulfilled or Cancelled. |
| **Contract value** | Total value of all contracts except Cancelled, in ZMW. |
| **Export contracts** | Contracts whose buyer's country is not Zambia. |
| **Documents issued** | Documents with the status Approved. |
| **Documents in draft** | Documents that are not yet Approved (Draft, Submitted or Rejected). |

Below are three tabs: **Contracts**, **All documents** and **Buyers**.

### Buyers

**Step by step: add a buyer.**
1. Click the **Buyers** tab, then **Add buyer** (the form is already open when you have no buyers).
2. Fill in the fields below and click **Save buyer**. A green message says "Buyer added."

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| **Buyer / company name** * | Required. |
| Type (drop-down) | Company, Exporter, Processor, Supermarket, Trader, Ngo, Government or Individual. Default is Exporter. |
| **Contact person**, **Phone**, **Email** | How to reach them. |
| **Street address**, **Town / city** | Their location. |
| **Country** * | Required. Default **Zambia**. |
| **Payment terms** | For example "30 days" or "cash on delivery". |

The list shows each buyer with name, type, place, contact, phone and payment terms. A green **Verified** tag appears next to buyers the platform has verified; you cannot set that yourself. The page has no button to edit or delete a buyer.

> **Tip:** The buyer's **Country** decides what paperwork the contract needs. A buyer outside Zambia makes the contract an export with the full certificate checklist. A buyer in Zambia only needs an invoice and a packing list.

### Contracts

**Step by step: create a contract.**
1. On the **Contracts** tab click **New contract**.
2. Fill in the form: **Commodity** (for example "Soya beans, Grade A"), the buyer (or "No buyer yet"), **Quantity**, **Unit** (default kg), **Price per unit (ZMW)**, **Deposit %**, a delivery date, and **Payment terms**.
3. Click **Create contract**. A green message says "Contract created." The contract starts as **Draft**.

The contract value is quantity times price, worked out for you. The contract table shows **Commodity**, **Buyer**, **Quantity**, **Value**, **Delivery**, **Status**, **Docs** (approved documents out of all documents) and **Open**.

**Contract statuses.**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Draft** | Being prepared. |
| **Offered** | The offer is with the buyer. |
| **Accepted** | The buyer agreed. |
| **In Progress** | Goods are being delivered. |
| **Fulfilled** | Done. |
| **Disputed** | There is a disagreement. |
| **Cancelled** | Dropped. |

Click a contract row to open it. In the contract screen you see the buyer, the quantity and price, the value and payment details, and **Move contract to:** with buttons for the next steps:

| From | You can move to |
|---|---|
| Draft | Offered |
| Offered | Accepted, Cancelled |
| Accepted | In Progress, Cancelled |
| In Progress | Fulfilled, Disputed |
| Disputed | Fulfilled, Cancelled |
| Fulfilled, Cancelled | (nothing; these are final) |

Two more buttons track money: **Mark deposit paid** (shown when a deposit percentage was set and is not yet paid) and **Mark fully paid**. They only tick a box on the contract. They do **not** create income in Finance.

> **Watch out:** A trade contract is not a sales order. Moving a contract to Fulfilled or marking it paid does not post income to Finance and does not take stock out of Inventory. If you want the sale in your books, also record it as a Sales order (and fulfil it) or add the income in Finance by hand, but only one of the two, not both.

> **Note:** Fulfilled, Disputed and Cancelled contracts, and verified buyers, are counted in your Credit Profile (see the Credit chapter). Keeping contract statuses honest and up to date helps your score.

**The top buttons of a contract.**
- **Contract PDF**: makes a "Produce Sale & Supply Contract" PDF (parties, goods and price, delivery and payment, terms) and opens it in a new tab. It carries a reference like TC-2026-1A2B3C4D.
- **Generate document pack**: creates every required document that is missing for this contract and makes all their PDFs. A message tells you how many PDFs and how many were newly created.

If your browser blocks pop-ups, allow them for AGRINUXES, or the PDF will not open.

### The document checklist

Each contract shows a **Document checklist** with a coloured dot for every document: grey (not created), amber (draft or submitted), green (approved) and red (rejected). Above it a line says "N required documents missing" in amber, or "All required documents present" in green.

**Which documents are required.**

| Situation | Required documents |
|---|---|
| Export of plant products | Commercial invoice, Packing list, Phytosanitary certificate, Certificate of origin, Customs declaration |
| Export of animal products (the commodity name contains a word such as cattle, goat, sheep, pig, beef, chicken, poultry, broiler, egg, fish, tilapia, honey, milk, dairy, hide, skin, livestock or chick) | Commercial invoice, Packing list, Veterinary health certificate, Certificate of origin, Customs declaration |
| Domestic sale (buyer in Zambia) | Commercial invoice, Packing list only |
| No buyer chosen yet | The export list above |

Other types you can add yourself (marked **optional**): **Fumigation certificate**, **Other**, and any of the above that are not required.

**Step by step: create one document.**
1. In the checklist find the document and click **Create & render**. Or use **Add another document type…**, pick a type and click **Create**.
2. The document is created from the contract (commodity, quantity, unit, destination country), numbered and rendered as a PDF. A message says, for example, "Commercial invoice created and rendered."

**Document numbers** have the form PREFIX-YEAR-NNNN, counted per type and year: **INV** (invoice), **PKL** (packing list), **PHY** (phytosanitary), **COO** (certificate of origin), **VHC** (veterinary health), **FUM** (fumigation), **CUS** (customs), **DOC** (other). Example: INV-2026-0001.

**What each row offers once the document exists.**
- A dark **PDF** button to open the file.
- A refresh button to regenerate the PDF.
- **Details** to edit the document.
- A bin button to delete it (the app asks "Delete [number]?").

**Document details (the editor).**

| Field | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Document number** | Prefilled; you can change it. |
| **Status** | **Draft**, **Submitted**, **Approved** or **Rejected**. You set it yourself as the paperwork moves. |
| **Issuing authority** | Prefilled for certificates with the usual Zambian authority; you can edit it. |
| **Commodity**, **Quantity**, **Unit**, **Destination country** | Copied from the contract. |
| **Issue date**, **Expiry** | The expiry must be after the issue date. |
| **Additional declaration / notes (printed on the document)** | Appears on the PDF. |

Click **Save & regenerate PDF**. A green message says "[number] saved and PDF regenerated."

**All documents.** This tab lists every document across all contracts with **Number**, **Type**, **Contract**, **Destination**, **Status**, **Issued**, **Expires** and a **PDF** link (or "not rendered").

**If something goes wrong.**
- *Failed to load*: a red message at the top with the reason. Click the refresh button.
- *Expiry date must be after issue date*, *Quantity cannot be negative*, *Commodity is required*: fix the field and save again.
- *A "Permission denied" message*: your role does not have the right (Sales for buyers and contracts, Trade & Export for documents).

---

## Finance

### Finance (income and expenses)

**What it is for.** Finance is your farm's money book: a list of every shilling in (income) and out (expense), with dashboards that show profit, the biggest costs and the month-by-month picture. Lenders and your credit profile read from this same ledger.

**Before you start.**
- The ledger is saved to your company account, shared by your team on every device.
- You need **view** to see it, **create** to add an entry, **edit** to change an entry and **delete** to remove one.
- With no access you see a padlock and "You do not have access to Finance".
- Most of your costs and income arrive automatically from production, sales and processing. Check the "Automatic entries" section before you type anything.

**What you will see.**
- A top bar with the heading **Finance**, a count such as "46 transactions" and, in amber, "N syncing" while automatic entries are still being delivered.
- A **period** drop-down: **Last month**, **Last 3 months** (the default), **Last 6 months**, **Last 12 months** and **All time**. It filters by the date of each entry, counting back from today.
- Two buttons, green **Income** and red **Expense** (grey until the ledger has loaded).
- Four tabs: **dashboard**, **income**, **expenses** and **all**.

### Adding an income or an expense

**Step by step.**
1. Click **Income** or **Expense**.
2. Choose a **Category** (the lists are below).
3. Type a **Description** (required), for example "Starter feed – 50 bags".
4. Type the **Amount** (required, above zero).
5. Check the **Date** (today by default).
6. Optionally choose a **Production Cycle** to link the entry to (the list shows your active and completed cycles with their status).
7. Optionally type a **Reference #** (receipt or invoice number) and **Notes**.
8. Click **Save**. The button is grey until there is a description and an amount above zero.

**Expense categories.**

| Category | Use it for |
|---|---|
| **Feed & Fodder** | Feed, hay, silage, supplements |
| **Medicines** | Vaccines, drugs, vet fees |
| **Seeds** | Seed and seedlings |
| **Fertiliser** | Compound D, urea, lime and similar |
| **Labour** | Wages, casual workers, salaries |
| **Equipment** | Tools and machines bought |
| **Transport** | Delivery, fuel for hauling, hire of trucks |
| **Utilities** | Electricity, water, fuel for generators |
| **Marketing** | Adverts, signs, market fees |
| **Repairs** | Maintenance and repairs |
| **Other Cost** | Anything that does not fit above |

**Income categories.**

| Category | Use it for |
|---|---|
| **Sales Income** | Money from selling produce, animals or products |
| **Grant / Subsidy** | Grants, subsidies, programme support in cash |
| **Other Income** | Anything else received |

### Reading the tabs

**dashboard.** Four cards (**Total Income**, **Total Expenses**, **Net Profit**, **Margin**) for the chosen period; a **Monthly Overview** with a green bar for income and a red bar for expense for each of the latest six months in the period, and the net on the right; and an **Expense Breakdown** with the top eight categories, each with a bar, amount and share of all expenses. When the period has no entries it says "No transactions yet." and "Click + Income or + Expense to get started."

**income / expenses / all.** A list, newest first. Each row shows the category icon, the description, the category and date, and the amount (green with a plus for income, red with a minus for expense). Click a row to open it and see the linked **Cycle**, the **Ref #** and the notes, with **Edit** and **Delete** links.

If a period has no entries the list says "No transactions in this period."

### Editing and deleting

**Edit:** open the row, click **Edit**, change the fields and click **Save Changes**. This needs "edit" permission. The type (income or expense) cannot be changed; delete and re-enter instead.

**Delete:** open the row and click **Delete**. This needs "delete" permission, which by default only the Director has.

> **Watch out:** **Delete** removes the entry at once, without asking you to confirm. Check the row before you click. You can edit and delete automatic entries too. A deleted automatic entry does not come back by itself.

### Automatic entries: what arrives without you typing

To avoid double entry, these things post to Finance for you. They show in the list with a note such as "Auto-recorded from production engine".

| What you do | Entry in Finance |
|---|---|
| Log a **purchase** event in production with a total cost | An **expense**. The category is guessed from the item's category text: feed, fodder or silage gives **Feed & Fodder**; medicine, vet, vaccination or treatment gives **Medicines**; seed gives **Seeds**; fertiliser gives **Fertiliser**; labour, wage or salary gives **Labour**; equipment or machinery gives **Equipment**; transport or delivery gives **Transport**; utility, electric, water or fuel gives **Utilities**; repair or maintenance gives **Repairs**; anything else gives **Other Cost**. Linked to the cycle. |
| Log a **treatment** or **vaccination** event with a cost | An expense in **Medicines**. |
| Log a **stocking** event (for example fingerlings) with a total cost | An expense in **Other Cost**. |
| Log a **direct sale** (sale) event with a total value | An **income** in **Sales Income**. |
| **Fulfil** a Sales order | An income in **Sales Income** for the full order total, dated the day you fulfil. |
| **Complete** a Processing batch | An expense in **Other Cost** for the batch cost. |

A **honey harvest** event adds honey to Inventory but does **not** post income. A **harvest** or an egg or milk collection adds stock only. Record the money when you sell.

Each automatic entry has a unique key, so the same event is never posted twice, even if your connection drops and the app tries again. While an entry is waiting, the Finance header shows "N syncing" and the entry appears at once on screen; it is replaced by the saved copy when delivered.

> **Watch out:** An automatic entry is only accepted if your login may add to Finance, or may create or edit the page it came from (production, inventory or sales). If it is refused, it is discarded and not retried. Ask your Director for the right permissions and add that one entry by hand.

> **Tip:** Linking an entry to a **Production Cycle** lets you see cycle-level profit in the Reports pages and on the Dashboard enterprise cards.

**Good to know.**
- The Dashboard uses the ledger for all-time totals; this page uses the period you choose.
- A weak connection never loses entries. Entries wait on your device and are delivered later (up to 20 tries).
- The ledger loads up to the latest 10,000 entries.

**If something goes wrong.**

| Message or sign | What it means |
|---|---|
| **The ledger could not be loaded** with **Try again** | A connection problem. Click **Try again**. |
| **You do not have access to Finance** | Your role has no Finance view. Ask your Director. |
| **description is required** | Fill in the description. |
| **amount must be greater than zero** | The amount must be above 0. |
| **date is required** | Choose a date. |
| **category "x" is not valid for income** (or expense) | The category does not fit the type. Choose from the list. |
| **Transaction not found** | It was already deleted. Reload. |
| **Permission denied: you need edit access to finance** (or create/delete) | Your role does not allow this. |

---

## Credit Profile and lender sharing

### The Credit Profile

**What it is for.** A bank or lender wants proof that a farm is a good risk. The Credit Profile builds that proof from the records you already keep in AGRINUXES: a score out of 100, a letter band from A to E, and the evidence behind every point. You decide who sees it, for how long, and you can cancel access at any time. Nothing is self-declared; the app counts only what is recorded.

**Before you start.**
- The Credit Profile is for farm accounts. You need **view** access to the Credit Profile page to see it, **create** access to give a lender a link, and **delete** access to cancel a link. By default the Director has all three and the Finance Manager can view and create.
- In the page itself, the sharing form is only shown to a **Director** or a **Finance Manager**. Anyone else sees "Only a director or finance manager can share the credit summary."
- The score is only as good as your records. Read "How to improve your score" below.

**Step by step: look at your score.**
1. Click **Credit Profile** in the left-hand menu.
2. Wait for "Computing your credit summary…".
3. Read the score and band at the top, then the five factors, then the detail cards.
4. Click the refresh button (top right) to recalculate after you have added records.
5. Click **PDF** to make a printable copy (see "The PDF" below).

**What you will see.**
- A round **score gauge** showing the score "out of 100".
- A large **band letter** (A to E) with a short description, your farm name, district and province, and the "as of" date.
- If your file is thin, an amber note: "Thin file: fewer than 3 months of records. The score becomes reliable as the farm keeps records."
- Five **factor bars**. Each shows the points earned out of the points possible (for example "14.5 / 25") and one line of evidence.
- Detail cards: **Farm**, **Financial performance**, **Monthly revenue, last 12 months** (a bar chart; or "No revenue entries in the last 12 months."), **Trading history**, and **Verification & support network** (ticks for what is in place).
- A footnote saying the summary was generated from your AGRINUXES records, is decision support only and uses five weighted rule-based factors (25 / 25 / 20 / 15 / 15).

### How the score is worked out

The score is the total of five factors. The weights add up to 100.

| Factor | Weight | What it measures |
|---|---|---|
| **Record-keeping consistency** | 25 | How regularly you keep production records. |
| **Profitability** | 25 | Whether your farm makes more than it spends. |
| **Trading history** | 20 | Contracts you have fulfilled and your buyers. |
| **Tenure & scale** | 15 | How long you have been on the platform and how big you are. |
| **Verification & support network** | 15 | Consent, extension officer, programmes, certificates, insurance, loan history. |

Here is exactly how each factor earns points.

#### Record-keeping consistency (up to 25)

- **Active months (up to 20 points).** The app looks at the last 12 months and counts how many have at least one production record. Each such month is worth about 1.7 points; 12 out of 12 gives the full 20.
- **Recency (up to 5 points).** If your last production record is 14 days old or less you get 5 points. If it is 15 to 45 days old you get 2.5. If it is older, or you have none, you get 0.

The evidence line reads like "4 of last 12 months with production records (31 records); last record 5 days ago", or "No production records yet".

#### Profitability (up to 25)

If the app finds **no costs** at all, this factor is 0 and the evidence says "No batch financials or ledger costs recorded".

**The basis.** If you have recorded at least one **expense** in the Finance ledger in the last 12 months, the Finance ledger is used, and each **month** counts as one unit. Otherwise the older batch records are used, where each **batch** is one unit. Keeping your costs in Finance is the simplest way to be scored.

The factor is built from three parts:
- **Return on cost (up to 12.5 points).** Profit divided by costs over the last 12 months, as a percentage. A return of 60% or more earns the full 12.5. A return of 30% earns half. Zero or negative earns nothing.
- **Share of profitable units (up to 7.5 points).** The share of months (or batches) where income was more than cost. If every month with costs was profitable you get the full 7.5.
- **Revenue trend (up to 5 points).** The last six months of revenue compared with the six months before. A rise of 50% or more earns the full 5; no change earns 2.5; a fall of 50% or more earns 0. If there is no income in the earlier six months, the app uses the neutral 2.5.

The evidence line reads like "ROI 22% and margin 18% over 12 months; 1/1 months profitable".

#### Trading history (up to 20)

- **Contracts fulfilled (up to 10 points).** Contracts in Trade & Export with the status **Fulfilled**. Six or more earns the full 10.
- **Fulfilment rate (up to 6 points).** Fulfilled contracts divided by (fulfilled plus disputed plus cancelled). With no contracts at all, this part is 0.
- **Verified buyers (up to 2 points).** Buyers the platform has verified, across your contracts. Three or more earns the full 2.
- **Active listings (up to 2 points).** Your active marketplace listings. Three or more earns the full 2.

The evidence line reads like "2 contracts fulfilled (ZMW 54,000), 0 disputed/cancelled, 1 verified buyers".

#### Tenure and scale (up to 15)

- **Time on the platform (up to 6 points).** 24 months or more earns the full 6.
- **Enterprises (up to 3 points).** Three active enterprises earn the full 3.
- **Batches completed (up to 4.5 points).** Six completed batches earn the full 4.5.
- **Registered land (up to 1.5 points).** Fields registered with their area. Ten hectares or more earns the full 1.5.

#### Verification and support network (up to 15)

| Item | Points |
|---|---|
| Data-sharing consent switched on (**Share with government & partners** in Settings) | 3.75 |
| An extension officer is assigned to your farm | 3 |
| Enrolled in a programme (active or completed) | 3 |
| At least one certification with status Certified | 2.25 |
| Insurance on record (a claim that is not draft or rejected) | 1.5 |
| At least one loan marked repaid and none defaulted | 1.5 |

**A defaulted loan takes 7.5 points off** this factor (it cannot go below zero).

### The bands

The total is rounded to a whole number and placed in a band:

| Band | Score | What it tells a lender |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | 80 to 100 | Strong — eligible for standard terms |
| **B** | 65 to 79 | Good — eligible, standard monitoring |
| **C** | 50 to 64 | Fair — consider with collateral or group guarantee |
| **D** | 35 to 49 | Weak — small ticket / input-in-kind only |
| **E** | 0 to 34 | Insufficient history — not yet scoreable |

**Thin file.** Your file is "thin" when you have fewer than 3 months with production records **and** there is nothing to score for profitability (no months with costs in the ledger and no batch records). A thin file can never be better than band **D**, even if the points would say A, B or C. The band text then reads "Thin file — fewer than 3 months of records; score provisional" and the amber notice appears. Recording expenses in Finance removes the thin-file limit, because it gives the app something to score.

### How to improve your score

Practical steps, in order of how much they are worth:

1. **Keep your costs and income in Finance every month.** This opens the 25 points of profitability, and removes the thin-file cap. Use the automatic entries from production, sales and processing so that you do not forget any (see the chapter on how the modules talk to each other).
2. **Keep production records going, and keep them recent.** Each month with a record is worth about 1.7 points. A record in the last 14 days is worth 5 points on its own. A farm that records every month for a year earns the full 20.
3. **Make sure income is really recorded.** Fulfil your sales orders and record the sales events so that revenue shows in the ledger. A revenue trend that is rising earns up to 5 points.
4. **Aim for profitable months.** The profitable-months share is worth up to 7.5 points. A return of 60% on cost earns the full 12.5.
5. **Use Trade & Export honestly.** Mark contracts **Fulfilled** when they are done. Six fulfilled contracts earn the full 10 points. Avoid leaving contracts as disputed or cancelled: each one lowers your fulfilment rate (worth up to 6 points).
6. **Switch on data-sharing consent** in Settings (**Share with government & partners**). This is the single easiest item: 3.75 points. Only the Director can switch it.
7. **Join a programme, ask for an extension officer, get certified and insured.** Each adds points in the verification factor. A repaid loan adds 1.5.
8. **Stay on the platform.** Tenure counts up to 24 months, and registering your fields and completing cycles also helps.
9. **Never default on a loan recorded in AGRINUXES.** It costs 7.5 points.

> **Note:** This page only reads loans, insurance claims, certificates and programme enrolments that already exist on your company account. It does not have screens for adding them. Loan history and insurance appear when they have been recorded for your business (for example by a programme partner or in the vendor and programme modules). Contact your extension officer or AGRINUXES support if one is missing.

> **Note:** Some inputs of this score, such as production records, enterprises and completed batches, are read from your company account's production data. If the evidence line says there are no records but you have been logging on the farm workspace, see Part 3 — Running Production: Cycles, Stages, Events and Outputs and ask support to check that your records are reaching your company account.

### Sharing with a lender

**What it is for.** Instead of printing a bundle of papers, you send the lender a private link. The lender opens it without needing an account. They see the same summary you see.

**Before you start.** You must be a Director or Finance Manager, with create access to the Credit Profile. Decide which lender, and what the purpose is.

**Step by step.**
1. On the Credit Profile page find the box **Share with a lender**.
2. Type the **Lender** name (required), for example Zanaco, NATSAVE, FINCA or Vision Fund.
3. Optionally type the credit officer's email or phone.
4. Check the **Purpose** (it starts as "Seasonal input loan application"; change it if needed).
5. Choose how long the link will work: **7 days**, **14 days**, **30 days** (the default), **60 days** or **90 days**.
6. Click **Create share link**.
7. A green message says "Share link created for [lender]. It expires in N days and can be revoked any time." A box titled **Send this link to the lender** shows the full link. Click **Copy** and send it by email or WhatsApp.

The link is your site address followed by a long secret code. Anyone who has the link can see the summary, so send it only to the lender.

**Who has access.** A list on the right shows every link you have made: the lender (and contact), the purpose, when it was created, when it expires, how many times it was opened ("opened 3×") and when it was last opened. A tag shows **Active**, **Expired** or **Revoked**. For active links there is a copy button to copy the link again, and a revoke button (a circle with a line) to cancel it.

**Step by step: revoke a link.**
1. Find the lender in **Who has access**.
2. Click the revoke button. The app asks "Revoke [lender]'s access?".
3. Confirm. A message says "Access for [lender] revoked." The lender's link stops working at once.

**What the lender sees.** The lender opens the link and sees a page headed "AGRINUXES — Farm credit summary" with "Shared by the farm for credit assessment · read-only". A line says "Prepared for [lender] · [purpose] · link valid until [date]". Below is the same report you see, and a **Print** button. The lender sees your farm's name, district and province, and the figures in the report. They do not see individual ledger entries, customers or other pages.

- The report is worked out again every time the lender opens it, so it shows your **current** records, not a frozen copy.
- Each opening is counted. You see this in **Who has access**.

**If the link does not work, the lender sees:**

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Invalid share link** | The link was typed or copied wrongly. |
| **This share link has been revoked by the farm** | You cancelled it. |
| **This share link has expired** | The days ran out. Create a new link. |

Under each message the page says "Ask the farm to issue a new share link from their Credit Profile."

**If something goes wrong on your side.**
- *"Permission denied: you need create access to credit"*: your role cannot make links.
- *"Permission denied: you need delete access to credit"* when you revoke: the revoke button shows for Finance Managers, but cancelling a link needs delete access, which by default only the Director has. Ask the Director to revoke it, or to give you delete access.
- *"Permission denied: farm account required"*: the Credit Profile is only for farm accounts.

### The PDF

Click **PDF** at the top of the page. A one- or two-page PDF opens in a new tab with the title "AGRINUXES FARM CREDIT SUMMARY", the farm name and place, the score and band, a table of the five factors with their scores, weights and evidence, then sections for **Farm**, **Financial performance**, **Trading history** and **Verification & support network**, and a closing note that it is decision support, not a credit decision. A green message says "PDF generated." If the PDF does not open, allow pop-ups for AGRINUXES.

> **Note:** The financial section of the PDF is headed "from batch financials", and the on-screen card says "batch financials", even when your figures come from the Finance ledger. If you have recorded expenses in Finance, the ledger is what is used.

---

## Reports

### Reports

**What it is for.** Reports turns your records into tables and charts that you can read, print and export. It has six tabs. Most of them read the Finance ledger, Inventory and Sales, which are shared by your team, and a few read device-only records (procurement, processing, health and staff), which show only what was saved on the device you are using.

**Before you start.**
- You need **view** access to Reports. The Production Manager, Finance Manager and Sales Manager have it by default.
- A report is only as complete as the records behind it. If a table looks empty, check the page where those records are made.

**Step by step.**
1. Click **Reports** in the left-hand menu (or **View Reports** on the Dashboard).
2. Click a tab along the top: **Overview**, **Profitability**, **Production**, **Inventory**, **Procurement** or **Export**.
3. On **Overview**, optionally set **From** and **To** dates to focus on a period; click **Clear** to remove them.
4. Click **Export CSV** on a table to download it as a spreadsheet file.

**Exports.** Reports exports **CSV** files (which open in Excel, Google Sheets and similar) and one **JSON** backup. There are no PDF or Excel downloads on this page. For a printable page, use your browser's print function.

### Overview tab

- A date range (**From**, **To**, **Clear**). It applies to the cards, the top-five lists and the enterprise share below. It does not change the six-month chart.
- Eight cards: **Total Revenue**, **Total Expenses**, **Net Profit**, **Profit Margin** (green above 20%, amber above 0%, red otherwise), **Active Cycles**, **Completed Cycles**, **Inventory Value** and **Low Stock Items** (with a red count badge).
- **Financial Trend — Last 6 Months**: green and red bars per month with the net in thousands.
- **Top 5 Expense Categories** and **Top 5 Income Sources**: bars by category (shown with their internal names such as "feed" or "sale_income").
- **Enterprise Revenue Share**: income per enterprise, only for income entries linked to a cycle of that enterprise. It is hidden when there is nothing to show.

Revenue and expenses come from the Finance ledger. Fulfilled sales orders are already in the ledger, so they are not added again.

### Profitability tab

This tab combines the ledger with a separate list of **production expenses**.

- Three cards: **Total Revenue** (the ledger income; the small text "Transactions + Orders" is misleading, because fulfilled orders are already in the ledger), **Total Production Costs** (production expenses plus ledger expenses) and **Net Profit** (revenue minus all costs).
- **Production Expenses**: a table and a **+ Log Expense** button. The form has **Enterprise**, **Cycle**, **Type** (**Feed**, **Vet**, **Chicks / Fingerlings / Seeds**, **Seeds**, **Fingerlings**, **Other**), **Description**, **Amount** and **Date**. All of Enterprise, Cycle, Description and Amount are needed or **Save Expense** does nothing. There is no way to edit or delete a logged expense.
- **Profitability by Enterprise**: for each enterprise, **Total Income**, **Prod Expenses**, **Other Expenses**, **Net Profit** and **Margin**, with a breakdown of the production expenses by type. Only ledger entries linked to one of that enterprise's cycles are counted.
- **Batch Profitability**: shown only when you have completed processing batches. You can type a **Selling price** per kg (starts at 28). The columns are **Batch ID**, **Input kg**, **Output kg**, **Yield %**, **Est. Revenue**, **Linked Exp**, **Net** and **Quality**.

> **Watch out:** The production expenses list on this tab is **saved on this device only** and is separate from the Finance ledger. It is subtracted from the net profit shown on this tab, so it can double-count a cost that you also have in Finance. The first time you open this tab on a device that has a cycle, four sample rows may already be listed (broiler starter feed ZMW 12,000, day-old chicks ZMW 24,000, Newcastle vaccine ZMW 1,850 and litter ZMW 2,200, total ZMW 40,050). They are examples, not your records, and they lower the net profit on this tab. For your real costs, use **Finance**; treat this tab's net profit with care and rely on the Overview tab and Finance for true totals.

> **Note:** The Batch Profitability table reads kilogram fields that the Processing page does not save, so its input, output, yield and revenue columns usually show 0 or a dash.

### Production tab

- Four chips with the number of cycles that are **active**, **completed**, **failed** and **paused**.
- **All Cycles**: **Cycle #**, **Enterprise**, **Status**, **Start**, **Days** (since start), **Stages** (done out of total), **Events** and **Records** (daily records). Button: **Export CSV**.
- **Stage Completion Heatmap**: one row per enterprise, with a numbered square for each stage of its template: green completed, amber in progress, grey pending. Hover over a square to see the stage name.
- **Event Type Breakdown**: how many events of each type were logged (for example purchase, vaccination, sale).
- **Mortality Analysis**: for cycles with mortality events: **Mortality**, **Starting**, **Rate %** (red above 5%).

### Inventory tab

- Cards: **Total Items**, **Total Value**, **Low Stock**, **Categories**.
- **Stock by Category**: a bar per category with the number of items, the value and the average cost per unit.
- **Low Stock Alert (N)**: a table of items at or below their minimum with **Item**, **Unit**, **Current**, **Min**, **Deficit** (the minimum minus the current) and **Reorder Cost** (deficit times cost per unit). Button: **Export CSV**.
- **Processing Efficiency**: **Avg Yield Ratio**, **Quality Pass Rate**, **Batches Completed**, **In Progress**. See the note above: the first two read fields that the Processing page does not fill, so they usually show 0.0%.
- **Full Inventory**: **Name**, **Category**, **Qty**, **Unit**, **Cost/Unit**, **Total Value** and **Status**. Button: **Export CSV**. Status is **CRITICAL** when the quantity is zero, **LOW** when it is at or below the minimum, and **OK** otherwise.

### Procurement tab

This tab reads your purchase orders and suppliers from the Procurement page, which is saved on this device only (see Part 5 — Farm Management, Intelligence and Devices).

- Cards: **Total POs**, **Pending / Ordered**, **Delivered**, **Total Spend**, **Pending Spend**, **Overdue**.
- **PO Status Breakdown**: a bar split by pending, ordered, delivered and cancelled.
- **Overdue Deliveries**: orders past their expected date and not delivered, with days overdue and value. Button: **Export CSV**.
- **Spend by Supplier**, **All Purchase Orders** (click the arrow on a row to see its lines; button: **Export CSV**) and **Supplier Ratings** (stars, number of POs and spend).

### Export tab (Export Centre)

Twelve cards, each with a download button:

| Card | What you get |
|---|---|
| **All Transactions** | The full ledger as CSV (ID, type, amount, date, category, description). |
| **Completed Cycles** | Finished production cycles. |
| **Inventory Items** | Current stock levels and costs. |
| **Staff & Time Logs** | Staff joined to their time entries. |
| **Purchase Orders** | All procurement orders. |
| **Supplier Directory** | Suppliers and ratings. |
| **Sales Orders** | Customer orders with totals and status. |
| **Activity Log** | The device activity history. |
| **Processing Batches** | Batch records (weights are blank for batches made on the Processing page). |
| **Crop Rotation History** | Field crop history by season. |
| **Animal Health Records** | Two files: vaccinations and treatments. |
| **Full Backup** | One JSON file with everything saved on this device, including the device-only pages. |

At the bottom a line says **Last backup:** with the date and time of your last Full Backup, or "Never".

> **Tip:** Press **Download Backup** on the Full Backup card regularly (for example every Friday) and keep the file safe. It is the only protection for the pages that are saved on this device only: Processing, Staff & Labour, Procurement, Animal Health and others. The ledger, stock and sales are already safe in your company account.

> **Note:** A card downloads nothing when its list is empty. In the "All Transactions" file the cycle column may be blank even for entries linked to a cycle.

**If something goes wrong.**
- *A download does not start:* your browser may be blocking it; allow downloads for AGRINUXES. If the list is empty, nothing is downloaded.
- *Numbers differ between the Dashboard and Reports:* the Dashboard is all-time. Reports Overview follows its date range, and the Profitability tab subtracts the device-only production expenses.

---

## How the modules talk to each other

AGRINUXES saves you from typing the same fact twice. Here is the whole picture in plain words.

```
PRODUCTION (Part 3)
  purchase event ............ expense in FINANCE  (+ stock in INVENTORY if "add to inventory" is ticked)
  treatment / vaccination ... expense in FINANCE  (Medicines)
  stocking .................. expense in FINANCE  (Other Cost)
  direct sale event ......... income in FINANCE   (Sales Income)
  harvest sent to Inventory . stock in INVENTORY
  egg / milk / honey ........ stock in INVENTORY  (Table Eggs / Fresh Milk / Honey)

SALES
  order fulfilled ........... income in FINANCE   (the full order total, Sales Income)
                              + stock out of INVENTORY (each line, matched by name)
  payment recorded .......... stays inside the order only (no new income)

PROCESSING
  batch completed ........... stock out of INVENTORY (inputs) and stock in (outputs)
  batch completed ........... stock in INVENTORY (finished goods)
                              + expense in FINANCE (batch cost, Other Cost)

TRADE & EXPORT
  contract / documents ...... nothing is posted to FINANCE or INVENTORY

FINANCE ..................... feeds the DASHBOARD, REPORTS and the CREDIT PROFILE
INVENTORY ................... feeds the DASHBOARD and REPORTS
```

Three things to remember:
1. **Automatic entries use a unique key.** The same event is never posted twice, even if your connection drops and the app tries again.
2. **Items match by name** (ignoring capital letters, spaces and punctuation). A harvest of "Maize" and a sales line "Maize" meet in the same stock item. "Maize" and "Maize grain" do not.
3. **An automatic entry needs permission.** If the login that triggers it may not add to Finance or Inventory, the entry is discarded and not retried.

### Do not enter twice: a checklist

Before you type something into Finance or Inventory, ask: is it already there?

| You did this | It is already recorded, so do NOT also... |
|---|---|
| Logged a **purchase** event with a total cost in production | Add the same cost as an **Expense** in Finance. |
| Ticked **add to inventory** on that purchase | Add a **Stock In** for the same goods in Inventory. |
| Logged a **vaccination** or **treatment** event with a cost | Add a Medicines expense in Finance. |
| Logged a **stocking** event with a cost per fingerling | Nothing. The total is worked out and posted to Finance for you. |
| Logged a **direct sale** event in production | Create a sales order for it, or add Sales Income in Finance. Choose one place. |
| **Fulfilled** a sales order | Add **Sales Income** in Finance, or a **Stock Out** in Inventory for those lines. |
| **Recorded a payment** on an order | Add income in Finance (the income was posted at fulfilment). |
| **Completed** a processing batch | Add the finished goods as **Stock In**, or the batch cost as an expense. |
| Logged an **egg**, **milk** or **honey** event | Add that stock again in Inventory. |
| Sent a **harvest** to Inventory | Add that harvest again as a Stock In. |

Things that are **not** automatic, which you must enter yourself:
- Money received for a sale that is **not** a sales order or a production sale event, grants, and other income.
- Wages, rent, electricity and other overheads (use Finance).
- Using feed or medicine from stock every day (use **Stock Out** in Inventory, or an Adjust after a count).
- Stock coming from outside production (a delivery not logged as a purchase event).
- Honey income, and all income from a Trade & Export contract.
- Raw materials used by a processing batch (see the warning in the Processing chapter).

> **Tip:** If you are not sure whether something was posted, search for it. In Finance open **all** and look for the description or reference. In Inventory open the item's **movements** tab. An automatic entry carries a note such as "Auto-recorded from production engine".

---

## Worked example: a month on a small broiler farm

This is an example with invented numbers for a farm near Chongwe. It shows how one month of activity moves through the pages. It is not a real customer.

**Setup.** Mrs Banda keeps 300 broilers. Her Director login sees everything. Her Finance ledger is empty for the month and she has Inventory items "Broiler Starter Feed" (unit: bag, minimum 10, cost per unit ZMW 420) and "Live broilers" (unit: bird).

**Day 1: buy feed and log it in production.**
1. In **Production**, she opens her broiler cycle and logs a **purchase** event: item "Broiler Starter Feed", quantity 40, unit bag, unit cost 420, total cost 16,800, category "Feed", and ticks **add to inventory**.
2. Result in **Finance**: one expense of ZMW 16,800 in **Feed & Fodder**, linked to the cycle, with the note "Auto-recorded from production engine".
3. Result in **Inventory**: "Broiler Starter Feed" rises by 40 bags, with a movement "Purchase logged in production". She did not type anything in Finance or Inventory.

**Day 1 to 35: other costs and feeding.**
4. She logs a **vaccination** event with a cost of ZMW 1,200: Finance gets a **Medicines** expense of 1,200.
5. She logs a **stocking** event with a total cost of ZMW 5,400 for the day-old chicks: Finance gets an **Other Cost** expense of 5,400.
6. Each week she opens the feed item and records a **Stock Out** of 10 bags with the note "Week 1 feeding", and so on. The stock falls; Inventory shows **Low** when it reaches 10 bags or fewer. The Dashboard **Inventory Items** card shows "1 low stock".

**Day 35: sell the birds.**
7. In **Sales** she clicks **New Order**, clicks **New** to add the customer "Chongwe Butchery", sets the due date 14 days ahead, and enters one line: description "Live broilers", quantity 300, unit bird, price 95. The total is ZMW 28,500. The order is **ORD-202611-001** (or whichever month she dates it), status **Draft**.
8. She clicks **Confirm**, then, when the birds are loaded, **Mark Fulfilled**.
9. Result in **Finance**: an income of ZMW 28,500 in **Sales Income**, dated that day, description "Sales order ORD-... — Chongwe Butchery". Result in **Inventory**: "Live broilers" is reduced by 300 birds (she had added the birds to stock when she sent the harvest to Inventory).

**Day 36 to 45: payments.**
10. The butcher pays ZMW 15,000 in mobile money. She opens the order, clicks **Record Payment**, types 15,000 and the note "Mobile money", and clicks **Record**. The payment status becomes **Partial** and **Outstanding** shows ZMW 13,500.
11. Nine days later, still before the due date, he pays the balance. She clicks **Record Payment** (the amount is pre-filled with 13,500) and **Record**. The status becomes **Paid**. If the due date had passed with the balance still open, the status would have read **Overdue** in red.
12. Nothing new appears in Finance for these payments: the income was posted at fulfilment.

**End of the month: read the picture.**
13. **Finance** (period **Last month** or **Last 3 months**): total income 28,500; total expenses 23,400 (16,800 + 1,200 + 5,400); **Net Profit** 5,100; **Margin** 17.9%. The **Expense Breakdown** shows Feed & Fodder at 72% of costs.
14. **Dashboard**: **Total Revenue** ZMW 28,500, **Total Expenses** ZMW 23,400, **Net Profit** ZMW 5,100 with the word **Surplus**, **Profit Margin** 17.9% (amber, since it is below 20%). The **Revenue vs Expenses** chart shows this month's bars, and **This Month Profit** shows 5,100.
15. **Credit Profile** (refresh it): because there are expenses in the ledger, the app judges profitability by month. This one month is profitable, so the profit factor gains points: about 0.36 of the return-on-cost part (return on cost is 22%), all of the profitable-months part, and the neutral half of the trend part, roughly 14 to 15 of the 25 points. The "thin file" note disappears because there is something to score, though the band stays low until she has more months of production records, more fulfilled contracts and the other items in the verification factor. If she keeps this up month after month, her record-keeping and tenure points grow too.

**What she did not do:** she did not type the feed purchase in Finance as well as in production, did not add the sale in Finance after fulfilling the order, and did not add the 300 birds out of Inventory by hand.

# Part 5 — Farm Management, Intelligence and Devices

## How to read this Part

This Part covers three groups of pages in the farm workspace: the **Farm Management** section, the **AI & Intelligence** section (which also holds the device and sensor pages) and the **Marketplaces** section as a farm user sees it.

These pages are different from the ones in Parts 3 and 4. Production, Inventory, Sales and Finance are saved to your company account. Almost every page in this Part is **saved on this device only**. Please read the next table before you rely on any page.

> **Watch out:** Many pages in this Part show **sample data** the first time you open them (sample staff, sample ponds, sample alerts, sample providers). The sample data is there so that you can see how the page works. It is not your data. Delete it or ignore it, and do not make decisions from it.

### What "saved on this device only" means

- The records are kept inside the web browser you are using, on that one phone or computer.
- Other people in your company do **not** see them. A second phone, or the same phone with a different browser, will show a blank or sample page.
- If you clear the browser's data, or reinstall the browser, the records are lost.
- They are **not** backed up automatically. Use **Backup & Restore** (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account) to download a backup file and to restore it on another device.

### Where each page keeps its records

| Page | Saved where | Real data or sample data? |
|---|---|---|
| Notifications | This device (reads the alert lists of the device pages) | Shows only what the device pages have saved |
| Animal Health | This device only | Records you type; sample records on first open |
| Staff & Labour | This device only | Records you type; 3 sample staff on first open |
| Procurement | This device only | Records you type; sample suppliers and orders on first open |
| Requisitions | This device only | Records you type; 4 sample requests on first open |
| Activity Log | This device only | **Sample entries only** (nothing writes real entries) |
| Crop Rotation | This device only | Records you type; sample fields and history on first open |
| Smart Engine | Reads company Finance, Inventory and Production, plus device pages | Calculated from your data and fixed norms |
| AI Insights | Reads company Production | Rules and fixed reference figures, not a learning model |
| Financial AI | Reads company Finance; the Loans tab is this device only | Real Finance figures; sample loans on first open |
| Smart Vision | This device only | **Simulated** (random sample answers) |
| Weather | **Company account** | **Live** forecast and satellite data |
| Sustainability | This device only | Records you type; sample carbon and water rows |
| Smart Devices, Water Mgmt, Aquaculture, Poultry Houses, Energy, Soil Depth | This device only | Sample devices; readings are **simulated** |
| Automation | This device only | You press **Run** yourself; nothing runs automatically |
| Device Gateway, Farm Gateway | This device only | Demonstration screens |
| Cold Chain | This device only | Part manual entry, part sample |
| AgriFood Market, AgriSupply, AgriServices | This device only | Sample listings plus your own listings |
| Agro Dealers, Equipment Hire, Vet Services | Live provider list from the server when available; your own listing is this device only | Sample providers when the server cannot be reached |

### Who sees these pages
