Each page appears in the left menu only if your account may use it. The owner and company administrators see everything. Other team members see a page only when their permissions include the matching area:

| Pages | Permission area needed |
|---|---|
| Animal Health | Biosecurity & Vet |
| Staff & Labour | Human Resources |
| Procurement, Requisitions | Procurement |
| Crop Rotation | Daily Planner |
| Smart Engine, AI Insights | Smart AI Engine |
| Financial AI | Financial Management |
| Smart Vision | Production |
| Sustainability | Reports & BI |
| Weather | Weather |
| Smart Devices, Water Mgmt, Aquaculture, Poultry Houses, Energy, Soil Depth, Automation, Device Gateway, Cold Chain, Farm Gateway | Devices |
| AgriFood Market, AgriSupply, AgriServices, Agro Dealers, Equipment Hire, Vet Services | Marketplace |
| Notifications, Activity Log | Everyone with the farm workspace |

See Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account for how the owner gives permissions to team members.

---

## Notifications

### Notifications Centre

**What it is for.** One list of the alerts raised by your device pages (Aquaculture, Water, Poultry, Energy, Soil and Devices). You can filter the list and mark alerts as resolved. The page is called **Notifications Centre** and its subtitle says "Aggregated alerts from all IoT modules".

**Before you start.** The Notifications Centre does not create alerts. It only collects the alerts that the device pages have already saved on this device. If you have never used the device pages, the list is empty. Cold Chain, Weather and Smart Devices alerts are not collected here.

**Step by step**

1. In the left menu, under **Farm Management**, click **Notifications**. You can also click the bell icon at the top of the screen.
2. Look at the four tiles at the top: **Critical**, **Warning**, **Info** and **Resolved today**. Click a tile to filter the list to that kind of alert.
3. To narrow the list yourself, use the severity buttons (**all**, **critical**, **warning**, **info**), the module drop-down (**All modules**, **Aquaculture**, **Water**, **Poultry**, **Energy**, **Soil**, **Devices**) and the status drop-down (**All statuses**, **Unresolved**, **Resolved**).
4. When you have dealt with a problem, click **Resolve** on its card. The card turns pale and shows a **Resolved** badge.
5. Click **Refresh** to read the saved alerts again (for example after you resolved something on a device page).
6. Click **Clear Resolved** to delete every resolved alert permanently. This button appears only when at least one alert is resolved.

**What you will see**

| Item | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Source badge | Which device page raised the alert (for example Aquaculture) |
| Severity badge | CRITICAL (red), WARNING (amber) or INFO (blue) |
| Title and detail line | What went wrong and the reading or message |
| Time | How long ago (just now, 5m ago, 3h ago, 2d ago) |
| Footer | "Showing X of Y total alerts" |

**Good to know**

- The page opens with the **Unresolved** filter on, so you see only open alerts.
- **Resolved today** counts resolved alerts whose own time stamp is today. It is not the time you clicked **Resolve**.
- The red number next to **Notifications** in the left menu counts all unresolved alerts. It is refreshed when you click **Notifications**.
- Resolving an alert here also marks it as resolved on the device page it came from, and the other way round (press **Refresh**).
- Some titles read in a general way, such as "Alert on tank", because the device pages store their alerts with slightly different details. The meaning is still visible in the detail line.
- Sample alerts shown on a device page reach the Notifications Centre only after that page has saved its data on this device, for example after you resolve an alert or change something there.

**If something goes wrong**

- "All clear — no active alerts" with "No alerts match the current filters." means nothing matches. Set the status filter to **All statuses** and the module filter to **All modules**.
- If an alert you expected is missing, open the device page it belongs to, then come back and press **Refresh**.

---

## Animal Health

### Animal Health Records

**What it is for.** A simple health diary for your animals. You record vaccinations, treatments (medicines given) and visits by a vet. It also warns you when a vaccination is overdue and when produce must not yet be sold because of a medicine **withdrawal period** (the days after a medicine during which meat, milk or eggs are not safe to sell).

**Before you start.** The records are tied to an **enterprise** (one of the farm activities you set up, such as Broilers or Tilapia). If you have no enterprise yet, a yellow note says "No enterprises configured yet" and you cannot link records. Create enterprises first (see Part 3 — Running Production: Cycles, Stages, Events and Outputs).

> **Watch out:** Animal Health is **saved on this device only**. It is **not** connected to the care guide, to production events, to Finance or to Inventory. A vaccination or treatment you type here does not tick a care-guide step and does not post a cost. If you also want the cost in Finance, record the event in Production as well (see Part 3).

**Step by step: add a vaccination**

1. Click **Animal Health** under **Farm Management**, then open the **Vaccinations** tab.
2. Click **Add Vaccination**.
3. Fill in the form (the fields are listed below).
4. Click **Save**. The record appears at the top of the table, newest first.

**Vaccination fields**

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Enterprise | The farm activity the animals belong to |
| Vaccine Name* | For example "Newcastle La Sota" |
| Batch Number | The batch number printed on the vial |
| Head Count | How many animals were vaccinated |
| Date Given* | Date of the vaccination |
| Next Due Date | When the booster is due; this drives the overdue warning |
| Given By | Name of the vet or staff member |
| Notes | Anything else, such as "Drinking water administration" |

**Step by step: add a treatment**

1. Open the **Treatments** tab and click **Add Treatment**.
2. Fill in the form and click **Save**.

**Treatment fields**

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Enterprise | The farm activity |
| Condition / Disease* | The illness, for example "E. coli / Colibacillosis" |
| Drug / Product | Medicine name |
| Dose | For example "1 ml / L" |
| Route | **Oral**, **Injection**, **Topical** or **Water** |
| Head Count | Number of animals treated |
| Start Date* | First day of treatment |
| End Date | Last day of treatment. **Always fill this in when you enter a withdrawal period.** |
| Withdrawal Period (days) | Days to wait after the End Date before selling or slaughtering |
| Treated By | Vet or staff name |
| Notes | Anything else |

When you type a withdrawal period above 0, an amber box appears: "Do not slaughter or sell produce from treated animals until the withdrawal period has elapsed after the end date."

**Step by step: add a vet visit**

1. Open the **Vet Visits** tab and click **Add Vet Visit**.
2. Fill in the form and click **Save**.

**Vet visit fields**

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Enterprise | The farm activity |
| Vet Name* | Name of the vet |
| Clinic / Practice | The vet's practice |
| Visit Date* | Date of the visit |
| Follow-up Date | When the vet will return |
| Purpose of Visit | For example "Routine check" |
| Findings | What the vet found |
| Recommendations | What the vet advised |
| Cost (ZMW) | The fee. The table shows it with a "K" in front of the number. |

**What you will see**

The **Overview** tab shows four tiles, a withdrawal warning and an upcoming-vaccination table.

| Overview item | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Total Vaccinations | Number of vaccination records |
| Active Treatments | Treatments whose End Date is today or later |
| Next Vaccination Due | The earliest Next Due Date in your records, with the vaccine name |
| Total Vet Visits | Number of vet visit records |
| Withdrawal period active | An amber banner for each treatment where today is still before the End Date plus the withdrawal days. It names the date from which you may sell. |
| Upcoming Vaccinations | The five earliest due dates. Status shows **Overdue** (red), **Due in Nd** (amber, 7 days or fewer) or **In Nd** (green) |

In the **Vaccinations** table a red **Overdue** label appears next to a Next Due Date that has already passed.

**Good to know**

- The three required fields are marked with a star. If one is empty, **Save** simply does nothing and no message appears. Check the star fields first.
- On the very first visit, if you already have an enterprise and all three lists are empty, the page adds **sample records** (two vaccinations, one treatment and one vet visit) to your first enterprise. Delete is not available, so ignore them or restore a clean backup.
- You cannot edit or delete a record once saved. Check your entries before you click **Save**.
- Records are saved on this device only. Make a backup with **Backup & Restore**.

**If something goes wrong**

- Nothing happens when you click **Save**: a star field is empty. Fill in the vaccine/condition/vet name and the date.
- The Overview page goes blank after you saved a treatment with a withdrawal period but no End Date. Avoid this by always filling in the End Date when a withdrawal period is entered.

---

## Staff & Labour

### People & Access

**What it is for.** A staff list for the farm: names, roles, contact details, which enterprises each person works on, and a list of ticked permissions per person.

> **Watch out:** This page is **saved on this device only**. It is a notebook, not a login system. Adding a person here does **not** give them a login, and ticking a permission here does **not** change what they can open in the app. To give someone a real login with real permissions, use **Team & Permissions** (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account). This page has no time logs, no attendance, no approvals and no payroll.

**Step by step: add an employee**

1. Click **Staff & Labour** under **Farm Management**. The **Directory** tab opens under the heading **People & Access**.
2. Click **Add Employee**.
3. Fill in the form and click **Add Employee**.

**Employee fields**

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Full Name* | The only required field |
| Role | farmhand, supervisor, driver, vet officer, accountant, manager, farm admin, owner. Choosing a role fills in that role's usual permissions |
| Email, Phone | Contact details |
| Hired Date | Date the person started |
| Enterprise Access | Tick the enterprises the person works on. "none = all". Shown only if you have enterprises |
| Active employee | Untick for someone who has left |

**Other buttons on a staff card**

- The green switch at the top right of a card makes the person **Active** or inactive. Inactive people show a red **Inactive** label.
- **Edit** reopens the form (**Save Changes**).
- **Permissions** opens the second tab for that person.
- The bin icon asks "Remove NAME?" with **Yes** and **No**.

**Roles & Permissions tab**

1. Click a name in the left list. The right panel shows the person's permissions in four groups.
2. Click the green tick or red cross next to a permission to switch it on or off. The change is saved at once.
3. Click **Reset to role defaults** to go back to the role's standard list.
4. The **Save Permissions** button only shows the message "Permissions saved". The changes were already stored when you clicked each permission.

| Group | Permissions in the list |
|---|---|
| Operations | View Dashboard, Manage Production, Manage Inventory, Manage Sales, Manage Procurement, Animal Health |
| Finance | View Finance, View Reports, Log Production Costs |
| IoT & Data | IoT & Sensors, Activity Log, Marketplace |
| Administration | Manage Staff, Farm Settings, System Administrator ("Grants full access to all modules") |

**What you will see.** The top of the Directory shows the count of employees, how many are active and how many different roles are in use.

**Good to know**

- The first time you open the page, three sample staff appear: Alice Banda (farmhand), Bob Mwale (supervisor) and Carol Phiri (manager). Edit or remove them.
- The role names also appear in the Requisitions and Procurement pages only as free text. They are not linked.

**If something goes wrong**

- Clicking **Add Employee** does nothing: the Full Name is empty.

---

## Procurement

### Procurement

**What it is for.** To record what you order from suppliers: purchase orders (POs), their delivery and payment status, and a small supplier list with star ratings. The page header says "Supplier orders, quotes, and delivery tracking".

> **Watch out:** Procurement is **saved on this device only**. Marking an order **Delivered** does **not** add stock to Inventory and does **not** post a cost to Finance. Record those in Inventory and Finance yourself (see Part 4 — Running the Farm Business: Inventory, Sales, Finance, Credit and Reports).

**Before you start.** Add at least one supplier before you create an order, because an order needs a supplier.

**Step by step: add a supplier**

1. Click **Procurement** under **Farm Management**, then the **Suppliers** tab.
2. Click **Add Supplier**.
3. Fill in the form and click **Save**.

| Supplier field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Name* | Supplier or shop name |
| Contact Person, Phone, Email | How to reach them |
| Category | Feed, Vet Supplies, Equipment, Seeds, Chemicals or Other |
| Rating | 1 to 5 stars (default 3) |
| Notes | For example "Bulk discounts available" |

**Step by step: raise an order**

1. Open the **Orders** tab and click **Add Order**. The window is called "New Purchase Order".
2. Choose the **Supplier** and (optionally) the **Enterprise**.
3. Choose the **Expected Delivery** date.
4. Under **Line Items**, fill **Item**, **Unit**, **Qty** and **Price** for each line. Click **Add Line** for more lines, or the X to remove a line. The **Total** updates as you type.
5. Add **Notes** if you wish, then click **Save**.

The order is created with the status **Draft** and payment **Unpaid**. The order number looks like PO-2026-1234 (the year and four digits made from the clock).

**Working with orders**

- Click a row to expand it and see the line items and notes.
- Use the search box (PO number or supplier) and the two filters (status and payment).
- **Mark Delivered** appears only on orders whose status is **Sent** or **Confirmed**. It sets the status to **Delivered** and records today's date.
- **Mark Paid** appears on any order that is not paid and not cancelled. It sets payment to **Paid** for the full total.

**What you will see**

| Item | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Statuses | Draft, Sent, Confirmed, Delivered, Cancelled |
| Payment | Unpaid, Partial, Paid |
| Balance Due | Total minus amount paid (red when above zero) |
| Overview tiles | Total Orders, Pending Delivery (Sent or Confirmed), Overdue (expected date passed), Spend This Month (all non-cancelled orders dated this month) |
| Upcoming Deliveries (Next 7 Days) | Sent or Confirmed orders due in the coming week |

Amounts are shown with a "K" (kwacha) and two decimals.

**Good to know**

- In this version there is **no button to move a Draft order to Sent or Confirmed**, no button to cancel, and no way to edit an order or a supplier after saving. Those statuses exist only in the sample orders.
- A **Partial** payment cannot be entered. Only the sample order shows it.
- The first time you open the page, three sample suppliers (FeedCo Ltd, AgroVet Supplies, SeedMasters Zambia) and four sample orders appear.
- A new order cannot be saved unless it has a supplier, an expected delivery date and at least one line with an item name. If one is missing, **Save** does nothing.

**If something goes wrong**

- "No orders match your filters." Clear the search box and set both filters to "All".

---

## Requisitions

### Requisitions

**What it is for.** An internal request-and-approve form: a worker asks for stock (feed, medicine, packaging and so on), a supervisor approves or rejects it, and someone marks it fulfilled when the goods arrive. It is the step before a purchase order.

> **Watch out:** Requisitions are **saved on this device only**. Names are typed by hand ("Requested By", "Reviewed by"). The page does not check who you are, so anyone using this device can approve any request.

**Step by step: make a request**

1. Click **Requisitions** under **Farm Management**.
2. Click **New Request**. The window is called "New Stock Request".
3. Fill in the form (below).
4. Click **Save as Draft** to keep it for later, or **Submit Now** to send it for approval. Both buttons stay grey until **Requested By** and every line's **Item name** are filled in.

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Requested By* | The employee's name |
| Enterprise | The farm activity the stock is for |
| Priority | Low, Normal or Urgent |
| Category | Feed, Medicine, Equipment, Seeds, Fingerlings, Packaging or Other |
| Justification | Why the stock is needed |
| Line Items* | Item name*, Description, Qty, Unit, Cost/unit (an estimate) for each line. **Add Line** adds a line; the X removes one (the last line cannot be removed) |

The footer shows "Est. Total: ZMW ..." as you type. Request numbers look like REQ-2026-005 and count upwards.

**Approving a request**

1. Open the **Pending Review** tab (requests with the status **Submitted**).
2. Click the arrow on a request card to expand it, then click **Approve** or **Reject**.
3. A **Review Decision** box opens. Type **Reviewed by** (your name) and an optional **Note**.
4. Click **Confirm Approve** or **Confirm Reject** (or **Cancel** to close the box).
5. For an approved request, click **Mark as Fulfilled** when the goods have arrived, or **Cancel** to withdraw it.

**What the buttons do, by status**

| Status | Buttons shown |
|---|---|
| Draft | **Submit for Approval**, **Cancel** |
| Submitted | **Approve**, **Reject** |
| Approved | **Mark as Fulfilled**, **Cancel** |
| Rejected, Fulfilled, Cancelled | None (these are final) |

**What you will see**

- Tabs: **All**, **Pending Review**, **Approved**, **History** (fulfilled, rejected and cancelled requests).
- On the **All** tab you can filter by status and category and click **Export CSV**.
- Header chips: total requests, how many are submitted, how many are approved, and "pending fulfillment" (the same number as approved).
- Each card shows the number, priority, category, status, date, who asked, the enterprise, the justification, the number of items and the estimated total in ZMW. Expanded, it shows the item lines, the reviewer's name and note and the fulfilled date.

**Good to know**

- Four sample requests appear on first open. One has a "linked PO" label. You cannot link a request to a purchase order in this version.
- Fulfilling a request does not add stock to Inventory and does not create a purchase order. Do that by hand.
- The **Export CSV** button may not produce a usable file in this version. If the download looks wrong, copy the list from the screen instead.

**If something goes wrong**

- "No requisitions found": change the tab or clear the filters.
- **Submit Now** is grey: a name or item name is missing.

---

## Activity Log

### Activity Log

**What it is for.** The page is built to show a time-ordered list of what happened in the farm app, with a search, filters and a CSV download.

> **Watch out:** In this version of the app **nothing writes real entries into the Activity Log**. The entries you see are **15 sample entries** that the page creates the first time you open it (for example "Recorded income: Feed Sales — ZMW 4,200"). They describe events that did not happen on your farm. Do not use this page as an audit trail. The Dashboard's recent-activity list reads the same sample entries.

**Step by step**

1. Click **Activity Log** under **Farm Management**.
2. Use the search box ("Search events..."), the module drop-down, the action drop-down (create, update, delete, login, export, system) and the date picker to filter. Click **Clear filters** to reset.
3. Click **Refresh** to read the list again.
4. Click **Export CSV** to download the filtered entries as a spreadsheet file.
5. Click **Clear** to delete every entry. The app asks "Clear all activity log entries? This cannot be undone." Confirm only if you are sure.

**What you will see**

- Four tiles: **Total entries**, **Today**, **Creates**, **System events**.
- Each entry shows a coloured module badge, an action badge (Created, Updated, Deleted, Login, Exported, System), a subject, a description, and how long ago it happened.
- The list keeps the newest 500 entries only.
- Footer: "Showing X of Y entries". If the filters match nothing: "No entries match the current filters."

**Good to know**

- After you click **Clear**, the list is empty. When you leave the page and open it again, the 15 sample entries are put back, because the page refills an empty log.
- For a real record of who did what with money and stock, use the dates and notes in **Finance**, **Inventory** and **Sales** (see Part 4).

---

## Crop Rotation

### Crop Rotation Planner

**What it is for.** To remember what you grew in each field in each season, and to get a simple warning and a suggestion about what to plant next. Rotating crops (not planting the same kind of crop in the same field season after season) lowers disease and pest build-up and keeps the soil in better condition.

> **Watch out:** Crop Rotation is **saved on this device only**. Its fields are **not** the same as the fields on the Weather page, and its season records are not linked to Production cycles.

**Step by step: add a field**

1. Click **Crop Rotation** under **Farm Management**.
2. Click **Add Field** at the top right.
3. Fill in **Field Name**, **Area (ha)**, **Soil Type** and **Location**. Only the name is required.
4. Click **Add Field**.

**Step by step: log a season**

1. Click **Log Season**.
2. Choose the **Field** from the list. Fill in **Crop**, **Season** (for example "2026-A" for the first rains season and "2026-B" for the second), **Start Date**, **End Date** and **Yield (kg/ha)**.
3. Choose the **Crop Family**: legume, cereal, brassica, solanaceae, cucurbit, root, leafy or fallow.
4. Click **Save Record**. Field, Crop and Season are required; if one is empty nothing happens.

> **Tip:** Families in plain words. Legume: beans, soybeans, groundnuts, cowpeas. Cereal: maize, sorghum, wheat. Brassica: cabbage, kale, rape, broccoli. Solanaceae: tomatoes, peppers, potatoes. Cucurbit: pumpkin, butternut, cucumber. Root: carrots, beetroot, sweet potato. Leafy: spinach, lettuce. Fallow: resting the land or a cover crop.

**The four tabs**

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Overview | Three tiles (Registered Fields, Season Records, Rotation Warnings), an amber warning card for each field with a problem, and for every field a "recommended crops" list with a tip |
| Fields | One card per field with area, soil, location, the latest crop and its last six seasons. The small x removes the field |
| History | A table of all season records: Field, Season, Crop, Family, Yield (kg/ha), Notes. The x at the end removes a record |
| Planner | A timeline per field, one block per season, ending with a dashed "Plan" block for the next season |

**How the warning and the suggestions are worked out**

- The page looks at the two most recent seasons of a field (by start date).
- It warns when the newest crop's family should not follow the previous family: cereal after cereal, solanaceae after solanaceae or brassica, brassica after brassica or solanaceae, root after root, cucurbit after cucurbit, or leafy after leafy. The message reads, for example, "Maize follows Maize — same family (cereal). Disease and pest pressure risk is elevated."
- Suggestions are the families that are neither the last family nor one of its "bad followers". Each is shown with example crops. The Overview shows up to four.
- Under the suggestions a tip explains the last crop, for example: legume "Excellent nitrogen fixer — great before cereals"; fallow "Soil rest improves structure; weed suppression"; cereal "Light residue; follow with legume to restore N".

**Good to know**

- The rotation is matched by **field name**. If you add a new record, pick the field from the list so the name is exactly the same.
- Removing a field does not remove its season records, and the x buttons delete at once without asking.
- The first time you open the page, three sample fields (Field A — North, Field B — South, Field C — East) and nine sample season records for 2023 and 2024 are added. Remove them if you do not want them.
- There is no box for notes when logging a season, although the History table has a Notes column.

**If something goes wrong**

- "No fields registered yet. Add your first field." Click **Add Field**.
- "No season records yet. Log a season to start tracking." Click **Log Season**.

---

## Smart Engine

### Smart AI Operations Engine

**What it is for.** A daily helper page. It reads your active production cycles, your Finance entries, your Inventory and some device pages, and turns them into (1) up to six plain-language insights and (2) a feed and water plan for today.

> **Note:** "Smart" here means **fixed rules and fixed feeding norms**, not a program that learns from your farm. The same inputs always give the same answers.

**Before you start.** The feeding plan needs at least one **active** production cycle (see Part 3 — Running Production: Cycles, Stages, Events and Outputs). Insights from Finance and Inventory need entries in those modules.

**Step by step**

1. Click **Smart Engine** under **AI & Intelligence**.
2. Read the four tiles at the top: **Today's Total Feed Need** (kg), **Today's Total Water** (litres), **AI Insights** (how many cards) and **Active Cycles**. A dash means the number cannot be worked out yet.
3. Read the **AI-Generated Insights** cards. Click **Refresh** to recalculate.
4. Scroll to **Per-Cycle Smart Recommendations (Today)** for the feed and water plan.
5. Look at the three cards at the bottom: **Feed Forecast — Next 7 Days**, **Active Cycles at a Glance** and **Tip of the Day**.

**What each insight rule looks for.** The cards appear in this order, and the page shows at most six.

| Rule | It appears when | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| Low DO in a pond — aerator off | A pond on the Aquaculture page has dissolved oxygen below 5 mg/L and its aerator is off | Critical |
| Temperature alert in a poultry house | A house temperature is below 18 or above 30 degrees C | Warning |
| Inventory below reorder point | One or more stock items have a quantity at or below their minimum stock level. Up to three names are listed | Warning |
| This month's income and expenses | There is at least one Finance entry dated this month. Shows income, expenses and net profit or loss | Information |
| Battery critically low | A battery bank is below 20 percent charge | Critical |
| Soil moisture critical | A soil zone on the Soil Depth page has the status Critical | Critical |
| All systems normal | Fewer than three cards were produced. This filler card says "All systems normal — no issues detected" | Information |

> **Watch out:** The pond, house, battery and soil rules read the **device pages**, which hold sample or simulated values (see "Understanding devices, sensors and readings" later in this Part). Also, as the app is built today, the poultry-temperature and battery rules look for values under names that the Poultry Houses and Energy pages do not use, so those two cards are unlikely to appear. The Inventory and Finance cards use your real company data.

**The daily feed and water plan.** For every active cycle the table shows the enterprise and day number, the stage, the feed type, feed in kg, water in litres and a note. The numbers are the number of animals (the total quantity in the cycle's production units; 100 if none is recorded) multiplied by a fixed daily norm for the enterprise category:

| Category | Feed per animal per day | Water per animal per day | Feed type shown |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poultry | 120 g | 250 ml | Broiler Grower |
| Livestock | 8 kg | 30 litres | TMR / Silage Mix |
| Aquaculture | 30 g | none | Floating Pellets |
| Crops | none | 2 litres per unit | not applicable |
| Horticulture | none | 1.5 litres per unit | Fertigation |
| Greenhouse | none | 1.2 litres per unit | Drip fertigation |
| Processing, Services | none | none | not applicable |

Enterprise types that are not in this list use the Crops norm.

**Good to know**

- The norms are the same for every age and stage. A broiler chick and a 5-week bird both get 120 g. Treat the plan as a rough guide and follow the care guide of your template for real feeding rates (see Part 2 — Templates, Record Fields and Care Guides).
- **Feed Forecast — Next 7 Days** is today's feed need times 7 plus 5 percent.
- **Tip of the Day** is the same sentence every day.

**If something goes wrong**

- "No active production cycles. Start a cycle in the Production module to see daily recommendations." Start a cycle first.

---

## AI Insights

### Predictive AI & Intelligence

**What it is for.** A set of five tabs that mix some live information from your cycles with fixed advice and reference figures: **Disease Alerts**, **Yield Forecast**, **FCR Optimizer**, **Market Demand** and **Crop Calendar**.

> **Watch out:** The subtitle talks about "AI models" and the corner badge says "AI models updated 2h ago". The badge is a fixed label. The page uses simple rules, not a trained model. The Market Demand prices are fixed reference figures and are **not** live market prices.

**Step by step**

1. Click **AI Insights** under **AI & Intelligence**.
2. Click a tab at the top.
3. If a high-risk disease alert exists, a red **High-Priority Disease Alert** banner appears above the tabs (the standard alerts below never reach "high" on their own).

**The five tabs**

| Tab | What it shows | Where it comes from |
|---|---|---|
| Disease Alerts | Cards with a title, a risk (Low, Medium, High), a "days horizon", an explanation, a **Recommended action** and a "Model confidence" bar | Two general advisories always appear ("Biosecurity — General Advisory", "Seasonal Disease Watch"). Poultry cycles older than 14 days add a "Newcastle Disease Watch" (Low up to day 28, then Medium). Aquaculture cycles add a "Low DO Alert" (Medium) |
| Yield Forecast | One card per active cycle: the current stage, "units stocked", "Day N" and the days left to the stage's target end date | Your production cycles. It is a progress card, not a harvest prediction |
| FCR Optimizer | One card per active poultry cycle with Actual FCR, Target FCR (1.65) and "Pending" | Your cycles. Actual FCR always shows a dash, with the note "FCR not yet measured — record feed and weight data in inventory to enable AI FCR tracking." |
| Market Demand | Four commodities with a demand badge, a price in ZMW and a trend arrow | Fixed list: live broilers, Grade A tomato, lettuce head, bulk maize |
| Crop Calendar | Dates from your cycles, then six suggested activities | Stages not yet completed whose target end date is within 60 days (or up to 3 days late), labelled overdue, upcoming (within 7 days) or planned. The six suggested chores (check water lines, biosecurity walk-through, review feed stock, check growth, sample soil or water, mid-cycle reconciliation) are generic, dated 3, 7, 10, 14, 21 and 30 days from today |

> **Tip:** **FCR** (feed conversion ratio) is the kilograms of feed a flock eats for each kilogram of weight it gains. A lower number is better. A broiler target near 1.65 is a good one.

**If something goes wrong**

- "No active production cycles. Start a cycle to see yield forecasts." and "No active poultry cycles. FCR tracking requires an active poultry enterprise." Start a cycle of the right type.

---

## Financial AI

### Financial AI & P&L

**What it is for.** To see profit and loss for each production cycle, a breakdown of costs, a break-even calculator and a loan tracker. The page title is **Financial AI & P&L**.

**Before you start.** The first three tabs read your **Finance** entries (see Part 4 — Running the Farm Business: Inventory, Sales, Finance, Credit and Reports). For the cycle figures to work, each Finance entry must be linked to a cycle: in Finance, choose the cycle in the **Cycle** field when you add the entry. Entries with no cycle count in the totals at the top but in no cycle.

**The four tiles at the top**

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Total Revenue | All income entries in Finance (fulfilled sales orders are already in the ledger) |
| Total Costs | All expense entries |
| Net Profit | Income minus expenses. The amount is shown without a minus sign; a red icon means a loss |
| Active Cycles | Cycles with the status active |

A dash appears when Finance has no entries.

**P&L tab.** Pick a cycle in the **Cycle** list (or click a row in **All Cycles**). The page shows **Revenue**, **Costs** and **Profit** for that cycle, plus **Profit Margin** (profit divided by revenue) and **ROI** (profit divided by cost). If nothing is linked to the cycle, it says "No transactions tagged to this cycle yet. Record income/expenses in Finance and link them to this cycle."

**Costs tab.** A bar for every expense category with its amount and its percentage of all costs, biggest first, and the **Total**. Empty: "Record expenses in the Finance engine to see your cost breakdown here."

**Break-Even tab.** A calculator. Type four numbers (the starting numbers are only examples):

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Fixed Costs | Costs you pay whatever you sell (rent, wages, loan instalments) |
| Variable Cost / Unit | Cost of each unit sold (feed, seed, packaging) |
| Selling Price / Unit | Price you get for each unit |
| Expected Units to Sell | How many units you plan to sell |

The page shows **Contribution Margin** (price minus variable cost), **Break-Even Units** (fixed costs divided by contribution margin), **Break-Even Revenue**, **Margin of Safety** (how far your expected sales are above break-even, in units and percent) and **Projected Profit**. If the price is not higher than the variable cost you see "Selling price must be greater than variable cost."

> **Example:** Fixed costs ZMW 48,000, cost per bird ZMW 42, price ZMW 75. Each bird leaves ZMW 33. You need 48,000 divided by 33, about 1,455 birds, to break even.

**Loans tab.** A record of the loans you have taken. It is **saved on this device only** and does not post to Finance or to your Credit Profile.

1. Click **Add Loan**.
2. Fill in **Lender**, **Purpose**, **Principal**, **Interest Rate (% pa)**, **Term (months)** and **Start Date**, then click **Add Loan**. Lender and principal are required.
3. Each month you pay, click **Record Payment** on the loan card. It adds one paid month. When all months are paid the loan becomes **Paid Off**.

The tiles show **Total Borrowed**, **Outstanding Balance**, **Monthly Obligations** and **Avg Interest Rate**. The monthly payment is worked out as an ordinary equal-instalment loan with the interest on the reducing balance. The status **Defaulted** exists but cannot be set from the screen.

**Good to know**

- On the first visit two sample loans appear (ZANACO, ZMW 50,000 at 18 percent; SANLAM, ZMW 20,000 at 22 percent). Replace them with your own.
- A recorded payment cannot be undone. Click **Record Payment** only after you have paid.

---

## Smart Vision

### Smart Vision & AI Intelligence

**What it is for.** Four tabs about cameras and images: **Camera Watch**, **Drone Intel**, **AI Vision** and **Community Intel**.

> **Watch out:** **Smart Vision is a demonstration. It does not analyse real pictures or video.**
>
> - It needs **no key and no account** with any image service, because it does not use one.
> - On **AI Vision**, you type the web address of a photo, but the photo is never opened or looked at. The page waits about two seconds and then picks one of a few ready-written answers for the type of scan you chose. The "confidence" percentage (between 72 and 93) is a random number.
> - The camera pictures on **Camera Watch** are placeholders. No video is connected.
> - The drone results are random numbers.
>
> Do not use these answers to treat sick animals or crops. For a real diagnosis use **Vet Services** (see the Marketplaces chapters below) or your extension officer (see Part 8 — Buyers, Government, Extension Officers and Programme Partners).

All four tabs are **saved on this device only**.

### Camera Watch

1. Click **Smart Vision** under **AI & Intelligence**; **Camera Watch** is the first tab.
2. Any unresolved alert is shown in a red box with a **Resolve** button. Three sample alerts appear at first (a count difference in a broiler house, unusual clustering, and an offline gate camera).
3. Click **Add Camera** and fill in **Camera Name \***, **Enterprise**, **Resolution** (720p, 1080p, 4K), **Location / Mount Point** and the **Enable AI analysis** box. A new camera is always shown as **Offline**, and there is no way to change that status.
4. Click the bin on a camera card to remove it (the app asks to confirm).
5. To compare a count with your records, click **Log Count**, choose the **Camera** (or "Manual count"), the **Enterprise \***, the **Counted Qty \*** and optional **Notes**, then **Submit Count**.

The expected quantity is the total quantity in the production units of the **active cycle** of that enterprise. If there is no active cycle, nothing is compared. The count is **ok** when it differs from the expected number by less than 1 percent; otherwise it is marked **discrepancy** (red). **Recent Stock Counts** lists the counts, newest first.

### Drone Intel

1. Click **Schedule Flight**, choose the **Enterprise**, the **Flight Type** (Survey, Spray, Inspection, Livestock Count) and **Notes**, then **Create Flight**. The flight is **planned**.
2. Click **Start** to set it **in flight**, then **Complete + AI Analyse**. The page invents an area covered (1 to 6 ha), a health score (one of 45, 61, 72, 78, 85, 90), a rating (poor, fair, good or excellent) and a short list of issues.

### AI Vision

1. Pick one of eight scan types: **Crop Disease**, **Pest ID**, **Livestock Health**, **Animal Count**, **Field Survey**, **Weed Detection**, **Soil Assessment**, **General Scan**.
2. Type the **Image URL \*** (it must be a web address). The hint suggests uploading the photo to a photo-sharing site first.
3. Optionally describe what you see, choose an **Enterprise** and tick **Share with community**. The description is not used and the share box does not publish anything.
4. Click **Run AI Analysis**. The result shows a diagnosis, severity (none, low, medium, high, critical), findings and numbered recommended actions. Past results are listed under **Recent Analyses**; click one to reopen it.

### Community Intel

A board of field reports. Click **Submit Report** and fill in **Title \***, **Category** (Crop Disease, Pest/Insect, Animal Disease, Nutritional, Yield Problem, Weed, Soil Issue, Best Practice, Other), **Crop / Animal**, **Location Hint** and **Description**. Use the category filter to narrow the list. The thumbs-up button adds one to the count each time it is clicked.

> **Note:** Although the board is described as cross-farm, the reports are kept on **this device only**. Other farms do not see what you post, and you do not see theirs. The two reports you see at first (a Newcastle-like outbreak in Lusaka, tomato spots in Chongwe) are samples.

---

## Weather

### Weather & Field Intelligence

**What it is for.** A live 7-day forecast, today's conditions, farm advice for each day, and satellite plant-health readings for your fields. Unlike most pages in this Part, **Weather uses real, live data and is saved to the company account**, so everyone in your company sees the same stations and fields on any device.

**Where the data comes from**

- Forecasts and current conditions: Open-Meteo, a free weather service, for the exact latitude and longitude of each of your weather stations.
- Plant health (NDVI): NASA MODIS satellite images at 250 metre detail, a new reading about every 16 days, for each of your fields.
- The farm advice and the alerts are written by the app from simple rules (see below).

**Before you start.** You need the GPS coordinates of your farm (latitude and longitude). Zambia example: Lusaka is about -15.33, 28.52 (south latitudes are negative). You can read them from a phone map app.

**Step by step: set up**

1. Click **Weather** under **AI & Intelligence**. If you have no station, a note says "No weather station yet".
2. Open the **Stations** tab. Click **Add station** if the form is closed.
3. Fill in **Name** (for example "Farm HQ"), **Latitude** and **Longitude**. Or click **Use my location** and allow the browser to share your position.
4. Click **Save & sync**. The station is saved and the first forecast is fetched.
5. To add a field for satellite readings, open **Fields & NDVI**, click **Add field**, fill the form (below) and click **Save field**.
6. Click **Sync now** at the top at any time to fetch fresh data for all stations and fields.

> **Note:** The platform can also refresh in the background, but do not rely on it. Press **Sync now** before you plan spraying or irrigation.

**Field form**

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Field name* | For example "Block A" |
| Crop | For example "Maize" |
| Stage | Fallow, Land Prep, Planting, Vegetative, Flowering, Fruiting, Harvest Ready, Harvested |
| Area (ha) | Size of the field |
| Latitude, Longitude | Field position (or pick the nearest station and leave blank) |
| Nearest station | A station to borrow the position from |

**The four tabs**

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Forecast | Seven day cards (temperature high and low, chance of rain and expected rain in mm), then a line of **farm advice** for each day |
| Current | Temperature, Humidity, Wind, UV index (highest today), Rain (last hour), Pressure |
| Fields & NDVI | For each field the latest NDVI value, a colour label, a trend chart and recommendations. The refresh icon pulls the newest satellite reading; the bin deletes the field and its history |
| Stations | Each station with its last readings, a live or idle dot, a refresh icon and a bin |

The blue banner at the top shows today's high, low, condition, rain chance, wind and humidity, the advice for today, and the source and time of the last sync. Three tiles show **Rain days (7d)**, **Expected rainfall** and **Avg max temp**. If you have more than one station, a list at the top right switches between them.

**Weather alerts.** A red line appears (for the last two days) when a reading shows one of these:

| Alert | When |
|---|---|
| Heat | Temperature at or above 35 degrees C |
| Frost | Temperature at or below 2 degrees C |
| Strong wind | Wind at or above 40 km/h |
| Heavy rain | 5 mm or more in the last hour |
| Heavy rain expected | 20 mm or more forecast for today |
| Thunderstorm | A thunderstorm forecast for today |

**Examples of the daily advice.** Dry day: good for spraying early in the morning, weeding and drying grain. 50 percent or more chance of rain: do not spray or feed the leaves; good for transplanting. Wind of 40 km/h or more: no spraying, stake young plants, check house covers. Heat of 35 degrees C or more: irrigate early or late, give shade and extra water. Frost risk: cover seedlings and keep chicks warm.

**NDVI colours.** NDVI is a number from 0 to 1 showing how green and healthy a field looks from space.

| NDVI | Label | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0.65 and above | Excellent | Dense, vigorous crop |
| 0.45 to 0.65 | Good | Healthy |
| 0.30 to 0.45 | Fair | Sparse or stressed; walk the field |
| Below 0.30 | Poor | Very little green cover; investigate (or expected if the field is fallow or just planted) |

**Good to know**

- "already up to date (MODIS publishes a new composite every 16 days)" is normal when you sync a field twice in a short time.
- The fields on this page are not the same as the fields in **Crop Rotation**.
- Deleting a station or field asks you to confirm.

**If something goes wrong**

- "Add a station first." or "No forecast yet — press Sync now.": add a station, then press **Sync now**.
- A red message under the title shows the first error from the weather service, for example when coordinates are wrong or the service is busy. Check the coordinates and try again later.
- "Station saved, but the first sync failed": the station exists; press **Sync now** again.

---

## Sustainability

### Sustainability & Compliance

**What it is for.** A small record book for four things buyers and certifiers ask about: the carbon you release and save, the water you use, the certificates you hold and the tasks you must finish to keep them.

> **Watch out:** It is **saved on this device only**. Nothing is calculated for you from Production, Energy or Water pages; you type each figure. The first time you open it, three sample carbon rows and three sample water rows are added.

**Four tiles.** **Total Emissions** and **Carbon Offsets** (tonnes of CO2 equivalent), **Net Carbon** (emissions minus offsets) and **Water Used (MTD)**. The water tile adds **all** water rows you have entered, not only this month.

**Carbon tab.** Shows **Emissions by Category** and **Carbon Offsets** with the **Net Position**. Click **Log Carbon Entry** and fill in:

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Category / Source | For example "Fuel Combustion", "Manure Management" |
| Type | **Emission** or **Offset / Sequestration** (carbon taken in by trees or soil) |
| CO2e (kg) | Kilograms of CO2 equivalent |
| Month | The month the figure is for |
| Notes (optional) | Any context |

**Water tab.** Click **Log Usage** and fill in **Use Type**, **Volume (m3)**, **Source** (borehole, river, municipal) and **Month**. Each row shows a bar and the total at the bottom.

**Certifications tab.** Click **Add Certification** and fill in **Certification Name** (for example GlobalG.A.P. or Organic Zambia), **Certifying Body**, **Status** (Planning, In Progress, Certified, Expired) and **Expiry Date**. On each card the **Advance Status** button moves Planning to In Progress to Certified. On a certified card the **View Certificate** button does nothing yet.

**Compliance tab.** Click **Add Task** and fill in **Task Title**, **Due Date**, **Priority** (Critical, High, Medium, Low) and **Related Certification**. Click the box on a task to mark it done (click again to reopen). Tasks are sorted by due date.

**Good to know**

- Entries cannot be edited or deleted after saving, so check them first.
- The red "overdue compliance task" banner is built into the page, but no task is ever set to overdue automatically, so you will normally not see it. Watch the due dates yourself.
- Keep the real certificate files with your own papers; this page stores only the details.

---

## Understanding devices, sensors and readings

Before you open the device pages, read this chapter. It explains the words and tells you honestly what these pages can and cannot do today.

**What is a sensor or device?** A sensor is a small instrument that measures something: soil moisture, air temperature, dissolved oxygen (DO, the oxygen in pond water), water level in a tank, battery charge. A device can also be something you switch on and off, such as a pump, a valve, an aerator (a machine that puts air into pond water), a fan or a generator. A **reading** is one measured value at one moment, for example "28.4 degrees C at 10:15".

**How can a reading get into the app?** There are three ways. Only the first two exist in this version.

| Way | What it means | Where you find it |
|---|---|---|
| Typed by you (manual entry) | You look at a thermometer or gauge and type the number | Cold Chain grain silos (**Log Reading**) and trucks (**Update**), tank level on Water Mgmt (**Update Level**), and the logbook forms (feeding, mortality, eggs, water change, irrigation) |
| Simulated | You press a button and the page makes up a believable number | **Simulate Reading** (Smart Devices), **Refresh Readings** (Aquaculture, Soil Depth), **Refresh** (Poultry Houses, Energy) |
| Sent by real hardware through a gateway | A sensor and a gateway box send numbers on their own | **Not available on these screens.** The Device Gateway and Farm Gateway pages are demonstration screens and do not receive data from physical sensors |

> **Watch out:** Numbers marked "live", "online" or "connected" on the device pages are **not** coming from real equipment. Every sample device, tank, pond, house, cold room and battery is made-up example data. Use the pages to practise, to keep a logbook, or to plan. Do not rely on them for decisions that protect animals, crops, stored grain or cold-room produce.

**Thresholds.** A threshold is a limit. When a reading goes above a maximum or below a minimum the page shows a warning colour. Where you can set your own limits it is described under each page (for example **Alert Min** and **Alert Max** on Smart Devices, **Alert Below (%)** on a tank). On other pages the limits are fixed by the app and listed in this Part.

**Alerts.** An alert is a message that something is outside its limit. On the device pages the alerts you see are either sample alerts or alerts that exist only to show how the page looks. In this version, a new alert is **not** created automatically when a reading crosses a limit. The Notifications page collects the saved alerts (see the Notifications chapter).

**Automation rules.** A rule says "IF this happens, THEN do that". In this version **no rule runs by itself**. You press **Run** to try a rule by hand (see the Automation chapter).

**Your data stays on the device.** All device pages are saved on this device only. Make a backup with **Backup & Restore** (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account).

---

## Smart Devices

### Smart Devices & IoT

**What it is for.** A register of your sensors and machines with a summary of their status, a simple rule list and a log of alerts. The title is **Smart Devices & IoT**. IoT means "internet of things", sensors that talk to the internet.

> **Watch out:** On this page a reading is **typed by the page itself** when you press **Simulate Reading**. The WhatsApp, SMS and satellite panels in the **Connectivity** tab are fixed pictures that always say "Connected" or "Active". No message is sent and no satellite feed is read.

> **Note:** Smart Devices and **Device Gateway** share one saved device list on this device but keep different details for each device. Choose one of the two pages for your list and use it consistently.

**Step by step: add a device**

1. Click **Smart Devices** under **AI & Intelligence**, then **Add Device**.
2. Fill in the form (below) and click **Add Device**. The button stays grey until you type a name.
3. A new device is shown as **offline** with a battery of 100 percent and no reading (a dash).
4. Open the **All Devices** tab and click the arrow on the device row. Click **Simulate Reading** to give it a pretend reading. The status then becomes **Online**, or **Warning** or **Critical** (chosen at random) if the pretend reading is outside your minimum or maximum.

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Device Name* | For example "Greenhouse Temp Sensor" |
| Sensor Type* | Choose from the grouped list (see below) |
| Connectivity | LoRaWAN, NB-IoT, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, 4G/LTE, Zigbee, Modbus (how the sensor would talk to a gateway) |
| Enterprise | The farm activity it belongs to |
| Location / Zone | For example "House 2, Pond A" |
| Enable threshold alerts | Tick to set limits |
| Min, Max | Your lower and upper limit, in the sensor's unit |

**Sensor types.** The list has 53 types in eleven families:

| Family | Types |
|---|---|
| Soil & Substrate | Soil Moisture, Soil Temperature, Soil EC, Soil pH, Soil NPK, Soil Salinity, Soil O2, Soil Compaction |
| Weather Station | Air Temperature, Humidity, Wind Speed, Rainfall, Solar Radiation, Air Pressure, Leaf Wetness, ET0 |
| Water & Irrigation | Water Level, Flow Rate, Pipe Pressure, Water Quality |
| Aquaculture | Dissolved Oxygen, Water pH, Water Temperature, Ammonia, Turbidity, TDS, Salinity |
| Livestock | RFID Reader, GPS Tracker, Weight Scale, Health Sensor, Activity Sensor |
| Poultry House | CO2, Ammonia (air), Egg Counter, Feed Consumption, Water Consumption |
| Energy | Solar Output, Battery Level, Energy Consumption, Generator Fuel |
| Cameras & Vision | IP Camera, Thermal Camera, Motion Sensor |
| Machinery | Tractor GPS, Fuel Level, Engine Hours |
| Storage & Cold Chain | Cold Room Temp, Cold Room Humidity, Grain Temperature, Grain Moisture |
| General / Other | Gas Detector, Light Intensity |

**The six tabs**

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Device Families | A card for each family that has devices, with the number of devices, how many are online and a coloured status (All OK, warning, critical). Click a card to go to **All Devices** |
| AI Dashboard | **Devices Needing Attention**, a few **AI Insights** and **All Device Readings** |
| All Devices | One row per device with its status dot, connectivity label, last seen time, reading, battery and an expandable panel |
| Automation | The rule list (IF/THEN). See below |
| Alert Log | A list of past alerts with severity and channel (WhatsApp, SMS, app) |
| Connectivity | A count of devices by connection type, and three fixed panels |

In the expanded device panel you see the type, unit, **Alert Min** and **Alert Max**, and three buttons: **Simulate Reading**, **Alerts On/Off** (a flag only; nothing is sent) and **Remove** (asks to confirm).

**The AI Insights rules.** They are simple: dissolved oxygen sensor below 5 mg/L ("Critical DO"); a humidity sensor above 80 percent ("High Humidity"); a water level sensor below 30 percent ("Low Water", with a rough number of hours left worked out as the level divided by 5); a battery at or below 20 percent ("Low Battery"). If none applies you see "All Systems Normal".

**Automation tab: adding a rule.** Click **New Rule**, then fill in **Rule Name \***, **Trigger Device \***, **Condition** (Goes above, Falls below, Equals), **Threshold** and **Action / Response \*** (any text, such as "Send WhatsApp alert + trigger pump relay"), then **Create Rule**. The switch turns a rule on or off and the bin deletes it. The card reads "IF device falls below 5 THEN action".

> **Watch out:** These rules are **notes**. The page never compares a reading with a rule and never carries out the action. The "Last triggered" time on the sample rules is made-up. For rules you can press **Run** on, see the Automation chapter.

**Good to know**

- On first open, 17 sample devices, 3 sample rules and 3 sample alerts are added. Remove what you do not need.
- The top-right badge shows the number of critical devices, or the number online.
- There is no way to type a real measured value on this page.

---

## Water Mgmt

### Water Management

**What it is for.** To keep track of your tanks and reservoirs, pumps, valves (taps that open or close a water line) and irrigation rules. Subtitle: "Tanks · Pumps · Valves · Irrigation automation".

> **Watch out:** Pumps and valves on this page are **records on screen**. Pressing **Start** or **Open** changes the picture in the app only; it does not switch any real pump. Tank levels change only when you type a new number. Sample tanks, pumps, valves, rules and two sample alerts are added on first open.

**The five tabs and the header.** The blue header shows **Total Capacity**, **Current Volume**, **Active Pumps** and **Open Valves**. Tabs: **Overview**, **Tanks (n)**, **Pumps (n)**, **Valves (n)**, **Rules (n)**. Alerts, if any, show in a red box with a **Dismiss** button.

**Tanks**

1. Open the **Tanks** tab and click **Add Tank**.
2. Fill in **Tank Name \***, **Type** (Water, Borehole, Rainwater, Chemical, Nutrient, Fuel), **Enterprise**, **Capacity (Litres) \***, **Alert Below (%)** and **Location**, then click **Add Tank**. A new tank starts **full**.
3. To record the real level, click **Update Level** under the tank, type the litres now in the tank (between 0 and the capacity) and click the tick.
4. The bin deletes a tank after you confirm.

A tank gauge turns amber below 60 percent and red below 30 percent, and shows a pulsing **LOW** badge when the level is at or below the tank's own **Alert Below** percentage.

**Pumps.** Click **Add Pump** and fill in **Name**, **Linked Tank**, **Flow (L/min)** and **Power (W)**, then **Save**. The **Start** or **Stop** button on a pump card changes its status. Statuses are **Running**, **Stopped**, **Fault** and **Auto** (Fault and Auto appear only on sample pumps).

**Valves.** Click **Add Valve** and fill in **Name**, **Zone** and **Linked Pump**, then **Save**. A new valve is **Closed**. The button on the card switches between **Open** and **Closed**. **Auto** appears only on a sample valve.

**Irrigation rules**

1. Open **Rules** and click **Add Rule**.
2. Fill in **Rule Name \***, **Trigger Type** (Time of day, Soil moisture below %, Tank level above %, Manual only), the **Time** or **Threshold %**, the **Valve**, the **Pump** and the **Duration (minutes)**. Click **Add Rule**.
3. To use a rule, click **Run**. The valve is set to Open, the pump to On, the run is counted, and a line is added to **Recent Irrigation Events** on the Overview tab with an estimate of the litres (pump flow multiplied by the minutes).
4. **Enable** and **Disable** switch a rule on and off. The bin deletes it.

> **Note:** Time and level triggers are not watched. A rule runs only when you press **Run**, and nothing closes the valve or stops the pump when the duration ends. Switch them off yourself with the **Stop** and **Close** buttons.

The **Overview** tab also draws a "Water Flow — Current State" picture (tank, then pump, then valves).

**If something goes wrong**

- "No irrigation rules configured yet." Add a rule.

---

## Aquaculture

### Aquaculture Dashboard

**What it is for.** A dashboard for fish ponds, tanks and cages: pond readings, aeration, feeding, water changes and fish deaths. Subtitle: "Real-time pond monitoring · aeration · feeding · water quality".

> **Watch out:** The pond readings (oxygen, pH, temperature, ammonia, turbidity, water level) **cannot be typed**. They are sample values that **Refresh Readings** changes at random. The "DO Trend — 24h" chart is a drawn pattern, not a record. Four sample ponds and three sample alerts are added on first open. For real monitoring of oxygen and ammonia, test the water yourself and record your results in the logbooks below or in your production cycle (see Part 3).

**Header and tabs.** Tiles: **Ponds / Tanks**, **Total Fish**, **Total Biomass**, **Critical Ponds**. Tabs: **Overview**, **All Ponds**, **Feeding Log**, **Water Quality**, **Records**. Two buttons at the right: **Feed** and **Add Pond**. Open alerts show in a red box with **Resolve**.

**Add a pond**

1. Click **Add Pond**.
2. Fill in **Name \***, **Type** (Earthen, Concrete, Cage, Raceway, Biofloc), **Enterprise**, **Species**, **Stocked (#)** and **Volume (m3)**, then **Add Pond**.
3. A new pond starts with ready-made readings (oxygen 7.0 mg/L, pH 7.2, 27 degrees C, ammonia 0.02 mg/L, turbidity 10, level 90 percent) and a biomass of 50 g per fish.

**The pond card.** It shows a status (Excellent, Good, Fair, Critical), the type, an **Aerating** badge, five reading chips (DO, pH, Temp, NH3, Turbidity), fish stocked, biomass and **Feed/day** (3 percent of biomass). The **On/Off** button switches the aerator; switching it on adds 1.5 mg/L to the oxygen figure on screen. If oxygen is below 5 mg/L and the aerator is off, a red line says "Critical DO — start aerator immediately" with a **Start** button.

**Reading colours.**

| Reading | Green (good) | Amber (watch) | Red |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dissolved oxygen (mg/L) | 6 to 10 | 4 to 5.9 | below 4 |
| pH | 6.5 to 8.5 | 6.0 to 6.4 | outside |
| Water temperature (degrees C) | 22 to 30 | 18 to 21 | outside |
| Ammonia (mg/L) | up to 0.05 | 0.05 to 0.1 | above 0.1 |
| Turbidity (NTU) | up to 20 | 20 to 40 | above 40 |
| Water level (percent) | 60 to 100 | 30 to 59 | below 30 |

**Logbooks**

| Where | Button | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| **Feed** button at the top right | **Log Feed** | Pond*, Feed (kg)*, Feed Type, Notes |
| Water Quality tab | **Log Water Change** | Pond, % Changed, Reason |
| Records tab | **Log Mortality** | Pond, Count, Avg Weight (g), Cause |

The Feeding Log tab also shows Today's Feed, Feed Events (total), Most Recent and Ponds Fed Today. The Records tab lists deaths, with the kilograms lost worked out from count and average weight. **Water Quality** also has a table of the current readings for all ponds.

**Good to know**

- The logbooks are lists only. Logging feed or fish deaths does **not** change the stocked number, biomass or feed per day on the pond card.
- Logging feed here does not take stock from Inventory or post a cost to Finance. Do that in Production and Inventory (see Parts 3 and 4).
- Ponds can be removed from the **All Ponds** tab (**Remove**, with confirmation). Log entries cannot be deleted.

---

## Poultry Houses

### Poultry House Dashboard

**What it is for.** To look at a house at a glance: climate (temperature, humidity, carbon dioxide, ammonia), controls (fan, heater, light, ventilation), eggs, feed and water, and bird deaths. Subtitle: "Climate control · egg production · feeding · mortality records".

> **Watch out:** The page holds **three sample houses** (a broiler house and two layer houses) with sample figures. You cannot add or remove a house. The climate figures change only when you press **Refresh** (made-up changes) or toggle a control. The switches do not control a real fan or heater. Four sample alerts for the first layer house are included.

**Tabs and buttons.** Tiles: **Houses**, **Total Birds**, **Eggs Today**, **Critical Houses**. Click a house card to select it. Tabs: **Overview**, **Climate & Controls**, **Production**, **Feed & Water**, **Records**. Buttons at the right: **Collect Eggs** (only for a layer or breeder house), **Feed Record** and **Mortality**.

**Overview tab.** Four tiles for the chosen house (birds, mortality rate, and either Eggs Today and Eggs This Week for layers, or Live Weight and FCR estimate for broilers), a climate card set with a **Refresh** button, and the **House Controls**: **Fan / Ventilation**, **Heater**, **Lighting** (ON/OFF) and a **Ventilation** slider (0 to 100 percent, saved when you move it). Switching the fan on lowers the temperature, carbon dioxide, ammonia and humidity figures on screen by a fixed amount.

**Climate limits used by the colours**

| Reading | Broiler target | Layer target | Amber from | Red from |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Temperature (degrees C) | 26 to 30 | 21 to 28 | 29 (broiler) / 28 (layer) | 32 (broiler) / 30 (layer) |
| Humidity (percent) | 50 to 70 | 50 to 70 | 70 | 80 |
| Carbon dioxide (ppm) | under 2000 | under 2000 | 2000 | 2500 |
| Ammonia (ppm) | under 20 | under 15 | 20 (broiler) / 15 (layer) | 25 (broiler) / 20 (layer) |

**Logbooks**

| Button | Fields | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Collect Eggs | House*, Egg Count*, Broken, Notes | Shows in the **Production** tab with the percentage broken |
| Feed Record | House*, Feed (kg)*, Feed Type, Notes | Shows in **Feed & Water** |
| Mortality | House*, Count*, Avg Weight (g), Cause | Shows in **Records** |

**Production tab.** The egg log and **Hen Day Average (HDA)** for each layer house. HDA is the eggs laid today as a percentage of the hens in the house. 85 percent and above is green, 75 to 85 amber, below 75 red.

**Feed & Water tab.** For each house, feed eaten against a daily budget (amber above 105 percent of budget) and water use against its budget, then the feed records.

**Records tab.** Bird deaths, and a **Mortality Summary by House**. A house is shown in red when its death rate is above 4 percent (broilers) or 2 percent (other houses).

**Good to know**

- Logging eggs, feed or deaths adds a line to a list only. It does **not** change the bird count, eggs today or mortality figures of the house, and it does not touch Production, Inventory or Finance. For farm records that feed your reports, use Production (see Part 3).
- The broiler FCR estimate assumes an average bird weight of 2.1 kg.

---

## Energy

### Energy Monitor

**What it is for.** To view solar arrays, battery banks, generators and the loads that use power, and to see a 30-day picture of energy use and cost. Subtitle: "Solar · Battery · Generator · Load analytics · Cost per kg".

> **Watch out:** This page is **view-only sample data**. You cannot add or edit arrays, batteries or loads. The 30 days of figures in **Analytics** are random numbers made when the page is first opened. Amounts of money are in **US dollars** at a fixed $0.12 per kWh for grid power (and $0.35 for generator power in the history), not in kwacha.

**What you can do**

- **Refresh** (top right) makes small random changes to the solar output and battery charge.
- In the **Generators** tab, **Start** or **Stop** a generator. Starting sets its output to 75 percent of its capacity on screen. It does not start a real machine.
- Click **OK** on an energy alert to clear it.

**Tabs**

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Overview | An energy flow picture, today's solar, generator, consumption and cost, and the battery banks |
| Solar Arrays | Each array's status (generating, idle, fault), output against capacity, today's kWh, efficiency and irradiance, panel temperature, tilt and month total |
| Battery Banks | Charge ring, capacity, available kWh, charge and discharge rates, cycle count and a warning above 35 degrees C |
| Generators | Status, fuel level, tank size, hours since the last service. **FUEL LOW** shows below 25 percent and **SERVICE OVERDUE** after 250 hours since the last service |
| Load Map | Each load's power, share of the total, priority, and an estimated cost for today at $0.12 per kWh |
| Analytics | Solar this month, energy cost (30 days), average daily solar, a CO2 saving estimate (0.45 kg per kWh) and four small charts |

The header tiles are **Solar Output**, **Battery SOC** (state of charge, how full the battery is), **Generator**, **Farm Load** and **Self-Sufficiency** (solar plus generator output as a share of the load, up to 100 percent; red below 50).

---

## Soil Depth

### Soil Depth monitoring

**What it is for.** To follow soil moisture at four depths (10, 30, 60 and 90 cm) in each field zone, to see how much of the plant-available water is used up, and to log irrigation. It also tells you roughly how many millimetres to apply.

> **Watch out:** You cannot type the moisture readings. **Refresh Readings** makes small random changes (moisture falls a little each time, or rises if the zone is marked as being irrigated). Four sample zones and two sample alerts are added on first open.

**Add a zone**

1. Click **Add Zone**.
2. Fill in **Zone Name \***, **Texture**, **Crop** and **Area (ha)**, then **Add Zone**.
3. A new zone starts with ready-made readings at about 85, 80, 75 and 70 percent of field capacity.

**Soil textures.** Field capacity (FC, the most water the soil holds after drainage) and permanent wilting point (PWP, the moisture below which plants cannot take up water) are set from the texture.

| Texture | FC (percent) | PWP (percent) |
|---|---|---|
| Sandy | 12 | 4 |
| Sandy Loam | 18 | 6 |
| Loam | 28 | 12 |
| Silt Loam | 32 | 14 |
| Clay Loam | 36 | 18 |
| Clay | 42 | 22 |

Crops in the list: Maize, Wheat, Soybean, Cassava, Tomato, Lettuce, Rice, Sorghum, Pasture, Fallow.

**How the status is worked out.** The page takes the average moisture of the four depths and works out the **depletion**: how much of the plant-available water (FC minus PWP) has been used. Then:

| Depletion | Status |
|---|---|
| 25 percent or less | Optimal |
| 26 to 50 percent | Adequate |
| 51 to 75 percent | Stress |
| Above 75 percent | Critical |

When depletion is above 50 percent the page shows an **Irrigation Recommendation**: "Apply approximately X mm to restore plant-available water to 70% field capacity", worked out from the depletion and the soil texture.

**Tabs.** **Overview** (one card per zone; click to open its profile), **Depth Profiles** (choose a zone, see a depth chart and a card for each depth with moisture, temperature and electrical conductivity), **Irrigation Log** and **Analysis** (a comparison table for all zones and a depletion bar for each).

**Log an irrigation event.** Click **Log Irrigation**, choose the **Zone \***, enter **Depth (mm) \***, choose the **Method** (Drip, Sprinkler, Furrow, Flood) and **Duration (min)**, then **Save**. The zone's "last irrigated" time is updated. The moisture readings do **not** change.

**Good to know**

- Open alerts show with a **Resolve** button at the top of the page.
- There is no button to delete a zone or an irrigation entry.
- Use your own probe or a hand test (squeeze a handful of soil) for real decisions.

---

## Automation

### Automation Rules

**What it is for.** To write IF/THEN rules for your devices, to keep a library of ready-made templates, and to try them out. Subtitle: "Compound conditions · cascade actions · schedules · templates".

> **Watch out:** **No rule on this page runs by itself.** The conditions (such as "soil moisture below 30%") are never checked against any reading. A rule does something only when you press **Run** on it. Even then it only changes the on/off state of pumps, valves, aerators, fans, heaters and generators that are recorded on the other device pages (Water Mgmt, Aquaculture, Poultry Houses and Energy). It does not switch real equipment. "Send Alert" and "Log Event" only write a line in the run log; no message is sent.

**Step by step: use a template**

1. Click **Automation** under **AI & Intelligence**, then the **Templates** tab.
2. Read the six templates: Soil Moisture Trigger, DO Emergency Aeration, Heat Stress Fan Trigger, Time-Based Schedule, Tank Low Alert, Cascade Irrigation.
3. Click **Use This Template**. It is added to the **Rules** tab as a disabled rule with blank device names.

**Step by step: build your own rule**

1. On the **Rules** tab click **New Rule**.
2. Fill in **Rule Name \***, **Category** (Irrigation, Aeration, Climate, Energy, Alert, Custom), **Trigger Logic** (ALL conditions (AND), ANY condition (OR), Scheduled), **Description** and **Cooldown (minutes)**.
3. Under **IF Conditions**, choose a condition type, type its value and (optionally) a device or zone name, then click **Add Condition**. Add as many as you need.
4. Under **THEN Actions**, choose an action, type the **Target device** and, where asked, **Duration (min)** and **Delay (min)**, then click **Add Action**.
5. Click **Create Rule**. It stays grey until the rule has a name, at least one condition and at least one action.

**Conditions you can choose (14).** Soil Moisture Below or Above; Tank Level Below or Above; Temperature Above or Below; DO Below; Ammonia Above; Humidity Above or Below; Time of Day Is; Day of Week Is; No Rain in Last (hours); Battery SOC Above.

**Actions you can choose (14).** Start or Stop Pump; Open or Close Valve; Start or Stop Aerator; Start or Stop Fan; Start or Stop Heater; Send Alert; Log Event; Start or Stop Generator.

**Working with rules**

- Use the category list at the top to filter the rules.
- Click the arrow on a rule to see its conditions, its actions in order, and its recent runs.
- **Run** appears only when the rule is switched on and its cooldown has passed. While the cooldown is running you see the minutes left. The cooldown only controls when **Run** is offered.
- The switch turns a rule on or off. The bin deletes it after you confirm.
- The **Run Log** tab lists every run with its result note.

> **Watch out:** The target name is matched as a piece of text. Typing "Pump 1" affects any pump whose name contains "pump 1". **A blank target matches every device of that kind**, so a template run with a blank target starts or stops all of them. Fill in the target before you press **Run**. The run log says it "worked" even when no device matched, for example when the other page has not saved any data yet.

Duration and delay values are shown on the rule but are not carried out (nothing waits, and nothing switches off after the duration).

On first open five sample rules are added (maize irrigation, pond aeration, poultry fans, morning irrigation, battery-first generator).

---

## Device Gateway

### Device Gateway

**What it is for.** A register of devices by family, with their status, battery, last reading, firmware and alerts. Subtitle: "Universal IoT device hub".

> **Watch out:** This is a **demonstration register**. It does not connect to hardware. A device you add shows **online** with **No readings yet**, and the page offers no way to enter or receive readings, to edit a device or to delete one. The 12 sample devices and 4 sample alerts are made-up.

> **Note:** It shares one saved device list with **Smart Devices** (see the note in that chapter).

**Step by step: register a device**

1. Click **Device Gateway** under **AI & Intelligence**, then **Add Device** (or the **Add Device** tab).
2. Fill in the form and click **Register Device**.

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Device Name* | For example "Field C Soil Sensor" |
| Device Family* | Soil Sensor, Weather Station, Water Sensor, Camera, Aerator, Pump, Valve, Energy Meter, Cold Sensor, GPS Tracker, Custom |
| Model | For example "SoilPro 300" |
| Location* | For example "Field D" |
| Enterprise ID | Free text; leave blank to use your first enterprise |
| Battery % (or type "Wired") | A number, or the word Wired |

**The four tabs**

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Overview | Tiles for Total Devices, Online, Offline, Low Battery (this also counts faults) and Active Alerts, a connectivity bar (the percentage online) and a card for each family |
| All Devices | A table with Name, Family, Location, Status (Online, Offline, Fault, Low Battery), Battery, Last Reading and Last Seen. Filter by family and status. Click a row to see all readings, the model and the firmware |
| Alerts | Alert cards with a severity and a **Resolve** button, and a **Refresh** button |
| Add Device | The registration form |

> **Tip:** Alerts resolved here are the ones the **Notifications** page lists under "Devices".

---

## Cold Chain

### Cold Chain Monitor

**What it is for.** To keep records for cold rooms, grain silos and refrigerated trucks. A **cold chain** is the unbroken cold storage and cold transport that keeps fresh produce, milk and eggs safe. Subtitle: "Cold rooms · Grain silos · Refrigerated trucks".

**What is real and what is not**

| Part | What you can do |
|---|---|
| Grain silos | Type real readings with **Log Reading** (temperatures, moisture, carbon dioxide). This is genuine manual entry |
| Refrigerated trucks | Type the truck's current temperature and status with **Update Temp & Status** |
| Cold rooms | Switch the compressor and door markers. The room temperature and humidity are fixed numbers; there is no box to update them |
| Alerts | Three sample alerts show on screen. New alerts are never created and there is no **Resolve** button |

Two sample cold rooms, two silos, two trucks and three sample alerts are added on first open.

**Overview tab.** A red or amber banner for active alerts, three tiles (Cold Rooms with compressors on, Grain Silos fumigating, Trucks in transit), the cold room temperatures, the silo conditions and the truck list.

**Cold Rooms tab**

1. Click **Add Cold Room**.
2. Fill in **Room Name \***, **Target Temp (°C) \***, **Alert Threshold (°C) \***, **Capacity (units)** and **Products (comma-separated)**, then **Add Room**.
3. Use **Stop Compressor** or **Start Compressor**, and **Open Door** or **Close Door**. An open door shows a red "Door is open — temperature may rise" band, but the temperature number does not change.

The thermometer is green within 2 degrees C of the target, amber within 5 and red beyond that.

**Grain Silos tab**

1. Click **Add Silo** and fill in **Silo Name \***, **Grain Type \***, **Capacity (tonnes)** and **Current Stock (tonnes)**.
2. Click **Log Reading** on a silo. Type **Top Zone Temp**, **Middle Zone Temp**, **Bottom Zone Temp**, **Moisture (%)** and **CO2 (ppm)**, then **Save Reading**. A blank box keeps the old value.
3. **Start Fumigation** and **Stop Fumigation** mark a silo as being treated; the date of the last treatment is updated when you start.

Colours: a zone is green below 25 degrees C, amber 25 to 30 and red above 30. Moisture is red above 14 percent and amber above 13. Carbon dioxide is red above 600 ppm and amber above 500. The fill level bar turns amber above 70 percent and red above 90.

> **Tip:** Warm spots and rising carbon dioxide in a silo are early signs of mould and insects. Log a reading often during the wet season and keep maize moisture at 13 percent or below for safe storage.

**Refrigerated Trucks tab**

1. Click **Add Truck** and fill in **Truck Name \***, **Plate Number \***, **Driver Name**, **Cargo Description**, **Origin**, **Destination**, **Target Temp (°C)** and **Est. Arrival**. The truck starts as **Loading**.
2. Click **Update Temp & Status** on a truck card. Type the **Current Temp (°C)** and choose the **Status**: Loading, In Transit, Delivered or Idle. Then click **Update**.

---

## Farm Gateway

### Farm Gateway

**What it is for.** A demonstration of what a farm "gateway box" screen could look like: the connection status, a queue of data waiting to be sent, a history of sends and the box settings. Subtitle shows the gateway name and place.

> **Watch out:** **Farm Gateway does not connect to anything.** The firmware version, the uptime, the "12 connected" devices, the queue items and the history lines are made-up samples. Pressing **Force Sync All** does not send data anywhere; it just clears the pending lines and adds a "Success" line to the history. The **Offline Mode** switch only changes a label on this page. It does not change how the real app works when your internet is down.

**The four tabs**

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Status | The gateway card with **Go Offline** / **Go Online**, firmware, uptime, last heartbeat and device count; counts of Pending, Failed and Total items; **Force Sync All (n)**; a **Module Sync Status** list for Aquaculture, Water Quality, Poultry, Energy and Soil; a connection picture |
| Sync Queue | Waiting items with module, action (create, update, delete), the data text, how long ago, retries and status (pending or failed). **Retry** puts a failed item back to pending. **Clear All Resolved** removes failed items that were retried more than three times |
| Sync History | Success rate, items synced today, total records and a table of past sends |
| Configuration | **Gateway Name**, **Location**, **MQTT Broker**, **MQTT Port**, **Sync Interval (minutes)**, the **Offline Mode** switch and **Save Configuration** |

The settings are saved on this device and can be changed, but they have no effect on any real equipment.

---

## Marketplaces in the farm workspace

The **Marketplaces** section of the left menu has six pages. They look alike but work in two different ways:

| Pages | Type | Real or sample? |
|---|---|---|
