**How the suggested price is worked out.** If you entered hectares and the machine has a per-hectare rate, the suggestion is hectares × the per-hectare rate. Otherwise, if the machine has a daily rate, it is the number of days (start to end, both days counted) × the daily rate. You can type a different amount.

> **Example:** A tractor with a daily rate of ZMW 1,200 is booked 3 to 5 October (3 days). Suggested amount: 3 × 1,200 = ZMW 3,600. With 8 ha and a per-hectare rate of ZMW 450, the suggestion would be 8 × 450 = ZMW 3,600.

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| End date must be on or after the start date | Fix the dates. |
| Give a client organisation or a client name | Choose or type a client. |
| Equipment not found | The machine was deleted. Reopen the form. |
| Enter a quoted amount to send a quote | You set the status to Quoted with no quoted amount. |

#### Invoicing a hire

1. When the booking is **Completed** and not paid in full, click **Invoice**.
2. The invoice has one line: "Hire of [machine] [start] → [end]", quantity 1, unit price the agreed amount (or the quoted amount if none is agreed). Add extra lines in the window (for example fuel or delivery) only if you want to replace that line. Save the draft.
3. In **Invoices** click **Send**, then **Mark paid**. The booking is marked paid in full.

### Maintenance

**What it is for.** A log of servicing and repairs per machine, so you know when each is due, what it has cost and how long it was out of use.

**What you will see**

| Card | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Service records | Number of log entries. |
| Due within 14 days | Entries whose "next due" date is within 14 days or already past. |
| Maintenance cost (all) | Total of costs logged. |
| Downtime days | Total days the machines were out of use. |

Below, the **Maintenance log** has a **+ Log service** button (disabled until you have added a machine). Columns: Date, Machine, Type, Work done, Cost, Hours, Next due (red when due soon), By, and a **Delete** button. Empty: "No service records yet." or "Add machines to the fleet first."

**Step by step: log a service**

1. Click **+ Log service**.
2. In the window **Log maintenance** fill in:

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Machine * | Choose the machine. |
| Type | Routine service, Repair, Inspection, Tyres / tracks, Other. |
| Date * | The date of the work. |
| Work done * | A short description, for example "Engine oil and filters". |
| Cost (ZMW) | The cost. |
| Hour-meter reading | The machine's hours at service. |
| Downtime (days) | Days the machine was out of use. |
| Next service due | The date of the next service. This feeds the "Service due" reminders. |
| Performed by | A mechanic or workshop name. |
| Notes | Anything else. |

3. Click **Save**.

**Good to know**

- You can delete a log entry but not edit it. If you made an error, delete and re-enter.
- Costs here are for your information. They are not posted to any accounting or finance screen.
- The "service due" reminder uses the latest **Next service due** dates you have logged. If you never fill it in, no reminder appears.
- Logging maintenance does not change a machine's availability. Open Fleet and untick **Available for hire** while it is in the workshop.

### First week checklist (Equipment Hire)

1. Finish the common checklist in Chapter 1.
2. Add every machine under **Fleet Management** with type, capacity, rates and whether the operator and fuel are included.
3. For each machine, log its last service in **Maintenance** with the **Next service due** date.
4. In **Marketplace Listing**, add any extra services (land preparation packages, delivery) under **Other services**.
5. Add your registration papers as certificates.
6. Enter your currently booked jobs with **+ Booking** so the diary is complete.
7. Make one test of the whole chain: Enquiry, Quote, Confirmed, Active, Completed, Invoice, Mark paid, then look at **Revenue**.

### Daily routine (Equipment Hire)

1. **Overview**: new enquiries and machines due for service.
2. **Bookings**: filter **Enquiry**; **Quote** each one and phone the farmer.
3. Update statuses as machines go out and come back (**Update**).
4. Log any repair the same day in **Maintenance**.
5. **Invoices**: chase overdue ones. Tick paid on bookings paid in cash.

### How farmers find and book you (Equipment Hire)

1. **Fill in** your Business profile (phone, district, districts served, service hours, "We travel to the farm"), your fleet with rates, and certificates.
2. **Appear in search.** In the farm workspace there is an **Equipment Hire** page under Markets. Farmers search by words in your name, tagline and description, and filter by district. Equipment hire providers are listed on this page from their marketplace profile.
3. **What the farmer does.** The farmer opens your card and clicks **Book / Enquire**, then fills in **Start Date**, **End Date**, **Hectares (optional)**, **Delivery / Site Address** and **Notes** (equipment type, soil conditions, special needs). They are shown a reference starting with HB- and told you will contact them on your phone.
4. **What you receive.** An **Enquiry** in **Bookings**, marked "marketplace", with no machine chosen. You are not sent an email or text, so check the app daily. Quote, agree, confirm, as described above.

> **Note:** The site address the farmer types is saved with the booking but is not shown on the Bookings screen. Ask the farmer for the location when you phone about the quote. The farmer's notes are shown as "Customer note" in the **Quote** or **Update** window.

---

## Chapter 4 — Transport & Logistics

The Transport & Logistics workspace is for haulage companies, truck owners and delivery services. Its tabs are:

| Tab | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Overview** | Vehicles available, open jobs, service due, money received. |
| **Transport Jobs** | Your runs, from request to payment. |
| **Vehicles** | Your trucks and other vehicles with rates. |
| **Maintenance** | Service and repair log (same as Chapter 3). |
| **Clients, Invoices, Revenue, Marketplace Listing, Users & Permissions, Settings** | The common tabs from Chapter 1. |

In **Marketplace Listing** the services card is titled **Routes & rates**, with the hint: "Per-km, per-tonne or per-trip rates and standard routes buyers can book."

> **Note:** The transport workspace has no **Bookings** tab. Work is managed in **Transport Jobs**.

### Overview (Transport)

| Card | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Vehicles | Available vehicles out of all vehicles, "available now". |
| Open jobs | Jobs in status Requested, Quoted, Scheduled or In progress. The note says "transport runs in progress or scheduled". |
| Service due (14 d) | Vehicles with a next-service date within 14 days or already past. |
| Received (30 d) | Money received in the last 30 days. |

Quick actions: **Jobs**, **Fleet**, **Invoices**, **Maintenance**.

### Vehicles

This tab works exactly like **Fleet Management** in Chapter 3, with different words: the button is **+ Add vehicle**, the card is **Vehicles**, and a new vehicle starts as type **Truck / transport**. Use the same fields: Name, Type, Make, Model, Year, **Capacity (HP, tonnes, m)** (for example "5 t"), **Daily rate (ZMW)**, **Per-hectare / per-km rate (ZMW)** (put your per-km rate here), Minimum hire days, **Operator / driver included**, **Fuel included**, **Available for hire** and Notes.

Vehicles you list here appear in the **Vehicle** drop-down when you create a job.

### Transport Jobs

**What it is for.** To record every haulage run: who it is for, where it goes, how far, how much it carries, which driver and vehicle, the price, and whether it was paid.

#### Job statuses

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Requested | A customer asked for a run. No price yet. |
| Quoted | You gave a price. |
| Scheduled | The date is agreed. |
| In progress | The vehicle is on the road. |
| Completed | Delivered. The completion date is stored automatically. |
| Cancelled | Not going ahead. |

#### The jobs screen

At the top, four cards: **Jobs** (all), **Scheduled**, **In progress** and **Unpaid completed** (the total agreed value of completed jobs not marked paid).

The **Transport jobs** card has a status filter and a **+ Job** button. Columns:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Ref / date | Scheduled date and time (or "TBC"), job reference and kind. |
| Job | The title, and the service name if one was picked. |
| Client | Name and phone. |
| Route | Pick-up, then "→ destination", then the distance in km. |
| Qty | Quantity and unit. |
| Assigned | Driver, and the vehicle below it. |
| Amount | Agreed amount, or "quote" and the quoted amount; "paid" in green when paid. |
| Status | Status label. |

The last column offers, depending on status:

| Button | Shown when |
|---|---|
| **Edit** | Always. |
| **Quoted**, **Scheduled**, **In progress**, **Completed**, **Cancelled** | The next steps allowed from the current status (see below). |
| **Invoice** | Completed and not paid. |
| **Delete** | Status Requested or Cancelled. |

The status steps offered as one-click buttons are:

| Current status | One-click buttons |
|---|---|
| Requested | Quoted, Scheduled, Cancelled |
| Quoted | Scheduled, Cancelled |
| Scheduled | In progress, Cancelled |
| In progress | Completed |
| Completed, Cancelled | none |

In **Edit** you can set any status.

#### Step by step: create a transport job

1. Click **+ Job**. The window **New transport job** opens.
2. Choose the client with the client picker.
3. Fill in:

| Field | What to enter |
|---|---|
| Kind | Service job, Installation, Consulting / advisory, Transport / haulage, Delivery, Other. For transport it starts as **Transport / haulage**. |
| Job title * | Required. For example "Maize to FRA depot". |
| Pick-up | Where the load is collected. |
| Drop-off / destination | Where it goes (shown for transport and delivery). |
| District | The district. |
| Distance (km) | The route length (shown for transport and delivery). It is not worked out by the app. |
| Quantity / Unit | The load and its unit. Unit starts as **tonnes**; you can write "trips" or "bags". |
| Date / Time | When the run is planned. Date starts as today. |
| Assigned to | A driver from your active staff (Settings > Team). |
| Vehicle | One of your vehicles. |
| Status | Starts as **Scheduled**. |
| Quoted (ZMW) / Agreed (ZMW) | The price you quoted and the price agreed. |
| Paid | Tick when the client has paid. |
| Description / scope | Details of the load, loading conditions, and so on. |
| Completion notes | Shown only when the status is Completed; for example "Delivered, signed by storekeeper". |

4. Click **Save job**. "Job saved." appears.

**Step by step: run a job through its life**

1. A customer phones: create the job with status **Requested**, or **Quoted** if you give the price straight away.
2. Click **Quoted** (if not done), then **Scheduled** once the date is agreed.
3. Assign the driver and the vehicle in **Edit**.
4. On the day click **In progress**, then **Completed** when delivered, and add **Completion notes** in **Edit**.
5. Click **Invoice**, send it, and mark it paid. Or tick **Paid** in **Edit**, remembering that revenue counts only when **Agreed (ZMW)** is filled in.

**Pricing, routes and rates.** The app does not calculate a price from the distance. You type the **Quoted** and **Agreed** amounts. Keep your standard rates in **Routes & rates** (Marketplace Listing) so your team and farmers can see them. For a per-tonne rate use the pricing model **Per unit** with the unit label "tonne", or **Per km** for kilometre rates, or **Fixed price** with "per trip" in the unit label.

> **Example:** A 5-tonne truck takes maize from Chongwe to a depot in Lusaka. Title: "Maize 5 t to depot"; pick-up "Chongwe Cooperative store"; destination "Lusaka depot"; distance 55 km; quantity 5 tonnes; quoted and agreed ZMW 2,800.

**Invoicing a job.** In the invoice window the line is the job title, quantity (the load, or 1 if empty) and unit, with the unit price equal to the agreed amount (or quoted amount) divided by the quantity.

**If something goes wrong:** "Give a client organisation or a client name" (no client), "Invalid status" or "Service not found" (the form is out of date; reopen it).

### Maintenance (Transport)

Identical to Chapter 3 "Maintenance". Log services, tyres and repairs on each vehicle with the hour or kilometre reading in **Hour-meter reading**, and set **Next service due**.

### First week checklist (Transport)

1. Finish the common checklist in Chapter 1.
2. Add each truck under **Vehicles** with capacity, per-km rate and whether the driver and fuel are included.
3. Add your drivers under **Settings > Team** with role **Driver / Operator**.
4. In **Marketplace Listing > Routes & rates**, add your common routes and rates.
5. Log the last service of each vehicle in **Maintenance**.
6. Enter this week's runs under **Transport Jobs**.
7. Invoice your first completed run.

### Daily routine (Transport)

1. **Overview**: open jobs and vehicles due for service.
2. **Transport Jobs**: update statuses as runs move; assign drivers and vehicles.
3. After each delivery: **Completed**, completion notes, invoice.
4. Evening: **Invoices** (overdue) and **Maintenance** for any repair.

### How farmers find and book you (Transport)

1. **Fill in** the Business profile (phone and WhatsApp most of all), districts served, service hours, Routes & rates and certificates. Your marketplace category is **Transport & Logistics**.
2. **Appear in search.** Your profile is stored in the provider directory under Transport & Logistics, searchable by name, tagline, description and specialties, and by district. In the farm workspace there is no separate Transport page under Markets in this version; farmers mainly reach transporters by phone, through the AgriServices page, or through programmes.
3. **When a request arrives.** Most requests will come by phone or WhatsApp; enter each one under **Transport Jobs** with **+ Job**, status **Requested**, so it counts in **Open jobs** and builds your client list.

---

## Chapter 5 — AgriServices Provider

The AgriServices workspace is for businesses that sell work rather than goods: land preparation, irrigation installation, fencing, soil and water testing, spraying, farm consulting, and similar. Its tabs are:

| Tab | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Overview** | Open jobs, completed jobs, clients, money received, and upcoming jobs. |
| **Services Catalog** | Your services and rates (the "Services & rates" list). |
| **Job Schedule** | Your jobs from request to payment. |
| **Clients, Invoices, Revenue, Marketplace Listing, Users & Permissions, Settings** | The common tabs from Chapter 1. |

### Overview (AgriServices)

| Card | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Open jobs | Jobs in status Requested, Quoted, Scheduled or In progress; under it, how many are new requests. |
| Completed (all time) | Number of completed jobs. |
| Clients | Number of clients. |
| Received (30 d) | Money received in the last 30 days. |

Quick actions: **New job**, **Services & prices**, **Clients**, **Invoices**. **Upcoming jobs** lists up to eight open jobs in date order, with date (or "TBC"), job, client, site, assigned person and status. Empty: "Nothing scheduled."

### Services Catalog

This is the **Services & rates** list from Chapter 1 ("Services and prices"). The hint says: "Land preparation, irrigation installation, fencing, soil and water testing, consulting — priced per ha, per hour or per job."

Use **Per hectare** for ploughing or spraying, **Per hour** for machinery or consulting time, **Fixed price** for a per-job price such as a drip kit installation, and **Negotiable / quote** where you quote each time. Give **Price max** if the price is a range.

> **Example:** "Ploughing and harrowing" — category Land preparation — Per hectare — price 450 — lead time 3 days. "Soil testing (basic)" — Fixed price — price 600 — unit label "per sample set". "Drip irrigation installation, 1 ha" — Fixed price — price 18,000 to 24,000.

The same list also appears in the **Marketplace Listing** tab under **Services & rates**.

### Job Schedule

**What it is for.** To plan, assign and track each job for a client.

**Job statuses** are the same as in Chapter 4: Requested, Quoted, Scheduled, In progress, Completed and Cancelled.

**Job kinds:** Service job, Installation, Consulting / advisory, Transport / haulage, Delivery, Other.

**The screen.** Four cards: **Jobs**, **Scheduled**, **In progress** and **Unpaid completed**. The **Job schedule** card has a status filter and **+ Job**. Columns are the same as in Chapter 4, except the fifth is **Site** (location) rather than Route. The action buttons and the status steps are the same too (Requested → Quoted or Scheduled or Cancelled, and so on), plus **Edit**, **Invoice** (Completed, unpaid) and **Delete** (Requested or Cancelled).

**Step by step: create and schedule a job**

1. Click **+ Job**. The window **New job** opens.
2. Choose the client (platform client or walk-in).
3. Choose **Kind**. For most work leave it as **Service job**.
4. In **Service from catalogue** pick one of your catalogue services. This fills the **Job title** (if empty) and the **Quoted** amount (if empty) from the service's price. Change them if needed.
5. Enter **Job title**, **Site / location**, **District**.
6. Enter **Quantity** and **Unit**. The unit starts as **ha**; write "hours", "samples" or anything else you need.
7. Choose **Date** and **Time**.
8. In **Assigned to** choose the technician or operator (active staff only). In **Equipment** choose the machine if the job uses one of your machines (all machines on your Fleet list appear; note that the AgriServices workspace has no Fleet tab, so this list is only filled for businesses that also keep equipment).
9. Set **Status** (starts as **Scheduled**) and the **Quoted (ZMW)** and **Agreed (ZMW)** prices.
10. Add the **Description / scope**. Click **Save job**.

**To move a job along** click the status buttons in the last column. Use **Edit** to add **Completion notes** (shown when the status is Completed) and to tick **Paid**.

**Assigning work.** Staff in **Settings > Team** with **Active** ticked appear in **Assigned to**. The assignee's name is shown in the **Assigned** column and on the Overview.

### Invoicing a job

When a job is Completed and unpaid, click **Invoice**. One line is created: the job title, quantity and unit, with the unit price equal to the agreed (or quoted) amount divided by the quantity. Add tax or notes, save the draft, then **Send** and **Mark paid** in the Invoices tab. The job is then marked paid.

> **Watch out:** As in other vendor types, a job that is only ticked **Paid** counts as revenue only if it has an **Agreed (ZMW)** amount.

### First week checklist (AgriServices)

1. Finish the common checklist in Chapter 1.
2. List all your services with prices in **Services Catalog**.
3. Add your technicians under **Settings > Team**.
4. Add your trade papers (for example ZEMA or ZABS) as certificates.
5. Enter your confirmed jobs for the next two weeks in **Job Schedule**.
6. Invoice and collect payment for your first completed job.

### Daily routine (AgriServices)

1. **Overview**: new requests and upcoming jobs.
2. **Job Schedule**: quote the requests, schedule them, and assign people.
3. As jobs finish: **Completed**, completion notes, **Invoice**.
4. **Invoices**: chase unpaid ones.

### How farmers find and book you (AgriServices)

1. **Fill in** your Business profile (phone, WhatsApp, district, districts served, specialties such as "Irrigation, soil testing"), and your Services Catalog with prices. Your marketplace category is **Agricultural Services**.
2. **Appear in search.** Your profile is stored in the provider directory under Agricultural Services. In the farm workspace, the **AgriServices** page under Markets is a separate listings board where service offers are posted as listings with a price and a phone number; on that page the offers a visitor posts are kept on that visitor's own device. Farmers who need your service can also contact you directly, so a clear phone number and WhatsApp number matter most.
3. **When a request arrives.** In this version there is no booking button for AgriServices. A farmer phones or messages you. Enter the request under **Job Schedule** with **+ Job** and status **Requested**. This keeps your diary, clients and revenue complete.

# Part 7 — Vendor Workspaces: Agro Dealers, AgriSupply, AgriFood Sellers and Processors

This part covers the four vendor workspaces that **sell goods or process produce**: **Agro Dealer**, **AgriSupply**, **AgriFood Seller** and **Agro Processor**. They share one engine. The first three (dealer, supply, food seller) are almost the same screens with different names. The processor adds a set of screens for raw material intake, processing lots and traceability.

Part 6 — Vendor Workspaces: Basics, Vet Services, Equipment Hire, Transport and AgriServices explains the things every vendor workspace has: how the workspace is created, the sidebar, **Clients**, **Invoices**, **Revenue**, **Marketplace Listing**, **Users & Permissions** and **Settings**. This part does not repeat them. Where they matter to a sale, this part tells you which tab to open and says "see Part 6".

## How these workspaces work

### What is saved and where

Everything in these four workspaces is saved to your company account. That means your whole team sees the same catalogue, orders, stock and lots, on any phone or computer, and nothing is lost if you clear your browser. You do not need **Backup & Restore** for these tabs.

### The pieces that make up a goods workspace

| Piece | What it is | Where you work with it |
|---|---|---|
| Listing (product) | One line in your catalogue, with a name, price, unit and stock quantity. | Catalogue, Stock and Pricing tabs |
| Order | A sale to a customer, with one or more lines, a total, a delivery choice and a paid flag. | Orders tab |
| Invoice | A bill you can print or send to a customer, optionally built from an order. | **Invoices** tab (Part 6) |
| Lot (processor only) | One batch of raw material from one source, followed through processing to finished product. | Processor tabs |
| Services | Extra things you offer (delivery, spraying, toll milling). | **Marketplace Listing** tab (Part 6) |

### The golden rule about stock

Your **stock** is the **Quantity in stock** on each listing. It changes in only three ways:

1. You change it yourself with **adjust** or **Edit**.
2. An order that contains that listing moves from **New** to **Confirmed** (or further). The quantities on the order lines are taken off the listing, one time only.
3. You create the listing, which sets the opening quantity.

The app never changes stock when an order is cancelled, deleted or delivered later. The details are explained under Orders below.

### Terms used in this part

- **Listing**: a product you sell. When it is **Active** (shown as "live in marketplace") farmers and buyers can see it.
- **Walk-in order**: a sale to someone who came to your counter or phoned you. You type it in yourself.
- **Lot**: a processing batch with its own code, such as LOT-2026-0001.
- **Yield**: finished output divided by raw input, as a percentage.
- **Toll milling**: milling a farmer's own grain for a fee, without buying it.

> **Note:** The tab names change by business type. Agro Dealer has **Product Catalog**; AgriSupply has **Supply Catalog**; AgriFood Seller has **Produce Listings**. They open the same kind of screen.

---

## Part A — Agro Dealer

Your sidebar shows: **Overview**, **Product Catalog**, **Orders**, **Stock Management**, then the common tabs from Part 6 (**Clients**, **Invoices**, **Revenue**, **Marketplace Listing**, **Users & Permissions** if you hold the team permission, **Settings**).

### Overview (Agro Dealer)

**What it is for.** A one-screen summary of your shop: what you have listed, what orders are waiting, who your customers are and how much money you have received.

**Before you start.** Nothing. The workspace is created the first time you open it. Set your district and phone number under **Settings** (see Part 6) so customers can reach you.

**Step by step.**
1. Sign in and open your workspace. **Overview** is the first tab.
2. Read the four number tiles at the top.
3. Use the **Quick actions** buttons to jump to a tab.
4. Scroll to the **Open orders** table at the bottom.

**What you will see.**

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Active listings** | Products currently live. The small text shows how many are **low on stock**. |
| **Open orders** | Orders still in progress (New, Confirmed, Packed, Out for delivery). The small text shows how many are **new**. |
| **Customers** | Different customers who have ordered (and, for processors, suppliers too). |
| **Received (30 d)** | Money actually received in the last 30 days. |

Below the tiles are a **Revenue received (last 6 months)** chart with year-to-date and 30-day totals, and a **Quick actions** box with four buttons: **New order**, **Catalogue & prices**, **Stock** and **Invoices**. Under the buttons the app lists reminders such as unpaid invoices, listings low on stock, or "Add a phone number in Settings so customers can reach you".

The **Open orders** table shows the first eight open orders with these columns:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Ref** | Order number, for example SO-2026-0001. |
| **Customer** | Who ordered. |
| **Items** | Each line as "name × quantity". |
| **Total** | Order total in ZMW. |
| **Delivery** | The delivery date, "to arrange" if no date was set, or "collect" if the customer collects. |

A **Status** badge follows. If there are none you see "No open orders."

**Good to know.**
- "Low stock" always means a live listing with 10 units or fewer. It is a fixed number, not a setting you can change.
- Revenue only counts money received: orders marked **paid** and paid invoices. An order is never counted twice if you also invoiced it (see Revenue below).

### Product Catalog (Agro Dealer)

**What it is for.** This is your list of products. Each product you add here is also your marketplace listing: it is what farmers and buyers see. It holds the price, the unit and the stock level.

**Before you start.** Know the product name, the unit you sell it in (for example bag), the price per unit in ZMW and how many you have.

**Step by step: add a product.**
1. Open **Product Catalog**.
2. Click **+ Add product** (top right of the catalogue card).
3. Type the **Name** (required). Include the pack size, for example "Compound D fertiliser 50 kg".
4. Optionally type a **Description**, such as brand, nutrient grade or crop use.
5. Enter **Quantity in stock** (required, zero or more).
6. Choose the **Unit** from the list.
7. Enter **Price per unit (ZMW)** (required).
8. Optionally set **Minimum order**, **Location / pick-up** and **Available until**.
9. Tick **Delivery available** if you can deliver.
10. Click **Save**. The button shows "Saving…" and then a green "Saved." message appears.

The product is **live immediately** after you save a new one. There is no separate publish button.

**What you will see: the form fields.**

| Field | Required? | What to enter |
|---|---|---|
| **Name** | Yes | Product name and pack size. |
| **Description** | No | Short notes for buyers. |
| **Quantity in stock** | Yes | How many units you hold now. Zero or more. |
| **Unit** | No (starts as kg) | One of: kg, bag, litre, tonne, crate, tray, head, piece, box, bale. |
| **Price per unit (ZMW)** | Yes | Price for one unit. Zero or more. |
| **Minimum order** | No (starts at 0) | Smallest quantity a buyer should order. |
| **Location / pick-up** | No | Where customers collect, for example "Chongwe Main Road shop". |
| **Available until** | No | A date after which the offer ends. |
| **Delivery available** | No | Tick if you deliver. |

There are no category choices, photo upload or barcode fields in this form. Put the category (seed, fertiliser, chemical, feed, tools) in the **Name** or **Description**, for example "Maize seed SC513 10 kg - hybrid seed".

**What you will see: the catalogue table.** At the top are four tiles: **Listings** (with how many are live in the marketplace), **Stock value (asking)** (live stock times price), **Low stock (≤ 10)** and **Marketplace views** (how many times your listings have been viewed). The table below has these columns:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Item** | Name, with the description under it. |
| **Stock** | Quantity, with a small **adjust** link. It turns amber at 10 or fewer. |
| **Unit** and **Price** | Unit and price per unit. |
| **Min order** | Minimum order quantity. |
| **Delivery** | A tick if delivery is available, a dash if not. |

After those columns come **Status** and an action column (**Edit**, **Unlist** or **List**, **Remove**).

**Statuses of a listing.**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Active** | Live in the marketplace. New products start here. |
| **Draft** | Hidden from buyers. This is what **Unlist** sets. |
| **Sold** | Stock reached zero through an order. Hidden from buyers. |
| **Expired** | Hidden. Can be listed again with **List**. |
| **Negotiating** | A status the platform knows, but this tab has no button to set it. |

**Step by step: edit a product.**
1. Find the product in the table and click **Edit**.
2. Change any field. The window title is "Edit" followed by the product name.
3. Click **Save**.

**Step by step: hide a product from the marketplace.**
1. Click **Unlist** on an active product. Its status becomes **Draft**.
2. To show it again click **List**. This appears for products in **Draft**, **Expired** or **Sold**.

**Step by step: remove a product.**
1. Click **Remove** (red).
2. Confirm the question "Delete [name]? This cannot be undone."
3. The product disappears from your catalogue. Behind the scenes it is marked cancelled, and past orders keep their text.

**Good to know.**
- Prices are always in ZMW.
- If you change a price, orders already recorded keep the price they were created with.
- **Delivery available** is only a promise shown to buyers. It does not create deliveries by itself.
- **Marketplace views** counts views of your listings by others.

**If something goes wrong.**

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| "Listing not found" | The product was removed or belongs to another company. Reload the page. |
| "Quantity and price cannot be negative" | Enter zero or a positive number. |
| "Invalid status" | The status you chose is not allowed. Reload and try again. |
| "Not authenticated" | You were signed out. Sign in again. |

Error messages appear in a coloured banner at the top of the page for a few seconds.

### Orders (Agro Dealer)

**What it is for.** To record every sale and follow it from new to delivered and paid. Orders can be walk-in sales you type in yourself, or sales that come from the marketplace, a programme voucher scheme, or phone calls.

**How orders arrive.** Orders are entered in this tab by you or your staff. When a buyer finds your listing or profile and contacts you (phone, WhatsApp, or a visit), you record the order here, choosing the **Source** that matches. The Orders tab itself does not receive orders automatically from buyers. The **Source** field simply tells you where the sale came from.

**Before you start.** Add the products first (see Product Catalog), so you can pick them from a list and have prices and stock handled for you.

**Step by step: create an order.**
1. Open **Orders** and click **+ Order**. A window called **New order** opens.
2. Choose the customer. Type at least two letters into **Search platform client (farm / coop)…** and click the matching farm or cooperative. Or leave that empty and type the **walk-in name** (required in that case) and **Phone**.
3. In the first line, open **From catalogue** and pick a product. The **Item**, **Unit** and **Unit price** fill in by themselves, showing the stock in the list, for example "Compound D fertiliser 50 kg (100 bag)".
4. Type the **Qty**. You can still change the unit or unit price.
5. For another product click **+ Add line**. To drop a line click the **×** at the end of it.
6. For something not in your catalogue leave **From catalogue** on "— custom item —" and type the item, quantity, unit and price yourself. Stock is not touched for custom items.
7. Choose the **Source**: **Walk-in / phone**, **Marketplace** or **Programme voucher**.
8. Choose the **Status** (starts at **Confirmed**).
9. Enter a **Discount (ZMW)** if you give one.
10. Tick **Delivery required** if you are delivering. Two more boxes appear: **Delivery address** and **Delivery date**.
11. Add **Notes** if you wish.
12. Tick **Paid** if the customer has already paid.
13. Check the **Total** shown at the bottom (lines added up, minus discount).
14. Click **Record order**. You see "Order recorded."

**What you will see: the order form.**

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Source** | **Walk-in / phone**, **Marketplace** or **Programme voucher**. |
| **Status** | **New**, **Confirmed**, **Packed** or **Delivered** when you create the order. |
| **Discount (ZMW)** | Amount taken off the total. The total never goes below zero. |
| **Delivery required** | Shows address and date fields. |
| **Paid** | Tick if money is already received. |
| **Notes** | Free text. |

**What you will see: the orders table.** At the top right are a status filter (**All statuses**, **New**, **Confirmed**, **Packed**, **Out For Delivery**, **Delivered**, **Cancelled**) and the **+ Order** button. The columns are:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Ref / date** | Order number (SO-year-number), the date created and the source. |
| **Customer** | Name and phone. |
| **Items** | Each line as "name × quantity unit @ price". |
| **Total** | Total in ZMW, with the discount shown under it if any. |
| **Fulfilment** | The delivery address and date, or "Collection". |

Then come **Paid**, **Status** and an action column. In **Paid** you see a tick if paid, otherwise a green **mark paid** link.

**Status flow.** The action column shows only the next steps that make sense:

| Current status | Buttons you see |
|---|---|
| **New** | **Confirmed**, **Cancelled**, and also **Delete** |
| **Confirmed** | **Packed**, **Out For Delivery**, **Delivered**, **Cancelled** |
| **Packed** (Packed / Ready) | **Out For Delivery**, **Delivered** |
| **Out For Delivery** | **Delivered** |
| **Delivered** | None. If it is not paid, an **Invoice** button shows. |
| **Cancelled** | None. |

Click a button to move the order. The app shows a message such as "SO-2026-0001 → Confirmed".

**Effect on stock.** This is the most important rule in the Orders tab.
- While an order is **New**, stock is **not** touched.
- The first time an order becomes **Confirmed**, **Packed**, **Out For Delivery** or **Delivered**, each line that was picked from the catalogue takes its quantity off that product's stock. This happens only once per order.
- If you create an order straight at **Confirmed** (the default), stock is taken off at once.
- If stock would go below zero it stops at zero. The app does not warn you or refuse the sale.
- If a product reaches zero, its status changes to **Sold** and it disappears from the marketplace until you add stock.
- **Cancelling** an order does **not** put stock back. Add it back yourself with **adjust**.
- Custom lines (typed without choosing a catalogue product) never change stock.

**Payment.**
- Click **mark paid** when the customer pays. There is no payment method field on the order itself.
- If you raise an **Invoice** from the order and mark the invoice paid on the **Invoices** tab, the order is marked paid too (see Part 6).
- Revenue counts a paid order once. If an invoice linked to the order is also paid, the invoice is counted and the order is not counted twice.
- An order's revenue date is its delivered date, otherwise its delivery date, otherwise the day it was created.

**Invoice from an order.**
1. When an order is **Delivered** and not yet paid, click **Invoice**.
2. A window opens saying "Invoice for [order number]". The customer and lines are taken from the order.
3. Add a **Tax / VAT amount**, a **Due date** and **Notes / payment details**, for example your mobile money number.
4. Click **Save draft**. Then use the **Invoices** tab to **Send** it and mark it paid (Part 6).

**Delete an order.** The **Delete** link shows only for **New** orders. Confirm "Delete [ref]? This cannot be undone." The app itself only allows deleting orders that are **New** or **Cancelled**. Delivered and confirmed orders stay on record.

**Good to know.**
- You cannot edit an order after saving it. To correct a mistake on a **New** order, delete it and enter it again. For a confirmed one, cancel it, restore stock with **adjust**, and enter a new order.
- Set the delivery date and address when you create the order. They cannot be changed later.
- Marking the order **Delivered** records the delivery time automatically.

**If something goes wrong.**

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| "An order needs at least one item" | Every line had an empty item name. Fill in at least one item. |
| "Give a client organisation or a client name" | Choose a platform customer or type a walk-in name. |
| "Item quantity and unit price must be numbers" | A quantity or price is not a number. |
| "Item values cannot be negative" | Use zero or more. |
| "Client organisation not found" | The platform customer no longer exists. Use a walk-in name. |
| "Order not found" | The order was removed. Reload. |
| "Only new or cancelled orders can be deleted" | You tried to delete a confirmed or delivered order. Cancel it instead. |
| "No orders yet. Record counter sales here; confirming an order deducts stock from the linked listings." | The empty list message. |

### Stock Management (Agro Dealer)

**What it is for.** To see how much you hold of each product and to correct it when stock arrives, gets damaged or is sold outside the app.

**Step by step: add or remove stock.**
1. Open **Stock Management**. The card is called **Stock on hand**.
2. Find the product and click the small **adjust** link next to its stock number.
3. A window called "Adjust stock" followed by the product name opens. It shows the current quantity.
4. Type a number. Use a positive number for stock received (for example 50) and a negative one for shrinkage or off-platform sales (for example -3).
5. Check the **New level** shown under the box.
6. Click **Apply**. You see "Stock updated."

The **Apply** button stays disabled until you type something.

**What you will see.** The screen is the same table as **Product Catalog** (same tiles, same columns, **+ Add product**, **Edit**, **Unlist/List**, **Remove**). Only the card title changes. Use **Stock Management** for counting and receiving, and **Product Catalog** for prices and descriptions.

**Low stock and reorder.**
- Anything at 10 units or fewer shows in amber and is counted in **Low stock (≤ 10)**, in the Overview tile, and in the reminder "listing(s) low on stock".
- There is no automatic reorder, supplier or purchase list in this workspace. Use the low stock number as your cue to buy more, then add the delivery with **adjust**.
- Stock cannot go below zero. If you type a negative number larger than what you hold, the new level shows as 0.
- If a product was **Sold** (zero) and you add stock, it is switched back to **Active** automatically.
- If you adjust a product down to zero, it stays **Active** with a quantity of 0. Click **Unlist** if you do not want it shown.

**If something goes wrong.** Errors appear as a banner, for example "Quantity and price cannot be negative" or "Listing not found". Reload and try again.

### How buyers find and order from you (Agro Dealer)

1. Your **Marketplace Listing** tab (Part 6) holds your public profile: name, description, contact numbers, district and the services you offer. Fill it in completely. Without a phone number buyers cannot reach you.
2. Your **Active** products are published to the marketplace listings, with price, unit, minimum order, location and delivery option.
3. Buyers can view your listings. The **Marketplace views** tile counts these views.
4. A buyer contacts you, or comes to the shop. You create the order in **Orders** (choose **Marketplace** as the source for sales that began on the platform).
5. Walk-in customers are recorded in the same way with **Walk-in / phone**.
6. Customers who are farm or cooperative accounts on the platform can be linked by searching for them, so they appear properly in **Clients** (Part 6).

### First week checklist (Agro Dealer)

1. Open the workspace and check **Overview** loads.
2. Go to **Settings** and fill in district and phone (Part 6).
3. Fill in **Marketplace Listing** (Part 6).
4. Add your top 10 products in **Product Catalog** with correct stock and price.
5. Add your delivery and advisory services on the **Marketplace Listing** tab (the hint says: Delivery, spraying, soil testing or agronomy advice you offer alongside products).
6. Record one test sale on **Orders** to practise the steps, then cancel it and restore stock.
7. Invite staff under **Users & Permissions** (Part 6).
8. Create one invoice from a delivered order.

### Daily routine (Agro Dealer)

- Morning: open **Overview**; check **Open orders** and the low stock reminder.
- During the day: record each sale in **Orders** the moment it happens, and move orders along as they are packed and delivered.
- When goods arrive from your suppliers: **Stock Management**, **adjust**, positive number.
- End of day: mark paid orders with **mark paid**, issue invoices for credit customers.
- Weekly: review **Revenue** and update prices in **Product Catalog**.

> **Example:** Mwansa runs an agro-dealer shop in Chongwe and sells 20 bags of Compound D to Mrs Phiri, who collects them and pays cash.
> 1. Product Catalog has "Compound D fertiliser 50 kg", unit bag, price 850, stock 100.
> 2. Orders, **+ Order**. Walk-in name "Mrs Phiri", phone 0966 111 222.
> 3. From catalogue: pick Compound D. Qty 20. Unit price 850 fills in. Subtotal 17,000.
> 4. Discount 500 for a loyal customer. Total shows ZMW 16,500.
> 5. Source **Walk-in / phone**, Status **Confirmed**, tick **Paid**. **Record order**.
> 6. Stock falls from 100 to 80 straight away. **Received (30 d)** rises by 16,500.
> 7. When she collects, click **Delivered** on the order.

---

## Part B — AgriSupply Provider

AgriSupply is for businesses that sell farm supplies in volume: packaging, crates, irrigation kit, farm chemicals and storage. The workspace is the dealer's, plus a **Deliveries** tab. Your sidebar shows: **Overview**, **Supply Catalog**, **Purchase Orders**, **Stock Management**, **Deliveries**, and the common tabs from Part 6. The services heading on the **Marketplace Listing** tab is "Services offered".

### Overview (AgriSupply)

It works exactly like the Agro Dealer **Overview** above: the tiles **Active listings**, **Open orders**, **Customers** and **Received (30 d)**, the revenue chart, **Quick actions** (**New order**, **Catalogue & prices**, **Stock**, **Invoices**), and the **Open orders** table.

### Supply Catalog

Same screen and rules as the Agro Dealer **Product Catalog**. The button reads **+ Add supply item**, the window is titled "Add supply item", and the hint on the card says: "Packaging, crates, irrigation kit, farm chemicals, storage. Buyers browse these in the marketplace." The empty message is "No supply items yet. Add your first one to appear in the marketplace."

Use these tips for supplies:
- Put sizes in the name: "Plastic crate 40 L", "Drip line 16 mm 1000 m roll".
- Pick the right unit from the list: **crate**, **piece**, **box**, **bale**, **litre**, **tonne**.
- Use **Minimum order** for bulk-only items, for example 10 crates.
- Use **Available until** for a promotion that ends on a date.

### Purchase Orders

This is the same **Orders** screen as the Agro Dealer, with the tab renamed. Here "purchase order" means an order a customer places **with you** for your supplies. It is not an order you place with your own suppliers.

Everything explained under Agro Dealer **Orders** applies: creating an order, the **Source** choices, the status buttons, the effect on stock, **mark paid**, **Invoice**, and **Delete**. Order numbers still start with SO.

### Stock Management (AgriSupply)

Same as the Agro Dealer **Stock Management**: **Stock on hand**, **adjust**, **New level**, **Apply**, and the low stock limit of 10 units.

### Deliveries

**What it is for.** To see only the orders that need delivering, so your drivers or dispatcher can work through them.

**Before you start.** Orders appear here only if **Delivery required** was ticked when the order was created. Remember to give the **Delivery address** and **Delivery date** at that time. You cannot edit them afterwards.

**Step by step.**
1. Open **Deliveries**. The card is titled **Deliveries**.
2. Use the status filter if you want only one stage, for example **Packed**.
3. Read the **Deliver to** column for the address and date. It shows "address n/a" if none was typed and "date to arrange" if no date was set.
4. As the order progresses click **Packed**, then **Out For Delivery**, then **Delivered**.
5. After delivery click **mark paid** (if the customer paid on delivery) or **Invoice** (if they pay later).
6. To add an order that is delivered, click **+ Order** and tick **Delivery required**.

**What you will see.** The same table as **Purchase Orders**, with the column **Fulfilment** renamed **Deliver to**. Cancelled orders are hidden. Orders that do not need delivery are hidden.

**Good to know.**
- The delivery status flow is the same as order status: **Confirmed** (or **Packed**) → **Out For Delivery** → **Delivered**.
- There is no driver, vehicle, route or proof-of-delivery field. Use **Notes** on the order for the driver's name and vehicle, and keep a signed delivery note on paper or in a photo outside the app.
- When you click **Delivered**, the app records the time. This is your delivery record.
- If you use a transport partner, see the **Transport & Logistics** workspace in Part 6.

**If something goes wrong.** The empty list says "No deliveries scheduled." If you expected an order, check that **Delivery required** was ticked, and that the order is not **Cancelled**.

### How buyers find and order from you (AgriSupply)

Buyers browse your supply items in the marketplace and contact you from your profile. You record the order in **Purchase Orders** with the **Marketplace** source, then deliver it through **Deliveries**.

### First week checklist (AgriSupply)

1. Complete **Settings** and **Marketplace Listing** (Part 6).
2. Add your supply items with correct units and stock in **Supply Catalog**.
3. Set **Minimum order** for bulk items.
4. Record a test purchase order with delivery and walk it through to **Delivered**.
5. Add your delivery service in **Marketplace Listing** services.
6. Add dispatchers and sales staff in **Users & Permissions** (Part 6).

### Daily routine (AgriSupply)

- Open **Deliveries** first. Move orders to **Out For Delivery** as vehicles leave.
- Mark **Delivered** when you get the signed note back.
- Receive new stock with **adjust** in **Stock Management**.
- Invoice credit customers from delivered orders.

> **Example:** A packaging supplier in Lusaka sells 200 crates to a vegetable cooperative. Order from the catalogue, qty 200, unit crate, delivery required, address "Chongwe Coop Shed", date next Tuesday, Status **Confirmed**. Stock drops by 200. On Tuesday, **Out For Delivery**, then **Delivered**. Click **Invoice**, add the mobile money details in **Notes / payment details**, and **Save draft**.

---

## Part C — AgriFood Seller

AgriFood Sellers sell fresh produce, grain, eggs, dairy and processed food. Listings are shown to buyers, processors and exporters. Your sidebar shows: **Overview**, **Produce Listings**, **Buyer Orders**, **Pricing & Stock**, and the common tabs from Part 6. The services heading on the **Marketplace Listing** tab is "Services offered", with the hint "Delivery, grading, packaging or aggregation services."

### Overview (AgriFood Seller)

Same tiles and table as the Agro Dealer **Overview**: **Active listings**, **Open orders**, **Customers**, **Received (30 d)**, the revenue chart, **Quick actions** and the **Open orders** table.

> **Note:** On an AgriFood Seller workspace, the **Catalogue & prices** and **Stock** quick-action buttons may not take you anywhere, because they point to tab names that are called **Produce Listings** and **Pricing & Stock** here. If a quick action does nothing, use the sidebar instead. **New order** and **Invoices** work normally.

### Produce Listings

**What it is for.** Your list of produce lines. Each line is a marketplace listing, for example "Tomatoes, grade A, 20 kg crate" or "Table eggs, tray of 30".

**Step by step: add a produce line.**
1. Open **Produce Listings**.
2. Click **+ Add produce line**. The window is called "Add produce line".
3. Fill in **Name**, **Quantity in stock**, **Unit** and **Price per unit (ZMW)**. These four are the essentials.
4. Optionally add **Description**, **Minimum order**, **Location / pick-up** and **Available until**.
5. Tick **Delivery available** if you deliver.
6. Click **Save**.

The form is the same one used by all goods sellers; see the field table under Agro Dealer, Product Catalog. For produce, these fields are used as follows.

| Field | How to use it for produce |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Produce, variety and pack: "Irish potatoes, 50 kg bag". |
| **Description** | Quality grade and condition: "Grade A, washed, no bruising". |
| **Quantity in stock** | How much you have ready to sell now. |
| **Unit** | kg, bag, crate, tray, head (for livestock), tonne, bale, piece, box or litre. |
| **Minimum order** | Smallest quantity per buyer, for example 5 bags. |
| **Location / pick-up** | Farm gate, depot or market stall. |
| **Available until** | The last date the produce is fresh or on offer. |

> **Watch out:** This form has **no separate fields** for quality grade or harvest date. Write the grade and harvest date in the **Description**, for example "Grade A. Harvested 14-10-2026." Use **Available until** for the date after which you will no longer sell it.

Everything else is the same as the Agro Dealer **Product Catalog**: the four tiles, the status badges, **Edit**, **Unlist**, **List** and **Remove**, and the same error messages. The hint on the card reads: "Fresh produce, grain, eggs, dairy, processed food. Listings appear to buyers, processors and exporters." The empty message is "No produce lines yet. Add your first one to appear in the marketplace."

**Good to know.**
- Fresh produce goes off. Unlist (**Unlist**) a line as soon as the batch is gone and add a new line, or use **Available until**.
- A line that sells out through orders switches to **Sold** and is hidden. When you add stock with **adjust**, it becomes **Active** again.

### Buyer Orders

This is the standard **Orders** screen, described in full under Agro Dealer, Orders. Buyers here are often traders, hotels, schools, processors and exporters. You can search for a buyer who has a platform account, or type a walk-in name and phone.

Key points for produce sellers:
- Use the **Discount (ZMW)** for bulk deals.
- Tick **Delivery required** and give the address and date when you will deliver. The dates cannot be edited later.
- Produce sold by weight can be entered with fractions, for example 12.5 kg.
- Orders move through **New**, **Confirmed**, **Packed**, **Out For Delivery**, **Delivered** and **Cancelled**, and deduct catalogue stock when confirmed.
- There is no **Deliveries** tab in this workspace. Filter **Buyer Orders** by status instead, and check the **Fulfilment** column for the address and date.

### Pricing & Stock

**What it is for.** A place to update prices and quantities quickly each day as the market moves.

**Step by step: update a price.**
1. Open **Pricing & Stock**. The card is called **Prices & offers**.
2. Click **Edit** on the line.
3. Change **Price per unit (ZMW)**.
4. Click **Save**.

**Step by step: update the quantity.**
1. Click **adjust** next to the quantity.
2. Enter a positive number to add stock or a negative number to take stock away (for example after wastage).
3. Click **Apply**.

The screen is the same table as **Produce Listings**. There is no separate price history and no special offer type; to run a special offer, lower the price or set **Minimum order** and **Available until**, then edit the price back when it ends.

**Good to know.**
- The **Stock value (asking)** tile adds up quantity times price for live lines. It is a useful figure to compare before and after a price change.
- Low stock is 10 units or fewer.

### How buyers find and order from you (AgriFood Seller)

1. Complete **Marketplace Listing** (Part 6) so buyers see who you are and where you are.
2. Keep produce lines **Active** with up-to-date price and stock.
3. Buyers contact you. Record the order in **Buyer Orders** with the **Marketplace** source.
4. Deliver, mark **Delivered**, mark **paid**, and invoice where needed.

### First week checklist (AgriFood Seller)

1. Fill in **Settings** and **Marketplace Listing** (Part 6).
2. Add your main produce lines with grade and harvest date in the description.
3. Set **Minimum order** and **Delivery available** for each.
4. Record one test order, confirm it, check stock reduced, then cancel and adjust stock back.
5. Add your delivery, grading, packaging or aggregation services in **Marketplace Listing**.

### Daily routine (AgriFood Seller)

- Morning: check market prices, then update **Pricing & Stock**.
- Add the day's harvest with **adjust**.
- Record orders as they come in.
- Evening: unlist anything that has gone, mark paid orders, check **Overview** for money received.

> **Example:** A grower in Kafue lists "Tomatoes, grade A, 20 kg crate" at ZMW 180, 60 crates in stock, minimum order 5, delivery available. A Lusaka restaurant orders 10 crates. You record a **Walk-in / phone** order with delivery to the restaurant on Thursday, status **Confirmed**. Stock drops to 50. On Thursday you click **Out For Delivery**, then **Delivered**, then **mark paid** after they pay.

---

## Part D — Agro Processor

An agro processor turns raw produce (maize into mealie meal, soya into oil and cake, milk into yoghurt) into finished goods. The workspace tracks the **lot**: each batch of raw material from one source, how it was processed, what came out, and who bought it. Your sidebar shows: **Overview**, **Farm Sourcing**, **Processing Logs**, **Inventory**, **Traceability**, **Orders**, and the common tabs from Part 6. The services heading on the **Marketplace Listing** tab is "Processing services", with the hint "Toll milling, drying, packaging or cold storage you offer to farmers."

### The lot, in plain words

A lot is one record that follows a batch from the day it arrives to the day it is sold. It carries:
- a **lot code** made by the app, in the form LOT-year-number (for example LOT-2026-0001);
- the **source** (a farm on the platform, or an outside supplier you name);
- the **raw material** with quantity, unit and price paid;
- the **dates**: received, processing started (set by the app), completed (set by the app);
- the **output**: finished product, quantity, unit, sold so far, expiry date;
- the **quality check**: pass, fail or not tested, plus notes;
- a **status**.

**Lot statuses.**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Received** | Raw material has arrived and is waiting. Shown as "Raw material received". |
| **Processing** | Work has started. The start date is filled in for you. |
| **Completed** | Finished product is ready. The completed date is filled in for you if empty. |
| **Sold Out** | All of the finished product has been sold. |
| **Rejected** | The raw material failed quality control or was refused. |

### Overview (Processor)

**What it is for.** To see lots in progress, finished stock and money received.

**What you will see.**

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Lots in process** | Lots that are **Received** or **Processing**. The small text shows total lots. |
| **Finished stock** | Units not yet sold across all **Completed** lots (output minus sold). |
| **Open orders** | Orders still in progress. The small text shows total orders. |
| **Received (30 d)** | Money actually received in the last 30 days. |

The **Quick actions** buttons are **Record intake** (opens **Farm Sourcing**), **Processing**, **Inventory** and **Orders**. The **Recent lots** table lists the latest eight lots with these columns: **Lot**, **Source**, **Input**, **Product**, **Yield** and **Status**. If there are none you see "No lots yet. Record raw-material intake under Farm Sourcing."

The revenue chart and reminders are the same as for the other goods sellers.

### Farm Sourcing

**What it is for.** To record raw material arriving at your gate, who supplied it, how much you paid and where it came from. This is the start of traceability.

**Before you start.** Know the supplier (a farm or cooperative on AGRINUXES, or a name for an outside supplier), the commodity, the quantity and unit, and the total price paid.

**Step by step: record an intake.**
1. Open **Farm Sourcing**.
2. Click **+ Record intake**. A window called **Record raw-material intake** opens.
3. To pick a farm on the platform, type at least two letters in **Source farm (platform)** and click the matching farm or cooperative. The **Source district** fills in for you.
4. For an outside supplier leave that empty and type the **or supplier name (off-platform)**. This box is switched off while a platform farm is chosen.
5. Check or type the **Source district**.
6. Set **Received on** (required; today by default).
7. Type the **Raw material** (required), for example "Maize grain".
8. Enter **Quantity** (required) and choose the **Unit** (kg, tonne, litre, bag, crate, head).
9. Enter **Price paid to source (ZMW)**, the total for the whole load, not per kilogram.
10. Leave **Status** at **Received**.
11. Optionally add **QC result**, **QC notes** and **Notes**.
12. Click **Save lot**. You see "Lot saved." and a lot code is created.

**What you will see: the intake form.**

| Field | Required? | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Source farm (platform)** | No | Search a farm or cooperative on AGRINUXES. |
| **or supplier name (off-platform)** | No | A supplier who is not on the platform. |
| **Source district** | No | Where the raw material came from. |
| **Received on** | Yes | Date of delivery. |
| **Raw material** | Yes | Commodity name. |
| **Quantity** and **Unit** | Yes | Amount received. Must be above zero. |
| **Price paid to source (ZMW)** | No | Total paid to the supplier. |
| **Status** | No | **Received** to start. |

The window also has an **Output (fill when processing completes)** section; see Processing Logs below. If you give neither a platform farm nor a supplier name, the source shows as a dash and is grouped under "Unknown" in the suppliers table.

**What you will see: the screen.**
- Four tiles: **Raw material received** (all input quantities added together, whatever the unit), **Suppliers**, **In process** and **Paid to farmers** (total price paid).
- A **Suppliers** card with **Supplier**, **District**, **Lots**, **Quantity supplied**, **Paid** and **Last delivery**. This shows how much each supplier has delivered, biggest first.
- A lot table titled **Farm sourcing — raw material intake** with a search box ("Search lot, supplier, product"). Search matches the lot code, supplier, product and raw material.

The lot table has these columns:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Lot** | The lot code, with the expiry date under it if set. |
| **Received** | Date received. |
| **Source** | Supplier and district. |
| **Input** | Quantity, unit and raw material. |
| **Product / output** | Finished product, quantity and unit, or a dash. |

Then **Yield**, **Remaining**, **QC** (pass in green, fail in red, dash if untested), **Status** and the action links.

**Good to know.**
- The supplier you pick from the platform is also added to your **Clients** tab as a **supplier** (Part 6), so you can see all your sourcing partners in one place.
- The unit totals mix kilograms, tonnes and bags, so treat the **Raw material received** tile as a rough guide unless you always use the same unit.
- **Paid to farmers** is only what you typed. This screen does not pay the supplier; it only records what you paid. Pay through your usual channel.

**If something goes wrong.**

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| "Input quantity must be positive" | Enter a quantity above zero. |
| "Source organisation not found" | The platform farm you chose no longer exists. Use the off-platform name. |
| "Invalid status" | Reload and pick a status from the list. |
| "Batch not found" | The lot was deleted. Reload. |

### Processing Logs

**What it is for.** To follow each lot while it is being worked on, and to record what came out.

**Step by step: start processing.**
1. Open **Processing Logs**. It lists only lots that are **Received** or **Processing**. The card is called **Processing — lots in progress**.
2. Find the lot and click **Start** (amber). The status becomes **Processing** and the start date is filled in. The **Start** link shows only for **Received** lots.

**Step by step: finish a lot and record the output.**
1. Click **Update** on the lot.
2. In the **Output** section type the **Finished product** (for example "Breakfast mealie meal 25 kg").
3. Enter the **Output quantity** and its **Unit**.
4. Optionally fill **Expiry / best before**, **QC result** (**Not tested**, **Pass** or **Fail**) and **QC notes** (for example "Moisture 12.5 %, aflatoxin < 10 ppb").
5. Set **Status** to **Completed**. The completed date is filled in for you.
6. Click **Save lot**.

The lot now leaves **Processing Logs** and appears in **Inventory**.

If the raw material fails, set **Status** to **Rejected** and write the reason in **QC notes**.

**What you will see: the output fields.**

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Finished product** | Name and pack of what you made. |
| **Output quantity** and **Unit** | How much came out, and in what unit (bags, kg, litres). |
| **Quantity sold** | How much of the output is sold so far. Starts at 0. |
| **Expiry / best before** | Shelf life date, shown under the lot code. |
| **QC result** | **Not tested**, **Pass** or **Fail**. |
| **QC notes** | Test readings and remarks. |
| **Notes** | Anything else. |

**Yield and loss.**
- The app shows **Yield** as output divided by input times 100, rounded to one decimal place. It simply divides the two numbers and does not convert units.
- There is no separate loss field. Loss is what is left when you subtract: input minus output, in the same unit.
- So for useful yield figures, record the output in the **same unit** as the input. If you receive 5,000 kg of maize and record the output as 160 bags, the screen shows 3.2%, which is not a real yield. If you record 4,000 kg of meal, it shows 80%.
- If you want to count bags as well, put the bag count in the **Finished product** name ("Breakfast mealie meal, 25 kg bags") and use kg for quantity.

**Good to know.**
- **Delete** (red) removes a lot completely, after you confirm "Delete [lot code]? This cannot be undone." There is no restore.
- Everything on a lot can be edited again later with **Edit**.
- Lots do not take anything out of your farm Inventory or Finance. This workspace is separate from the farm workspace (Part 4). Costs and stock here live only on the lots and listings.

### Inventory

**What it is for.** Your finished goods. It combines two lists, one above the other.

**Part 1: finished-goods lots.** The first card, **Finished-goods inventory**, shows **Completed** lots that still have some product left to sell (output greater than quantity sold). The **Remaining** column shows output minus quantity sold. The **+ Record intake** button is hidden here. The empty message is "No finished stock. Complete a lot with an output quantity to see it here."

To record sales against a lot:
1. Click **Edit** on the lot.
2. Raise **Quantity sold** by the amount sold.
3. Click **Save lot**. **Remaining** and the **Finished stock** tile on the Overview drop.
4. When everything is sold, set **Status** to **Sold Out**.

> **Watch out:** Orders you record do **not** change **Quantity sold** on a lot. Update the lot yourself, or your **Remaining** and **Finished stock** numbers will be too high.

**Part 2: product catalogue.** Below the lots is the same catalogue screen the dealers use, for the finished goods you offer for sale. The hint reads: "Finished goods offered for sale (mealie meal, oil, feed, packaged produce). Stock here is what buyers can order; lots under Processing track where it came from." The button is **+ Add finished product**, the window is titled "Add finished product", and the card is called "Catalogue" with a count. All the dealer rules apply: **Name**, **Quantity in stock**, **Unit**, **Price per unit (ZMW)**, **Minimum order**, **Location / pick-up**, **Available until**, **Delivery available**, **Edit**, **Unlist**, **List**, **Remove** and **adjust**. See Agro Dealer, Product Catalog.

The two lists are separate:
- The **catalogue** quantity is what buyers can order and what orders reduce.
- The **lot** quantity is your production record and traceability.

When a lot is completed, add the finished goods to the catalogue by creating or adjusting a product with the new quantity. The app does not do this for you.

### Traceability

**What it is for.** To prove where a product came from. A recall, a buyer's question, or a quality audit can be answered by finding the lot.

**How batch codes link the chain.** Each lot has one code. It links farm to lot to product like this:
1. **Farm**: the supplier or farm named under **Source**, with district, date received, the quantity and the price paid.
2. **Lot**: the code (LOT-year-number), the raw material, the processing dates, the QC result and notes.
3. **Product**: the **Finished product** name, output quantity, expiry date and how much has been sold.

Print or write the lot code on the packaging of the finished product. If a customer reports a problem, search the code to see the source farm and the quality test.

**Step by step: look up a lot.**
1. Open **Traceability**.
2. Type in the search box ("Search lot, supplier, product") a lot code, farm name, product name or raw material. The list narrows as you type.
3. Read the row: the lot code (and expiry), received date, source and district, input, product and output, yield, remaining, QC result and status.
4. Click **Edit** to open the full lot with all the fields and QC notes.

**What you will see.** The card is called **Traceability — lot lookup**. It shows every lot you have, in every status, in the same table as above. The line above the table says: "Every lot code links finished product back to the farm it came from, the day it was received, QC result and expiry. Print the lot code on packaging." You can also use **+ Record intake** here.

**Good to know.**
- Traceability is only as good as the data. Always record the source and the QC result.
- The lot code is made by the app and cannot be changed.
- Buyers cannot look up a lot code on a public page. The lookup is inside your workspace only. Give the buyer the details from the lot when they ask.

### Orders (Processor)

The **Orders** tab is the standard orders screen (see Agro Dealer, Orders). Buyers are wholesalers, shops, schools and farmers. Lines can be picked from your finished-product catalogue (found under **Inventory**) so that stock is deducted when the order is confirmed.

Remember that orders reduce the catalogue only, not the lots (see Inventory above).

### Processing services (toll milling and similar)

Toll milling, drying, packaging and cold storage are services you offer to farmers who bring their own produce. They are set up on the **Marketplace Listing** tab in the block titled **Processing services**. Click **+ Add** and fill in the form.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Name** (required) | For example "Toll milling - maize". |
| **Category** | For example Milling or Drying. |
| **Pricing model** | Fixed price, Per unit, Per hectare, Per head / animal, Per hour, Per day, Per km, Negotiable / quote, or Free. |
| **Price** and **Price max (range)** | Price, or a range from price to price max. |
| **Unit label (e.g. 50 kg bag)** | What the price is for, for example "per 50 kg bag". |
| **Lead time (days)** | How long the job takes to start. Blank means "Immediate". |
| **Available** | Untick to pause the service. |
| **Description** | Details. |

These services appear on your public profile. They are not orders: the workspace has no special tab for toll milling jobs. To record a toll milling job, enter it as an **Orders** entry with a **custom item** (for example "Toll milling - maize, 20 bags"), or raise an invoice on the **Invoices** tab. Custom items never change stock.

You can also record a toll milling intake in **Farm Sourcing** if you want a lot code and traceability: enter the farmer as the source, put the raw material, and leave **Price paid to source (ZMW)** empty because you did not buy it. Add "Toll milling for the farmer, not purchased" in **Notes**.

### How buyers find and order from you (Processor)

1. Your profile in the marketplace shows your processing services and contact details (**Marketplace Listing**, Part 6).
2. Your finished products (in the **Inventory** catalogue) are public marketplace listings with price, unit, minimum order and delivery option.
3. Farmers who want toll milling see your **Processing services** and contact you.
4. Buyers of finished goods contact you; you record their order in **Orders** (source **Marketplace** or **Walk-in / phone**).
5. Farmers who supply you appear as suppliers in **Farm Sourcing** and in **Clients**.

### First week checklist (Processor)

1. Complete **Settings** and **Marketplace Listing** (Part 6), including your processing services.
2. Add your finished products in **Inventory** with price and opening stock.
3. Record every delivery of raw material in **Farm Sourcing**, with source, quantity and price.
4. Move each lot through **Start** and **Update** until **Completed**, with output and QC.
5. Check **Traceability**: search a lot code and confirm the source is shown.
6. Record your first sale in **Orders**, then update **Quantity sold** on the lot.

### Daily routine (Processor)

- Record each arrival of raw material before it is tipped into the store.
- In **Processing Logs**, click **Start** when work begins. Update the lot when the run ends.
- Add the finished goods to the catalogue stock with **adjust**.
- Record orders. After sales, update **Quantity sold** on the lot.
- Weekly: review **Suppliers** for who delivered most, and **Paid to farmers**.

> **Example:** Milling 5 tonnes of maize in Chongwe.
> 1. **Farm Sourcing**, **+ Record intake**. Source farm: "Green Acres" (picked from the search). Raw material "Maize grain", Quantity 5000, Unit kg, Price paid ZMW 17,500, Received on today. **Save lot**. Lot LOT-2026-0001 appears with status **Received**.
> 2. On the day of milling open **Processing Logs** and click **Start**. Status is now **Processing**.
> 3. After milling click **Update**. Finished product "Breakfast mealie meal, 25 kg bags". Output quantity 4000, unit kg. Expiry date six months ahead. QC result **Pass**, QC notes "Moisture 12.5 %". Status **Completed**. **Save lot**.
> 4. The lot shows Yield 80%, Remaining 4000 kg, and appears in **Inventory**. Loss is 1,000 kg (the bran and dust).
> 5. In the **Inventory** catalogue click **+ Add finished product**: "Breakfast mealie meal 25 kg", unit bag, Quantity 160, price 250. It is live in the marketplace.
> 6. A shop orders 40 bags. In **Orders**, pick the product, Qty 40. Stock falls to 120.
> 7. Edit the lot: **Quantity sold** 1000 (40 bags × 25 kg). **Remaining** becomes 3000 kg.
> 8. A buyer asks for the source: search "LOT-2026-0001" in **Traceability** and read the farm, district, date and QC result.

---

## Comparing the four workspaces

### Which tab for which job

| Job | Agro Dealer | AgriSupply | AgriFood Seller | Agro Processor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| List what you sell | **Product Catalog** | **Supply Catalog** | **Produce Listings** | **Inventory** (lower catalogue) |
| Record a sale | **Orders** | **Purchase Orders** | **Buyer Orders** | **Orders** |
| Change stock | **Stock Management** | **Stock Management** | **Pricing & Stock** | **Inventory**, **adjust** |
| Change price | **Product Catalog**, **Edit** | **Supply Catalog**, **Edit** | **Pricing & Stock**, **Edit** | **Inventory**, **Edit** |
| Delivery list | **Orders** (filter) | **Deliveries** | **Buyer Orders** (filter) | **Orders** (filter) |
| Raw material in | Not available | Not available | Not available | **Farm Sourcing** |
| Production run | Not available | Not available | Not available | **Processing Logs** |
| Prove origin | Not available | Not available | Not available | **Traceability** |
| Optional services | **Marketplace Listing** | **Marketplace Listing** | **Marketplace Listing** | **Marketplace Listing** |

The services block on **Marketplace Listing** is headed "Services offered" for dealers, AgriSupply and AgriFood Sellers, and "Processing services" for processors.

### What is the same in all four

- The statuses of an order and the stock rule.
- The listing form and statuses (**Active**, **Draft**, **Sold**, **Expired**).
- The low stock limit of 10.
- Invoices, Clients, Revenue, Settings and Users & Permissions (Part 6).
- Everything is saved to the company account and shared by the team.

### Where the money shows

An order counts as revenue only when it is marked **paid** (or when its invoice is paid). Paid invoices, paid orders and the other vendor payments all feed the **Revenue** tab and the **Received (30 d)** tile. See Part 6.

# Part 8 — Buyers, Government, Extension Officers and Programme Partners

This part is for people who use AGRINUXES but do not run a farm workspace or a vendor workspace of their own. It covers four kinds of account:

- **Individual (customer) accounts** for buyers who want to browse the marketplace.
- **Government accounts** for ministries, district offices and national planners.
- **Extension officers and supervisors** who advise farms in a district or camp.
- **Programme partners** (donors, NGOs, FAO-style projects) who run programmes, enrol farms, deliver support and report results.

It also explains, from the farmer's side, what happens to a farm that agrees to share data, is enrolled in a programme or is assigned an extension officer. The last chapters help you choose the right account type and show what each account can and cannot see.

> **Note:** Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account explains how to register and sign in. This part repeats only the registration fields that are specific to these account types.

---

## Chapter 1: The Individual (Customer) Account

### What this account is for

An **Individual** account is for a buyer, a farmer who only wants to look around, or anyone who wants to find vets, equipment hire, input dealers, produce sellers and farm services without managing a farm. An individual account has no business workspace: no production cycles, no finance, no stock. It has a small dashboard with four pages: **Home**, **Marketplace**, **Saved** and **Profile**.

> **Watch out:** In the current version, the individual dashboard is a browsing and contact tool. You can search, filter, view details, mark favourites and phone the seller. The **Order** and **Enquire** buttons on the individual dashboard are not yet connected to a request form. There is no screen for tracking requests, cancelling them or leaving reviews on an individual account. This chapter says exactly what is there and what is not, so you are not left guessing. Section "Booking and ordering from a business account" later in this chapter describes the booking forms that do exist (they are inside the farm workspace).

> **Note:** The individual screens and the public marketplace page show the product name **AgroNexus** in their top bar. This is the same platform as AGRINUXES.

### Browsing before you sign in

You do not need an account to look at the marketplace. When you open the AGRINUXES address without signing in, you see the public marketplace page.

**What you will see**

- A top bar with **Sign in** and **Register free** buttons.
- A banner that says **Browse free — no account needed**, with two buttons: **Register as Individual** and **Register Farm / Business**.
- Four small tiles: the number of listings, **Verified sellers**, **AI farm tools** (for business accounts) and **Instant booking** (once registered).
- A search box with the hint **Search providers, products, categories…** and a count such as "27 listings · phone calls always free".
- Category buttons: **All**, **Vet Services**, **Equipment Hire**, **Agro Dealers**, **AgriFood**, **AgriSupply**, **AgriServices**. Each shows how many listings it holds.
- Listing cards in a grid. If nothing matches, you see **No results for "…"** and a **Clear filters** link.

**What is on a card**

| Item | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Photo and category label | The kind of listing, for example Vet Services. |
| **Verified** badge | The platform has checked this seller. Not every listing has it. |
| Name and one-line description | The business or product. |
| Price in ZMW | Products show a price per unit (for example ZMW 175 per bag). Providers show a "From" price such as "ZMW 850/hr". |
| Location | The town or district. |
| Stars and number | The average rating and how many ratings. |
| **View details** | Opens the details window. |

**The details window** shows the photo, category, name, description, price or "Starting from" price, location, a **Verified** mark, the rating, tags (for example "VBZ Registered", "Mobile", "24/7") and three buttons:

- **Call directly**: dials the listed phone number on a phone. Calls are always free of platform charges.
- **Video call**: shown only for listings tagged for video or teleconsultation. It asks you to sign in.
- **Order** (for products) or **Book / Enquire** (for providers): asks you to sign in. A yellow note says: "Sign in (or register free) to book, order, and start video calls — takes under 2 minutes."

> **Note:** The listings on the public page and on the individual dashboard are a built-in sample catalogue that shows how the marketplace looks (for example "AgriVet Zambia", "ZamTractor Hire Co", "Pioneer 30Y87 Maize Seed"). They are examples, not live stock. Live provider profiles and live listings are managed by the vendors themselves (see Part 6 — Vendor Workspaces: Basics, Vet Services, Equipment Hire, Transport and AgriServices and Part 7 — Vendor Workspaces: Agro Dealers, AgriSupply, AgriFood Sellers and Processors).

### Creating an individual account

**What it is for.** Gives you a personal login so you can save favourites and use the dashboard.

**Before you start.** Have your name, an email address, a phone number and a password of at least six characters.

**Step by step**

1. On the public marketplace page, press **Register free** (or **Register as Individual**).
