2. In the window that opens, choose the **Individual** tab.
3. Fill in the form (table below).
4. Tick the box "I agree to the Terms of Service and Privacy Policy".
5. Press **Create Account**. You go straight to your dashboard.

**The fields**

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Full name** | Yes | Shown on your dashboard and profile. |
| **Email address** | Yes | Used to sign in. |
| **Phone number** (for example +260 977 000 111) | Yes | The form refuses to continue without it. |
| **Country (optional)** | No | Zambia, Zimbabwe, Malawi, Tanzania, Mozambique, Botswana, Kenya, Uganda, Other. |
| **Password (min 6 characters)** | Yes | Click the eye icon to show or hide what you type. |
| **Confirm password** | Yes | Must match. |
| Terms checkbox | Yes | The **Create Account** button stays greyed out until it is ticked. |

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | What it means |
|---|---|
| Please agree to the Terms of Service to continue. | The terms box is not ticked. |
| Passwords do not match. | The two passwords differ. |
| Password must be at least 6 characters. | Choose a longer password. |
| Please enter a phone number. | The phone field is empty. |
| A user with this email already exists. / A user with this phone number already exists. | That email or phone is already registered. Sign in instead. |

> **Tip:** On the same window, the link **Register a farm or business instead** switches to the business form, and **Sign in** returns to the sign-in tab. If you later decide to run a farm, register a separate Business / Farm account (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account). The Profile page reminds you of this.

### Signing in

Open the **Sign In** tab, type your **Email address** and **Password** and press **Sign In**. Wrong details give the message "Invalid phone/email or password." A disabled account gives "Account is disabled."

### The individual dashboard

After sign-in, a top bar shows the logo, four page buttons (**Home**, **Marketplace**, **Saved**, **Profile**), a green chip with your first name and a **Sign out** button. On a phone the page buttons sit behind a menu button.

#### Home

**What it is for.** A welcome page and shortcut to the marketplace.

**What you will see**

| Part | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Green welcome card | "Welcome back," your full name and your email, with two buttons: **Browse Marketplace** and **Saved (n)**, where n is how many favourites you have marked in this visit. |
| Tile **Saved** | Number of favourites in this visit. |
| Tile **Browsed** | The total number of listings in the catalogue (not a history of what you opened). |
| Tile **Calls** | Always shows 0. Calls happen on your phone and are not counted. |
| **Featured providers** | The first four verified providers. **View all** opens the Marketplace page. |

#### Marketplace

**What it is for.** Searching and filtering the catalogue.

**Step by step**

1. Open **Marketplace**.
2. Type in the search box (hint: "Search providers, products..."). The list narrows as you type. The search looks at the name, the category and the one-line description. It does not search the location, tags or long description.
3. Press a category button to narrow by type. The buttons are **All**, **Vet Services**, **Equipment Hire**, **Agro Dealers**, **AgriFood**, **AgriSupply** and **AgriServices**. Search and category work together.
4. Read the count under the buttons, for example "6 listings".
5. On a card, press the heart to save it, **Call** to phone, or **Details** to open the details window.

**What is on a card.** Category label, heart, name, one-line description, price per unit (products only), location with a **Verified** mark, stars with rating and number of ratings, and the **Call** and **Details** buttons.

**The details window** adds the full description, "From" price for providers, tags, the seller name for products, and these buttons:

- **Call directly**: phone the seller.
- **Video**: shown only for listings that offer video (for example "TeleFarm Vet").
- **Order** (products) or **Enquire** (providers): see the watch-out at the start of this chapter. These buttons are shown but do not yet open a request form.

**Good to know**

- Press the grey background or the X to close the window.
- Prices are shown in ZMW.

#### Saved

**What it is for.** Your shortlist of favourites.

**Step by step**

1. Press the heart on any card. It turns red.
2. Open **Saved** to see only the cards you marked. Press the red heart again to remove one.
3. With nothing saved you see "Nothing saved yet" and a **Browse marketplace** link.

> **Watch out:** Favourites are kept only while the page stays open. If you refresh the page, close the browser or sign out, the list is empty again. If you need to remember a supplier, write down the phone number.

#### Profile

**What it is for.** Shows who you are signed in as.

**What you will see**

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Picture | A letter badge with the first letter of your name. |
| Name and label | Your name and "Individual customer". |
| **Email** | Your sign-in email. |
| **Phone** | Your phone number, or a dash if none. |
| **Account type** | "Individual / Customer". |
| Amber box **Want to manage a farm?** | Tells you to sign out and register a Business / Farm account to get production tracking, payroll and AI analytics. |
| **Sign out** | Signs you out and returns to the public page. |

> **Note:** The Profile page is read-only. There are no fields to edit your name, phone or password on the individual dashboard.

### Booking and ordering from a business account

Because the individual dashboard has no request forms, buyers who need to book or order inside the platform do it from a **Business / Farm** account. Inside the farm workspace, the sidebar has a **Marketplaces** group with six pages: **AgriFood Market**, **AgriSupply**, **AgriServices**, **Agro Dealers**, **Equipment Hire** and **Vet Services**. (Whether you see them depends on your role; see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account.)

#### Listing boards: AgriFood Market, AgriSupply and AgriServices

**What they are for.** Classified-style boards where farms post produce, inputs and services for sale, and others browse.

**Step by step (as a buyer)**

1. Open the board from the sidebar.
2. On the left, use the **Browse** tab, the search box (**Search listings…**) and the category drop-down (**All Categories**).
3. Press a listing. The right side shows title, price per unit, whether the price is negotiable, quantity available, description, seller name and phone, location and posting date.
4. Press **Call Seller**. This board does not take orders inside the app; you agree the sale by phone.

**Categories**

| Board | Categories |
|---|---|
| **AgriFood Market** | Poultry, Livestock, Fish & Seafood, Vegetables, Fruits, Grains & Cereals, Dairy, Eggs, Processed Foods, Other |
| **AgriSupply** | Seeds, Animal Feed, Fertiliser, Chemicals & Pesticides, Medicines & Vaccines, Day-Old Chicks, Fingerlings, Equipment, Irrigation, Storage, Packaging, Other Inputs |
| **AgriServices** | Veterinary, Agronomy, Transport, Equipment Hire, Aerial Services, Storage, Irrigation Services, Finance & Accounting, Training, Labour, Other Services |

Posting your own listing uses the buttons **Sell Food**, **Sell Inputs** or **Offer Service**; see Part 7 — Vendor Workspaces: Agro Dealers, AgriSupply, AgriFood Sellers and Processors for selling.

> **Watch out:** On these three boards, the listings you post yourself and the sample listings are kept in this browser only. Saved on this device only; they are not shared with other users and are not backed up automatically. Use **Backup & Restore** (see Part 1 — Getting Started and Your Account) if you want a copy.

#### Provider directories: Equipment Hire, Vet Services and Agro Dealers

**What they are for.** Directories of service businesses with a profile, services list, reviews and certifications, and a booking or enquiry form.

**Finding a provider**

1. Open **Equipment Hire**, **Vet Services** or **Agro Dealers**.
2. Stay on the **Browse All** tab. (The **My Listings** tab shows businesses you registered yourself.)
3. Use the search box (**Search providers...**), the **All Districts** drop-down and the **All Categories** drop-down.
4. Press a card to open the profile.

**Categories**

| Directory | Categories |
|---|---|
| **Equipment Hire** | Land Prep, Planting, Spraying, Harvesting, Irrigation, Transport, Conservation, Mapping |
| **Vet Services** | Consultation, Vaccination, Diagnostics, Laboratory, Surgery, Biosecurity, Teleconsultation, Nutrition, Breeding, Water Quality |
| **Agro Dealers** | Seeds, Fertiliser, Chemicals, Animal Feed, Equipment, Day-Old Chicks, Solar & Irrigation, Subsidised Inputs |

**The provider card** shows the name, tagline, location and up to two districts, **Verified** and **Certified** badges, the star rating, up to three tags, the first two services with prices (shown as K for kwacha) and **View Profile**.

**The profile window** has four inner tabs:

| Tab | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Overview** | Description, coverage areas (districts), tags, and extras such as specialist crops. |
| **Services (n)** | Each service with description, price (or POA when no price is set), unit, category and whether it is Available. |
| **Reviews (n)** | Average rating and each review with reviewer name, stars, comment, service used and how long ago. |
| **Certifications** | Body, type, number and expiry. |

A contact strip shows the location, phone, email, website, operating hours and licence number. The footer has **Call**, **Email**, **Website**, **Book / Enquire** and, for vets, agronomists and any verified provider, a video call button.

> **Note:** If the live provider service cannot be reached, a yellow banner says "Showing sample data — connect to AgroNexus backend to see live provider profiles". A blue banner says "Loading provider directory…" while it loads.

**Booking equipment (Equipment Hire)**

1. Open a provider and press **Book / Enquire**. The window is titled **Book Equipment**.
2. Fill in the form.
3. Press **Submit Request**.

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Start Date** | Yes | Defaults to today. |
| **End Date** | Yes | Defaults to today. |
| **Hectares (optional)** | No | For example 5.0, when hire is priced per hectare. |
| **Delivery / Site Address** | No | Farm location or GPS coordinates. |
| **Notes** | No | Equipment type, soil conditions, special needs. |

**Booking a vet (Vet Services)**

1. Open a vet and press **Book / Enquire**. The window is titled **Book Vet Service**.
2. Fill in the form and press **Submit Request**.

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Service Type** | Yes | General Consultation, Vaccination, Treatment / Procedure, Diagnosis / Lab, Deworming, Pregnancy Check, Teleconsultation (Remote), Other. |
| **Appointment Date** | Yes | Defaults to today. |
| **Species** | No | For example Broilers, Tilapia. |
| **Animal Count** | No | For example 500. |
| **Symptoms / Reason** | No | What the visit is for. |
| **Additional Notes** | No | Anything else. |

**Sending an enquiry (Agro Dealers)**

1. Open a dealer and press **Book / Enquire**. The window is titled **Send Enquiry**.
2. Type your message ("Describe what you need, quantities, location, timing…").
3. Press **Submit Request**.

**What you will see after submitting.** A green tick and "Request submitted!". If the platform returns a reference, it is shown ("HB-…" for a hire booking, "VA-…" for a vet appointment) with the line "{provider} will contact you on {phone}." If the platform cannot confirm straight away, you see a softer message such as "Enquiry saved — provider will contact you shortly." or "Appointment request saved — provider will confirm."

**Statuses.** A new hire booking starts as an **enquiry** and a new vet appointment starts as **requested**. The provider then confirms, completes or declines it in their own workspace (see Part 6 — Vendor Workspaces: Basics, Vet Services, Equipment Hire, Transport and AgriServices).

**Good to know**

- Booking forms are not available on your own listings (the **Book / Enquire** button is hidden for "My Business").
- The video call button starts a live consultation room with the provider.
- The Agro Dealers enquiry is sent to the dealer as a general consultation request with your message as the note.
- There is no screen in the directories where a buyer lists past requests, cancels one or posts a review. Reviews already approved appear on the **Reviews** tab. To change or cancel a booking, call the provider.

---

## Chapter 2: Government Accounts

### What this account is for

A **government** account is for ministries, provincial offices and district agricultural coordinators (DACO offices). It gives you de-identified, summed-up numbers about the farms in your area, a national map, an ecosystem view of all businesses, and a way to send advisories to farmers.

The most important rule is **consent and de-identification**:

- Only farms and cooperatives that have turned on **Share with government & partners** in their settings are counted in the production, market, disease and climate dashboards.
- Even then, you only see totals and counts. No farm name, owner, member, record or field is ever shown on the government dashboards.

### Who can register and which roles exist

On the public page, press **Register free**, open the **Gov / Partner** tab and pick the role card:

| Role card | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Dashboard viewer** (Aggregated district & national data) | Government viewer role. |
| **Extension officer** (Caseload of farms in your camp / district) | See Chapter 3. |
| **Supporting partner (FAO, donor, NGO)** (Run programmes: enrol farms, deliver inputs & training, track results) | See Chapter 4. |

The **Gov / Partner** form fields:

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Your full name** | Yes | |
| **Official email address** | Yes | |
| **Phone number** | Yes | |
| **Organisation** | Yes | For example "Ministry of Agriculture — Chongwe DACO". |
| Organisation type drop-down | Yes | **Government ministry / district office**, **NGO / development partner**, or **Donor programme**. Choosing the supporting partner card changes "government" to "donor" for you. |
| **Province** and **District** | Optional (required for an extension officer) | Where the organisation works. |
| **Password (min 6 characters)** and **Confirm password** | Yes | |
| Terms checkbox | Yes | |

Press **Create partner account**.

> **Note:** The person who creates the organisation becomes its **administrator** (shown by a shield icon in the team list). An administrator has full access to every page of that organisation, whatever role card they picked. The administrator can then add colleagues with narrower roles (see "Team & Branches" below).

**Government roles**

| Role | What it is for | What it can do |
|---|---|---|
| **Administrator** (gov_admin) | Manages the team, branches and advisories; sees everything. | Full access: all dashboards, registry, advisories (view, create, edit, delete), caseloads, visits, programmes, enrolment, support, M&E, notices, team and settings. |
| **Dashboard viewer** (gov_viewer) | Read-only district and national dashboards. | View only: the dashboards (including Ecosystem and National Map), District Registry and Programmes. Cannot issue advisories, cannot add or edit programmes, cannot manage the team. |
| **Extension supervisor** | Oversees officers in the branch and issues advisories. | See Chapter 3. |
| **Extension officer** | Caseload of farms, visits, direct advice. | See Chapter 3. |

Only the administrator can change someone's role, branch or per-page permissions (see "Team & Branches").

### The government screen

The left sidebar shows only the pages your permissions allow. A button with three lines collapses the sidebar to icons. The sidebar footer says "Read-only aggregates. Farms appear only after opting in; individual farms are never identified." and has **Sign Out**.

| Page | Needs | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| **National Overview** | Dashboards | Headline numbers, weekly trend, enterprise mix and top hotspots. |
| **Ecosystem** | Dashboards | All business types, service gaps and programme coverage. |
| **National Map** | Dashboards | Map of districts with layers. |
| **Production** | Dashboards | Harvest by week and enterprise categories. |
| **Markets & Prices** | Dashboards | Commodity prices and marketplace supply. |
| **Disease & Alerts** | Dashboards | Diagnoses by severity, farmer reports and hotspots. |
| **Climate & Sustainability** | Dashboards | Emissions, offsets, water, certifications. |
| **District Registry** | Registry | One row per district. |
| **Advisories** | Advisories | Send and review advisories. |
| **Programmes** | Programmes | The same programme tools as Chapter 4, for your organisation's own programmes. |
| **Team & Branches** | Team | Members, branches and audit log. |

### The scope bar: province and district filters

Every dashboard page except Ecosystem, National Map, Advisories, Programmes and Team has a header bar with:

- The organisation name and a line "Scope: National · updated 14:32:10" (or the province or district you chose, and the time of the last load).
- A **Province** drop-down (**All provinces** plus every province that has at least one sharing farm).
- A **District** drop-down (**All districts** plus each district, with its number of sharing farms in brackets, narrowed to the chosen province).
- A refresh button (circular arrow) to reload the numbers.

When you pick a province, the district list resets. Picking a district narrows everything to that district.

> **Note:** If your account belongs to a district office or camp (a branch, see "Team & Branches"), your scope is fixed to that district and you cannot widen it. A provincial branch is fixed to its province but can pick districts inside it. Headquarters members see the nation.

**If something goes wrong.** A red banner shows the message. "Not authenticated" means you must sign in again. "Permission denied: government / partner access required" means the account has no government role. "Permission denied: you need view access to gov-dashboards" means the administrator has switched the dashboards off for you.

### National Overview

**What it is for.** The headline picture for the chosen scope.

**What you will see**

Eight number tiles:

| Tile | What it counts |
|---|---|
| **Farms reporting** | Farms and cooperatives that are active and have agreed to share. The small text shows "x% of N farms opted in", where N is every active business account on the platform (farms and vendors together), so the percentage can look low. |
| **Districts covered** | Districts that have at least one sharing farm, with the number of provinces. |
| **Active enterprises** | Active enterprises (for example a maize field, a broiler flock), with the number of active batches or cycles. |
| **Records (30 days)** | Production records entered in the last 30 days, with the number of harvest records and total harvest units. |
| **Unresolved farmer reports** | Farmer-reported problems not yet resolved. Turns amber when above zero. |
| **Critical alerts open** | Unresolved alerts of critical severity from farm devices. Turns red when above zero. |
| **Active market listings** | Active marketplace listings from sharing farms. |
| **Carbon offsets logged** | Total kilograms of CO2-equivalent offsets recorded. |

Below the tiles:

- **Weekly activity (12 weeks)**: a chart with three lines: **Production records**, **Harvests** and **Farmer reports**, per week starting on Monday.
- **Enterprise mix**: a ring chart and list showing each enterprise category, the number of enterprises and its share in percent.
- **Top disease & pest hotspots (90 days)**: a table with **District**, **Province**, **Issue**, **Reports** and **Farms**.

> **Good to know.** Harvest quantities are summed exactly as farms typed them. A farm recording in bags and another in kilograms are added together as plain numbers, so treat the harvest totals as an activity indicator, not a verified yield.

### Ecosystem

**What it is for.** Shows the whole agricultural economy, not only farms: cooperatives, agro dealers, vets, equipment hire, food sellers, supply providers, service providers, processors and transport businesses. It helps you find districts where farmers have no vet, no input dealer or no buyer nearby.

**Privacy rule.** Counts of organisations by type and public marketplace profiles do not need consent, because they are already public or are plain counts. No organisation is named on this page. The page header says "Counts only; no organisation is named."

> **Note:** On this page the figures cover the whole country (or your branch's area). The Province and District drop-downs on other pages do not apply here.

**What you will see**

Six tiles:

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Organisations** | All active business organisations, split into farms and vendors. |
| **Sharing data** | The percentage and number of those that opted in. |
| **Districts** | Districts and provinces that have at least one organisation. |
| **Marketplace providers** | Public provider profiles that are active, and how many are verified. |
| **Market activity (90 d)** | Hire bookings plus vet appointments in 90 days, with active listings. |
| **Programmes** | Active programmes reaching these organisations, with participant count and support value in ZMW. |

Then these sections:

| Section | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Organisations by type** | A table of business type (Farms, Cooperatives, Agro dealers, Vets, Equipment hire, AgriFood sellers, AgriSupply, AgriServices, Processors, Transport) with **Total**, **Sharing** and **Districts**. |
| **Participants by district** | A stacked bar chart, one bar per district, coloured by business type. |
| **Service coverage by district** | A table with **Province**, **District**, **Farms**, **Vets**, **Dealers**, **Hire**, **Processors**, **Transport**, **Score** and **Gaps**. |
| **Marketplace providers (public profiles)** | Table by provider type: providers, verified, districts, average rating, services. |
| **Market activity (last 90 days)** | Equipment-hire bookings (with completed count and ZMW value), vet appointments (with completed), active listings, contracts fulfilled (with value), and a line of listings by commodity. |
| **Programme coverage by district (all partners)** | District, programmes, participants, support value and partner names. |
| **Programmes running in scope** | Each programme with its partner, partner type, participants, districts, ZMW support and the participant types it targets. |

**How the service coverage score works**

The **Score** is out of 5. A district earns one point for each of these that exists: a vet, an input dealer, equipment hire, a processor or buyer, and transport.

- Marketplace provider profiles in the district count as well as registered businesses.
- "Dealers" includes agro dealers and AgriSupply providers.
- "Processors" includes processors and AgriFood sellers.
- A number is shown in red when it is zero and the district has farms.
- The **Gaps** column lists, for districts with farms, what is missing: "No veterinary service", "No input dealer", "No equipment hire", "No processor / buyer", "No transport". A district with none missing shows "Fully served".
- Score colours: green for 4 or 5, amber for 2 or 3, red for 0 or 1.

> **Good to know.** The market-activity, listing and programme figures on this page are counted over all organisations in scope, as the page header says, not only those that opted in. Only identities are protected (nobody is named).

### National Map

**What it is for.** Shows every district as a circle on a map of Zambia so you can see where organisations, programmes, disease reports and services are concentrated.

**Before you start.** You need the dashboards permission. The map needs an internet connection to load the background map.

**Step by step**

1. Open **National Map**.
2. The heading line reads, for example, "Zambia · 120 organisations in 14 districts · 80 programme participants · 12 disease / pest reports (90 d). Districts are drawn at their centroid; nobody is identified."
3. Use the **Colour districts by** drop-down (top right) to choose the number that sizes and colours the circles.
4. Use the checkboxes at the bottom left of the map to switch layers on or off.
5. Zoom with the mouse wheel, drag to move.
6. Hover over a circle for a quick summary; click a circle (or a row in the right-hand list) to open the district panel.
7. In the panel, press **Open {district} dashboards** to jump to the National Overview filtered to that district. Press the X to go back to the list.

**Colour options (metrics)**

| Option | Hint on screen | What the circle shows |
|---|---|---|
| **All organisations** | Farms, cooperatives and every vendor type | Number of organisations. |
| **Farms & cooperatives** | Producers only | Number of producers. |
| **Vendors** | Dealers, vets, hire, processors, transport… | Number of vendors. |
| **Sharing data** | Organisations that opted in | Number that consented. |
| **Programme participants** | Enrolled in any partner or government programme | Active enrolments. |
| **Disease / pest reports** | Last 90 days, consenting farms | Farmer-reported problems (excluding "success" reports). |
| **Service coverage** | 0 = no services near farms, 5 = fully served | Coverage score; all circles are the same size, coloured green (4 or 5), amber (2 or 3), red (0 or 1). |

For the other options, the circle gets bigger and darker (pale yellow to dark brown) with larger numbers. Hotspot colouring uses grey for zero and then amber, orange and dark red as reports rise.

**Layers (checkboxes)**

| Layer | What it draws |
|---|---|
| **Districts (participants)** | The main circles. |
| **Disease / pest hotspots** | Red dotted rings around districts with reports. |
| **Programmes** | A purple dot beside districts with enrolled participants; size grows with the number of programmes. |
| **Weather stations** | Blue dots for weather stations of sharing farms, rounded to about 1 kilometre so a station cannot point to one farm. |
| **Marketplace providers** | Orange dots (colour by type) for active public provider profiles. |

A dashed circle outline means the district is not in the built-in list of district centres, so the circle sits at the middle of its province ("placed at province centroid"). A note under the layers says how many organisations have no usable district and are not drawn.

**The district panel** shows tiles for **Organisations**, **Farms**, **Vendors**, **Sharing data**, **Programmes**, **Participants**, **Support (ZMW)**, **Reports 90 d**, **Stations** and **Providers**. Below are a **By type** list (for example "Farms 12", "Vets 1"), **Service coverage** with the score and gaps, **Hotspot categories** (Crop disease, Pest, Animal disease, Nutritional, Yield issue, Weed, Other) and **Partners active here**.

**Good to know**

- No individual farm is ever drawn.
- Hotspot and weather-station layers use only farms that gave consent.
- If your account is limited to a branch, the map shows only your area.

### Production

**What it is for.** Looks at what farms are recording.

**What you will see**

- **Harvest volume by week (units, as recorded)**: a bar chart over 12 weeks.
- **Enterprise mix**: the ring chart again.
- **Enterprises by category**: a table with **Category**, **Enterprises**, **Active batches** and **Farms**.

### Markets & Prices

**What it is for.** Prices and supply reported by sharing farms.

**What you will see**

| Table | Columns |
|---|---|
| **Commodity prices reported by farms** | **Commodity** (with unit), **Latest**, **As of**, **30-day avg**, **Range (30d)**, **Samples**. |
| **Marketplace supply (active listings)** | **Commodity**, **Listings**, **Farms**, **Quantity available**, **Avg asking price**. |

"Latest" is the most recent price ever reported for that commodity, unit and currency. The 30-day average, range and sample count use the last 30 days only; where there are no samples they show a dash.

### Disease & Alerts

**What it is for.** Early warning of disease and pests.

**What you will see**

- **AI diagnosis severity (90 days)**: horizontal bars for None, Low, Medium, High and Critical, coloured from green to red.
- **Farmer-reported issues by type (90 days)**: bars by category.
- **Hotspots by district**: **District**, **Province**, **Issue**, **Reports**, **Farms affected**.
- **Diagnoses by analysis type**: **Analysis**, **Severity** (coloured label) and **Count**.

Empty states read "No AI diagnoses in scope." and "No farmer reports in scope."

### Climate & Sustainability

**What it is for.** Indicative climate-smart agriculture figures.

**What you will see**

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **CO2e emissions logged** | Kilograms of emissions entries and the number of entries. |
| **CO2e offsets logged** | Kilograms of offsets. |
| **Net CO2e** | Emissions minus offsets. Amber when positive, green otherwise. |
| **Water used** | Cubic metres and number of entries. |
| **Farms certified** | Number of farms with at least one certification marked "certified", and the number of active certifications. |

A note states: "Figures are farmer-recorded totals across consenting farms in the selected scope. They are suitable for indicative NDC / climate-smart agriculture reporting and programme targeting, not for verified carbon accounting."

### District Registry

**What it is for.** A continuously updated substitute for a periodic census: counts per district of consenting farms. Individual farms are not identified.

**Columns:** **Province**, **District**, **Farms**, **Enterprises**, **Active batches**, **Records (30d)**, **Open alerts** (red when above zero) and **Unresolved reports** (amber when above zero). The empty state reads "No data in this scope yet."

### Advisories

**What it is for.** Sending a message to every member of every sharing farm in a chosen area, for example a fall armyworm warning for Central Province.

**Before you start.** You need the **Advisories** permission with "create". The administrator has it. A dashboard viewer does not.

**Step by step**

1. Open **Advisories**. The left card is **Issue an advisory**.
2. Fill in the form.
3. Press **Send advisory**.
4. A green line reports how many farms and people received it: "Sent to 12 farms (37 people)."

| Field | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Title** | Required, up to 200 characters. |
| **Message to farmers** | Required. |
| Category | **Weather warning**, **Disease / pest alert**, **Market / price notice**, **Programme / subsidy**, **General advisory**. |
| Priority | **Info**, **Warning**, **Critical**. |
| Province | **All provinces** or one province. |
| District | **All districts** or one district of the chosen province. |
| Enterprise type | **All enterprise types**, or one of: Crop, Livestock, Aquaculture, Horticulture, Dairy, Apiculture, Greenhouse, Mixed ("… farms only"). |

**Who receives it.** Every active member of every consenting farm or cooperative in the area (and with the chosen enterprise type, if you set one). The message arrives as an in-app notification titled "[Chongwe] Your title" with your organisation's name at the bottom. Vendors are not included, because advisories go to the farming businesses in the production dashboards.

**Advisories issued** (right card) lists your organisation's past advisories: title, message, a coloured priority label, then category, area (district, province or National), enterprise type, "farms / people reached" and the date and name of the sender. With none, it reads "No advisories issued yet."

**Good to know**

- If you belong to a branch, your area is fixed to the branch's district or province and your choice is overridden.
- There is no edit or delete: a sent advisory cannot be recalled.

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Invalid advisory category / Invalid advisory priority | The value was not one of the lists above. |
| Government account has no organisation | Contact your administrator. |
| Permission denied: you need create access to advisories | Ask the administrator for access. |

### Programmes (for government)

The **Programmes** page is the same programme workspace that partners use. A government organisation can run its own programmes (for example a subsidised input scheme). See Chapter 4. A **Dashboard viewer** has read-only access: the **New programme** and other buttons may be shown, but the platform refuses saves with "Permission denied: you need create access to programmes".

### Team & Branches

**What it is for.** Adding colleagues, setting their role and page access, and arranging offices.

**Three tabs: Members, Branches, Audit log.**

**Members.** Table with **Member**, **Role**, **Branch**, **Access**, **Status** and action buttons. Only an administrator can use the actions: **Add member** (Full name, Phone, Email optional, Role, Branch, optional password; a temporary password is shown once), change role, change branch, the shield button for per-page permissions (a grid of View, Create, Edit, Delete for each module with "Reset to role default"), make or remove administrator, deactivate or reactivate. Non-administrators see "read-only (you are not an administrator)".

**Branches.** For a government organisation the kinds are **Headquarters**, **Provincial office**, **District office (DACO)**, **Agricultural camp** and **Research station**. A branch has a name, optional code, province, district, address, phone and a **Headquarters** checkbox. The rule: members of a district office or camp only see and act on their own district; provincial members see their province; headquarters sees the nation. A branch with no members can be deleted.

**Audit log.** Administrators see who changed whose access and when.

---

## Chapter 3: Extension Officers and Supervisors

### What this account is for

An **extension officer** is a government or NGO field adviser who looks after a list of farms (the **caseload**) in a district or camp. The officer sees each farm by name, sees the signals that say a farm needs help, logs visits, sets follow-ups, closes farmer problem reports and sends advice. This replaces the paper visit book.

**Consent is the gate.** An officer can only add farms that (a) have turned on data sharing and (b) are inside the officer's working area. The farm is told when it is added, and the farm can see its officer and visit log.

### Registering

Use **Register free**, **Gov / Partner** tab, card **Extension officer**. **Province** and **District** are required for this role because they decide the working area. See Chapter 2 for the other fields.

| Role | What it is for | Default access |
|---|---|---|
| **Extension officer** | Caseload of farms, visits, direct advice. | View dashboards, registry, programmes, enrolment and support; view, create and edit caseload and visits; create advisories. |
| **Extension supervisor** | Oversees officers in the branch, issues advisories. | View dashboards, registry and settings; full access to caseload and visits; create advisories; view, create and edit programmes, enrolment, support, M&E and notices. |

> **Note:** A person who registers the organisation is its administrator and sees every page, including **Team & Branches**. Officers added later by an administrator only see the pages their role allows.

> **Note:** The extension screens are the same for officers and supervisors. There is no separate "all officers" overview page: each person sees their own caseload and visits.

**Working area.** The area is the district (or, if none, the province) of the officer's organisation. If the officer belongs to a district-office or camp branch, the branch's district wins. The working area is shown in the page header: "Working area: Chongwe".

### The extension screen

Sidebar pages (shown only when permitted):

| Page | What it is |
|---|---|
| **My Caseload** | Your farms, ranked by priority. |
| **Visits & Follow-ups** | Log a visit and list visits. |
| **Add Farms** | Find consenting farms in your area and add them. |
| **Programmes** | Programme tools (Chapter 4), read-only by default for officers. |
| **Team & Branches** | Same as Chapter 2. |

The sidebar footer reads "Only farms that opted in to data sharing in your area can be added to your caseload."

### My Caseload

**What it is for.** See at a glance which farms need you first.

**What you will see**

Seven tiles:

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Farms on caseload** | Farms you have added. |
| **Need attention** | Farms with a priority score of 3 or more. |
| **Unresolved reports** | Open farmer reports across your caseload. |
| **Follow-ups overdue** | Your follow-up dates in the past that are not marked done. |
| **Follow-ups this week** | Follow-ups due from today to seven days ahead. |
| **Visits (30 days)** | Visits you logged in the last 30 days. |
| **Farms available** | Consenting farms in your area not yet on your caseload. |

The **Caseload — highest priority first** table has these columns:

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Priority** | A coloured number: red at 8 or more, amber at 3 to 7, grey below 3. |
| **Farm** | Farm name, then district and the director's name. |
| **Enterprises** | Number of active enterprises and their categories. |
| **Alerts** | Open alerts; red text shows how many are critical. |
| **Reports** | Unresolved farmer reports. |
| **Severe AI dx** | AI diagnoses of high or critical severity in the last 30 days. |
| **Last visit** | The date of your last visit to that farm, or "never". |
| **Follow-up** | The soonest open follow-up date; red with "overdue" if past. |

**How the priority score is worked out.** Each farm scores: 5 points per critical alert, plus 2 per open alert, plus 3 per unresolved report, plus 3 per severe AI diagnosis in 30 days, plus 4 if a follow-up is overdue, plus 2 if you have never visited. Higher scores come first; ties go alphabetically.

An **Add farms** button on the table opens the **Add Farms** page. With an empty caseload you see: "No farms on your caseload yet. Use Add farms to pick consenting farms in your area."

Click any row (or **Open →**) to open the farm.

### Add Farms

**What it is for.** Building your caseload.

**Step by step**

1. Open **Add Farms**. The card is titled "Farms in your area that opted in to data sharing".
2. Optionally type part of a farm name in **Search by farm name**. The list updates after a short pause. It shows up to 100 farms, sorted by name.
3. Each row shows the farm name, district and province, number of enterprises and, if any, "n open reports".
4. Press **Add to caseload**.
5. A green line says "{farm} added to your caseload — the farm has been notified."

**What the farm gets.** Every active member of the farm receives a notification titled "Extension officer assigned": "{your name} ({your organisation}) is now your extension officer. Contact: {phone or email}."

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Farm not found in your working area, or it has not opted in to data sharing | The farm is outside your area or has withdrawn consent. |
| No more farms available in your working area. | Everyone eligible is already on your caseload. |

> **Good to know.** Only farm accounts appear here, not other business types. Farms must have their province and district set in settings, or they will not match your area (see Chapter 6).

### The farm view

Click a farm on the caseload.

**Header.** A **Caseload** back link, the farm name with its priority number, "district, province", the director's name and phone (tap to call), and two buttons: **Log visit** and **Remove**.

- **Remove** asks "Remove this farm from your caseload?" and, if you confirm, ends the assignment. Past visits stay on record. You can add the farm again later.

**Tiles:** **Enterprises**, **Active batches**, **Open alerts**, **Unresolved reports**, **Records (30d)**.

**Farmer reports.** A list of the farm's problem reports (up to 50, unresolved first). Each shows the title, category and crop or animal, description, the AI diagnosis if there is one, date, a severity label and either **Resolved** or a **Resolve** link.

To resolve a report:

1. Press **Resolve**.
2. Type what was done or advised in the box "What was done / advised".
3. Press **Save** (disabled until you type something). **Cancel** closes the box.
4. The report shows as resolved with your notes and your name, and the farmer who submitted it is notified. The page says "Report resolved — farmer notified."

**Send advice to this farm.** A small form: **Title**, **Message**, a priority drop-down (**Info**, **Warning**, **Critical**) and **Send**. It delivers an in-app notification to every active member of this one farm and shows "Advice sent to {n} people." Your name and organisation are added to the message.

**Recent AI diagnoses.** Up to eight, with severity, analysis type, the diagnosis text and date.

**Alerts.** Up to eight alerts with severity, title (struck through if resolved) and source.

**Enterprises.** Name, category, production type and number of active batches.

**Visit history.** All visits to this farm (by any officer), newest first.

### Logging a visit

**Before you start.** The farm must be on your caseload, and you need the "visits create" permission.

**Step by step**

1. Either press **Log visit** on a farm's page, or open **Visits & Follow-ups**, where the form is always shown at the top.
2. Fill in the form **Log a visit**.
3. Press **Save visit**.

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Farm drop-down | Yes (only on the Visits page) | Lists your caseload farms. |
| Date | Yes | Defaults to today. |
| Visit type | Yes | **Field visit**, **Phone consultation**, **Video consultation**, **Training / field day**, **Follow-up**. |
| **Purpose** | Yes | For example "Armyworm scouting". |
| **Findings** | No | What you observed. |
| **Recommendations (sent to the farm as a notification)** | No | Your advice. |
| **Follow-up date** | No | When you plan to check again. |
| **Related farmer report** | No | Shown only when the farm has unresolved reports. Links the visit to that report. |
| **Notify farm of recommendations** checkbox | Ticked by default | If ticked and you wrote recommendations, every active member of the farm gets a notification titled "Extension visit: {purpose}". |

After saving you see "Visit logged." A farm that is not on your caseload gives "Farm is not on your caseload". An unknown visit type gives "Invalid visit type". A report that does not belong to that farm gives "Farmer report not found on this farm".

### Visits & Follow-ups

**What it is for.** Your own log across all farms, and the follow-up list.

**What you will see**

- The **Log a visit** form (or "Add farms to your caseload before logging visits." when your caseload is empty).
- A card titled **All visits** with a checkbox **Pending follow-ups only**. When ticked, the card is titled **Pending follow-ups** and lists only visits that have a follow-up date not yet done, soonest first.

Each visit shows the purpose, type and farm name, then **Findings:** and **Advice:**, the visit date, and the follow-up status: "Follow-up done", or "Follow-up {date}" in amber (red with "overdue" if past) with a **mark done** link. Press **mark done** when you have completed the follow-up. It does not delete the visit.

### What the farm sees

See Chapter 6: the farm sees your name, organisation, phone and email, and its visit log with your findings and advice.

### Good to know

- You cannot see farms that have not opted in, and you cannot see farms outside your area.
- The extension screen has no page for area-wide advisories. Use **Send advice to this farm** for one farm. To broadcast to a whole district, ask a colleague whose account uses the government screen (an administrator) to send an advisory from the **Advisories** page described in Chapter 2.
- The **Programmes** tab shows programmes run by your own organisation. Officers cannot create or edit them unless an administrator grants it.

---

## Chapter 4: Programme Partners — Running a Programme

### What this account is for

A **programme partner** is a donor, NGO, FAO-style project or government scheme that supports farms and other agricultural businesses. A **programme** is a funded piece of work, for example "Hand-in-Hand Zambia", with a budget, a target area, a list of participants, deliveries (inputs, cash, training) and results to report to the donor.

Partners are different from government viewers in one important way. A partner sees the **name** of every organisation it has enrolled (that is what enrolment means). Everything else stays de-identified and consent-gated.

### Registering and roles

Use **Register free**, **Gov / Partner** tab, card **Supporting partner (FAO, donor, NGO)**. The organisation type is set to **Donor programme** for you (you may change it to **NGO / development partner**). See Chapter 2 for the form. The person who registers is the organisation's administrator.

Partner roles (for NGO and donor organisations):

| Role | What it is for | What it can do |
|---|---|---|
| **Administrator** (partner_admin) | Manages team, branches and programmes. | Everything below, plus Team & Branches. |
| **Programme manager** (partner_manager) | Runs programmes: enrolment, support, notices. | Full control of programmes, enrolment, support ledger, M&E and notices. View-only on the dashboards and registry. Can view and create advisories. |
| **M&E officer** (partner_me_officer) | Records indicator readings and views results. | View programmes, enrolment, support, dashboards and registry. Create and edit results readings (see the note on screens in Chapter 5). |
| **Observer (donor / auditor)** (partner_observer) | Read-only access to programmes and dashboards. | View everything in programmes, enrolment, support, M&E, notices, dashboards and registry. Can also create the donor report. |

> **Note:** If your organisation was registered as a **government** organisation (not NGO or donor), the government role list from Chapter 2 applies instead, and the **Programmes** page in the government screen offers the same programme tools.

> **Watch out:** On the Programmes pages, buttons are shown to everyone who can open the page. If your role is read-only and you press a save button, the platform refuses and a red message appears, for example "Permission denied: you need create access to programmes". Nothing is changed.

### The partner screen

The sidebar has three sections:

| Section | Pages |
|---|---|
| **Programmes** | **Programmes** |
| **District data** | **National Overview**, **Ecosystem**, **National Map**, **Production**, **Markets & Prices**, **Disease & Alerts**, **Climate & Sustainability**, **District Registry**, **Advisories** |
| **Admin** | **Team & Branches** |

The "District data" pages are the same as the government pages in Chapter 2 and show the same de-identified numbers. A page appears only if your role allows it (for example the M&E officer and observer have no **Advisories** page). The sidebar footer says: "Enrolled farms are visible by name; everything else is de-identified and consent-gated."

### The programme list

Open **Programmes**.

**Top tiles**

| Tile | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Programmes** | Number of programmes your organisation runs, with how many are active. |
| **Participants enrolled** | Distinct organisations enrolled across your programmes. |
| **Districts reached** | Distinct districts of those participants. |
| **Total budget** | Sum of all programme budgets. |
| **Support delivered** | Value of every support record. |
| **Budget used** | Support delivered divided by total budget, as a percentage (a dash if no budget). |

**Programme cards** under **Your programmes** show: name, funder and reference code, dates (start → end), a status label, **Enrolled** (for example "40 / 100"), **Support** in the programme currency, **Budget used** percentage, a green progress bar when an enrolment target is set, and a line of targeting such as "Chongwe, Kafue · Farms, Cooperatives · Crop". With none yet: "No programmes yet. Create one to start enrolling farms and vendors."

**Programme statuses**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Planning** | Being set up. |
| **Active** | Running. The **Enrol participants** button and "Enrol from {district}" on the map are shown only when active. |
| **Suspended** | Paused. The platform refuses new enrolments ("Programme is suspended; enrolment is not open"). |
| **Closed** | Finished. Enrolment is refused ("Programme is closed; enrolment is not open"). |

Press a card to open that programme's workspace.

### Creating a programme

**Before you start.** You need "programmes create" permission (administrator or programme manager). Decide the budget, the target area, who may take part and which indicators you will report.

**Step by step**

1. On the programme list, press **New programme** (top right).
2. Fill in the form (table below).
3. Press **Create programme**. You land in the new programme's workspace.

**The fields**

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Programme name** | Yes | For example "Hand-in-Hand Zambia". Refused if empty: "Programme name is required". |
| **Funder** | No | Pre-filled with "FAO". Change it, for example to "EU" or "USAID". |
| **Reference code** | No | The donor or internal reference, for example GCP/ZAM/123. |
| **Objective / description** | No | A short statement. It appears on the donor report. |
| **Start** | Yes | Defaults to today. Refused if missing: "Start date is required". |
| **End** | No | Must not be earlier than the start: "End date must be after start date". |
| **Budget** | No | A number of zero or more ("Budget cannot be negative"). |
| Currency box | No | A short code, pre-filled with "USD". Change to ZMW if you budget in kwacha. |
| **Enrolment target (participants)** | No | How many participants you aim to enrol. Drives the percentage and progress bar. |
| Status drop-down | Yes | **Planning**, **Active**, **Suspended**, **Closed**. Pre-set to Active. |
| **Target provinces, comma-separated (blank = all)** | No | For example "Lusaka, Central". |
| **Target districts, comma-separated (blank = all)** | No | For example "Chongwe, Kafue". See **Pick on map** below. |
| **Who can be enrolled** buttons | No | Switch on any of: Farms, Cooperatives, Agro dealers, Vets, Equipment hire, AgriFood sellers, AgriSupply, AgriServices, Processors, Transport. Blank means any organisation that has opted in. |
| **Target enterprise types (farms only)** | No | Crop, Livestock, Aquaculture, Horticulture, Dairy, Apiculture, Greenhouse, Mixed. A farm qualifies if it has at least one active enterprise of a chosen type. |
| **Results framework (indicators)** | No | A quick list. See below. |

**Pick on map.** Next to the districts box, the button **Pick on map** opens a small map with a dot for every district. Use the province drop-down in the map's header to show one province, then click a dot to select a district (it turns green, and the box and a count update), and click again to remove it. **Clear** removes all, and the X closes the map. This writes the district names for you.

**Quick indicators.** Each row has four boxes: **key** (a short code, for example farms_trained; spaces become underscores and capitals become small letters), **Label** (for example "Maize yield"), **Unit** and **Target**. Press **+ Add indicator** for another row and the X to delete a row. The form starts with one example, "Farmers trained". Rows without both a key and a label are dropped. Keys must be unique ("Indicator keys must be unique and non-empty"). Detailed settings (baseline, dates, means of verification, breakdowns, computed values) are added later in the **Results framework** tab (Chapter 5).

> **Tip:** If you belong to a district or provincial branch, your programmes are automatically confined to that area. A district-branch member cannot target another district: "Your branch only runs programmes in {district}".

**Editing.** In the workspace press **Edit programme** to open the same form filled in; the button reads **Save changes**. You need "programmes edit" permission. A message "Programme updated." confirms.

**Unknown participant types** are refused: "Unknown participant type(s): …".

### Who is eligible

An organisation is **eligible** for a programme when all of these are true:

- It is an active private-sector account with **data sharing turned on** (farm or any vendor type).
- Its business type is in the programme's "Who can be enrolled" list (if the list is empty, any type).
- Its province is in the target provinces, if you set any.
- Its district is in the target districts, if you set any.
- It has an active enterprise of a target type, if you set any (this applies to farms only).

Organisations that match and are not yet enrolled appear in the eligible list (below), and as amber circles on the programme map.

### The programme workspace

Press a programme card. At the top:

- A **Programmes** back link, the programme name, a status label, and "funder · start → end".
- **Donor report** (see "Reports for donors").
- **Edit programme**.

Below are six tabs: **Results**, **Map**, **Participants (n)**, **Support ledger (n)** (n is the number of support records), **Results framework** and **Notices**.

#### Results tab

**What it is for.** Live progress against the target and budget.

| Block | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Enrolment** | Number enrolled over the target, the percentage with a green bar, and a line "x active · y districts · z enterprises · w active batches". |
| **Budget used** | Amount of budget used in the programme currency, the percentage with a bar (amber if above 100 percent) and "n support records to m farms". |
| **Records since start** | Production records the participating farms entered since the programme start date. |
| **Harvest logged** | Total harvest quantity and number of harvest records since start. |
| **Market activity** | Participants' active marketplace listings and contracts fulfilled since start. |
| **Participants by type** | How many participants of each business type. |
| **Women · youth** | Number of women-headed and youth-led active participants, household members reached and how many profiles are recorded. The block is amber when some active participants have no profile yet. |
| **Reports resolved** | Farmer problem reports closed since the start. |
| **Reports open** | Farmer problem reports still open. |

Below: **Support by type** (a bar chart of value by type), **Indicator progress** (each indicator with its latest value over target, a progress bar and an "as of" date) and **Results by district** (**Province**, **District**, **Farms**, **Records**, **Harvest**, **Support value**).

> **Note:** "Records", "Harvest" and "Reports" come from the farms' own record-keeping, for farms that are enrolled. Harvest units follow what each farm typed (kg, bags, birds), so they are added as plain numbers.

#### Map tab (programme map)

**What it is for.** Shows where participants are, where support has gone and where eligible organisations still wait to be enrolled.

**How to read it**

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Purple filled circle | Enrolled participants in that district. Bigger means more. |
| Amber hollow circle | Eligible organisations not yet enrolled. Bigger means more. |
| Green ring | A district named in your targeting. |
| Small white circle with green edge | A targeted district with nobody eligible or enrolled yet. |
| Dashed outline | The district is not in the built-in list, so the circle sits at the middle of its province. |

The line above the map reads, for example, "Hand-in-Hand: 40 participants in 6 districts · 85 eligible organisations not yet enrolled · ZMW 120,000 support delivered." If some participants have no usable district, it adds how many are not drawn.

**Step by step**

1. Click a circle or a row in the right-hand list (rows are sorted by participants, then eligible).
2. The panel shows **Participants**, **Eligible**, **Support events** and **Support (ZMW)**, then "Enrolled by type" and "Eligible, not yet enrolled" by business type.
3. If the programme is active and the district has eligible organisations, press **Enrol from {district}**. This jumps to the **Participants** tab with the district filter already filled in.
4. Press the X to go back to all districts.

#### Participants tab (enrolling and managing)

**What it is for.** Enrolling organisations and tracking their status.

**Enrolling one by one**

1. Open the **Participants** tab and press **Enrol participants** (shown when the programme is active).
2. A card **Eligible organisations (opted in to data sharing, matching targeting)** opens with three boxes: **Search by farm name**, **District (from the map)** and **Cohort (optional, e.g. 2026 Season A)**.
3. Narrow the list. It shows up to 200 organisations, sorted by name. Each row has the name, business type, district and province, and enterprise categories.
4. Type a cohort name if you want to label this group (for example "2026 Season A" or "Camp 3"). The cohort you typed is applied to the next organisation you enrol.
5. Press **Enrol** on a row. The row disappears and a green message says "{name} enrolled — the organisation has been notified."
6. Repeat for each organisation. Press the X to close the card.

> **Note:** There is no "select all" or bulk-enrol button. Enrolment is one organisation at a time. To enrol many in a district, filter by that district and press **Enrol** down the list. Set the cohort first so every enrolment carries it.

**What the organisation gets.** Every active member receives a notification titled "Enrolled in {programme}": "{your organisation} has enrolled your farm in "{programme}" ({funder}). You will receive programme notices and support records here."

**The Enrolled participants table**

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Participant** | Name of the organisation. |
| **Type** | Business type (Farms, Cooperatives, Agro dealers and so on). |
| **Household** | If a household profile exists: "Woman-headed", "Man-headed" or "Head n/a", plus "· youth" and "· n members". Press it to open the profile. If none, an amber **Add profile** link. |
| **District** | Where it is based. |
| **Cohort** | Cohort label or a dash. |
| **Evidence** | For farms and cooperatives: records since start and harvest quantity. For other types: active listings and contracts fulfilled. |
| **Support** | Number of support records and their value in the programme currency. |
| **Status** | **Active**, **Completed** or **Withdrawn**. |
| Actions | For active rows: **Complete** and **Withdraw**. For others: **Reactivate**. |

**Enrolment statuses**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Active** | Takes part. Counts in the indicator values, notices and support recording. |
| **Completed** | Finished the programme. No longer counted as active. |
| **Withdrawn** | Left or was removed. No longer counted as active. |

Pressing **Complete** or **Withdraw** shows "{name} marked completed." or "{name} marked withdrawn." **Reactivate** sets it back to active. An organisation that was withdrawn can be enrolled again; its enrolment date is reset.

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Farm is not eligible: it must have opted in to data sharing and match the programme targeting | The organisation withdrew consent or no longer matches. |
| Farm is already enrolled | It is already active in this programme. |
| Programme is closed; enrolment is not open (or suspended) | Change the programme status first. |
| Permission denied: you need create access to enrolment | Ask the administrator. |

**Household profile (from the partner side).** Press the **Household** cell to open **Household profile — {name}** and fill the same form the farm sees (see Chapter 5 and Chapter 6). A note says it is "Used only for disaggregated programme results (women, youth, household members reached). The participant can also fill this in from their own settings." You can save a profile only for organisations enrolled in one of your programmes, and only if you have "programmes edit" permission: "Permission denied: only the organisation's director / admin or a partner it is enrolled with can set this profile".

#### Support ledger tab

**What it is for.** The record of what the programme actually delivered: inputs, cash, training.

**Record support delivered**

1. Open **Support ledger**. The card **Record support delivered** needs at least one active participant ("Enrol farms first.").
2. Fill in the form.
3. Press **Record**.

| Field | Required | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Participant drop-down | Yes | Active participants, shown as "name — district". |
| Support type drop-down | Yes | **Input voucher**, **Seed / seedlings**, **Fertiliser**, **Livestock / fingerlings / chicks**, **Equipment / tools**, **Cash grant**, **Training**, **Extension service**, **Market linkage**, **Working capital / grant**, **Certification support**, **Digital tools / devices**, **Cold chain / storage**, **Other**. |
| Date delivered | Yes | Defaults to today. |
| **Description** | Yes | For example "50kg D-compound + 10kg SC627 seed". |
| **Qty** and **Unit** | No | Quantity defaults to 1. |
| **Value** and currency box | No | Money value of the delivery. Currency defaults to the programme currency. This value adds up to "Support delivered" and "Budget used". |
| **Voucher / GRN reference** | No | Voucher, goods-received or receipt number. |
| **Notify farm** checkbox | Ticked by default | Sends the farm a notification titled "{programme}: {type} received" with the description, quantity, value and reference. |

A green message says "Support recorded — the farm has been notified." (or just "Support recorded.").

**The Support ledger table** lists **Date**, **Farm**, **Type**, **Description**, **Qty**, **Value** and **Ref**. With nothing recorded: "Nothing recorded yet."

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Farm is not actively enrolled in this programme | Reactivate or enrol the participant first. |
| Value and quantity cannot be negative | Use zero or more. |
| Invalid support type | The type was not on the list. |

> **Watch out:** The ledger has no edit or delete. If you record the wrong amount, tell your administrator; there is no screen to remove a support record.

#### Notices tab

**What it is for.** Sending a message to everyone actively enrolled.

**Step by step**

1. Open **Notices**. The card is titled **Send a notice to all actively enrolled farms**.
2. Type a **Title** (for example "Input collection this Friday") and a **Message**.
3. Choose a priority: **Info**, **Warning** or **Critical**.
4. Press **Send notice**. A green line says "Notice sent to 40 farms (122 people)."

Each active member of each actively enrolled organisation receives a notification titled "[{programme}] {title}" with your organisation name at the bottom. Completed and withdrawn participants are not included. There is no way to pick individual recipients; for one farm, an extension officer can use **Send advice to this farm**.

### Reports for donors

**What it is for.** A ready-made results report for the donor, as PDF or Excel.

**Before you start.** You need "programmes view" permission. Observers can export too.

**Step by step**

1. In the programme workspace press **Donor report** (top right).
2. A small card **Results report — reporting period** opens with **From** (defaults to the programme start) and **To** (defaults to today).
3. Press **PDF** or **Excel**. The file opens in a new tab and a green message says, for example, "PDF results report generated for 2026-01-01 → 2026-10-18."

**What the report holds**

| Section | PDF | Excel sheet |
|---|---|---|
| Cover | Partner, programme name, period, funder, reference, status, dates, objective, targeting | **Summary** |
| 1. Summary of results | Farms enrolled against target, districts, active batches, production records, harvest, support delivered, budget used, issues resolved and open, notices issued | **Summary** |
| 2. Results framework | Each indicator against baseline and target with progress, automatic values marked with a star, the sex and age breakdown, participant figures and the reading history | **Indicators**, **Indicator readings** |
| 3. Enrolment and production | Per district: farms, active, records, harvest, support; plus the list of enrolled farms | **Districts**, **Enrolled farms** |
| 4. Support delivered | By type and the full ledger | **Support by type**, **Support ledger** |
| Methodology | A note that enrolment, support and readings are recorded by the partner and production figures come from the farms' own records | — |

The period limits the production records, harvest and support rows. The report uses the same calculations as the screens, so it matches what you see.

**If something goes wrong.** "Period end must be after period start" means the dates are reversed.

---

## Chapter 5: Programme Partners — Monitoring and Evaluation (Results Framework)

### What it is for

The **Results framework** tab is a logical framework (logframe). It lets you write the chain of results your donor expects, attach indicators to each level, record readings and let the platform compute indicators for you from participants' own records.

**Before you start.** You need "programmes edit" permission to build or change the framework (administrator or programme manager). Everyone with programme access can read it.

> **Watch out:** In the current screens, the framework-editing buttons (**Goal**, **Outcome**, **Indicator**, **+ Reading**, edit and delete icons) appear only for users who have "programmes edit" permission. The M&E officer role normally has view access to programmes and edit access to M&E, so an M&E officer may see the framework but not the buttons. If that happens, ask an administrator to give the M&E officer edit access to **Programmes** in **Team & Branches**.

### Key words

| Word | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| Goal / impact | The big change the programme hopes to contribute to. |
| Outcome | A medium-term change for participants (for example higher yields). |
| Output | What the programme delivers directly (for example farmers trained). |
| Activity | A piece of work that produces an output. |
| Indicator | A number you track to show progress. |
| Baseline | The starting value before the programme. |
| Target | The value you aim to reach. |
| Means of verification | How you will prove the number (for example training registers). |
| Disaggregation | Splitting a number by group (women and men, youth and adults). |

### The result chain

The chain has four levels, each with a coloured label: **Goal / impact**, **Outcome**, **Output**, **Activity**.

**Building the chain**

1. Open the **Results framework** tab. With nothing there, you see "No results framework yet. Start with the goal, add outcomes and outputs, then attach indicators — or add a computed indicator such as "Participants enrolled" straight away."
2. Press **Goal**. (This button disappears once a goal exists.) Type the **Goal / impact statement**; optionally a code (the box says "Code 1.1") and **Assumptions / risks (optional)**. Press **Save**.
3. Press **Outcome** at the top to add an outcome under the goal. Or hover over a goal and press **+ Outcome**.
4. On an outcome, press **+ Output**. On an output, press **+ Activity**. Each child sits under its parent and is indented with a line.
5. Use the pencil icon to edit a statement, and the bin icon to delete.

**Rules and messages**

- An outcome sits under a goal, an output under an outcome and an activity under an output. A wrong placement is refused ("An outcome cannot sit under a …").
- Deleting a result asks "Delete this {level} and everything under it?" and also removes everything below it. Indicators attached to it are kept and move to "Indicators not linked to a result".

### Indicators

Press **Indicator** at the top (a free indicator, linkable later), or **+ Indicator** on any result (attached to it).

**The indicator form**

| Field | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Indicator** (for example "Farmers trained in GAP") | Required. The platform makes the internal key from this label. |
| **Unit** | For example farmers, kg, ZMW. |
| Result drop-down | **Not linked to a result**, or any result in the chain. |
| Source drop-down | **Manual readings**, or a computed source (see below). |
| **Baseline** and **Baseline date** | Starting value and when it was measured. |
| **Target** and **Target date** | Goal value and the date to reach it. |
| **Means of verification (e.g. training registers)** | How you will verify. |
| **Data source / responsible** | Who or what supplies the number. |
| **Disaggregate by** buttons | **Sex**, **Age (youth ≤ 35)**, **Participant type**, **District**. The form starts with Sex and Age ticked. |

Press **Save indicator**. A duplicate label gives 'Indicator key "…" already exists in this programme'.

**What an indicator row shows**

- The label, a green "computed" mark for automatic indicators, and "MoV: …".
- "Baseline 20 (01/2026) → Target 100 by 12/2026 · Achieved 64 farmers as of 2026-10-18 · 55%".
- A progress bar (green at 100 percent or more, blue at 50 or more, amber below).
- The breakdown by each chosen group.

**How progress is worked out.** If there is a baseline and it differs from the target: (current value minus baseline) divided by (target minus baseline), times 100. If there is no baseline: current value divided by target, times 100. With no target there is no percentage.

### Computed (automatic) indicators

A computed indicator updates itself each time you open the page, using only **actively enrolled** participants. Pick one from the Source drop-down (each is marked with a lightning symbol):

| Source | What it counts |
|---|---|
| **Participants enrolled (active)** | Active participants. |
| **Women-headed participants** | Active participants whose household head is recorded as female. |
| **Youth-headed participants (≤ 35)** | Participants with a youth head (see the household profile). |
| **Household members reached** | Sum of household sizes (counts 1 where no size is recorded). |
| **Participants who received support** | Distinct participants with at least one support record. |
| **Support events delivered** | Number of support records. |
| **Training events delivered** | Number of support records of type Training. |
| **Value of support delivered** | Sum of support values. |
| **Harvest quantity logged since start** | Harvest quantities participants entered since the programme start. |
| **Active market listings** | Participants' active marketplace listings. |
| **Contracts fulfilled since start** | Participants' fulfilled trade contracts since the start date. |

Computed indicators do not take manual readings (the **+ Reading** button is hidden; the platform refuses with "This indicator is computed automatically from platform records; it does not take manual readings").

Breakdown groups for computed values:

- **Sex**: Women, Men, Other, Not recorded.
- **Age**: Youth (35 and under), Adult (36 to 59), Senior (60 and over), unknown. Age comes from the year of birth. If only "youth-led" is known, it is Youth or Adult.
- **Participant type** and **District**.

> **Tip:** A note under the framework reminds you: "add computed indicators "Women-headed participants" and "Youth-headed participants" — donors ask for both." For these to be meaningful, participants need household profiles (see below and Chapter 6).

### Recording a manual reading

**Step by step**

1. On a manual indicator press **+ Reading**.
2. A card **Record reading — {indicator}** opens.
3. Fill in:

| Field | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Period end** | The last day of the reporting period. Defaults to today. |
| **Total value** | Required. The overall number for the period. |
| **Source / notes** | For example "crop-cut survey, 40 plots". |
| Breakdown boxes | One box per group for each breakdown chosen on the indicator: Sex (Women, Men, Other), Age (Youth, Adult, Senior), Participant type (the main types), District (type a district name, press Enter, then fill its number). |

4. Press **Record**. A green message says "Reading recorded."

**Checks.** For Sex and Age, the groups must add up to the total. As you type, a line such as "= 120 of 120" appears green when it matches and red when it does not. The platform refuses a mismatch ("sex breakdown (110) must add up to the total (120)") and negative numbers ("Disaggregation values cannot be negative").

The indicator row then shows the **latest reading by period** as the "Achieved" value. Older readings stay in the history.

### Manual reading history

Under the framework, a card **Manual reading history** lists each reading: indicator label, notes and breakdown on the left, value and unit on the right, with the period and the name of who recorded it.

### Editing and deleting

- Pencil on an indicator opens the form again.
- Bin on an indicator asks 'Delete indicator "…" and its readings?' and removes both.
- Indicators not linked to any result appear in a group titled **Indicators not linked to a result**.

> **Tip:** The quick indicators you typed on the programme form appear here automatically as indicators with a key, label, unit and target. Edit them here to add baselines, dates and breakdowns.

### The household profile of participants

Disaggregated results need to know who is behind each participating organisation. The **household profile** holds that. It can be filled in by the organisation itself (Chapter 6) or by the partner it is enrolled with (the **Household** cell in the Participants tab).

| Field | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Head of household** | **Sex — not recorded**, **Female**, **Male**, **Other / prefer not to say**. |
| **Year of birth** | For example 1990. Must be a believable year ("Birth year looks wrong"). A head aged 35 or under counts as youth. |
| **Household size** | 0 to 60. |
| **Land (ha)** | Zero or more. |
| **Female members** and **Male members** | 0 to 60 each. |
| **National ID (stored hashed)** | Optional. Stored only as a scrambled code, used to avoid counting the same person twice. The field then says "On file — enter to replace". |
| **Household includes a person with a disability** | Tick box. |

Press **Save profile**. Only counts are shared with the partner: women, youth, household members, disability counts. The results tiles show them.

If a number is out of range, you see messages such as "household size out of range", "Land cannot be negative" or "Sex must be F, M or X".

---

## Chapter 6: The Farmer's Side — Data Sharing, Programmes and Extension Officers

This chapter is for a farm, cooperative or vendor that is looked at by government, a programme partner or an extension officer. It explains what you control, what others can see and what you receive.

### The consent switch: Share with government & partners

**What it is for.** Decides whether your organisation is counted in the government and partner dashboards, and whether extension officers and programmes can find you.

**Before you start.** Only the **director** of the organisation can change the switch or the location boxes. Other members see them greyed out with the hint "Only a director can change this". Your **province** and **district** must be filled in, because they decide which dashboards, advisories, officers and programmes can reach you.

**Step by step**

1. Open **Settings** (farm workspace: **Platform Settings**; vendor workspace: the **Settings** tab). The card is titled **Share with government & partners**.
2. Read the explanation: when enabled, your counts (enterprises, records, prices, reports) are included in de-identified district and national dashboards used by the Ministry of Agriculture, extension services and development partners. Your organisation's name is never shown on those dashboards. You also receive district advisories.
3. Press the switch. It turns green and the message "Sharing enabled." appears, with "Sharing since {date}". Press again to turn it off: "Sharing disabled."
4. Fill in **Province** (for example Lusaka) and **District** (for example Chongwe) and press **Save**. You see "Location saved." The note under the boxes says "Province and district place your organisation on district dashboards and target advisories to you."

**What turning sharing on allows**

| Who | What they can do |
|---|---|
| Government and partner dashboards | Include your totals (never your name) in the production, market, disease, climate and registry figures for your district. |
| Advisories | Government or partner advisories for your area and enterprise type reach every member of your organisation. |
| Extension officers | Officers in your district can find your farm by name and add it to their caseload. |
| Programme partners | Partners can find you in their eligible list and enrol you. Once enrolled, they see your organisation's name and the evidence they report on (records, harvest, listings). |
| Lenders | Your credit summary shows "Data-sharing consent" as a plus point when you share it (see "The lender share link" below). |

**What turning sharing off does.** From then on you drop out of the dashboards and out of the "eligible" and "available" lists, so no new officer or programme can add you. The platform does not automatically remove an extension officer or programme enrolment that already exists; if you want that to end, ask the officer or partner to remove you, or contact them by phone.

**If something goes wrong**

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Permission denied: only a director can change data-sharing consent | You are not the director. |
| Only farm organisations can share data | The account is not a business account. |

### The household profile

**What it is for.** Programmes report results by sex, age and household size (for example "women-headed farms trained"). This profile gives them those counts. Only counts are ever shared; your name and details are not.

On the same Settings card there is a section **Household profile** with a button **Add profile** (or **Edit** once saved). Only the director sees the button.

**Step by step**

1. Press **Add profile**.
2. Fill in the form (see the table in Chapter 5): **Head of household** (Female, Male, Other / prefer not to say), **Year of birth**, **Household size**, **Land (ha)**, **Female members**, **Male members**, **National ID (stored hashed)** and the tick box **Household includes a person with a disability**.
3. Press **Save profile**. You see "Household profile saved." The summary line reads, for example, "Woman-headed · head aged 34 (youth) · 7 household members · 2 ha · ID on file".

Until you save, the card says "Not recorded yet."

**Good to know**

- The national ID is stored only as a scrambled code. It cannot be read back; it is only used to avoid counting the same person twice.
- A partner that has enrolled you may also fill in this profile for you from the Participants tab; you can correct it any time.
- Nobody can see the profile unless you are enrolled in a programme.

### Your extension officer
