When an officer adds your farm to a caseload, every active member of your organisation receives a notification: "Extension officer assigned: {name} ({organisation}) is now your extension officer. Contact: {phone or email}."

In the farm workspace **Settings**, a card **Your extension officer** shows:

- Each assigned officer's name, organisation, phone (tap to call) and email (tap to write).
- With no officer: "No extension officer is assigned yet. Officers in your district can add your farm once data sharing is enabled and your province / district are set."
- A **Visit log** listing the last ten visits: the purpose, date and officer, **Findings:**, **Advice:** and "Follow-up planned {date}" when a follow-up is still open.

You also receive notifications when the officer: logs a visit with recommendations ("Extension visit: {purpose}"), sends advice ("{title}") or resolves one of your problem reports ("Report resolved: {title}").

### Development programmes you are enrolled in

When a partner enrols you, every active member receives "Enrolled in {programme}". In the farm workspace **Settings**, a card **Development programmes** appears (it is hidden until you are enrolled in at least one). For each programme it shows:

- The programme name, partner name and "funded by {funder}".
- A status label (**Active** in green) and your cohort, if any.
- **Support received** with the total value, and each delivery: type and description, reference, date and value.

You are also notified for each support record the partner chose to notify you about ("{programme}: {type} received") and for every programme notice ("[{programme}] {title}").

> **Note:** The **Your extension officer** and **Development programmes** cards are shown in the farm workspace Settings. Vendor workspaces show the **Share with government & partners** card in their **Settings** tab; programme notices and advisories still reach vendors as notifications.

### The lender share link

**What it is for.** A farm can give a bank or microfinance institution a private, read-only credit summary built from its own records. The summary includes the programmes you take part in and your extension officer, which lenders find reassuring.

**Before you start.** Only a **director** or **finance manager** can create or revoke a link. Open **Credit Profile** in the farm workspace (see Part 4 — Running the Farm Business: Inventory, Sales, Finance, Credit and Reports for the score itself).

**Step by step**

1. In **Credit Profile**, find the card **Share with a lender**. It explains: "Creates a private, read-only link. The lender sees this summary without an account. You choose how long it lasts and can revoke it at any time."
2. Fill in **Lender** (for example Zanaco, NATSAVE, FINCA, Vision Fund), **Credit officer email / phone (optional)** and **Purpose**.
3. Choose how long the link lasts: **7 days**, **14 days**, **30 days**, **60 days** or **90 days**.
4. Press **Create share link**. A green box titled "Send this link to the lender" shows the link; press **Copy**.
5. Send the link to the lender yourself (for example by email or WhatsApp). The platform does not send it for you.

**Who has access.** The card to the right lists each link: lender, contact, purpose, dates created and expiry, "opened n times" and when it was last opened, with a label **Active**, **Expired** or **Revoked**. For active links there is a copy button and, for directors and finance managers, a **Revoke** button (the ban sign). A revoked or expired link stops working at once.

**What the lender sees.** A read-only page titled "AGRINUXES — Farm credit summary — Shared by the farm for credit assessment · read-only", with the line "Prepared for {lender} · {purpose} · link valid until {date}", and a **Print** button. It contains the farm card, **Financial performance (batch financials)**, **Monthly revenue, last 12 months**, **Trading history** and **Verification & support network**. The last card lists with ticks: data-sharing consent, extension officer assigned (with visits in 12 months), programme enrolments (programme and partner names), certifications, insurance and loan history, plus the programme support value received.

If the link is wrong, expired or revoked, the lender sees an error page that says "Ask the farm to issue a new share link from their Credit Profile."

---

## Chapter 7: Which Account Should I Use?

### Quick decision guide

| If you are… | Use this account | Why |
|---|---|---|
| A buyer, or someone just looking for a vet, tractor or seed supplier | **Individual** | Free to browse, save favourites on the dashboard and call sellers. No farm tools. |
| A farmer or cooperative who wants to keep records, sell and book services | **Business / Farm** (business type Farmer / Farm or Cooperative) | Full farm workspace, marketplaces with booking forms, and the data-sharing and programme features from Chapter 6. |
| A vet, equipment-hire firm, dealer, food seller, supplier, service provider, processor or transporter | **Business / Farm** with the matching business type | Vendor workspace with its own tabs (see Part 6 and Part 7). |
| A ministry or district office that needs numbers and wants to warn farmers | **Government** (dashboard viewer or administrator) | De-identified dashboards, national map, ecosystem view, advisories. |
| A government or NGO adviser who visits farms | **Extension officer** (or supervisor) | A named caseload, visit log, follow-ups and direct advice. |
| A donor, FAO-type project or NGO that gives inputs, cash or training | **Supporting partner** | Programmes, enrolment, support ledger, notices, results framework and donor report. |
| An auditor or donor representative who only reads | **Partner Observer** (added by a partner administrator) | Read-only view of programmes, results and dashboards. |

### Things to keep in mind

- **One login, one role.** Each account has one role. If you are both a farmer and an extension officer, use two accounts with different emails and phone numbers.
- **You cannot change account type later.** An individual who wants to run a farm registers a separate Business / Farm account.
- **The first person is the administrator.** Whoever creates a government or partner organisation becomes its administrator and sees every page. Add colleagues with narrower roles from **Team & Branches**.
- **Government and partner accounts hold no farm data of their own.** They read others' numbers (de-identified) or work with farms that agreed to be enrolled or assigned.
- **Extension supervisors, administrators and observers** are created by an administrator from **Team & Branches**; they are not choices on the public registration form (which offers only Dashboard viewer, Extension officer and Supporting partner).

---

## Chapter 8: What Each Account Can and Cannot See

### Who sees what

| Information | Individual | Farm / vendor (own data) | Gov viewer | Gov admin | Extension officer | Partner manager | Partner M&E / observer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Public marketplace listings and provider profiles | Yes | Yes | Counts and map only | Counts and map only | Counts and map only | Counts and map only | Counts and map only |
| Their own farm records, finance, stock | No (none) | Yes | No | No | No | No | No |
| Another farm's name | No | No | No | No | Only farms on own caseload | Only farms enrolled in own programmes | Same as manager (read-only) |
| Another farm's records and reports | No | No | Totals only, never per farm | Totals only | Caseload farms only: enterprises, reports, AI diagnoses, alerts, record counts | Enrolled farms: record counts, harvest quantity, listings, support | Same, read-only |
| District and national totals | No | No | Yes (consenting farms only) | Yes | No (no dashboard pages) | Yes (read-only) | Yes (read-only) |
| Ecosystem and National Map | No | No | Yes | Yes | No | Yes | Yes |
| Farms that did not opt in | — | — | Counted as organisations only | Counted as organisations only | Never | Never | Never |
| Household profile | No | Own only | No | No | No | Enrolled participants only | Enrolled participants (view) |
| Credit summary | No | Own; can share link | No | No | No | No | No |

### What each role can do

| Action | Gov viewer | Gov admin | Extension officer | Extension supervisor | Partner manager | Partner M&E officer | Partner observer | Partner admin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| View dashboards, ecosystem, map, registry | Yes | Yes | No page | No page | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Send an advisory to an area | No | Yes | No page | No page* | Yes* | No | No | Yes |
| Manage a caseload and log visits | No | No | Yes | Yes | No | No | No | No |
| Resolve a farm's problem report and send advice | No | No | Yes (caseload farms) | Yes (caseload farms) | No | No | No | No |
| Create or edit a programme | No | Yes | No | Yes | Yes | No | No | Yes |
| Enrol participants and record support | No | Yes | No | Yes | Yes | No | No | Yes |
| Send a programme notice | No | Yes | No | Yes | Yes | No | No | Yes |
| Build the results framework | No | Yes | No | Yes | Yes | See note in Chapter 5 | No | Yes |
| Create a donor report (PDF / Excel) | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Manage team, roles and branches | No | Yes | No | No | No | No | No | Yes |

*Advisories: the permission exists for these roles, but the extension screen has no Advisories page. A partner manager sees an **Advisories** page and can create (not edit or delete).

> **Tip:** An administrator can change any person's page access one module at a time from the shield button in **Team & Branches**. The table above shows the standard defaults.

### What the farm always controls

- Whether it is counted at all (the consent switch).
- Its province and district (so that dashboards and advisories reach it).
- Its household profile.
- Its lender links (create, copy, revoke).
- Its own users: only the director changes consent.

### Privacy in one paragraph

Government dashboards show only totals from farms that said yes, and never a farm name. Partners see the names of farms they enrol, and only the evidence that matters to the programme. Extension officers see only farms they add, and only in their own area. Nobody outside a farm can read its finance, inventory, staff or credit records through these accounts.

# Appendix A — The Template Catalogue

The app ships with **94 built-in templates**.

Every built-in template, grouped by category. "Stages" lists the stages a cycle created from the template will contain, in order, with the typical length where the template gives one. Every template also comes with a starter care guide (see Appendix D) and the daily record fields of its category (see Appendix B). Your company can copy and change any of them (see Part 2).

## Poultry and Birds (12 templates)

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Broiler Chicken** | Commercial broiler production — batch-based grow-out from day-old chick to harvest. | Brooding (14 d) → Grower (14 d) → Finishing (14 d) → Harvest / Sale (1 d) |
| **Layer Chicken** | Commercial egg production — pullet rearing through peak lay and beyond. | Chick (56 d) → Grower / Developer (70 d) → Point of Lay (28 d) → Peak Production (126 d) → Late Lay & Depletion (224 d) |
| **Broiler Breeder** | Breeding flock producing hatching eggs for broiler hatchery. | Egg Production (280 d) |
| **Meat Duck (Pekin/Muscovy)** | Fast-growing ducks for meat production. | Brooding → Growing → Harvest / Slaughter |
| **Layer Duck (Khaki Campbell/Indian Runner)** | High-laying duck breeds producing 250–330 eggs/year. | Rearing → Laying Production → Depletion / Cull |
| **Turkey (Broad-Breasted / Bronze)** | Commercial turkey grow-out for festive/premium meat market. | Brooding & Starting → Growing & Finishing → Slaughter & Sale |
| **Guinea Fowl (Helmeted)** | Hardy native African bird for meat and eggs. | Brooding → Range Rearing → Harvest |
| **Quail (Japanese / Coturnix)** | Fast-maturing quail for eggs (6 weeks to lay) and meat. | Brooding → Laying Production → Deplete / Meat Sale |
| **Indigenous / Village Chicken** | Free-range indigenous chickens for local markets. | Brooding → Range Growing → Market / Sale |
| **Goose (Meat & Foie Gras)** | Embden or Toulouse geese for meat, fat, and feather production. | Brooding → Growing & Grazing → Finishing & Harvest |
| **Ostrich** | Commercial ostrich farming for premium meat, leather, and feathers. | Chick Rearing → Growing → Finishing & Slaughter |
| **Pigeon (Squab Production)** | Breeding pairs produce squabs for gourmet market. | Loft Set-Up & Pairing → Continuous Production |

## Livestock (9 templates)

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Dairy Cattle** | Cow milk production — herd management, lactation tracking, and milk sales. | Dry Period (60 d) → Calving (3 d) → Early Lactation (100 d) → Mid & Late Lactation (205 d) |
| **Beef Cattle** | Beef production — cow-calf, stocker, or feedlot operation tracking. | Receiving & Adaptation (21 d) → Growing / Stocker (90 d) → Feedlot Finishing (90 d) |
| **Pig Production** | Commercial pig production — from farrowing to finisher. | Gestation (114 d) → Farrowing & Suckling (28 d) → Weaner (42 d) → Grower (42 d) → Finisher (42 d) |
| **Meat Goat (Boer / Kalahari Red)** | Commercial meat goat production. | Mating → Gestation → Kidding → Growing to Market |
| **Dairy Goat (Saanen / Toggenburg / Alpine)** | Dairy goat production for fresh milk, cheese, and yoghurt. | Dry Period → Freshening / Kidding → Peak Lactation → Mid & Late Lactation |
| **Meat Sheep (Dorper / Damara / Merino x)** | Commercial mutton/lamb production. | Joining / Mating → Gestation → Lambing → Growing to Market |
| **Rabbit (New Zealand White / Californian)** | Intensive rabbit meat production. | Mating / Kindling → Nursing / Litter Rearing → Growing to Slaughter |
| **Deer Farming (Venison)** | Semi-intensive deer farming for premium venison meat, velvet antler (velvet for medicine), and trophy/breeding stock sales. | Rut & Breeding (45 d) → Pregnancy (233 d) → Fawning & Early Life (60 d) → Weaning & Venison Grow-Out (365 d) → Velvet Harvest / Slaughter (14 d) |
| **Alpaca / Llama / Camel** | Camelid production for premium fiber (alpaca), milk, meat, or pack animal services. | Mating & Pregnancy (345 d) → Birthing (60 d) → Cria Grow-Out & Weaning (180 d) → Annual Shearing & Fiber Harvest (7 d) |

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Tilapia (Pond)** | Pond-based tilapia grow-out — from fingerlings to harvest. | Pond Preparation (14 d) → Stocking (1 d) → Grow-out (150 d) → Harvest (2 d) |
| **Catfish (Tank / Pond)** | Catfish production in tanks or earthen ponds. | Grow-out (120 d) |

| **Black Soldier Fly (BSF)** | BSF larvae convert organic waste into high-protein animal feed. | Breeding Colony Management (21 d) → Egg Incubation (4 d) → Larval Grow-Out & Waste Bioconversion (14 d) → Harvest & Processing (3 d) |

| **Mealworm Farming** | Mealworm beetle larvae are a high-protein feed additive for poultry, fish, and humans. | Beetle Colony (30 d) → Larval Grow-Out (70 d) → Pupation (21 d) → Harvest & Processing (2 d) |
| **Silkworm (Sericulture)** | Commercial silkworm rearing for raw silk thread. | Egg Incubation & Hatching (10 d) → Early Instars (I–III) — Young Age Rearing (12 d) → Late Instars (IV–V) — Late Age Rearing (12 d) → Mounting & Cocooning (5 d) → Cocoon Harvest & Reeling (3 d) |
| **Vermiculture (Earthworms)** | Continuous worm composting converts organic waste into premium vermicompost and worm protein biomass. | Bed Set-Up & Inoculation (14 d) → Continuous Feeding & Production (90 d) → Harvest (7 d) |
| **Snail Farming (Heliciculture)** | Giant African Land Snail or European snail farming for meat, slime (cosmetics), and shells. | Breeding & Egg Laying (60 d) → Egg Incubation & Hatching (28 d) → Nursery (60 d) → Grow-Out to Market Weight (150 d) → Harvest & Processing (7 d) |

## Aquatic Plants and Algae (2 templates)

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Seaweed Farming** | Marine seaweed cultivation on longlines or nets. | Site Selection & Infrastructure Setup (14 d) → Seeding (3 d) → Grow-Out (52 d) → Harvest & Drying (7 d) |
| **Spirulina / Microalgae** | Intensive raceway or tank cultivation of Spirulina for high-protein nutraceutical powder, animal feed additive, or aquaculture larval feed. | Culture Build-Up (14 d) → Continuous Production (365 d) → Harvesting, Filtration & Drying (2 d) |

## Forestry and Agroforestry (2 templates)

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Timber Forestry** | Long-rotation timber plantation management. | Site Preparation (60 d) → Planting (30 d) → Establishment (1095 d) → Thinning (730 d) → Final Harvest (180 d) |
| **Agroforestry / Silvopastoral** | Integrated land-use system combining trees with crops and/or livestock on the same block. | System Design & Planning (30 d) → Tree Establishment (365 d) → Crop & Livestock Integration (365 d) → Tree Harvest (60 d) |

## Agro-Processing (6 templates)

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Maize Milling** | Maize grain → mealie meal, bran, and grits. | Grain Receiving & QC (1 d) → Cleaning & Conditioning (1 d) → Milling & Grading (1 d) → Packaging & Dispatch (1 d) |
| **Dairy Processing** | Raw milk → pasteurised milk, yoghurt, cheese, or butter. | Milk Receiving & Testing (1 d) → Pasteurisation (1 d) → Yoghurt Production (1 d) → Packaging & Cold Store (1 d) |
| **Honey Processing & Packing** | Post-harvest honey processing: extraction, settling, filtration, bottling, and labelling. | Receive & Quality Inspection → Uncapping & Extraction → Settling, Filtering & Drying → Bottling & Labelling |
| **Cassava Processing (Flour & Chips)** | Fresh cassava roots converted to high-quality cassava flour (HQF), dried chips, or wet starch. | Receiving, Washing & Peeling → Grating, Pressing & Crumbling → Flash Drying or Sun Drying → Milling, Sieving & Packaging |
| **Fruit Juice Processing** | Small to mid-scale juice processing from mango, passion fruit, orange, guava, and other tropical fruits. | Receiving & Sorting → Extraction & Clarification → Pasteurisation & Hot-Fill |
| **On-Farm Feed Mill** | Small-scale on-farm or commercial feed mill producing compounded feeds (mash or pellets). | Raw Material Procurement & QC → Grinding, Weighing & Mixing → Bagging, Labelling & QC |

## Agricultural Services (5 templates)

| Template | What it is for | Stages |
|---|---|---|
| **Veterinary Clinic / Mobile Vet Services** | Service-cycle template for a veterinary practice or mobile vet service. | Client Intake & Animal Triage → Diagnosis & Treatment → Follow-up & Records |
| **Agricultural Consulting & Advisory** | Consulting service cycle: initial diagnostic, farm plan development, on-site monitoring visits, and final evaluation report. | Initial Farm Diagnostic → Farm Plan Development → Implementation & Monitoring Visits → End-of-Season Evaluation |
| **Agricultural Equipment Hire (Custom Hiring)** | Custom hiring service for tractors, planters, harvesters, threshers, sprayers, and irrigation sets. | Booking & Pre-Season Scheduling → Field Operations → Scheduled Maintenance & Log |
| **Farmer Training & Extension Services** | Training cycle for farmer groups: needs assessment, curriculum design, classroom and field training, and follow-up evaluation. | Needs Assessment & Curriculum Design → Training Delivery → Follow-up & Impact Evaluation |
| **Agricultural Laboratory Testing** | Laboratory service cycle for soil fertility analysis, water quality testing, crop residue testing, and food safety (aflatoxin, heavy metals, pesticide residues). | Sample Intake & Chain of Custody → Laboratory Analysis & QC → Result Review & Report Issuance |


# Appendix B — Daily Record Fields by Category

These are the boxes on the **Daily Log** for each category before you change anything. Fields marked "Required" must be filled in to save a record. "Shown for" tells you which cycle types show the field; "all" means every cycle type of that category. A field marked "Calculated" fills itself in from other boxes. Your company can add, remove and change fields (Part 2).

## Categories without a specific form

If a template belongs to a category that has no form of its own, the Daily Log offers **Activity Today** (text) and **Workers on Site** (number). You can add your own fields (Part 2).

# Appendix C — Cycle Types by Category

When you start a cycle you choose a cycle type. It decides which daily fields show and gives the cycle a sensible expected length. Typical days is the length the app suggests; you can change dates.

## Poultry and Birds

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Broiler Grow-Out** | Day-old chicks → live weight / slaughter. Typically 35–45 days. | 42 |
| **Layer — from Day-Old** | Day-old chicks → pullets → full lay. Includes brooding & growing phases. | 500 |
| **Layer — from Point-of-Lay** | Start with 18-week+ pullets ready to lay. | 380 |
| **Layer — Continuing Flock** | Already-laying flock, tracking ongoing production season. | 90 |
| **Egg Incubation & Hatch** | Set fertile eggs → candle → lock-down → hatch → chick grading. | 22 |
| **Breeder Flock** | Roosters + hens managed for fertile egg production. | 365 |
| **Turkey Grow-Out** | Poults → market weight. Typically 16–20 weeks. | 120 |
| **Duck Cycle** | Ducklings → meat or layer production. | 60 |
| **Quail Cycle** | Quail chicks → eggs or meat. | 90 |

## Livestock

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Dairy Production Season** | Track lactation, milk yield, breeding and dry-off for a dairy herd. | 305 |
| **Beef Grow-Out** | Weaners / stockers → slaughter weight. | 180 |
| **Pig Grow-Out** | Weaners → market weight. Typically 16–20 weeks post-weaning. | 120 |
| **Sow Farrowing Cycle** | Pregnancy → farrowing → weaning for a sow or group of sows. | 90 |
| **Goat Production Season** | Dairy or meat goats — kidding, milking, or grow-out. | 180 |
| **Sheep Season** | Lambing or meat lamb grow-out. | 120 |
| **Rabbit Batch** | Kits at weaning → market weight. Typically 70–90 days. | 80 |

## Aquaculture

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Pond Grow-Out** | Fingerlings stocked into pond → harvest weight. | 180 |
| **Cage Culture** | Fish in floating cages in a lake or dam. | 150 |
| **Tank / RAS System** | Recirculating aquaculture system — controlled indoor environment. | 120 |
| **Shrimp Pond Cycle** | Post-larvae stocking → harvest. Typically 90–120 days. | 100 |
| **Aquaponics Cycle** | Fish + plants in a closed-loop system. | 120 |
| **Hatchery / Nursery Cycle** | Broodstock conditioning → spawning → larvae rearing → fingerlings. | 45 |

## Crops and Grains

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Rain-Fed Season** | Single rainy-season crop — maize, sorghum, groundnuts etc. | 120 |
| **Irrigated Season** | Irrigated crop — can span any calendar period. | 90 |
| **Nursery / Seedbed** | Seed germination and seedling raising before transplanting. | 30 |
| **Ratoon Crop** | Second harvest from the same root system (sugarcane, banana). | 180 |

## Horticulture and Vegetables

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Transplant Cycle** | Seedling transplant → harvest. For tomato, cabbage, kale, pepper. | 90 |
| **Direct-Sown Crop** | Seed direct in field — carrot, beetroot, onion, watermelon. | 75 |
| **Perennial Season** | Track a growing season on a perennial vegetable (chilli, eggplant). | 120 |

## Greenhouse

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Hydroponic Cycle** | NFT, DWC, or drip lettuce / herbs / spinach cycle. | 35 |
| **Greenhouse Tomato** | Transplant to first pick to end-of-season for greenhouse tomato. | 180 |
| **Cut Flower Production** | Planting to first cut and repeat cycles for roses, carnations. | 90 |
| **Strawberry Cycle** | Transplant to first harvest and peak production window. | 75 |

## Orchard and Tree Fruit

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Annual Production Season** | Track flowering → fruit set → harvest → post-harvest for one year. | 365 |
| **New Orchard Planting** | Land prep → planting → establishment phase for new trees. | 180 |
| **Harvest Window** | Focus on the active harvest period only. | 45 |

## Apiary and Bees

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Honey Production Season** | Colony build-up → main flow → extraction → post-harvest assessment. | 120 |
| **Colony Build-Up** | Package or nuc → full colony establishment. | 60 |
| **Queen Rearing Cycle** | Cell grafting → capping → emergence → mating → laying. | 30 |

## Mushroom

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Spawn Block Cycle** | Inoculation → colonisation → pinning → fruiting → end-of-life. | 60 |
| **Bag Spawn Cycle** | Substrate bags inoculated and cycled through fruiting. | 45 |

## Agro-Processing

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Processing Batch** | A defined batch of raw material through the processing line. | 7 |
| **Monthly Operation** | Track one calendar month of processing activity. | 30 |

## Agricultural Services

| Cycle type | What it is | Typical days |
|---|---|---|
| **Service Contract** | A defined service engagement — consulting, training, vet rounds. | 30 |
| **Equipment Hire Period** | Track utilisation and returns for hired equipment. | 14 |


# Appendix D — Starter Care Guides at a Glance

All 94 built-in templates have a starter care guide (1011 items in total): a list of feeding programmes, vaccinations, medicines, sprays, fertiliser, weighing, checks and tasks, each with the day it is due counted from the start of the cycle (day 0). They describe typical practice only. **Always confirm products and doses with your vet, agronomist or extension officer and the product label**, then save your own version (Part 2).

# Appendix E — Roles and Permissions

Every member of a company has one **role**. The role decides which modules they can open and what they can do there. An organisation administrator (the owner/director) can also give one person a different set of permissions from their role, in **Team & Permissions** (Part 1). The four actions are **View** (open and read), **Create** (add new records), **Edit** (change existing records) and **Delete** (remove records).

In the lists below, modules are grouped by what the role may do in them.


## Farm, cooperative and business accounts

| Role | Who it is for |
|---|---|
| **Director / Owner** | Full access, manages the team |
| **Production Manager** | Runs production, inventory and procurement |
| **Finance Manager** | Finance, sales, credit profile, reports |
| **Sales Manager** | Sales, marketplace, trade documents |
| **Supervisor** | Day-to-day production records |
| **Farmhand** | Logs daily records |
| **Veterinary Officer** | Animal health and biosecurity |
| **Driver** | Mobile app, deliveries |

### Director / Owner

Full access, manages the team.

- **Full access (view, add, change, delete):** Dashboard, Smart AI Engine, Daily Planner, Broiler Poultry, Village Chicken, Piggery, Fish Farming, Duck Management, Goats & Sheep, Horticulture, Production Records, Feed & Inventory, Sales & Customers, Financial Management, Credit Profile, Human Resources, Procurement, Biosecurity & Vet, Weather & Fields, Smart Devices & IoT, Reports & BI, Trade & Export, Marketplace, Assets & Infra, Mobile App, Settings, Team & Permissions

### Production Manager

Runs production, inventory and procurement.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** Daily Planner, Broiler Poultry, Village Chicken, Piggery, Fish Farming, Duck Management, Goats & Sheep, Horticulture, Production Records, Feed & Inventory, Procurement, Biosecurity & Vet
- **View only:** Dashboard, Smart AI Engine, Reports & BI, Settings, Weather & Fields, Smart Devices & IoT, Marketplace

### Finance Manager

Finance, sales, credit profile, reports.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** Financial Management, Sales & Customers, Feed & Inventory, Procurement, Credit Profile
- **View only:** Dashboard, Daily Planner, Reports & BI, Trade & Export, Settings, Marketplace

### Sales Manager

Sales, marketplace, trade documents.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** Sales & Customers, Trade & Export, Marketplace
- **View only:** Dashboard, Daily Planner, Feed & Inventory, Reports & BI, Settings, Weather & Fields

### Supervisor

Day-to-day production records.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** Broiler Poultry, Village Chicken, Piggery, Fish Farming, Duck Management, Goats & Sheep, Horticulture, Production Records, Feed & Inventory, Biosecurity & Vet
- **View only:** Dashboard, Daily Planner, Procurement, Settings, Weather & Fields, Smart Devices & IoT

### Farmhand

Logs daily records.

- **View and add only:** Broiler Poultry, Village Chicken, Piggery, Fish Farming, Duck Management, Goats & Sheep, Horticulture, Production Records
- **View only:** Dashboard, Daily Planner, Mobile App, Weather & Fields

### Veterinary Officer

Animal health and biosecurity.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** Biosecurity & Vet
- **View and add only:** Broiler Poultry, Village Chicken, Piggery, Fish Farming, Duck Management, Goats & Sheep, Horticulture, Production Records
- **View only:** Dashboard, Daily Planner, Mobile App

### Driver

Mobile app, deliveries.

- **View only:** Dashboard, Daily Planner, Mobile App

Modules in this account type: Dashboard, Smart AI Engine, Daily Planner, Broiler Poultry, Village Chicken, Piggery, Fish Farming, Duck Management, Goats & Sheep, Horticulture, Production Records, Feed & Inventory, Sales & Customers, Financial Management, Credit Profile, Human Resources, Procurement, Biosecurity & Vet, Weather & Fields, Smart Devices & IoT, Reports & BI, Trade & Export, Marketplace, Assets & Infra, Mobile App, Settings, Team & Permissions.


## Government accounts

| Role | Who it is for |
|---|---|
| **Administrator** | Manages the team, branches and advisories; sees everything |
| **Dashboard viewer** | Read-only district & national dashboards |
| **Extension supervisor** | Oversees officers in the branch, issues advisories |
| **Extension officer** | Caseload of farms, visits, direct advice |

### Administrator

Manages the team, branches and advisories; sees everything.

- **Full access (view, add, change, delete):** District & national dashboards, District registry, Advisories, Extension caseload, Visits & follow-ups, Programmes, Participant enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices, Team & Permissions, Organisation settings

### Dashboard viewer

### Extension supervisor

Oversees officers in the branch, issues advisories.

- **Full access (view, add, change, delete):** Extension caseload, Visits & follow-ups
- **View, add and change (no delete):** Programmes, Participant enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices
- **View and add only:** Advisories
- **View only:** District & national dashboards, District registry, Organisation settings

### Extension officer

Caseload of farms, visits, direct advice.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** Extension caseload, Visits & follow-ups
- **View and add only:** Advisories
- **View only:** District & national dashboards, District registry, Programmes, Participant enrolment, Support ledger

Modules in this account type: District & national dashboards, District registry, Advisories, Extension caseload, Visits & follow-ups, Programmes, Participant enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices, Team & Permissions, Organisation settings.


## Programme partner (NGO / donor) accounts

| Role | Who it is for |
|---|---|
| **Administrator** | Manages team, branches and programmes |
| **Programme manager** | Runs programmes: enrolment, support, notices |
| **M&E officer** | Records indicator readings and views results |
| **Observer (donor / auditor)** | Read-only access to programmes and dashboards |

### Administrator

Manages team, branches and programmes.

- **Full access (view, add, change, delete):** Programmes, Farm enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices, District & national dashboards, District registry, Advisories, Team & Permissions, Organisation settings

### Programme manager

Runs programmes: enrolment, support, notices.

- **Full access (view, add, change, delete):** Programmes, Farm enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices
- **View and add only:** Advisories
- **View only:** District & national dashboards, District registry, Organisation settings

### M&E officer

Records indicator readings and views results.

- **View, add and change (no delete):** M&E indicators
- **View only:** Programmes, Farm enrolment, Support ledger, District & national dashboards, District registry, Organisation settings

Read-only access to programmes and dashboards.

- **View only:** Programmes, Farm enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices, District & national dashboards, District registry

Modules in this account type: Programmes, Farm enrolment, Support ledger, M&E indicators, Programme notices, District & national dashboards, District registry, Advisories, Team & Permissions, Organisation settings.


## Notes

- The owner (organisation administrator) always has full access to every module of the company, whatever their role is called.
- A person with a custom permission set uses that set instead of their role's default.
- Government, extension and partner staff only ever see de-identified information about farms that have chosen to share their data (Part 1, Data-sharing consent).
# Appendix F — Current Limits and Preview Features

This appendix lists, in one place, what the platform does not do yet, which screens show sample or simulated information, and behaviours that can surprise you. It is based on how the platform was reviewed in October 2026. Features marked here may change; check with your administrator before relying on anything listed.

## Screens that show sample or simulated information

These screens exist so you can see how a feature will look. They are not live data about your farm.

| Screen | What you see |
|---|---|
| The public landing page and the individual (buyer) dashboard | A built-in catalogue of sample listings (27 on the public page). They show how the marketplace will look. |
| Smart Vision | Random sample answers. It does not analyse real pictures or video, and the camera pictures are placeholders. |
| Smart Devices, Water Management, Aquaculture, Poultry Houses, Energy and Soil Depth | Sample devices. Readings come from buttons such as **Simulate Reading** or **Refresh**; they are not sent by real sensors. |
| Device Gateway and Farm Gateway | Demonstration screens. They do not receive data from hardware. |
| Energy | View-only sample figures. You cannot add arrays, batteries or loads. |
| Activity Log | Sample entries only. Nothing on the platform writes real entries to it yet. |
| First open of several pages | Sample records are put in place the first time you open Animal Health, Staff and Labour, Procurement, Requisitions, Crop Rotation, Sustainability and the Loans tab of Financial AI, so you can see how they work. Delete them and enter your own. |
| Reports: Profitability tab | Four sample production expenses (total ZMW 40,050) are placed on the device the first time the tab opens if there are cycles and no expenses. They cannot be edited or deleted. |
| AgriFood Market, AgriSupply and AgriServices (farm side) | Sample listings plus any listing you register on this device. A listing you register there stays on this device only. |
| Agro Dealers, Equipment Hire and Vet Services (farm side) | A live list of providers from the platform when it can be reached, otherwise sample providers (a yellow bar says so). |

## Things that are not available yet

**Account**
- There is no working **Forgot password?** and no screen to change your password, name, phone or photo. Ask your company administrator or AGRINUXES support.
- The farm workspace has no **Sign Out** button. Vendor workspaces and buyer dashboards have one. On a shared computer use a private browsing window.
- Plans, free-trial counters and payment screens are not connected to the screens you use today.
- The sign-in box asks for an email address; phone-only sign-in may be refused by the browser.

**Buyers and marketplaces**
- On the individual dashboard the **Order** and **Enquire** buttons do not open a request form yet. Contact the seller by phone.
- Individual accounts cannot track, cancel or review requests, and cannot edit their profile. Favourites are kept in the page only and are lost when you refresh.
- Farmers have no screen to write a review, and the farm workspace has no list of past orders or bookings with vendors.
- Vendors are not sent an email, text or in-app message when a buyer sends a request. Check your tabs regularly.
- Farm-side directory cards for live providers may show no services, reviews or certificates.
- There is no farmer-facing booking page for transport or AgriServices providers. Record such requests in your own workspace as jobs.

**Vendor workspaces**
- Orders are recorded by your staff after a buyer makes contact. There is no automatic intake of marketplace orders into your **Orders** tab.
- Saved orders cannot be edited (cancel and record again). Listings have no photo upload, and there are no quality-grade or harvest-date fields for food listings (use the description).
- Invoice **Send** changes the status only. It does not send an email or text.
- On an AgriFood Seller workspace, the **Catalogue & prices** and **Stock** quick-action buttons on the Overview may not take you to a tab.

**Production**
- There is no button that creates a production program from scratch, so **Transfer Output** is only available on cycles that already belong to a program.
- Insects and worms, aquatic plants and forestry enterprises have no event types on the **Events** tab (the Daily Log and Care Plan still work).
- Entering **Hatching Eggs** in an egg collection adds them to Inventory but does not start an incubation cycle for you.
- Editing or deleting an event does not change the Finance or Inventory entry it created. Correct those pages as well.

**Business pages**
- Custom processing recipes have no inputs or outputs, and there is no recipe editor.
- A few Reports figures read information that is not saved, and show zero: Processing Efficiency, Batch Profitability, and the per-enterprise revenue strip on the Dashboard and in Reports.
- Trade contract **deposit paid** and **fully paid** only tick a box. They do not create income in Finance.
- On Credit Profile, a Finance Manager sees **Revoke** for a lender link but may get a permission message, because revoking needs delete access.
- Requisitions **Export CSV** may not produce a usable file.

**Tools and intelligence**
- Smart Engine rules for poultry-house temperature and battery charge cannot fire, because the Poultry Houses and Energy pages save the information under different names.
- Notifications for water, poultry, energy and soil alerts may show generic titles.
- Smart Devices and Device Gateway share one saved device list with different record shapes. Use only one of them.
- In Animal Health always fill in **End Date** when you enter a withdrawal period. A treatment with a withdrawal period and no end date can break the Overview page.

**Government, extension and partners**
- There is no bulk-enrol control. Enrol organisations one at a time.
- The support ledger cannot be edited or deleted. A wrong amount must be raised with your administrator.
- Turning data sharing off does not remove existing extension assignments or programme enrolments.
- The National Map and Ecosystem pages ignore the province and district filter bar.
- Whoever creates a government or partner organisation becomes its administrator, whichever role card was chosen. The extra roles (supervisor, partner administrator, M&E officer, observer) are added by an administrator.
- The default M&E officer role may not see the **+ Reading** button on the results framework, because that button needs permission to edit Programmes.

## Behaviours worth knowing

- Items from other modules are matched to your stock **by name**, ignoring capital letters, spaces and punctuation. A sale of "Live broilers" does nothing to stock if no item has that name.
- Income shown in Finance, the Dashboard, Financial AI and Reports is the Finance ledger only. A fulfilled sales order is already in it. Draft and confirmed orders are not income yet.
- The Sales page's own **Revenue** tile adds all orders that are not cancelled, including drafts, so it can be higher than Finance.
- Government overview percentages count all active business accounts as the total, but only farms and cooperatives as "reporting", so the percentage can look low.

# Appendix G — Glossary

Words used in the platform and in this manual, in alphabetical order.

**Account** — Your sign-in. It belongs to a company (a farm, a vendor, a government office or a programme partner) or, for buyers, to one person.

**Administrator (organisation administrator)** — The owner or director of a company. Can manage the team, permissions, branches and settings, and always has full access.

**Advisory** — A message from government or an extension service to farmers in an area, for example a pest warning.

**Agro-dealer** — A business that sells farm inputs such as seed, fertiliser and chemicals.

**Backup and Restore** — A page that saves the information kept in your browser to a file and puts it back on another device. It does not contain data already kept in your company account.

**Baseline** — The starting value of an indicator before a programme begins, used to measure progress.

**Batch** — One group of animals, plants or product handled together, for example one flock of broilers or one lot of milled maize.

**Benchmark** — A target or normal value suggested by a template, for example feed conversion of 1.7 for broilers.

**Branch** — A site or office of a company, such as a farm location or shop. Staff can be limited to a branch.

**Business type** — The kind of business you register as: Farmer / Farm, Cooperative, Agro Dealer, Vet / Animal Health, Equipment Hire, AgriFood Seller, AgriSupply Provider, AgriServices Provider, Agro Processor or Transport / Haulage.

**Care guide** — A list of feeding, vaccination, medicine, spray, fertiliser, weighing, checking and task reminders, each due on a day of the cycle (see Part 2).

**Care Plan** — The tab on a cycle that shows what is due today, what is overdue and what is coming.

**Caseload** — The farms an extension officer looks after.

**Cohort** — A group of participants enrolled in a programme at one time.

**Company account** — The shared, saved copy of your company's information on the platform, seen by everyone in the company on every device.

**Consent (data-sharing consent)** — Your company's choice to let government, partners and lenders see de-identified or shared information about your farm.

**Cooperative** — A group of smallholders who run crops, livestock or marketing together. Uses the farm workspace.

**Credit profile** — A summary of your farm's records, finances, trading and support network that you can share with a lender.

**Cycle** — One run of production from a template: a batch of broilers, a maize season, a pond of fish. A cycle has stages, daily records, events and a care plan.

**Cycle type (variant)** — The kind of cycle you choose when you start one, for example Broiler Grow-Out or Layer from Day-Old. It decides which daily fields show.

**Daily Log** — The tab where you record each day's measurements for a cycle.

**Day 0** — The day a cycle starts. Care-guide items are counted in days from day 0.

**Disaggregation** — Splitting an indicator by a group, for example by sex or age, so a programme can see who benefits.

**Enrolment** — Adding a farm or organisation to a programme.

**Enterprise** — One line of business on your farm, such as broilers, maize or tilapia ponds. It is built from a template.

**Event** — Something that happens during a cycle that is not a daily measurement: a purchase, treatment, vaccination, mortality, harvest, sale and so on.

**Extension officer** — An agricultural adviser who visits farms and advises farmers.

**FAMACHA** — A method of judging anaemia in goats and sheep from eyelid colour, used to decide whether to deworm.

**FCR (feed conversion ratio)** — Feed eaten divided by weight gained. Lower is better.

**Fulfil** — To mark a sales order as delivered. In AGRINUXES this posts the income to Finance and takes the goods out of Inventory.

**Hen-day production** — The percentage of hens that laid on a day.

**Indicator** — A measure of a programme's results, for example the number of farmers trained.

**Inventory** — Your stock of feed, medicine, seed, produce and other items.

**Item (stock item)** — One thing you keep in stock, with a unit, minimum level and cost.

**KPI** — A key figure that shows how well something is going.

**Ledger** — The record of every income and expense in Finance.

**Logframe (results framework)** — A programme's plan of results and indicators, with targets and how they will be checked.

**Lot** — A processing batch given a code so the product can be traced back to the farms that supplied it.

**M&E (monitoring and evaluation)** — Tracking a programme's results and judging whether it worked.

**Marketplace** — The part of the platform where buyers find vendors and where vendors show what they offer.

**Movement** — A change to stock: stock in, stock out, adjustment or transfer.

**Normal range** — The minimum and maximum a daily field should normally fall within. Values outside it are flagged.

**Onboarding (set-up wizard)** — The five steps a new company goes through to set up its workspace.

**Output** — What a cycle produces and where it goes: sale, inventory, processing, own use and so on.

**Partner (programme partner)** — An NGO, donor or programme organisation that runs support programmes for farms.

**Permission** — What a person may do in a module: view, create, edit or delete.

**Phase (care guide)** — A care-guide item that applies for a whole span of days, such as "Starter feed, day 0 to 13".

**Phase (production program)** — One step of a multi-step production program.

**Programme** — A funded support project that enrols farms and delivers inputs, cash or training.

**Provider** — A vendor's public profile on the marketplace.

**Record field** — One box on the daily log, such as Feed Consumed or Water Temperature.

**Requisition** — An internal request to buy or be given something.

**Role** — A named set of permissions, such as Director, Production Manager or Farmhand.

**Saved on this device only** — Information kept in the browser of one device, not shared with colleagues and not backed up unless you use Backup and Restore.

**Stage** — A step within a cycle, for example Brooding, Grower, Finishing.

**Starter guide** — The built-in care guide that comes with every template, offered as typical practice only.

**Support ledger** — A programme's record of what it has delivered to each participant.

**Template** — A ready-made production plan with stages, daily record fields, targets and a starter care guide. You can copy and change one and save it as your own.

**Thin file** — A farm with too little history to be scored properly for credit.

**Traceability** — Being able to follow a product back from the shelf to the lot and the farms that supplied it.

**Transfer (stock)** — Moving stock out of an item to another place you name.

**Transfer output (production)** — Moving part of a cycle's output to another cycle or selling it, recorded as a decision.

**Vendor** — Any business account that is not a farm or cooperative: agro-dealers, vets, equipment hire, transport, AgriFood, AgriSupply, AgriServices and processors.

**Version (template)** — A number that goes up each time you save changes to one of your saved templates.

**Withdrawal period** — The days after a medicine or spray during which meat, milk, eggs or produce must not be sold or eaten.

# Appendix H — Troubleshooting and Frequently Asked Questions

Short answers to the questions people ask most. Each points to the part of the manual with the full steps.

## Signing in and your account

**I forgot my password.** The **Forgot password?** link does not do anything yet. Ask your company administrator or AGRINUXES support (Part 1; Appendix F).

**I cannot sign in with my phone number.** The sign-in box asks for an email address, and a browser may refuse a phone number. Use the email address on your account.

**The page says I am signed out after a while.** Sessions last about 8 hours. Sign in again.

**There is no Sign Out button in my farm workspace.** That is how the farm workspace works today. On a shared computer, use a private browsing window and close it afterwards. Vendor workspaces and buyer dashboards do have **Sign Out**.

**A new team member sees the set-up wizard.** Add team members only after the owner has finished the wizard. A member who signs in while the workspace is empty is shown the wizard too (Part 1).

**A menu item is missing.** Each person sees only what their role allows. Ask an administrator to open **Team & Permissions** and change your role or permissions (Part 1; Appendix E).

**A page says "You do not have access".** Your role does not include that area. An administrator can change it.

**A page says it "could not be loaded".** The platform could not be reached. Check your internet connection and press **Try again**. Automatic entries you made meanwhile are kept and sent when the connection returns.

## Where is my data? Why can my colleague not see it?

**My colleague cannot see the records I entered.** Some pages keep information only in the browser on the device you used (for example Animal Health, Staff and Labour, Procurement, Requisitions, Crop Rotation and the device pages). The farm workspace, Finance, Sales, Inventory, Trade and Export and Credit Profile are shared. Part 1 and Part 5 list every page (Part 1, "Where your data is kept").

**I changed phone or browser and my records are gone.** Records kept on one device do not move by themselves. Use **Backup & Restore** to save them to a file and restore them on the other device. Records in your company account are there on any device.

**Is my data backed up?** Company information is kept on the platform's server. Information kept only in your browser is not backed up unless you make a backup file.

## Production, templates and the daily log

**I picked the wrong template when I set up.** Add the right enterprise from **Settings**, start your cycles there, and stop using the old one (Part 2).

**A box I expect is missing from the Daily Log.** The boxes follow your template and your cycle type. Choose the correct cycle type when you start a cycle, or add the box yourself with **Customise fields** (Part 2).

**Can I change a template just for one cycle?** Fields and the care guide follow the enterprise's template. Switch the enterprise to a different saved template, or save a new version. A cycle keeps the stages it was created with (Part 2).

**The care guide says to give a vaccine on a day that does not suit me.** Starter guides are typical practice. Change the day in **Set my standards** and save it as your template. Confirm every medicine and dose with your vet (Part 2).

**My spreadsheet will not import.** Open the preview and read the Status column. Common causes: the date cannot be read, a number box contains text, a required box is empty, or the stage name does not match. Download the **Template** button's file and copy its headings (Part 2).

**Do I log a purchase in Production and again in Finance?** No. A purchase, treatment, vaccination or sale event posts to Finance for you. Enter it once (Part 4, "How the modules talk to each other").

**I corrected an event but Finance still shows the old amount.** Editing or deleting an event does not change the Finance entry it made. Correct the entry in Finance as well.

**I closed a cycle too early.** A closed cycle cannot be reopened. Start a new cycle (Part 3).

## Stock, sales and money

**My stock did not go down after I fulfilled an order.** Stock is matched by name. The order line "Live broilers" only changes an item called "Live broilers" (capital letters, spaces and punctuation do not matter). Rename the item or the line so they match (Part 4).

**A stock-out was refused.** You tried to take out more than is in stock (the message says how much there is). Record the stock-in first, or correct the quantity.

**Finance shows less income than the Sales page.** Finance counts income from fulfilled orders. The Sales **Revenue** tile also counts drafts and confirmed orders (Part 4).

**A payment was refused.** A payment cannot be more than the balance due, and a cancelled order takes no payments.

**How is "overdue" decided?** An order that is not fully paid and whose due date has passed shows **Overdue** (Part 4).

## Vendors and buyers

**A buyer contacted me but nothing appears in Orders.** Orders are recorded by your staff after contact. Record the order with **New Order** (Part 7).

**I do not appear when farmers search.** Complete your **Marketplace** tab (name, description, district, services, certificates) and keep your services up to date. "Verified" appears only after a platform administrator checks your certificates (Part 6).

**I pressed Order on the buyer dashboard and nothing opened.** The **Order** and **Enquire** buttons are not connected yet. Phone the seller (Appendix F).

## Government, extension and partners

**The government dashboard shows few farms.** Only farms that have switched on data sharing are counted in the detailed figures. Ask farms to tick the consent box in **Settings** (Part 1; Part 8).

**An officer cannot see a farm.** The farm must have given consent and be in the officer's area or caseload.

**I cannot add a reading to the results framework.** The **+ Reading** button needs permission to edit Programmes. Ask your administrator (Part 8).

## Good habits

- Enter each fact once, in the place that matches the job. Let the platform post it everywhere else.
- Record daily, even if only a few boxes.
- Review the starter care guide for each enterprise before you rely on it, and save your own version.
- Make a backup file from **Backup & Restore** regularly if you use pages that are saved on one device.
- Keep your own sign-in private. Give each colleague their own account under **Team & Permissions**.

# Appendix I — First-Week Checklists

Tick these off in your first week. Each line points to where the steps are explained.

## Farm or cooperative owner

**Day 1**
- Register a **Farm / Business** account and sign in (Part 1).
- Finish the set-up wizard: choose **Farmer / Farm** or **Cooperative**, name the business, choose categories, add one enterprise from a template and press **Finish setup** (Part 1).
- Open **Team & Permissions** and add your key people with the right roles. Do this after the wizard (Part 1).
- Open **Settings** and check your business details and the approval workflow (Part 1).

**Day 2**
- Open **Production** and start your first cycle from the enterprise: choose the cycle type, enter the numbers, name it (Part 2; Part 3).
- Open the **Care Plan** tab. Read the starter guide with your vet or extension officer. Use **Set my standards** to change it and save it as your own template (Part 2).
- Open the **Daily Log** and use **Customise fields** to add the boxes you really want, such as feed brand (Part 2).

**Days 3 to 7**
- Record the **Daily Log** every day and tick off the **Care Plan** items (Part 2).
- Log events as they happen: purchases, treatments, vaccinations, mortality, harvest, sales (Part 3).
- Add your stock items in **Inventory** with minimum levels (Part 4).
- Add customers in **Sales** and record your first order (Part 4).
- Look at **Finance** and the **Dashboard**. Check that the automatic entries look right (Part 4).
- Make a backup in **Backup & Restore** if you use pages saved on one device (Part 1).

**Later**
- Turn on data sharing if you want an extension officer, a programme or a lender to see your farm (Part 1; Part 4).
- Check your **Credit Profile** and see what would improve your score (Part 4).

## Agro-dealer, AgriSupply provider or AgriFood seller

- Register a Farm / Business account with your business type and finish the wizard (Part 1).
- Open **Settings** and set your district.
- Fill in the **Marketplace** tab so buyers can find you: description, services and certificates (Part 6).
- Build your **Product Catalog** (or **Supply Catalog** or **Produce Listings**): name, unit, price, stock (Part 7).
- Record your opening stock in **Stock Management** or **Pricing & Stock** (Part 7).
- Record each order in **Orders**, move it through its statuses and raise an invoice (Part 6; Part 7).
- Add your staff under **Users & Permissions** (Part 6).

## Agro processor

- Register, finish the wizard and fill in the **Marketplace** tab (Part 1; Part 6).
- Record raw material intake in **Farm Sourcing** (Part 7).
- Record each batch in **Processing Logs** and give every lot a code (Part 7).
- Keep finished goods in **Inventory** and look up lots in **Traceability** (Part 7).
- Record **Orders** and invoice them (Part 6; Part 7).

## Vet, equipment hire, transport or AgriServices business

- Register, finish the wizard, set your district and fill in the **Marketplace** tab (Part 1; Part 6).
- Add your services and prices in **Services Catalog** (or routes and rates for transport) (Part 6).
- Vet: add patients, take appointments, write prescriptions (Part 6).
- Equipment hire and transport: add your machines or vehicles in the fleet tab, record bookings or jobs and log maintenance (Part 6).
- AgriServices: schedule jobs in **Job Schedule** (Part 6).
- Raise an invoice for each completed job or visit and watch **Revenue** (Part 6).

## Individual buyer

- Register as an **Individual** (Part 1).
- Browse the marketplace by category, search and filter (Part 8).
- Save favourites and call the seller to order (Part 8).

## Extension officer

- Sign in (an administrator creates supervisor accounts) (Part 1).
- Look at **Farms available** and add farms to your caseload (Part 8).
- Record each visit with notes and follow-up dates (Part 8).
- Send advisories to your farmers (Part 8).

## Government staff

- Register on the **Gov / Partner** route or be added by an administrator (Part 1).
- Choose your area and open the dashboards (Part 8).
- Explore the **National Map** and **Ecosystem** pages (Part 8).
- Send advisories (administrators) (Part 8).

## Programme partner

- Register on the **Gov / Partner** route and add your team with the right roles (Part 1; Part 8).
- Create a programme: sector, dates, budget, targeting districts, eligibility (Part 8).
- Enrol eligible farms one at a time and record support in the support ledger (Part 8).
- Build the results framework with indicators, baselines and targets, and record readings (Part 8).
- Generate the donor report (Part 8).
